# Project: Neev (नींव) — UPPSC Exam Prep Platform

## Branding (renamed 2026-07-13: PrayasUP → Neev)
The product is **Neev** (Devanagari **नींव**, "foundation"). This replaced the old
working name "PrayasUP" across the whole codebase on 2026-07-13.
- **Wordmark convention (the ONE place this is decided):** English brand = `Neev`
  (Latin), Hindi brand = `नींव` (Devanagari). नींव is the real Hindi word for
  "foundation" — a deliberate choice, NOT a transliteration of "Neev" (नीव) and NOT
  the old Devanagari transliteration "प्रयासUP". Both message files, the two PWA
  manifests, `brand-mark.tsx`, and the satori share-card/OG renderers follow this.
  The old two-tone wordmark accented "UP" because it meant *Uttar Pradesh*; "Neev"
  has no equivalent sub-token, so it renders monochrome (the gauge glyph carries the
  color). NOTE: "प्रयास" (attempt/effort) is a common Hindi word used ~36× in
  `hi.json` for genuine UI copy — only the brand form "प्रयासUP" was renamed, never
  bare प्रयास.
- **Mentor persona** now introduces itself as Neev (नींव) — `services/mentor/prompts.ts`.
  Every other AI persona (examiner/rubric, question-setter, notes/chapter/CA/qgen
  authors) is role-framed and never named a brand, so none needed a rename.
- **Scope/domain/keys:** the workspace package is `@neev/shared` (was `@prayasup/shared`);
  the production domain is **`neevstudy.com`** (was `neev.app`, which was itself
  `prayasup.app` before that — see the Domain-portability sweep session below); client
  storage/IDB keys and Render/CI infra names are `neev-*` (the infra/key naming didn't
  need a second rename, only the literal domain string did).
- **Deliberately left as-is (historical/technical, not live branding):** (1) the
  session-log entries BELOW keep the old name / `prayasup.app` / `demo@prayasup.app` /
  `neev.app` as a factual changelog of what was done when. (2) `use-srs-review-queue.ts`'s
  `LEGACY_LOCALSTORAGE_KEY = "prayasup-srs-reviews"` — the *source* of a one-time
  migration; renaming it would orphan reviews written under the old key.
- **FOLLOW-UPS (not done in the rename session):** (a) the GitHub repo is still
  `studyApp` — renaming it can break CI/CD/remote hookups, so do it deliberately as
  its own step. (b) The existing demo DB account is still `demo@prayasup.app` (that's
  data, not code); the seed default is now `demo@neevstudy.com`, so re-run `pnpm demo:seed`
  to mint the current-domain account when wanted.

## What this is
A bilingual (Hindi/English equal-first) web platform for UPPSC (UP PCS) aspirants.
Flagship feature: AI Answer Writing Evaluation for Mains descriptive answers
(typed or handwritten-photo upload), scored against a UPPSC-style rubric with
streaming feedback. Also: PYQ-based MCQ practice, syllabus-mapped learning,
AI-summarized current affairs (UP-specific focus), FSRS spaced repetition,
analytics, RAG doubt-solving chatbot.

## Architecture (FIXED — never deviate)
- Monorepo: /apps/web (Vite + React 19 SPA, TypeScript, React Router v7),
  /apps/api (Express, TypeScript), /packages/shared (types, zod schemas).
- NO Next.js anywhere. The web app is a pure SPA; the ONLY backend is the
  Express API. ONE explicit exception: binary blob storage. The browser
  uploads/reads handwritten-answer page photos directly against Supabase
  Storage with the anon key (apps/web/src/lib/supabase.ts), behind the
  bucket's own dev-permissive RLS policy — this avoids proxying multi-MB
  images through Express. Every other read/write, including the submission
  row itself, still goes through the API with zero exceptions.
- DB: Supabase cloud Postgres + pgvector. SAME project for local dev and
  production. All schema changes via SQL migration files in
  /supabase/migrations, applied with supabase CLI (db push).
- UI: shadcn/ui + Tailwind v4. Server state: TanStack Query. Client state:
  Zustand (minimal). i18n: react-i18next; all app URLs are /:locale/* with en
  as default.
- Fonts: Inter (latin) + Noto Sans Devanagari via Fontsource. Hindi typography
  is first-class, never a fallback.
- LLM (app runtime): Anthropic API — claude-sonnet-5 for answer evaluation +
  doubt chat; claude-haiku-4-5 for high-volume tasks (summaries, MCQ
  explanations, translation drafts). Model ids live in ONE constants module,
  never inline.
- Embeddings: OpenAI text-embedding-3-small (1536-dim, matches vector(1536)),
  behind src/lib/embeddings.ts so the provider is swappable.
- OCR (handwritten answer submissions): behind apps/api/src/services/ocr/
  (`OcrProvider` interface — `transcribe({pages, language, onDelta, signal})
  -> {text, confidence}`). Only `claudeVisionProvider` (claude-sonnet-5
  vision) is implemented; a Google Cloud Vision fallback can slot in later
  behind the same interface without touching call sites (evaluate.ts,
  routes/stream.ts) — e.g. tried when claude-vision errors or returns
  low confidence. `getOcrProvider()` is the one place that picks.
- All AI responses stream via SSE from Express under /api/v1/stream/*. The web
  app consumes SSE ONLY through src/lib/sse.ts built on
  @microsoft/fetch-event-source (POST + Authorization: Bearer <Supabase JWT>
  headers — auth landed in the Auth phase; see the session log).
- NO mock data anywhere. Every screen reads real rows from Supabase.

## Dev conventions
- **Node 22+ is a hard requirement, not just a preference.**
  `@supabase/supabase-js`'s realtime client requires native `WebSocket`,
  which only exists in Node 22+ — on Node 20 it throws "Node.js detected
  but native WebSocket not found" the moment any Supabase client is
  constructed (`apps/api/src/lib/supabase.ts`, called at boot from
  `index.ts`). This broke for real: `ca-run.yml` was failing in CI and the
  Render deploy (`apps/api/Dockerfile`, `node:20-slim`) was very likely
  crash-looping on every boot. Fixed by moving every pinned Node version
  to 22: `apps/api/Dockerfile` base image, root `package.json`
  `engines.node`, and `node-version:` in every `.github/workflows/*.yml`
  that runs Node (`ca-run`, `ci`, `daily-build`, `nightly-settle`,
  `notifications`, `qgen-topup` — `backup.yml` runs no Node, untouched).
  Never downgrade any of these back to 20.
- pnpm workspaces. `pnpm dev` runs web (:3000) + api (:4000) concurrently.
- Env: /apps/web/.env.local uses VITE_* and holds browser-safe values ONLY
  (Supabase URL, anon key, API URL). /apps/api/.env holds all secrets (service
  role, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY). .env.example files always current.
- Auth (LANDED — Auth phase): every /api/v1/* request (incl. SSE) carries a
  Supabase JWT; `requireAuth` verifies it (JWKS/ES256, `authenticated` audience
  + expiry) and binds the user id via AsyncLocalStorage (`currentUserId()`).
  The API talks to Postgres with the SERVICE ROLE (bypasses RLS) and scopes
  every query by `currentUserId()`; strict per-user RLS (0053) is the
  defense-in-depth layer for the browser's direct anon-key access (storage).
  The old DEV_USER_ID stand-in is gone; run `pnpm --filter api migrate:dev-user`
  once to fold pre-auth dev data into your real account.
- Bilingual content: JSONB {"hi","en"} columns named *_i18n. Publish gate =
  both languages present.
- **Embeddings MUST stay in sync with published content.** A published/edited
  question, syllabus node, note, or current-affairs item that has no embedding
  is invisible to the mentor's RAG grounding — this is now backstopped by
  automation, not memory alone: `.github/workflows/ca-run.yml` runs `ca:embed`
  right after every `ca:run`, and `.github/workflows/nightly-settle.yml` runs
  `ingest:embed --missing-only` + `ca:embed` + `notes:embed --missing-only`
  every night at 00:05 IST regardless of WHICH path published the content
  (Review Queue admin approval, a manual bulk-ingest session, `ca:run`'s own
  inline embed silently failing on one item) — so max staleness is bounded to
  well under 24h even if a same-session re-embed is forgotten. STILL run
  `pnpm ingest:embed` (or `--missing-only`) yourself at the end of any session
  that publishes/edits question/syllabus text (a PYQ ingest/load, the
  CSAT/answer-key gate `ingest:regate`, a `resolve`+reload, the Hindi overlay,
  any `is_published` flip) — the nightly cron is a safety net for what gets
  forgotten, not a reason to skip same-session correctness (a student could hit
  the gap in the hours before the next nightly run). `ingest:embed` clears each
  re-embedded source's old chunks first (no shrink-orphans) and has a
  `--missing-only` low-churn mode (inserts only, durable/convergent on re-run —
  prefer it when only closing a coverage gap, since a full re-embed churns the
  HNSW index hard enough to trip Postgres `statement_timeout`). VERIFY with
  `pnpm ingest:embed:verify` (per source_type: eligible vs embedded,
  missing/orphan; `--purge-orphans` clears source-deleted orphans a re-embed
  can't reach). The same coverage delta is surfaced in `pnpm cost:report`.
  Notes have `pnpm notes:embed [--missing-only]`; current-affairs have
  `pnpm ca:embed [--all] [--limit N]` (missing-only by default). Every embed
  writer shares one statement-timeout-hardened upsert (`lib/embed-upsert.ts`).
- API: Express routes under /api/v1/*, zod-validated inputs, {data,error}
  envelope. SSE endpoints under /api/v1/stream/*.
- Routing: app pages under /:locale/*; route modules lazy; anything shareable
  (filters, tabs) lives in URL/search params.
- Content acquisition is automated: /content-sources.yaml + `pnpm
  content:fetch` download real UPPSC files into /content-raw (binaries
  gitignored; manifest committed). NEVER ask the user to manually download
  something the script can fetch; on fetch failure, print the exact URL for a
  one-click manual grab.
- Commit after every working feature. Never commit secrets. Mobile-first:
  verify every screen at 390px.
- **HARD RULE — run a FULL production build before pushing any change that
  touches a shared/root-level file** (anything under `providers/`, `lib/`,
  `main.tsx`, `router.tsx`, the app shell, or any module imported by them).
  `pnpm --filter web build` (and `pnpm --filter api typecheck`), NOT just
  `pnpm dev`. This is not a nice-to-have: a root-level provider that throws on
  mount takes down EVERY route at once, and `tsx watch`/Vite's dev-mode module
  resolution is more forgiving than a real build. **Verified empirically
  (2026-07-28):** deleting `apps/web/src/lib/guest.ts` makes the build fail
  cleanly and immediately with `error TS2307: Cannot find module '@/lib/guest'`,
  naming BOTH call sites (`use-claim-trial.ts`, `auth-provider.tsx`) — so this
  bug class genuinely cannot survive a local build. A green `pnpm dev` proves
  nothing about it. Prefer also loading the built output (`apps/web/dist`) in a
  real browser once, not just trusting a quiet dev server — the DoD's "no
  console errors" means the BUILT app, not the HMR session.
- **Portable paths only — never hardcode a machine-specific absolute path**
  (`/Users/…`, `/home/…`, `C:\Users\…`, `…/Desktop/Code/…`). <!-- portable-paths-allow: documents the forbidden patterns --> Every script
  resolves filesystem paths from `import.meta.url`/`import.meta.dirname` (reuse
  `ROOT`/`CONTENT_RAW`/`PARSED_DIR` from `apps/api/src/ingest/_shared.ts` or the
  `ROOT` in `scripts/fetch-content.ts`), never a hardcoded prefix or an assumed
  `process.cwd()`, so a script works the same run from repo root, from inside a
  subpackage, in CI, or in the API Docker image. `pnpm check:paths`
  (`scripts/check-portable-paths.mjs`) is a CI-enforced standing guard against
  this — it ALSO fails on a hardcoded known-stale domain (`prayasup.app`,
  `neev.app`) or a bare Cloudflare Pages auto-domain (`*.pages.dev`); read a
  domain from `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`/`ALLOWED_ORIGIN_SUFFIXES`/`VITE_SITE_URL`
  config instead of hardcoding one (the current production domain is
  `neevstudy.com`, see the Branding note above). See `docs/operations.md` →
  "Portability guard". Also: `apps/api`/`packages` import specifiers use a
  `.js` extension on `.ts` sources — never flip one to `.ts`.
- **NEVER hand-roll CLI argument parsing — there is exactly ONE parser.**
  Every CLI must call `parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2), spec, "script:name")`
  from `apps/api/src/ingest/_shared.ts` with an explicit `FlagSpec`
  (`value` / `boolean` / `positiveInt` / `positiveNumber` / `nonNegativeNumber`).
  The spec is REQUIRED so the compiler forces every caller to declare what it
  accepts. This is not style: a permissive parser silently dropped a `--dry-run`
  and ran a real, billed, production-DB-writing pass on 2026-07-31
  (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §0d) — a bare positional produced NO key, a
  shell-collapsed multi-word token became a NONSENSE key, and a valueless
  value-flag became boolean `true`, each of which silently WIDENS or REDIRECTS a
  scoped run. The repo had **six** hand-rolled dialects before consolidation;
  `pnpm check:cli-args` (`scripts/check-cli-args.mjs`, CI-enforced) now fails the
  build on any local arg parser or direct `process.argv` flag-sniffing, and
  `pnpm --filter api test:args` regression-locks all 41 CLIs' specs. Only
  `process.argv.slice(2)` (the parser's input) and `process.argv[1]` (the
  invoked-directly module guard) may touch argv directly. **When verifying a CLI
  guard, use the pure argv harness — never invoke the real pipeline; that is
  precisely what caused the incident.**
- **NEVER `git checkout -- <file>` / `git restore <file>` TO UNDO AN
  EXPERIMENTAL EDIT. It is not an undo — it restores from the INDEX/HEAD and so
  silently discards every uncommitted change in that file, not just the one you
  made.** This destroyed a whole session's work on `mentor-insights.ts`
  (2026-08-12) while reverting a one-line `sed` from a negative control;
  nothing was recoverable — `git fsck --unreachable` held no commit and no blob,
  because a popped stash's objects had already been pruned — and the file had to
  be rewritten from scratch. **The pattern for any deliberate temporary edit
  (negative controls, A/B arms, "does this guard actually fire"):**
  `cp <file> /tmp/backup` first, restore with `cp`, and verify the restore with
  `shasum -a 256` every single time. `git checkout` is only safe on a path with
  no uncommitted work — which is exactly the case you cannot be sure of
  mid-experiment.
- **NEVER `git stash` in this checkout.** Concurrent sessions are normal here
  (see the co-stage incidents below), and a stash momentarily rewrites the
  shared tree out from under them — including their untracked files when
  `--include-untracked` is used. To measure a "before" state, read it without
  touching the tree: `git show HEAD:<path>`, or a separate `git worktree`.
- **TEST/VERIFICATION CLEANUP MUST DELETE ONLY BY AN EXPLICIT, PRE-GENERATED,
  UNIQUE TAG OR ID FOR THAT RUN'S OWN SYNTHETIC ROWS — NEVER BY BROAD CRITERIA.
  Non-negotiable, permanent, all future sessions.** Any script/step that seeds
  synthetic rows for a live-DB test or verification and then cleans them up may
  target for deletion ONLY: (a) the exact row ids the seed step captured at
  insert time (`.in("id", createdIds)` / `.eq("id", id)`), or (b) a unique
  sentinel value the run generated and wrote onto every synthetic row before
  inserting it (e.g. a `content_hash`/tag prefix like
  `verify-${uniqueRunId}:...`, deleted via `.like("col", "${tag}:%")`, or a
  dedicated `purpose`/`source_id` used by nothing else). This DB is the SAME
  Supabase project for dev AND production (see Architecture), so a cleanup
  delete that matches a real row is real data loss.
  - **FORBIDDEN — a delete filter that could ever match a row the run did not
    itself create:** a time window (`created_at >= runStart`), a content/name
    pattern that also occurs in real data, `status`/`type`/`paper_code`-style
    category filters, "it looks like test data", OR — the specific trap that
    caused the incident below — a **before/after snapshot diff** (`select` all
    ids before, `select` all ids after, delete the difference). That diff is
    doubly unsafe: any unpaged `select` silently truncates at PostgREST's
    1000-row cap (see the embeddings/1000-row-cap gotchas), AND even a fully
    paged snapshot races any concurrent writer. Assign the tag/id up front and
    delete by it; do not compute "which rows are mine" after the fact.
  - **ROOT CAUSE — 2026-07-23 `current_affairs_items` deletion incident:** a
    throwaway billing-verification script computed "my test's items" as
    `afterIds.filter(id => !beforeSet.has(id))` where both snapshots were an
    UNPAGED `select("id")`. Each truncated to 1000 rows in an unstable order,
    so the diff was ~588 essentially-random real rows, which the script then
    deleted (plus their embeddings + CA MCQs). Had it tagged its 3 seeded items
    with a unique `content_hash` prefix and deleted by that, zero real rows
    could have been touched. The three committed cleanup scripts already comply
    (`eval-harness.ts` explicit `createdIds`; `rls-security-check.ts` explicit
    throwaway auth-user ids; `seed-demo-account.ts --reset` scoped to one
    explicit account id) — this rule keeps every future ad-hoc script the same.

## Definition of done for any UI session
Runs locally with `pnpm dev`, renders REAL Supabase data, works at 390px and 1440px, both locales render (language toggle), no console errors.

These are known-pending items (mostly owner/dashboard actions the code can't do for itself). Keep this list current — cross items off as they land.
- **Enable Google OAuth (dashboard config, not code).** `/auth/v1/settings` currently reports `external.google=false`, so "Continue with Google" fails. The app code (`signInWithOAuth`) is correct. Manual Linking (for Google guest-conversion) still to confirm.]** Enable Supabase anonymous (guest) sign-ins + manual linking (dashboard config, not code). The guest/browsing experience (see the "Guest/anonymous auth" session below) needs Authentication → Sign In / Providers → **Anonymous Sign-Ins** turned ON at the project level (`/auth/v1/settings` `external.anonymous_users` must read `true` — it now does; the full guest E2E + `guest:rls` passed against it). NOTE: `mailer_autoconfirm` is currently OFF (email confirmations required), so a guest converting via email must click the emailed confirmation link; with the built-in email sender rate-limited (see the SMTP item below), **Google is the smoother guest-conversion path**. `supabase/config.toml` already sets `enable_anonymous_sign_ins`/`enable_manual_linking = true` for a LOCAL `supabase start`, but the CLOUD project is governed by the dashboard toggles, which can only be changed there.
- **Custom SMTP for auth emails.** Supabase's built-in email sender is hard-throttled (a few/hour → the "email rate limit exceeded" on the sign-in screen). Configure a real provider (Resend/SendGrid) in Supabase → Auth → Emails so OTP + signup-confirmation + password-reset emails aren't rate-limited. Until then, prefer email+password login (sends no email; `pnpm --filter api set-password --email <e> --password <p>` sets one on an OTP/Google-created account).
- **Founder account still on its weak dev password.** In-app change/reset-password UI now exists (see the Auth-gap-closure session below), so this is a pure action item: sign in as the founder and use Profile → Settings → Change password to replace `123456789` before any real deploy. Not done here since it needs the actual current session/credentials.
- **Rate-limit shared store for multi-instance deploy.** `lib/rate-limit.ts` is an in-process Map keyed by user id — correct for single-instance, but a multi-instance/autoscaled deploy MUST swap it for Redis/Postgres keyed the same way (noted in the file).
- **`standard` model pricing is a placeholder.** `lib/models.ts` `MODEL_PRICING[*].standard` is a guess (intro pricing is real). Update once Anthropic publishes post-intro prices for `claude-sonnet-5`/`claude-haiku-4-5` (see Session 13).
- **Data-migration idempotency confirmed done.** `migrate:dev-user` has already folded the pre-auth dev data into the founder's real account (dev profile deleted); the script now correctly refuses to re-run. Nothing to do — listed so nobody re-runs it.
- **VAPID/Sentry keys are dev-only.** `apps/api/.env`/`apps/web/.env.local` have real, working VAPID keys (generated this session, gitignored) so push notifications function locally — a production deploy should generate its own pair (`npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`) rather than reuse the dev ones. `SENTRY_DSN`/`VITE_SENTRY_DSN` are unset (Sentry is a documented no-op until a real DSN is configured); wire one up before relying on error tracking in production.
- **[RESOLVED 2026-07-26, fully — no dashboard action needed after all] Prerendered routes need clean-URL hosting.** Superseded: Vercel is no longer the deploy target (the ₹0 Free-tier pivot to **Cloudflare Pages** landed after the Deploy-Prep session this bullet described — see `docs/operations.md`'s "Free-tier (₹0) deploy" section). Cloudflare Pages' build command was `pnpm --filter web build` with no prerender step at all — confirmed the root cause of zero Google discoverability, since the live site's raw HTML was always an empty `<div id="root">` for every crawler. First fix attempt (earlier the same day) added `apps/web`'s `build:ci` script and asked for the Cloudflare dashboard's Build command to be changed to it by hand — **that request was based on an assumption that turned out to matter: a later live check (a real browser against production, not just an HTTP status code) proved prerendering was STILL not active**, whether because nobody had made the dashboard change yet or because it silently failed (the `--with-deps` system-package install needing root on Cloudflare's build image was always a disclosed, never-actually-verified risk). Rather than repeat the same "ask for a manual dashboard click" request, fixed it so no dashboard access is needed at all: `apps/web/scripts/postbuild.mjs`, chained onto the end of the plain `build` script, detects Cloudflare's own `CF_PAGES=1` system env var and runs the chromium-install + prerender steps only then — neither GitHub Actions CI nor the dormant `vercel.json` sets that var, so both stay completely unaffected, and the plain `build` command Cloudflare is ALREADY configured to run becomes self-sufficient. Verified locally: `CF_PAGES=1 pnpm --filter web build` produces real prerendered HTML (e.g. 33KB with correct title/OG/JSON-LD) for all 32 public routes; a plain build with no `CF_PAGES` produces none, confirming CI/local dev timing is unaffected. **A REAL BUG almost sank this fix on its first deploy**: `postbuild.mjs` originally used `execSync`, which throws on a non-zero child exit — chained onto `build` with `&&`, that would fail the ENTIRE Cloudflare build over prerendering alone failing, blocking every future deploy too. This is exactly what happened: the first push showed zero live change after 18+ minutes of polling (a real, working `dist/` from the plain Vite build had already succeeded, but the postbuild step's exception killed the whole command before Cloudflare could publish it). Fixed by wrapping both the chromium install and the prerender pass in try/catch, always exiting 0, and added a fallback `playwright install chromium` (no `--with-deps`, no root needed) if the primary install fails. **CONFIRMED LIVE after that second deploy** — a real `curl` (following the redirect Cloudflare now correctly applies to `/en/about` → `/en/about/`, itself proof a real prerendered directory exists) shows every one of the 16 spot-checked public URLs, both locales, serving its correct distinct per-page `<title>` and real byte-substantial content (26-33KB vs. the 1.7KB generic shell), not just locally. `vercel.json`'s buildCommand remains unprerendered (documented paid-tier fallback, not live).
- **[INCIDENT, same day, fixed within minutes — real production outage caused by this assistant] `_redirects`' `__spa-fallback.html` "fix" caused a live infinite redirect loop, taking down the entire authenticated app.** Follow-up to the entry above: after confirming prerendering worked, tried to also fix Cloudflare's build-linter false-positive that flags `/* -> /index.html 200` as an "infinite loop" and silently drops it (Cloudflare's own acknowledged bug: cloudflare/workers-sdk#11824) — the community-recommended workaround is pointing the fallback at a non-`index.html` filename, so shipped `scripts/copy-spa-fallback.mjs` copying `dist/index.html` to `dist/__spa-fallback.html` and changed `_redirects`' last line to point there. **This was WRONG and broke production**: Cloudflare Pages auto-strips the `.html` extension from any served path, so `/__spa-fallback.html` itself got redirected to `/__spa-fallback` — which matched the exact same `/*` wildcard rule and redirected right back to `/__spa-fallback.html`, forever. Confirmed live (both by this assistant's own `curl -sIL` showing 50+ chained 308s, and independently by the user hitting the real error in Safari: "Load cannot follow more than 20 redirections") that `/en/dashboard` — every real authenticated app route, not just the new feature pages — was completely down for actual users. **Reverted within minutes**: `_redirects` back to the plain `/* -> /index.html 200` line (the one the linter flags and drops), relying on Cloudflare Pages' own separate, undocumented, but repeatedly-verified-reliable built-in "serve index.html for an unmatched SPA path" default instead — confirmed live post-revert that the loop is gone (`/__spa-fallback` → clean 200, 0 redirects) and all 8 spot-checked authenticated routes + all 19 public pages are healthy. Deleted the now-dead `copy-spa-fallback.mjs`. **The lesson, recorded so it isn't relearned the hard way again: any change to `_redirects`' routing/fallback destination needs to be verified against Cloudflare's OWN clean-URL redirect layer specifically (e.g. a real preview deploy), not just a local `wrangler pages dev`/`curl` pass — that layer is exactly what broke here, and nothing local reproduces it.** Do not attempt the `__spa-fallback.html`-style fix again without that verification step, however tempting the "silently dropped rule" cosmetic issue looks — the dropped rule was never actually a live problem, the fix for it was.
- **CSR-snapshot prerendering, not true SSR/hydration.** `main.tsx` calls `createRoot(...).render()`, not `hydrateRoot` — the prerendered HTML is a crawler/first-paint snapshot that gets fully replaced once React mounts, not reused. This is safe (no hydration-mismatch risk from auth-state-dependent rendering) but means a synthetic Lighthouse LCP/TTI reading can look worse than pre-prerendering, since the browser now does a real paint followed by a full remount. True hydration would fix this but needs careful handling of the landing page's auth-dependent render (signed-in vs signed-out) — flagged as a follow-up, not attempted.
- **Deploy is configured but not executed.** See the Deploy-Prep session below — all repo-side config (Dockerfile, render.yaml, vercel.json, CI) is written and verified, but no Vercel/Render account, domain, or production secret has actually been created/entered yet (none of that is possible non-interactively). `docs/launch-checklist.md` has NOT been run for real. Don't tag `v1.0.0` until it has.
- **EDGE CASES from the current-affairs "two-lives" re-engineering (commit `c7a1959`, migration `0065`) — magazine follow-ups owned by the concurrent magazine rewrite.** [RESOLVED 2026-07-13 — sub-items (1)-(4) below all landed with the magazine rewrite (`services/magazine.ts` uses the new columns/taxonomy, web routes are now `magazine-{index,mains,prelims,month}.tsx`, `ca/compile.ts` uses `compilePrelimsEdition`/`compileMainsEdition`). Only the "Also minor" per-item try/catch note remains open — see `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §0 (S1) + B5.] That change made the shared `currentAffairsItemSchema` REQUIRE new fields (`status`, `prelims_relevance`, `mains_relevance`, `prelims_facts`, `mains_brief`, `possible_questions`, `node_significance`), so **every surface that returns a `CurrentAffairsItem` to the client MUST select the full column set** — reuse the exported `CURRENT_AFFAIRS_COLUMNS` from `apps/api/src/services/current-affairs.ts` (that constant is how the CA list/item/`getNodeDetail` related-CA selects were kept schema-valid; a partial select makes the client-side zod parse throw and errors the whole page). Still outstanding, ALL inside the magazine feature that was being rewritten concurrently (new prelims/mains/deep-dive editions + `0068_magazine_deep_dives.sql`), so left for whoever owns that rewrite to fold in — **when running "check all edge cases", verify these**: (1) `services/magazine.ts` `compileMagazine`'s `CA_COLUMNS` still selects only the legacy columns → any month with published CA would fail the client parse; switch it to `CURRENT_AFFAIRS_COLUMNS` (or the new magazine's own item shape). (2) The magazine web route (`apps/web/src/routes/magazine.tsx`) renders only the legacy `detail_i18n` fields, so re-engineered items (which have `detail_i18n = null` and carry their content in `prelims_facts`/`mains_brief`) show title+summary only — render the new two-lives structure. (3) `CATEGORY_ORDER`/any category list must use the new 12-value taxonomy (`polity_governance, economy, international_relations, environment_ecology, science_tech, security, social_issues, art_culture, schemes, reports_indices, places_persons, up_special`). (4) `apps/api/src/ca/compile.ts` (the `pnpm ca:compile` CLI) references the now-removed `compileMagazine`/`MagazineMonthSummary.item_count` and currently fails `tsc` — update or retire it. Also minor, in `apps/api/src/ca/pipeline.ts`: the per-item triage/enrich LLM calls aren't wrapped in try/catch, so a single LLM failure aborts the whole `ca:run` (already-inserted items persist and `content_hash` dedup lets a re-run continue, so it's not data-loss — but a per-item catch that logs+skips would be more robust).

  - PDF reality (IMPORTANT): the UPPSC PDFs encode Hindi in a **legacy non-Unicode font** — the embedded text layer is mojibake (garbled latin, 0 Devanagari codepoints). `pdf-parse` returns garbage; even `claude-sonnet-5`'s native PDF read transcribes the broken text layer. So the PYQ path (a) extracts with sonnet's native PDF read for clean English + structure, then (b) **detects mojibake Hindi** (`isMojibakeHindi` in `_shared.ts`: expected Devanagari but got latin, differs from English) and **regenerates Hindi from the clean English via batched `claude-haiku-4-5` translation** (`translateBatch`), flagging `meta.machine_translated=true`. Syllabus structuring sidesteps this because sonnet renders correct Devanagari from its own knowledge. Scanned-PDF vision fallback is wired in `ingest:syllabus` but the 2026 syllabus PDFs text-extract cleanly (not triggered).
  - `ingest:pyq` writes `content-raw/parsed/pyq_*.json` (git-ignored review artifacts) and STOPS; `ingest:pyq:load` loads reviewed JSON. `is_published` only when bilingual-complete (publish gate, enforced by DB trigger too).
  - RUN: `ingest:syllabus` end-to-end → **294 syllabus_nodes** across all 10 papers, 100% bilingual from source (0 machine-translated, both official PDFs parsed), tree parent-linkage verified (0 orphans). `ingest:verify` confirms per-paper counts. `ingest:pyq` run on ONE paper (`uppsc_prelims_2024_gs1`) and STOPPED for review: **150/150 questions extracted, 150/150 answer-key-verified** against the official key (cross-check caught 1 discrepancy at q29 → kept official), **150/150 syllabus-classified**, Hindi regenerated for 307 mojibake strings (150 questions machine_translated, flagged), **138/150 bilingual-complete** (12 match-list/statement Qs had incompletely-captured options → stay drafts under the publish gate). tests/embed built + typecheck-clean; not yet run (await PYQ review + `ingest:pyq:load`, then `ingest:tests`/`ingest:embed`).

New shared schemas in `packages/shared/src/learn.ts` (`PaperSummary`, `SyllabusNodeWithStats`, `SyllabusNodeDetail`).
  - `GET /syllabus/papers/:code/tree` (`getPaperTree`) annotates every node in a paper's tree with **subtree-aggregated** PYQ count + accuracy (own count/correct rolled up through every ancestor via a single deepest-first pass over the flat row set) — a chapter-level row would otherwise always show 0, since only leaf topics carry `questions.syllabus_node_id` directly. `GET /syllabus/nodes/:id` (`getNodeDetail`), by contrast, deliberately reports **own-only** (exact-match) counts, identical to the `/questions?node=` filter it sits next to — the two numbers must never disagree.

Both this filtered view and the node-detail page share `components/learn/pyq-list.tsx` (grouped-by-year, paginated via `?page=`, MCQ options with the correct one highlighted, explanations collapsed behind a per-question toggle — bilingual (EN+HI stacked) when expanded, unlike the rest of the locale-scoped UI, since revision content benefits from both).

Fixed by explicitly stacking title (its own row) above a second `flex-wrap` row for badges/actions, rather than relying on wrap behavior to rearrange one row into two.

All synthetic `srs_cards`/`tests` rows created during verification were deleted afterward.
- Session 7 (2026-07-05): the MCQ engine, part 1 — `/:locale/practice` (PYQ Papers / Sectional / Custom tabs) and the full test-taking flow at `/:locale/practice/test/:testId` (pre-start → full-screen player → results), all against the real Session-3 PYQ data (132-question PRE_GS1 2024 paper + its sectional splits).
  - Backend additions on top of the attempts/tests services that already existed (start/answers/submit, `POST /tests/custom` — both landed between earlier sessions, not built from scratch here): `GET /attempts/:id` (attempt + its `attempt_answers`, for hydrating a resumed player); `startAttempt` is now idempotent per `(user_id, test_id)` — a second "Start Test" click returns the existing **unsubmitted** attempt instead of creating a duplicate, so the server-authoritative `started_at` (and the countdown derived from it) survives a page reload; `listTests`/`getTestDetail` now join the dev user's submitted attempts to surface `best_score`/`attempts_count` per test; `createCustomTestBody` gained an optional `difficulty` filter.
  - New shared schema: `attemptDetailSchema` (`{attempt, answers}`) for the resume-hydration endpoint. No DB migration needed — everything reads/writes existing `attempts`/`attempt_answers`/`tests` columns.
  - `src/lib/offline-queue.ts`: a generic localStorage-persisted retry queue (`createOfflineQueue`), written provider-agnostic so the later PWA offline-sync work can reuse it directly. Contract: callers always enqueue the FULL current record for a dedupe key (never a partial patch) — the queue does last-write-wins per key with no merge logic of its own, which is what keeps it simple. `useAttemptAnswers` wraps one queue instance per attempt and autosaves every option pick + per-question time-spent tick via `POST /attempts/:id/answers`.
  - Test player (`components/practice/test-player.tsx`) lives at a router path **outside** the `app-shell` layout (`router.tsx`) — deliberately no sidebar/bottom-tab/topbar chrome during an exam, own minimal header instead (title, language toggle, countdown). One question per screen, numbered palette (answered=tulsi/marked=marigold/unanswered=muted, current ring), keyboard shortcuts (arrows navigate, 1-4 pick the option at that position regardless of its A/B/C/D key, `m` toggles mark), `beforeunload` guard while in progress. Mobile: the palette moves into the existing `Sheet` bottom-sheet component instead of the always-visible `lg:`-only sidebar. "Marked for review" is client-only state (not a DB column) persisted to `localStorage` per attempt id.
  - Countdown timer is computed once from `attempt.started_at + test.duration_minutes` (never a client-side elapsed-time clock) and auto-submits on expiry; tests with no `duration_minutes` (sectional/custom) simply show no timer and never auto-submit.

Selecting by `paper_code` value instead of label fixed it — a reminder that real ingested copy won't always match what a session log or a schema field name implies.

- Session 8 (2026-07-05): `/:locale/practice/attempt/:attemptId/result` — the post-submit results page, replacing the old inline `TestResults` (deleted; its submit flow now navigates here instead of holding local result state).
  - `GET /attempts/:id/result` (`getAttemptResult` in `services/attempts.ts`) is additive, computed entirely from existing data — no new migration. It recomputes marks/percentile/timing from `attempts.meta` (question_ids/question_marks/marking_scheme) + `attempt_answers` rather than re-deriving `is_correct` (that stays whatever `submitAttempt` persisted), so a retracted-since-submission question can't silently flip a review row's correctness.
  - Percentile is `(scores_below + 0.5*scores_tied) / total_submitted_attempts_for_this_test * 100` across **every** submitted attempt on the test, not just the dev user's — with one real user today that's the same population, but the query itself doesn't filter by `user_id`. Topic breakdown groups review rows by the question's own `syllabus_node_id` (the same leaf a `/learn/:paperCode/:nodeId` deep link needs), with an unlinked "unmapped" bucket for PYQs that never got a node assigned; "weak" = attempted > 0 and accuracy < 50%.

Had to explicitly tell the model "no markdown, no headers, no bold asterisks" in the system prompt; without it haiku free-styled `#`/`**` formatting that rendered as literal characters in the plain-text review UI.
  - `POST /srs/cards/from-question` (`addQuestionToRevision`) mirrors the existing from-node path onto the same `(user_id, source_type, source_id)` unique index, `source_type='question'` — front = stem, back = correct option + explanation (whichever locale each field has).
  - Frontend: `useExplainQuestion` wraps `streamEvents` per-question (one hook instance per review row, not a keyed map — simpler since each row is already its own component); on the `done` event the parent page patches the cached `attemptResult` query directly via `queryClient.setQueryData` instead of refetching. Topic breakdown and review render as stacked flex rows/cards (not a literal `<table>`), consistent with the dashboard's earlier call to avoid layouts that don't survive 390px.
  - "Ask a doubt" is a literal stub per the brief: a `Link` to `/:locale/doubts?question=&attempt=` even though no `doubts` route exists yet — clicking it today falls through to the catch-all redirect; wiring the real route later is a router-only change.

All attempts/custom `tests`/`srs_cards` rows created during verification were deleted afterward.
- Session 9 (2026-07-06): the FLAGSHIP — AI Answer-Writing Evaluation engine in the API. New service dir `apps/api/src/services/evaluation/` (`rubric.ts`, `grounding.ts`, `prompts.ts`, `evaluate.ts`), shared contract in `packages/shared/src/evaluation.ts`, `POST /answers/submissions` (`routes/answers.ts`), and the SSE runner `GET /stream/evaluations/:submissionId` (added to `stream.ts`).
  - Rubric **v1** (`rubric.ts`) — six weighted dimensions summing to exactly 1.0 (a startup assertion enforces it): structure_flow .20, content_coverage .30, keywords_concepts .15, examples_data .15, presentation .10, word_limit_language .10. Each scored 0-10; weighted fraction projected onto the question's max marks. `RUBRIC_VERSION`/dimension keys live in shared; weights + examiner descriptions are server-only.
  - Two-pass evaluation with `claude-sonnet-5`: **pass 1** = strict structured-JSON analysis (per-dimension score+justification, reference_points, missed_key_points, factual_errors, is_off_topic, overall_comment) via `structuredJson`; **pass 2** = three streamed calls — strengths, then improvements, then a within-word-limit model answer. SSE order (spec-exact, live-verified): `status → dimension_score ×6 → analysis → feedback_delta (strengths, improvements) → model_answer_delta → done {evaluation_id}`. Persists everything to `evaluations` incl. aggregated tokens + cost (Anthropic model spend; the RAG embedding cost is sub-cent-negligible and each call is also logged to `llm_calls`).
  - **RAG grounding** (`grounding.ts` + migration `0027_match_embeddings.sql`): a `match_embeddings` RPC does cosine-distance ANN over the pgvector `embeddings` store (PostgREST can't express a `<=>` ORDER BY). The operator/type are addressed as `OPERATOR(extensions.<=>)` / `extensions.vector(1536)` because pgvector lives in the non-search_path `extensions` schema. Grounding embeds the question text (OpenAI, via `lib/embeddings.ts`), pulls node-scoped chunks first then top-k global, dedupes by id, and **degrades gracefully** (empty grounding, evaluate ungrounded) on any embed/RPC error. Real: 852 live embeddings in the dev DB.
  - Guardrails: max input length (zod — `typed_text` ≤20k, custom prompt ≤2k, empty rejected), **one concurrent evaluation per user**, cost logged via `llm_calls`, and an honesty guardrail (off-topic/empty/injection → low scores + `is_off_topic=true`, never hallucinated praise). Migration `0028` adds `answer_submissions.meta` to carry a custom prompt's word_limit/marks from the POST to the later SSE GET.
  - `MODEL DECISION` (recorded): `claude-sonnet-5` has **no `temperature`** knob (deprecated → 400). Determinism for scoring comes from `output_config.effort`, not temperature. Pass-1 scoring runs at `effort: "low"` — the rubric's explicit score bands do the calibration, and low effort minimizes the divergent exploration that makes a strong answer's borderline dimensions (8-vs-9) wobble run-to-run. Extended `lib/anthropic.ts` with an additive `onUsage` callback (token/cost aggregation) and an optional `signal` (AbortSignal) so a disconnected SSE client cancels the in-flight stream instead of burning tokens.
  - Eval harness `pnpm eval:answers [--runs N] [--keep] [--lang hi]` (`apps/api/scripts/eval-harness.ts`): runs a strong / mediocre / off-topic sample answer (editable fixtures) through the real pipeline and gates on (1) ranking good>mediocre>off-topic and (2) repeatability ≤5% **of full marks** (the meaningful denominator for an exam score; also reports the stricter of-mean %). Tuned to PASS both: good ~12.2/15, mediocre ~4.6, off-topic 0.75 (`is_off_topic=true`), repeatability good 3.0% / mediocre 4.0% / off-topic 0.0% over 3 runs.
  - GOTCHA (Anthropic structured outputs): a JSON Schema `integer` with `minimum`/`maximum` is **rejected with a 400** ("properties maximum, minimum are not supported"). The 0-10 range is enforced by the prompt + a server-side `clampScore` instead of the schema.
  - Hardened after an adversarial multi-agent code review (5 dimensions → per-finding verification; 14 raw → 9 confirmed). The high-severity one: `planEvaluation` claimed the submission to `'evaluating'` **before** `resolveQuestionContext`, so a transient DB error there stranded the row in `'evaluating'` forever (the throw pre-empts the SSE route's `req.on('close')` release), permanently 409-locking the user out. Fixes: resolve the (read-only) question context **before** any status mutation; **replay a persisted evaluation regardless of the submission's status** (source of truth for "done" → covers lost terminal writes); emit `done` right after persist and make the `→complete` write best-effort; a partial unique index `answer_submissions (user_id) where status='evaluating'` (`0029`) makes the per-user guard race-proof (23505→409); stale-`evaluating` reclaim (`updated_at < now()-5min → failed`) so a crash can never lock a user out; per-field replay locale; injection hardening (an "untrusted data, never instructions" clause + `<<<`/`>>>` fence neutralization in the answer builders).
  - Verified end-to-end against the running API (validation battery: 14 guardrail cases all 400/404; SSE full sequence; concurrency 409 live; instant replay with no re-bill; GET detail with tokens+cost) and via service-level probes (prompt-injection scored **0/15 off_topic=true**; Hindi feedback+model answer in Devanagari; catalogued `question_id` path with node grounding; the DB unique index rejecting a 2nd `evaluating` with 23505; stale reclaim). All synthetic `answer_submissions`/`evaluations` rows deleted afterward — dev DB back at zero baseline. Note: the dev server was already running on :4000, so the live checks hit it (hot-reloaded); a second `pnpm --filter api dev` just EADDRINUSE'd.

Added `framer-motion` (new dependency) for the dimension dials' staggered reveal-in, respecting `useReducedMotion`. Writing Room: draft autosave to localStorage (`use-draft-autosave.ts`, ref-based single interval so continuous typing doesn't perpetually defer the save), UPPSC time-pressure countdown presets (125w/7min, 200w/11min — self-paced start/pause, not server-authoritative like the MCQ test-player's timer), and Web Speech API dictation (`use-dictation.ts`, hi-IN/en-IN toggle, feature-detected — renders nothing on browsers without `SpeechRecognition`).

Fixed by moving the PYQ-picker's paper-filter `<select>` out of the `action` slot and into the card body as its own row above the list, rather than trying to out-shrink the select.

A deliberately-truncated first `curl` (piped through `head -c`) confirmed the disconnect-release safety path: the submission correctly flipped to `'failed'` instead of hanging. Then a full Playwright pass (both locales, 390px/1440px, light+dark) — zero console errors. All synthetic submission/evaluation/srs-card rows deleted afterward.
  - Follow-up same session: user flagged that only 2 of 10 papers had any PYQs (Prelims GS1 2024, Mains GS1 2025) — the Session-3 ingestion was a single-paper proof of concept, not a full run. Content pipeline already had PDFs downloaded (via `content:fetch`, gitignored binaries) for 5 more Prelims papers (2023/2024/2025 × CSAT/GS1) and 15 more Mains papers (2024/2025 × GS1-6/Essay/General Hindi) — asked the user for ingestion scope (small/Mains-only/full), got **full breadth**. Ran all `ingest:pyq` parse steps **in parallel** (per explicit user request) as 19 background Bash tasks, then `ingest:pyq:load --all`: **999 questions upserted, 486 published**.
  - DATA REALITY (recorded): **prelims MCQ questions can only publish with a verified `correct_option_key`**, and the content-raw manifest only has an official UPPSC answer key for **2024** Prelims (CSAT + GS1) — 2023 and 2025 Prelims papers extract fine but load as `is_published=false` drafts (0/100, 0/145, 0/100, 0/150 bilingual-complete) since `questionPublishable()` requires a correct answer matching an option, and none exists to cross-check against. This is the ingest pipeline correctly declining to publish an MCQ with an unverified/unknown correct answer, not a bug — Prelims PYQ coverage today is genuinely only 2024 (CSAT 100 + GS1 132 published). Mains (descriptive) has no such gate (only bilingual stem completeness), so all 8 Mains papers × both years published in full (`MAINS_GS2`-`GS6` = 40 PYQs each, `MAINS_ESSAY`/`GH` = 18/16). - Session 11 (2026-07-06): the handwritten-mode half of Answer Writing — "Upload handwritten" on `/answers/write`, the OCR trust-loop confirm screen at `/answers/confirm/:submissionId`, and the API pipeline behind both (this is the work Session 10 saw in flight and left alone).
  - DB: `0030_answer_images_bucket.sql` creates the private `answer-images` Storage bucket (8MB/file cap, jpeg/png/webp only) with a wide-open dev policy on `storage.objects` for `bucket_id = 'answer-images'` — same "REPLACED IN AUTH PHASE" pattern as `0013`'s table RLS. `answer_submissions.image_paths`/`ocr_text`/`ocr_confidence` and the `handwritten`/`ocr_done` enum values already existed from `0008`/`0002` (scaffolded ahead of need) — no new columns required.
  - UPLOAD PATH DECIDED: the browser uploads page photos **directly to Supabase Storage** with the anon key (`apps/web/src/lib/supabase.ts`, the first time apps/web talks to Supabase at all — `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` had sat unused since Session 1) rather than proxying multi-megabyte image bytes through Express. This is a deliberate one-off exception to "the ONLY backend is the Express API" (that rule is about app *logic*, not binary blob transport) — every other read/write, including everything about the submission row itself, still goes through the API. `POST /answers/submissions` only ever receives/stores the resulting storage paths (strings).
  - OCR service (`apps/api/src/services/ocr/`) — see the new Architecture bullet above for the provider-interface shape. `claude-vision-provider.ts` runs two calls: a **streamed** plain-text transcription (so the confirm screen can show text appearing live, matching the rest of the app's "AI responses always stream" rule) with an explicit instruction to mark unreadable spans `[अस्पष्ट]`/`[illegible]` rather than guess, then a fast low-effort `structuredJson` self-reported confidence (0-1). `storage.ts`'s `downloadImageAsBase64` (service-role client, bypasses the bucket's RLS entirely) is the one place that turns a storage path into vision-API bytes.
  - New `evaluate.ts` exports: `planOcr`/`executeOcr` (mirrors `planEvaluation`/`executeEvaluation`'s replay-if-already-persisted contract exactly — a submission with `ocr_text` set replays it as a single `done`, never re-billing) and `confirmOcr` (PATCH, writes the user's reviewed/edited text into `typed_text`, leaving raw `ocr_text` untouched as an audit trail). `planEvaluation` gained one guardrail: a handwritten submission with no confirmed `typed_text` yet throws 400 rather than silently evaluating raw unconfirmed OCR output.
  - PRESENTATION DIMENSION + IMAGE (per the brief): `planEvaluation` best-effort-downloads the submission's first page image and threads it through to pass-1 analysis as an actual vision content block (`prompts.ts`'s `buildAnalysisUserContent` now returns `string | ContentPart[]`); the system prompt explicitly scopes the image to the presentation dimension only ("do not re-transcribe it, the OCR text is the authoritative content") so a hard-to-photograph page can't drag down content/keyword scoring. A download failure here degrades gracefully (evaluate without the image) rather than failing the whole run — presentation-via-image is an enrichment, not a requirement.
  - New routes: `GET /stream/ocr/:submissionId` (`stream.ts`, same pre-flight-then-SSE shape as `/stream/evaluations/*` — `planOcr` runs before the stream opens so 404/400 return as real JSON) and `PATCH /answers/submissions/:id/confirm-ocr` (`answers.ts`).
  - Frontend: `HandwrittenUpload` (`components/answers/handwritten-upload.tsx`) manages capture (camera-capture `<input capture="environment">` + a plain multi-file picker), reorder (up/down, not drag-and-drop — simpler and still fully keyboard-accessible), rotate, and remove for up to `MAX_ANSWER_IMAGES` (6, shared constant) pages; rotation is CSS-only for the live thumbnail preview, then actually **baked into the pixels via canvas** at submit time (`lib/answer-images.ts`'s `prepareAnswerImage`) before `browser-image-compression` runs — OCR reads bytes, not CSS transforms. `answers-write.tsx` gained a typed/handwritten `Tabs` split; submitting the handwritten tab uploads all pages in order, creates the submission, then navigates to `/answers/confirm/:id` instead of straight to `/answers/evaluation/:id`.
  - Confirm screen (`routes/answers-confirm.tsx`) is the trust loop: streams the transcription live via `useOcrStream`, shows it beside the page-photo thumbnails (signed URLs fetched client-side via the anon key against the private bucket — the dev-permissive storage policy covers `createSignedUrl` too), then an editable textarea with a confidence badge (banded via the existing `scoreBandColor` coral/marigold/tulsi thresholds) before "Confirm & evaluate" hands off to the unmodified evaluation SSE flow. Re-visiting the screen after an already-confirmed edit prefers the persisted `typed_text` over the raw OCR replay, so navigating back never silently discards an edit. `submission-history-list.tsx` now links an unconfirmed/unevaluated handwritten row back to this screen (previously only `complete` rows were clickable) so an interrupted trust-loop session isn't stranded.
  - Content pipeline generalized for the smoke test: `content-sources.yaml` gained a `handwriting_samples` section (three verified public-domain Devanagari manuscript-page JPEGs from Wikimedia Commons, found via web search + license-checked, not guessed — Rigveda/Krishna-Yajurveda/an undated Hindi-variant manuscript) and `scripts/fetch-content.ts` gained a `ContentKind` (`pdf` | `image`) split — image sources validate via JPEG/PNG/WEBP magic bytes instead of `%PDF`, skip `pdf-parse`, and keep their source URL's own extension instead of a hardcoded `.pdf`. `.gitignore` extended to cover the new image extensions the same way PDFs are excluded.
  - Smoke-tested the full pipeline twice without a real handwriting photo (per the brief): a one-off script uploaded a sample straight to Storage via the service-role client and drove create→OCR-stream→confirm→evaluate-stream over the real running API, then a full **Playwright** browser pass (installed as a temporary devDependency and removed again afterward, same convention as Sessions 4/8) drove the actual UI end to end — attach file, thumbnail render, rotate, submit, wait for the confirm screen, wait for live transcription, confirm, wait for the evaluation screen to complete — zero console errors. Both runs correctly triggered the **honesty guardrail** rather than a bug: the archaic manuscript samples aren't real UPPSC handwriting, so the model marked the text `[अस्पष्ट]`/declined to transcribe it as a "handwritten exam answer" and scored ~0.15/15 with `is_off_topic`-shaped low scores across all six dimensions — exactly the "don't hallucinate praise" behavior from Session 9, and independent confirmation the presentation-dimension image block is actually reaching the model (its justification explicitly referenced "the image"/photograph rather than only the transcribed text). A true happy-path legible transcription is left for the user's own real handwriting photo, as planned. All synthetic submission/evaluation rows and the smoke-test storage object were deleted afterward.
- Session 12 (2026-07-06): the current-affairs pipeline + `/:locale/current-affairs` hub, end to end — replacing the Session-4 list-only placeholder.
  - New `apps/api/src/ca/` (`sources.ts`, `prompts.ts`, `pipeline.ts`, `run.ts`, `scheduler.ts`), run via `pnpm ca:run [--days N] [--max-per-source N] [--max-total N]` (default days=3). SOURCE VERIFICATION (recorded, not guessed): every RSS URL in `sources.ts` was fetched live today. **PIB** (`pib.gov.in/ViewRss.aspx`) turned out to be a client-rendered Angular shell — curl (any non-JS fetcher, i.e. any real RSS reader) gets an empty "JavaScript must be enabled" page regardless of `reg`/`lang`, no working feed exists. **UP government** (information.up.gov.in / up.gov.in) has no RSS at all — every guessed path 302s to a 404. Substituted both with sources that actually work: Insights on India's daily current-affairs feed (UPSC-curated, often digesting PIB itself) and two genuinely UP-focused regional feeds (Hindustan Times Lucknow, IndiaTV Uttar Pradesh). Final 6: The Hindu (national), Indian Express (India), Livemint (news), Insights on India, HT Lucknow, IndiaTV UP.
  - Per item: `classifyItem` (haiku) decides is_relevant/is_up_specific/is_important/category/syllabus_node_ids (from ~260 depth 1-2 candidate nodes) in one call; `summarizeItem` (haiku) writes a fresh bilingual paraphrase — title/summary/what-happened/why-it-matters/key-facts/question-angle — explicitly instructed never to copy the source snippet verbatim (ToS: RSS + our-own-words summary + source link, never mirrored article text). Bilingual publish gate mirrors the questions-table pattern (`i18nComplete` on both title and summary). Embeds both locales into `embeddings` (source_type='current_affairs'). "Important" items get 2 MCQs generated (haiku, fact-constrained prompt) inserted as real `questions` rows, always `is_published=false`.
  - MODEL GOTCHA (recorded): `claude-haiku-4-5` **rejects the `effort` param** (400 "This model does not support the effort parameter") — unlike sonnet (Session 9), haiku calls in this codebase never pass `effort`; `lib/anthropic.ts`'s existing `translate`/`translateBatch` were the tell, missed it on the first `ca:run` attempt.
  - Idempotency: `content_hash` (sha256 of the item's link) + a partial unique index (`0031_current_affairs_pipeline.sql`) — re-running (cron or manual) never duplicates an item; the pipeline still re-fetches full feeds every run but skips any already-seen hash before spending an LLM call on it, so steady-state re-runs cost scales with genuinely-new items only, not total feed size. Dev-only `node-cron` (`ca/scheduler.ts`) re-runs every 6h inside the already-running API process; production would need a real external cron hitting `pnpm ca:run` directly (not built/deployed — no prod host exists yet).
  - DESIGN DECISION + BUG FOUND LIVE (recorded): CA-generated MCQs are *never* published (`is_published=false` permanently — there's no reviewer role/UI in this pre-auth app to ever flip it) so they stay out of the general PYQ catalog/search, but they ARE meant to be the "Quiz me on this week" button's content. First ship of `createCustomTestFromCurrentAffairs` + `getTestDetail` both still required `questions.is_published=true` — the quiz test was created but showed **zero questions**, and (found by the user testing live, not by me) starting/closing that empty quiz fell through to the generic practice page. Root cause was broader than the one function: `attempts.ts` has the *same* `is_published=true` filter independently in four places (`startAttempt`'s test_questions join AND its ad-hoc-question_ids path, `submitAttempt`, `getAttemptResult`) — all four needed the same "except when the test's `paper_code === 'CURRENT_AFFAIRS'`" exception, not just the one in `getTestDetail`, since the pre-start detail view, the actual attempt start, grading, and the result page each re-fetch questions independently with their own copy of the filter.
  - PostgREST GOTCHA: `.select("detail_i18n->key_facts_i18n as key_facts_i18n")` is invalid — PostgREST's rename syntax is `alias:column`, not SQL's `column as alias`; with `as` it silently returns a field literally named `key_facts_i18naskey_facts_i18n` (concatenated, no error) instead of erroring, which is what actually broke the per-fact "Add to revision" endpoint until caught by a live test. Fixed by dropping the alias — PostgREST already names a bare JSON-path selection after its last path segment.
  - Frontend: `routes/current-affairs.tsx` — date-grouped feed (Today/Yesterday/formatted, IST calendar day) with a category `<select>` + a prominent UP-only toggle button (tulsi-colored, not buried in the select); `components/current-affairs/item-detail-sheet.tsx` is the item detail `Sheet` (structured summary, per-fact "Add to revision" via a deterministic sha256-derived uuid-shaped `srs_cards.source_id` so one item's several facts don't collide under the existing `(user_id, source_type, source_id)` unique index — no migration needed since `source_id` only needs to look like a uuid, not be a real one), `linked-syllabus-node.tsx` resolves each related node's title/paper via the existing `GET /syllabus/nodes/:id` (reused, not duplicated), `quiz-button.tsx` posts `/current-affairs/quiz` and navigates straight into the player on success (same convention as `custom-test-builder.tsx`).
  - Ran the pipeline for real against the live feeds: **37 items published** (15 UP-specific), **38 MCQs generated** across 19 "important" items, 74 embedding chunks. Verified end-to-end against the running app (both locales, 390px/1440px) — zero console errors on the feed/detail-sheet pass. All synthetic quiz-test/srs-card rows created during verification (curl + Playwright) were deleted afterward. NOTE: mid-session, a separate concurrent process was actively committing its own handwritten-mode audit fixes (`b1040e8`) — left entirely untouched, confirmed disjoint via `git status` before staging.
  - EDGE CASES the user found live (all fixed, not just the one they clicked into): (1) starting the quiz showed **zero questions**, and closing that empty player fell back to `/practice` — traced to `attempts.ts` independently re-implementing the same `questions.is_published=true` filter in **four** places (`startAttempt`'s test_questions join AND its ad-hoc-question_ids path, `submitAttempt`, `getAttemptResult`), not just the one already fixed in `getTestDetail` — all four now skip that filter when the test's `paper_code` is the new exported `CURRENT_AFFAIRS_PAPER_CODE` (`services/tests.ts`). (2) "the Practice page doesn't show all the papers" — re-running `pnpm ingest:tests` to backfill the missing PRE_CSAT `pyq_full`/sectional tests (dormant since Session 10 loaded CSAT's 100 MCQs but nobody re-ran the assembler) also built `pyq_full`/`sectional` wrapper tests for all **8 Mains (descriptive) papers**, which the Practice tab's MCQ-only test-player can't run — `listTests` now excludes non-`PRE_%` paper codes for those two kinds, and `createCustomTestFromNode` now requires `type='mcq'` so "Practice this topic" can never build a broken set from a Mains-only node either. Re-verified the full flow live afterward: quiz → 38-question player with real bilingual MCQs rendering correctly; Practice → PYQ Papers shows exactly PRE_GS1 + PRE_CSAT, Sectional shows all 14 real sections, zero Mains entries, zero console errors.
- Session 13 (2026-07-06): cost-hardening pass over the evaluation pipeline — no new features, four changes.
  - **Prompt caching** (`lib/anthropic.ts`): `structuredJson`/`streamText`'s `system` param now also accepts `PromptSegment[]` (`{text, cache?}`), turned into Anthropic `TextBlockParam[]` with `cache_control: {type:"ephemeral"}` (5-min TTL) on the marked segment(s). Applied to (a) the pass-1 analysis system prompt (`buildAnalysisSystem`, fixed per locale+hasPageImage), (b) a new shared system segment for the strengths/improvements calls, and (c) the OCR transcribe system prompt (`buildTranscribeSystem`, fixed per locale). `message.usage.cache_read_input_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` are now recorded into new `llm_calls.cache_read_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens` columns (`0033_llm_calls_cache_tokens.sql`) and folded into `estimateCostUsd` (reads 0.1x input price, writes 1.25x).
  - For (b), `prompts.ts`'s old `buildFeedbackUserContent` (previously passed as **user content**, identically, to both the strengths and improvements calls) is now `buildFeedbackSharedContext`, moved into a **system** segment placed first and marked `cache:true`, with the persona instruction (`buildStrengthsSystem`/`buildImprovementsSystem`) as an uncached second segment after it — moving it to `system` matters because Anthropic's cache key includes everything before the breakpoint INCLUDING system, so if the shared block had stayed in user content while system differed between the two calls, it could never hit. LIVE-VERIFIED (a) and (c) hit the cache correctly (first call of a batch writes, every subsequent identical-locale call reads) via the eval harness's 6 back-to-back evaluations. (b) did NOT hit in that same run — Anthropic enforces a **~1024-token minimum** for a cacheable block on Sonnet models, and the harness's short synthetic answers put the shared block right at ~1000 tokens (confirmed via the observed input-token counts), just under the line. The code/API usage is correct (proven by (a)/(c) hitting with the identical mechanism) — real Mains answers nearer the word limit with a richer pass-1 analysis are expected to clear 1024 and cache reliably; this is a real, disclosed limitation of the restructuring, not a bug.
  - **Model-answer reuse**: new `question_model_answers` table (`0034_question_model_answers.sql`; unique on `question_id, locale, rubric_version`). In `executeEvaluation`, the model-answer step now checks this table first for catalogued questions (`submission.question_id` set) — a hit replays the stored text as a single `model_answer_delta` (no model call, mirroring `replayEvaluation`'s pattern); a miss generates as before and persists the fresh text + that call's own tokens/cost (isolated from the aggregate `onUsage` via a small per-call usage capture). Custom-prompt submissions (no `question_id`) always generate and are never persisted. Bumping `RUBRIC_VERSION` naturally invalidates this cache (the unique key includes `rubric_version`), same as it already invalidates the eval harness's repeatability baseline. LIVE-VERIFIED against a real catalogued Mains question: two evaluations of two different candidate answers to the same question → exactly one `answer_eval_model` row in `llm_calls` across both runs, and byte-identical returned model-answer text.
  - **Centralized question visibility** (`lib/question-visibility.ts`, new): the one place encoding "which questions may this context see" — `"catalog"` scope (`is_published=true`, no exceptions) vs `"test"` scope (adds the current-affairs exception, `paper_code === CURRENT_AFFAIRS_PAPER_CODE`), exposed as a PostgREST `.or()` filter string. `CURRENT_AFFAIRS_PAPER_CODE` moved here from `services/tests.ts` (still re-exported there for callers). Deleted every inline copy of the filter: `services/tests.ts` (`getTestDetail`, `createCustomTestFromNode` now correctly uses `"catalog"` scope so a syllabus node's topic-practice set can never pull in CA MCQs despite them carrying a real `syllabus_node_id`, `createCustomTestFromCurrentAffairs` gets the filter as a defense-in-depth no-op), `services/attempts.ts` (`startAttempt`'s two paths, `submitAttempt`, `getAttemptResult` — this also deleted the now-unnecessary `isCurrentAffairsTest`/`isCurrentAffairsQuiz` lookups entirely, since the `.or()` filter is unconditional and keys off the *question's own* `paper_code`, not the test's), and `services/questions.ts` (all four reads, `"catalog"` scope). LIVE-VERIFIED: the full CA-quiz flow (create → detail → start → submit → result, 38 real questions) still works end-to-end on the new unconditional `.or(..., {referencedTable:"questions"})` filter; CA questions confirmed absent from `listQuestions`/`getQuestionById`; a normal non-CA published test still serves/grades correctly.
  - **Cost dashboard**: `pnpm cost:report [--days N]` (`apps/api/scripts/cost-report.ts`) — last 30 days from `llm_calls` grouped by `(purpose, model)`: calls, cache-hit-rate (% of calls with any `cache_read_tokens`), tokens, cost recomputed under BOTH `MODEL_PRICING[model].intro` and `.standard` (not the historical stored `cost_usd`, which mixes whichever schedule was live at call time) so a future price jump is visible ahead of an invoice; plus cost-per-evaluation (`answer_eval_*` cost / count of `answer_eval_analysis` calls, since that call fires exactly once per real run) and cost-per-CA-run (approximated as CA-purpose cost / distinct UTC calendar days with `ca_*` activity — no run id is recorded anywhere, so this is a labelled approximation, not an exact per-invocation figure). `lib/models.ts`'s `MODEL_PRICING` now carries `{intro, standard, standardEffectiveDate}` per model instead of one flat price; `estimateCostUsd` picks the schedule in effect at call time via the new `priceSetFor`/`costFromPriceSet` (the latter also reused directly by the report to price under an arbitrary schedule regardless of the date). **The `standard` figures are a placeholder** (not confirmed against Anthropic's published pricing page, same honesty-over-guessing convention as the exam-date sourcing in Session 5) — update them once the real post-intro price for `claude-sonnet-5`/`claude-haiku-4-5` is announced.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm build` clean; `pnpm eval:answers --runs 2` ranking (good>mediocre>off-topic) and repeatability (≤5% of full marks) both PASS with scores matching Session 9's documented calibration (~12/4.95/0.75) — confirms the prompt restructuring didn't change scoring semantics. All synthetic rows from every live verification (submissions/evaluations/attempts/tests/question_model_answers) deleted afterward.

Both wired as root + api pnpm scripts.

Raising the author call to `maxTokens:28000` + an explicit "both `hi` and `en` must be fully populated" instruction fixed it; the regenerated note came back full (EN overview 1150 chars, 14 facts, both languages). Also hit a transient `APIConnectionTimeoutError`/socket "terminated" on one node's calls — re-running succeeded (the SDK's connection-error retries don't always cover a mid-stream socket terminate).

- Session 15 (2026-07-07): the DAILY-ENGAGEMENT ENGINE — six features, one commit each, backend + real UI + live-DB verification. (Note: "Session 15" is also the label older comments use for the future Auth phase; that's still pending — this session is the engagement engine, not auth.)
  - Shared IST layer `lib/ist.ts` (`istToday`/`istDayRangeUtc`/`istClockUtc`/`formatDateBilingual`) centralises the IST day boundary every daily feature keys off. Reusable i18n-merge helper pattern used to patch both `messages/{en,hi}.json` per feature.
  - **1 — Daily quiz** (`apps/api/src/daily/{config,quiz,run,scheduler}.ts`, `services/daily.ts`, `routes/daily.ts`): a `daily_quiz` test assembled per IST day mixing 40% generated-on-weak-topics / 30% spaced-reuse PYQs / 20% this-week CA MCQs / 10% random (ratios+size 20-30 in config); short slices **backfill from the other pools and log it**, never a thin quiz. All pool selection goes through `questionVisibilityOrFilter` (catalog scope for gen/pyq/random, test scope for CA). `pnpm daily:build`; dev scheduler fires 5:00 AM IST (node-cron, `Asia/Kolkata` tz). Yesterday's quiz stays a published test (makeup); `/daily-quiz/archive` + a self-heal `POST /daily-quiz/today`. Practice "Daily Quiz" tab. UPPSC one-third negative marking (-0.33, matching `ingest:tests`).
  - **2 — Daily answer set + essay-v1 rubric**: `rubric.ts` is now a **versioned registry** — `v1` (GS) and `essay-v1` (UPPSC Essay paper: one ~700-word essay / 50 marks, verified vs the official 3-section pattern via web search). Both reuse the SAME six shared dimension keys (so the web renders any variant unchanged; the UI labels are key-based in `rubric-labels.ts`) with version-specific weights + examiner descriptions. `evaluate.ts` picks `essay-v1` when the submission's question is paper `MAINS_ESSAY` (constants in new `lib/exam-papers.ts`), or a custom prompt sets `meta.rubric`; the version threads through pass-1 scoring, the model-answer prompt/reuse (`question_model_answers` keyed by rubric_version), and persistence. The daily answer set (`services/answer-set.ts`, `GET /answers/daily-set`) is **computed deterministically per IST day, no storage**: 4 GS descriptive Qs rotating across the six GS papers (incl. GS-V/VI UP) + a weekly ESSAY slot on Sundays, from published+approved descriptive PYQs; per-question status = has a completed evaluation. Shown on `/answers`.
  - **3 — Today guided mode + streak engine**: `services/daily-progress.ts` is the ONE source of "what did the user do today" (all IST-day-bounded), feeding both the checklist and the streak so they agree. `daily/streak.ts` `refreshStreak` runs on every dashboard load AND nightly 00:05 IST; a study day = the any-activity rule (daily quiz done / 10+ SRS reviews / 1 answer / 1 attempt / whole checklist); advances `streak_count`+`last_active_date`, breaks a stale streak lazily, idempotent per day. Dashboard top card is now a 4-item checklist (`components/dashboard/guided-today-card.tsx`) with an SVG `ProgressRing`; the streak flame animates once on the load that advances it (`StreakFlame` `animate` prop, framer-motion, reduced-motion-aware). `DashboardGreeting` gained `streak_incremented_today`/`streak_active_today`; `DashboardToday` gained `checklist`/`checklist_completed`/`checklist_total`.
  - **6 — notification_schedule** (`0039`): `notification_schedule` table (quiz_ready / streak_at_risk / srs_due, dedupe per user+day). `services/notifications.ts` `generateForUser` is idempotent AND resolves nudges whose reason is gone. Quiz-ready 5 AM, SRS 7 AM, streak-at-risk only after ~8 PM IST with no activity. Generated hourly by the scheduler + on every `GET /notifications` (self-heal); the in-app list surfaces only nudges whose `scheduled_for` has passed (web push reads the same rows in Session 21). A `NotificationBell` (badge + right Sheet, read/dismiss) in the TopBar.

- Session 16 (2026-07-08): real FSRS scheduling for the `srs_cards`/`srs_reviews` tables that Session 8-14's "Add to revision" hooks had been writing into all along with no scheduler behind them. **`ts-fsrs@5.4.1`** (pinned, recorded here per instruction) added to `apps/api` only — all scheduling logic lives server-side in the new `apps/api/src/lib/fsrs.ts` (`reviewCard`/`previewIntervals`, wrapping `fsrs()`/`scheduler.next()`/`scheduler.repeat()`).
  - **BUG FOUND BY THE "verify every existing hook" ask, not by a user report**: none of the six existing add-to-revision write paths (`addNodeToRevision`, `addQuestionToRevision`, `addEvaluationToRevision`, `addCurrentAffairsFactToRevision` in `services/srs.ts`; `addNoteDeckToRevision`, `addNoteBlockToRevision` in `services/notes.ts`) ever touched `fsrs_state` — it sat at the column default `'{}'::jsonb` forever, and `fsrs_state ->> 'due_at'` on an empty object is SQL `NULL`, which never satisfies a `<= now()` due filter. Every card ever added to revision (including the 3 real published notes' SRS decks from Session 14) was **invisible to the due queue from the moment it was created** — it could never be reviewed, so it could never earn a real `due_at` either. Confirmed live: before the fix, `srs_cards` had 10 real rows for the dev user, all with `fsrs_state='{}'`, 0 ever due.
  - Fixed at the DB layer, not by touching six call sites: migration `0048_srs_fsrs_defaults.sql` adds a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger (`srs_card_default_fsrs_state`) that stamps a fresh "New card, due now" FSRS state (`due_at=now(), state=0, stability=0, difficulty=0, reps=0, lapses=0, last_review=null`) whenever `fsrs_state` is left at the bare `'{}'` default — fires only on INSERT, so the idempotent upserts every add-to-revision path already uses can keep never mentioning `fsrs_state` without risk of resetting an already-reviewed card's progress on a re-add. Also backfills the 10 pre-existing stuck rows. Live-verified: `GET /srs/due` immediately returned all 10 previously-invisible cards after the migration.
  - New endpoints in `routes/srs.ts` (service fns of the same name in `services/srs.ts`): `GET /srs/due?limit=` (queue + `due_count`, each card carrying a `preview` of all four ratings' next-due dates via `previewIntervals` so the player never needs FSRS logic client-side), `GET /srs/stats` (due today / reviewed today / retention % over a 30-day window, non-`Again` share / 7-day forecast — day 0 absorbs overdue backlog Anki-style, days 1-6 are exact IST-day buckets), `POST /srs/reviews {reviews:[{card_id,rating}]}` (batched to match the offline-queue's flush-a-batch contract; reschedules via `reviewCard` and logs `srs_reviews`), full manage-view CRUD (`GET/POST /srs/cards`, `PATCH/DELETE /srs/cards/:id`), and the two real-data one-tap seeds (`POST /srs/seed/wrong-answers` — reuses `addQuestionToRevision` over the user's actually-missed MCQs; `POST /srs/seed/note-facts` — reuses `addNoteDeckToRevision` over notes the user has actually opened, keyed off the existing `note_read` event's `props.note_id`).
