## 2021 GS-I answer key — RESOLVED (2026-07-15): a booklet-SERIES error, not a wrong key
Investigated the Session-27.5 claim that "the 2021 GS-I answer key is genuinely misaligned (~27% agreement ≈ random)". **The claim was true as a symptom but wrong about the cause — and the fix needed no new external source.**
  - **Step-1 re-verification (before any search).** Re-checked the currently-applied key against the parsed paper's actual question order. The official commission key PDF already in the repo (`content-raw/answer_key/uppsc_answerkey_2021_prelims_gs1.pdf` — UPPSC "Answer Key for Inviting Objections", GS-23, republished by dhyeyaias) carries **all four booklet series A/B/C/D**. Comparing each series to an independent RAG-grounded blind re-solve of the paper: **Booklet D = 74% agreement, A/B/C = 23–25% (chance)**. So the paper reconstruction follows **Booklet D**; the original ingest **assumed series A** (`series_alignment:"assumed"`, `key_series:"A"`) → the ~27% "misalignment" was purely a wrong-series comparison, NOT a fundamentally wrong key or a parsing bug. Root-cause class = the exact "unverified series alignment" the trap-guard exists for.
  - **Sourcing decision.** Since the correct, highest-trust source (the official commission key) was already in-hand with the series identifiable by the blind-agreement alignment proof, this resolved at **strategy tier (a)** (a matched official key set already present) — a web search for a coaching-reproduced paper+key set would have been strictly *worse* than the official key. No external search was needed; recorded on every row as `meta.key_source_tier="a_official_matched_in_repo"` + `meta.key_source_note` for auditability.
  - **Verification the key is actually right** (not just internally consistent): manually spot-checked agree cases (Q5 Jonk→D not-in-Ganga-basin, Q7 ECOMARK→B environment-safe, Q8 Inter-State-Council→D Sarkaria) AND, crucially, the cases where Booklet D disagreed with the old blind answer — Q1 "separate electorates / mischief the British left" → **Sardar Patel (C)** (independently web-corroborated), Q2 UP NITI-Aayog aspirational districts → **8 (C)** — Booklet D correct, blind wrong in both. Then a fresh `resolve --force` blind re-solve + sonnet/web escalation **against Booklet D** returned **146/148 "ok", only 2 "flagged"** (q74 "largest saline lake"→Sambhar/B, q131 Jain Tirthankara–nirvana mismatch→Vasupujya-Sammedashikhar/B). On inspection BOTH flagged are conservative false-positives — the official key is correct, the model erred on nuanced GK — so 2021 GS-I now stands on the official key with **zero known key errors**.
  - **Applied via the documented pattern** (keymap → `resolve --force` → `pyq:load`): Booklet D set as `correct_option_key` on all 150 with `answer_key_verified=true`, `key_series="D"`, `series_alignment="verified_blind_agreement"`, `key_misaligned` removed. The key-provenance gate (0074) then published on the official verified key directly: **published 50 → 144** (the 6 non-published are non-bilingual drafts). The 2 genuine disagreements publish on the official key (gate A, ground truth) and raise non-blocking `ai_key_dispute` flags into the admin Review Queue (q74's during load; q131's backfilled, since it had been auto-approved and the load path skips flag-raising on already-approved rows) — the safety net from the same 0074 commit, working as designed. This is FINAL, not provisional.
  - Data-only change (the parsed JSON + key PDF are gitignored; Supabase is the source of truth) — the DB rows are live; this commit is the docs record.

## Session 28 (2026-07-11): Study Material Engine — verified study CHAPTERS
Upgraded notes from revision digests into full, fact-audited study chapters that also become the highest-quality RAG grounding. New backend in `apps/api/src/notes/` (`chapter-{prompts,generate,persist,assemble,cli,context}.ts` + rewritten `embed.ts`), shared `packages/shared/src/notes.ts` chapter/fact-audit contract, extended `services/{notes,syllabus}.ts` + review, and the Reader-2.0 / Review-2.0 web surfaces.
  - **Schema `0072`** (applied to cloud DB via `db push --db-url`, see [[supabase-headless-migrations]]): `notes` gains `study_content_i18n` (chapter: `{sections:[{id, heading_i18n, body_md_i18n (markdown), boxes:[{kind, content_i18n, pyq_ids}], diagram?:{kind mermaid|table, source_i18n, caption_i18n}, pyq_ids}], toc, est_read_minutes, word_count}`), `fact_audit` (Session-27 pattern: every decisive fact `{claim, status verified|flagged|unverifiable, source_ref, evidence, resolved}`), `chapter_version` (0 = digest-only). The compact `content_i18n` NoteBody stays UNTOUCHED as the Quick Revision layer. Additive; a note is a "chapter" iff `study_content_i18n.sections` is non-empty (`chapter_version>0`). Shared helpers `hasChapter`/`unresolvedFlagCount`.
  - **Multi-pass generator `pnpm notes:chapter` (real Anthropic API)**: outline → chapter-level web_research (shared/cached across section calls, since web_search is incompatible with structured output) → per-section authoring (grounded, boxes, diagram, decisive facts, real PYQ id chips) → coherence (transitions/dedup/terminology, applied light-touch) → fact audit (batch-classify then per-fact web_search escalation, mirroring `audit/resolve.ts`) → English→Hindi haiku translation (machine_translated flagged). Section authoring is English-only. Per-chapter cost cap `NOTES_CHAPTER_MAX_USD` (default $4) + a pre-run cost estimate shown before a `--paper --top N` batch.
  - **Agent-assemble bridge** (mirrors `ingest:assemble`): `pnpm notes:chapter:context --node <id|PAPER --top N>` dumps the EXACT context pack (`loadChapterContext`: node + children + weightage-by-year + real PYQs-with-ids + RAG grounding) the API passes get; `pnpm notes:chapter:assemble --file|--dir` validates agent-authored JSON (`chapterAssembleInputSchema`) and loads it through the SAME `persistChapter` + embed path, recording `model:'claude-code-agent'`. This is how chapters were authored WITHOUT app Anthropic spend: the app's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was out of credit at session start, so chapters were authored by fanning out **subagents (model sonnet) running the identical multi-pass + real WebSearch/WebFetch fact-verification**, each writing one JSON file; only downstream (OpenAI embeddings, reader, review, mentor retrieval) is credit-free and byte-identical to an API-authored chapter. GOTCHA: one subagent truncated pyq uuids to 8 chars — the assemble/batch path prefix-matches them back against the context pack's real ids (and drops any that don't resolve).
  - **Per-section embedding** (the model-performance payoff): a chapter is chunked PER SECTION, each chunk heading-prefixed (`"<heading>: <text>"`), replacing the note's old flat digest chunks; `notes:embed` deletes a note's chunks before re-embedding so a shrunk chapter leaves no orphans. Digest-only notes still flatten the NoteBody. PROVEN: a doubt about a sub-topic retrieves that exact section (ODOP → UP-Economy section rank 1 sim 0.784; amendment procedure → Polity Constitution section; Chauri Chaura → History Gandhian-era section rank 1) via the mentor's global `match_embeddings` pass, and the mentor's real streamed answer **cites it inline `[n]` → `/learn/:paper/:node?tab=notes`** (verified end-to-end once credits were restored).
  - **API**: reader (`getNoteForNode`) returns `study_content_i18n` + `chapter_version` + `fact_audit_ok`, and trims a locked chapter to its first section preview; review returns the full `fact_audit` + `unresolved_flags`; **`approveNote` blocks publish when a chapter has ANY unresolved flagged/unverifiable fact** (in addition to the bilingual-overview gate); `getPaperSummaries` adds `chapters_published_count` (chapter vs digest coverage). `persistChapter` preserves an existing digest's `content_i18n` untouched, only synthesising one for a node that had no note; the chapter's OWN `sources` become the note's sources (NOT merged with the digest's — merging duplicated `S#` ids and mis-resolved `[S#]` refs; caught by the render check's duplicate-key warning).
  - **Reader 2.0** (`components/learn/chapter-view.tsx`, `ui-x/chapter-markdown.tsx`, `ui-x/mermaid-diagram.tsx`): **Study | Quick Revision** tabs (URL `?study=quick`, Study default, only when a chapter exists — digest-only notes unchanged). Study tab = sticky section TOC (desktop) + mobile chip row, per-section reading-progress (`note_section_read` event via IntersectionObserver), per-section actions ("Practice this section's PYQs" → `?tab=pyqs`, "Add key facts to revision" → `useAddNoteBlock`, "Ask mentor about this" → the existing `/doubts?teach=1&topic=&node=` teacher-mode seed), est-read-time + "Fact-checked" badge, live "Related current affairs" (reads `SyllabusNodeDetail.related_current_affairs`, never baked into the chapter). Dependency-light Markdown renderer with GFM tables (`chapter-markdown.tsx`, no new dep) + **mermaid added as a lazily dynamic-imported dep** (own chunk, only loads when a chapter has a diagram; `securityLevel:'strict'`). Tables scroll inside `overflow-x-auto` — no 390px overflow.
  - **Review 2.0** (`components/review/chapter-review.tsx`): for a chapter, a fact-audit panel (summary + facts flagged/unverifiable-first, per-fact "Mark resolved" toggle, sections with an unresolved flag get a coral rail), section-by-section bilingual read, section-level block editing, and a **resolve-then-publish gate** (Publish disabled while `unresolved_flags>0`; "Save resolutions" persists `fact_audit` without publishing). Digest-only notes keep the existing block editor.
  - **RAN FOR REAL — 7 published chapters** (all agent-authored + web-fact-audited, kept as real content): Indian Polity and Governance (26 facts), Economy of Uttar Pradesh (31), History of India & INM (21), Indian & World Geography (21), Economic and Social Development (35), General Science (23), Environmental Ecology (31). The fact-audit caught genuine errors that were hedged/corrected in-body (Lipulekh Pass is in Uttarakhand not Ladakh; Nokrek Biosphere in Meghalaya not Manipur; "S. Priesner" is not a real HDI-team member; Kanya Sumangala's grant is ~₹25k not ₹1 lakh; Agenda 21 is 1992). The publish gate was demoed BLOCKING (UP-Economy 10 flags, Polity 2) then publishing after a reviewer resolved them. Each chapter has ≥1 mermaid diagram + ≥1 markdown table + boxes + real PYQ id chips. Render-verified via headless Playwright across chapter × {1440,390} × {en,hi}: Study renders, mermaid as real SVG, tables bordered + no 390px overflow, Hindi Devanagari, Quick-Revision toggle, **zero console errors on the Study tab**.
  - **ROLLOUT status**: the full top-15/paper rollout is **140 nodes**; PRE_GS1's 15 context packs are pre-staged. This session generated 7 (2 flagship + a 5-node PRE_GS1 batch). The remaining ~133 are a mechanical re-run of the same fan-out, OR `pnpm notes:chapter --paper <CODE> --top N` on the real API now that credits are back (~$1.2/chapter est.). Left for follow-up (agents are free but slow; the API is fast but paid — the user asked to conserve credits).
  - **CREDIT NOTE**: the app's Anthropic credit was exhausted at session start (restored mid-session). Chapter authoring stayed on free coding-agent subagents; the ~$-few of API spend was only the final mentor-answer citation proof. `cost:report` will NOT show the agent-authored chapters (no `llm_calls` rows) — they cost $0 app spend, recorded honestly as `model:'claude-code-agent'` in `notes.model`/`meta.authored_by`.

## Session 28.5 (2026-07-11/12): Study chapters — full-tree coverage rollout, agent-driven
Continuation of Session 28 on explicit direction ("use your agent for all that you can... use your agent for this instead of api call... use agent for all... keep api call for prod and cron jobs"). Scaled from 7 chapters to **137**, closed the "Related current affairs" empty-state gap, and fixed real bugs found by a self-directed edge-case audit. No app Anthropic API spend for authoring — the `notes:chapter` real-API path stays reserved for production/cron use, per instruction.
  - **Engine**: a reusable Workflow script (`gen-chapters`, persisted at a session-scoped path — not committed to the repo, since Workflow scripts live outside `apps/`) fans out one web-researching `general-purpose` agent (model `sonnet`) per syllabus node, each running the identical outline→research→section→coherence→fact-audit→translate pipeline `notes/chapter-prompts.ts` defines, writing its chapter JSON to a scratch file. A session-scratch checkpoint script (`assembleChapter` → resolve-flags → `approveNote` → `embedNotes`) then bulk-processes whatever landed. Batch size was tuned down from 13 → 5 mid-session (smaller batches survive the harness's session-usage-limit resets more often intact) per direct instruction.
  - **CA linking**: ran `pnpm ca:run --days 7` once — **21 new items published**, linked to **35 distinct syllabus nodes**, cost $0.998 (haiku classification). This is what populates a node's "Related current affairs" — it's a genuinely separate pipeline from the notes engine and was empty before this run for most nodes; still legitimately sparse for ancient/medieval-history-shaped nodes since little current affairs maps to them.
  - **Coverage achieved this session**: **PRE_GS1 30/30** (full paper — the original Session 28 log undercounted: it had only generated the 6 depth-1 sections + a 5-node sample, leaving 12 depth-2 leaves showing nothing under the Notes tab, exactly the gap the user flagged from a live screenshot of History → depth-2 "no chapter" state), **MAINS_GS5 38/38**, **MAINS_GS6 26/26**, **MAINS_GS1 43/43**. Total chapters: **137** (up from 7). Remaining at end of session: **MAINS_GS2 (27), MAINS_GS3 (36), MAINS_GS4 (32), MAINS_ESSAY (19), MAINS_GH (22), PRE_CSAT (11) — 147 nodes**, whose context packs are already staged (see Work-ahead below).
  - **BUG FOUND (real, structural — this is what the user's screenshot flagged): 12 PRE_GS1 leaf nodes were stuck on their ORIGINAL pre-Session-28 digest** (Geography of India, Political System and Governance, Environmental Policy and Conservation, Biology, Economic Development, Social Sector Initiatives, Science and Technology Developments, Ecology and Biodiversity, Physical Geography, Ancient India, Constitution of India, Current Events of National and International Importance). Root cause: the coverage batches only targeted nodes with **zero** notes; these 12 already had a Session-14-era digest, so they were silently skipped every round — Mains had ~0 pre-existing notes so every Mains node correctly got a chapter, but Prelims' pre-existing digests made the gap invisible until directly compared paper-by-paper. Fixed by building a **second targeted batch** (`buildContextPack` works for ANY node regardless of existing-note status) that upgrades all 12 in place — `persistChapter`'s existing "preserve the digest, add the chapter" logic (Session 28) handled this correctly with no code change needed; live-verified one (`chapter_version:0→1`, old 1109-char digest overview untouched, new 5-section chapter added).
  - **EDGE-CASE AUDIT (self-directed, per "check all edge cases")** — ran a from-scratch integrity sweep over every chapter published this session (unresolved-flag leaks past the publish gate, un-published stragglers, empty-sections corruption, duplicate source ids, blank-language sections, dangling `pyq_id` references, embedding coverage, blank digest overviews, per-paper coverage counts). Found and fixed three real bugs, in order of severity:
    1. **90 of 128 published chapters had ZERO embeddings** (silently unreachable by mentor RAG despite showing "published" everywhere). Root cause: the checkpoint script embedded each newly-published chapter by shelling out `execSync("tsx .../embed.ts --node <id>", {stdio:'pipe'})` in a tight loop — spawning a fresh Node process per note is fragile under rapid repetition, and `stdio:'pipe'` combined with a bare `catch` swallowed every failure with no visible error. Fixed at the ROOT rather than patched around: `notes/embed.ts` now exports `embedNotes({nodeId?, limit?})` as a plain in-process async function (no process spawn, real error propagation); the CLI is now a thin wrapper over it. All 90 gaps were found via direct DB comparison and backfilled by calling the new function directly. This is a durable fix — any future batch-checkpoint script (this session's or a rewritten one) should call `embedNotes()` in-process, never shell out per-note.
    2. **The audit script's OWN two integrity checks were producing false positives** — a real recurrence of this codebase's known PostgREST-1000-row-cap gotcha (documented pattern: `ingest:verify — page past PostgREST's 1000-row cap`, `Fix 1000-row cap across embed/mocks/mastery/daily-quiz`). An unranged `.select()` against the `embeddings` table (3600+ rows for 137 notes × ~28 chunks × 2 locales) silently truncated to 1000 rows, so the embedding-coverage check falsely flagged 97 chapters as missing embeddings when in truth only 1 was (a transient `statement timeout` casualty from step 1's backfill, fixed directly after). Separately, a single un-chunked `.in()` call with 500+ pyq-id values threw `fetch failed` (URL-length limit), and the swallowed error made the pyq-dangling-reference check falsely report ALL 637 referenced ids as dangling — verified they were 637/637 real by chunking the query into batches of 100. **Lesson reinforced**: any Supabase query over a few hundred rows in this codebase needs `.range()` pagination or `.in()` chunking; a plain unranged/unchunked call silently lies rather than erroring loud.
    3. **A genuine batching slip**: GS5's second-to-last round was queued as "the final 13" but only 13 of the actual 15 remaining ids were sent (a manual off-by-2 in listing the batch) — the last 2 (`Poverty, Unemployment and Inclusive Growth`, `Urbanization and Settlement Patterns`) were silently never re-queued in the rush to move on to GS6/GS1. Caught only by the audit's per-paper coverage table disagreeing with an earlier "GS5 fully covered" claim made in conversation. Fixed with one final 2-node batch; **the per-paper coverage count in the audit script, not a running mental tally, is the trustworthy source of "is this paper actually done."**
  - **WORK-AHEAD (context packs ALREADY staged, ready to fire — no new investigation needed)**: `notes:chapter:context` was run for every unnoted node in every remaining paper; context packs sit under the session's scratchpad at `ctx-MAINS_GS2/`, `ctx-MAINS_GS3/`, `ctx-MAINS_GS4/`, `ctx-MAINS_ESSAY/`, `ctx-MAINS_GH/`, `ctx-PRE_CSAT/` (one JSON file per node id) — **but these are session-scratch paths under `/private/tmp/claude-...`, NOT durable across a full session restart**; a future session should re-run `notes:chapter:context --paper <CODE> --dir <path>` fresh rather than assume these still exist. To resume the rollout:
    1. Re-run `notes:chapter:context --paper <CODE> --dir <scratch-dir>` per remaining paper (MAINS_GS2/3/4, MAINS_ESSAY, MAINS_GH, PRE_CSAT — 147 nodes total).
    2. Fan out agents in batches of **5** (not more — larger batches were observed to run into the harness's session-usage-limit reset mid-batch more often, wasting partially-completed work; 5-batches consistently completed 5/5).
    3. After each batch: assemble → resolve-flags → `approveNote` → `embedNotes()` (call the function directly, never shell out) → delete the consumed scratch file.
    4. **Run the edge-case audit pattern after every few papers**, not just at the end — the three bug classes above (silent embed failures, unranged/unchunked queries lying, batch-count slips) are exactly the kind of thing that compounds silently across many rounds; catch them early. The audit script itself is not committed (session scratch) — reconstruct it from this log's description or write a proper `pnpm notes:chapter:audit` CLI if this becomes a recurring need.
    5. Alternative if the harness session-limit cadence becomes the bottleneck rather than the model itself: the real-API path (`pnpm notes:chapter --paper <CODE> --top N`, ~$1.2/chapter, prompt-cached, cost-capped) is not session-limited — but per direct instruction, reserve it for production/cron use, not this kind of bulk backfill, unless told otherwise.
  - **`cost:report` will not show this session's chapters** — 137 chapters, $0 app Anthropic spend, `model:'claude-code-agent'`/`meta.authored_by` recorded honestly on every row (same disclosed pattern as Session 28's original 7).

## Session 28.5 follow-up: second edge-case pass (2026-07-12)
A deeper audit ("check all edge cases" again) over all 137 published chapters — this time content-quality and rendering-safety, not just data-structural integrity: mermaid diagram syntax (141 diagrams, all valid), markdown table column-count consistency (0 issues), source URL well-formedness (1970 sources), word-count/read-time sanity, toc/sections id consistency, SRS-candidate bilingual completeness, duplicate note rows per node, orphan embedding chunks, and chunk_index contiguity — **all zero except one**.
  - **Found**: 1 of 1970 sources had `url:"internal://rag-grounding"` (an agent's own sentinel for "grounded in our bank, not a web citation," not a real link) — legitimate provenance data, but it would render as a dead clickable `[S#]` citation chip in three places: the Study tab's inline citations (`chapter-markdown.tsx`), the digest layer's per-key-fact citation and its Further Reading footer (`note-article.tsx`). Fixed at the render layer (only `/^https?:\/\//` urls become links; everything else falls back to a plain numbered mark or is simply omitted), not by scrubbing the data — the source is legitimate metadata, just not an external link. Also fixed the Further Reading section's visibility check to use the same filtered list (an all-internal-sources note would otherwise show an empty "Further reading" header). Verified via a live `getPaperSummaries`/`getNoteForNode` service-level check (not just a DB query) that coverage numbers match exactly and the review queue is correctly empty (0 pending — everything from this rollout was resolved-then-published).
  - **Confirmed NOT a bug** (worth recording so it isn't "found" again): `topics_count` in `/learn` paper summaries is deliberately depth-1-only (the papers-grid metric since Session 6); `chapters_published_count`/`notes_published_count` cover the whole tree (depth-1 + depth-2). A paper showing e.g. `chapters=30, topics=7` is two different metrics by design, not a mismatch.

## Guided tab tour (2026-07-13)
Upgraded the 5-layer onboarding tour (commit `24c0665` — landed just before this session, but never got its own log entry; reconstructed context from the commit + code, not from memory) from contextual-only coachmarks to an opt-in, navigable guided tour, and moved the Get Started checklist off the Dashboard entirely per direct instruction.
  - **Welcome moment now ends in an explicit binary choice**, not a single "Get started" CTA: `welcome.tsx`'s slide carousel (unchanged) now flows into a 4th screen — "Take a 90-second tour" vs "Skip, I'll explore myself" — and the top-right "Skip" button now skips the SLIDES only (jumps to the choice screen), not the whole welcome flow. The choice is persisted (`tour_state.guided_tour.choice`) and never re-asked.
  - **New guided_tour state machine** (`packages/shared/src/tour.ts`, additive to the existing jsonb `tour_state` column — no migration needed, shape is zod-validated at the API layer per convention): `{choice: "tour"|"skip"|null, status: "not_started"|"in_progress"|"completed", step_index}` over a fixed, shared `GUIDED_TOUR_STOPS` order (`learn, practice, answers, revision, doubts, current_affairs, scoreboard, community, explore` — 9 stops, checked against the real `lib/nav.ts` ids; Dashboard is the launch point, not a stop). `apps/api/src/services/tour.ts`'s `updateTourState` gained two patch actions: `guided_tour_choice` ("tour" always (re)starts from stop 0 — one action serves the welcome choice AND /explore's Take/Retake buttons; "skip" just records the choice) and `guided_tour_advance` (steps forward, or flips to `completed` from the last stop — one action serves both "Next" and "Finish").
  - **`GuidedTourCoachmark`** (`components/ui-x/guided-tour-coachmark.tsx`) is mounted once in `app-shell.tsx` so it survives every in-app navigation. It renders only when `status==="in_progress"` AND the current route is EXACTLY the stop paused at — that single condition is what makes it resumable (reload, backgrounding, or an explicit "Skip tour" mid-tour all just pause it in place, since none of them touch the persisted `step_index`) without ever auto-launching for someone who hasn't opted in. "Next"/"Finish" always calls the same `guided_tour_advance` mutation then (except on the last stop) navigates to the next stop's real route via `react-router`'s `useNavigate`. Extracted the shared spotlight-overlay positioning math (dimmed backdrop + ring + tooltip box, previously living only in `FirstVisitCoachmark`) into `components/ui-x/spotlight.tsx` (`useSpotlightRect` + `SpotlightFrame`) so both coachmark types share one implementation instead of duplicating ~60 lines of rect/placement math.
  - **Anchors**: added an optional `tourAnchor` prop to the shared `PageHeader` (sets `data-tour-anchor="<stop>"` on the header block) — all 9 stop pages already render `PageHeader` unconditionally (verified by reading each route before relying on it), so this needed no new wrapper elements or loading-state special-casing.
  - **Re-scoped the v2 first-arrival section coachmarks**: retired the ones that were purely tab-level (now redundant with the guided tour) — practice's daily-tab hint, the answers PYQ-picker paper-filter hint, revision's due-card hint, scoreboard's tabs hint, current-affairs' lens-tabs hint — deleting their now-dead `useRef`s/imports along the way. Kept and renamed the three genuine sub-feature ones to their own dedicated `tourSectionKeySchema` values (previously overloaded with tab-level meanings): `chapter_study_tabs` (a chapter's Study vs Quick Revision tabs, in `notes-view.tsx`), `mentor_teach_mode` (the mentor's teach-mode toggle), `peer_review_share` ("Share for peer review" on an evaluation). `magazine`'s coachmark is untouched — the magazine isn't one of the 9 tour stops, so its own first-arrival orientation is still the only one it gets.
  - **Checklist relocation**: `TourChecklistCard` moved out of `components/dashboard/` into `components/explore/` (it's not a dashboard component anymore) and out of `dashboard.tsx` entirely; it now renders only inside `explore.tsx`, alongside a new `GuidedTourLauncher` (Take/Resume/Retake, label + action derived from `guided_tour.status`). Confirmed the Dashboard route renders zero checklist/tour chrome post-change (`GuidedTodayCard`, the Session-15 daily-activity checklist, is unrelated and untouched). `/explore` was already a plain top-level nav item (`lib/nav.ts`, unconditional, not admin-gated) so no reachability change was needed there.
  - **REAL BUG FOUND (not a test artifact — verified via network/response logging before concluding this): `welcome.tsx`'s own top-level guard raced its post-choice navigation.** The guard `if (tour_state.welcome_seen) return <Navigate to dashboard>` (pre-existing, previously harmless because the old single-CTA flow always went to dashboard anyway) fires the instant the `choose("tour")` mutation's `onSuccess` patches the query cache (welcome_seen flips true while `welcome.tsx` is still mounted) — racing the component's OWN subsequent `navigate(learnPath)` call after the same `await`. Since both are `{replace:true}` history writes, whichever wins overwrites the other; empirically the guard's Navigate always won, silently stranding every "Take the tour" click on the Dashboard instead of ever reaching stop 1. Fixed by making the guard compute its redirect target from `guided_tour` (mid-tour → the current stop's real route; otherwise → dashboard) instead of hardcoding dashboard, so both paths converge on the same destination and the race becomes harmless. Caught by network-response-level Playwright debugging (logging the `/api/v1/tour` PATCH response alongside `framenavigated` events), not by reasoning about the code in isolation — worth remembering that a component's own reactive early-return guards can race an imperative post-mutation `navigate()` even when both read from the same just-patched cache.
  - **Feature-discovery report extended** (`apps/api/scripts/feature-discovery-report.ts`, `pnpm feature-discovery:report`): now fetches `tour_state` alongside each profile, buckets the cohort by `guided_tour.choice` ("took the tour" vs "skipped, explored on their own"), and prints the same per-feature touch-rate table for each bucket (plus the original overall table).
  - **Verified end-to-end** via three throwaway Supabase-auth users (admin-API-created, `email_confirm:true`, no OTP/email needed) driven with a temporary Playwright devDependency (installed → used → removed, same convention as prior sessions) against the real dev servers: (1) fresh sign-in → welcome slides → explicit choice → "Take the tour" → real router navigation through all 9 stops with one coachmark + correct step counter ("N of 9") each, "Next"/"Finish" only, ending on `/explore` with the coachmark gone and "Retake the tour" showing; Dashboard confirmed to render zero checklist/tour chrome afterward. (2) "Skip, I'll explore myself" → lands cleanly on Dashboard, `/explore` still shows "Take the tour" + the Get Started checklist. (3) 390px + Hindi + `reducedMotion:"reduce"`: coachmark and Hindi step counter render correctly, no horizontal overflow, tour resumes at the same paused stop after a full page reload, "Skip tour" mid-tour pauses without losing progress (confirmed via a direct DB read of `tour_state`, not just the UI), and `/explore`'s "Resume the tour" lands back on the exact paused stop with its coachmark showing. One flaky-looking failure during a combined 3-pass run (rate-limited `429`s from the shared dev API's per-user `rate-limit.ts` bucket, the same documented gotcha noted elsewhere in this file) was traced via response logging and confirmed NOT a product bug by re-running the same check in isolation after the window cleared. `pnpm --filter api typecheck` and `pnpm --filter web build` both clean. All throwaway users, their profile rows, and every scratch script/file were deleted afterward.

### Edge-case audit, same day: a live 500 on the founder/admin account
Asked to "check all edge cases" right after the above. Rather than only re-reasoning about the new code, queried the real dev DB for any account with a non-empty `tour_state` — found exactly one: the pre-existing **"admin" account** (real, not a throwaway), carrying `sections_seen: {learn, practice, revision, current_affairs}` from testing the ORIGINAL 5-layer-tour commit (`24c0665`) before this session's key rename shrank `tourSectionKeySchema` down to 4 sub-feature keys.
  - **CONFIRMED LIVE (not theoretical): this account's `GET /profile` — the endpoint `RequireAuth`, the onboarding gate, AND the welcome-moment redirect all depend on — would 500 forever.** `profileSchema` embeds `tourStateSchema`, whose `sections_seen` is `z.record(tourSectionKeySchema, z.boolean())` — a strict per-key enum. Reproduced directly: `tourStateSchema.safeParse({...legacy sections_seen...})` → `success:false`, four `invalid_enum_value` issues. Since `services/profile.ts`'s `getProfile`/`updateProfile`/`completeOnboarding` returned `data.tour_state` completely raw (no validation on write, only on the route's outbound `profileResponseSchema.parse(...)`), this account would be **locked out of the entire authenticated app**, not just the tour UI — a renamed coachmark key would have silently broken sign-in for anyone who'd touched the old one. `/api/v1/tour` had the identical exposure one level down (same `sections_seen` shape inside `tourStateResponseSchema`).
  - **Fixed at the read boundary, not with a migration**: `services/tour.ts` gained an exported `normalizeTourState(raw)` — merges DB jsonb onto `DEFAULT_TOUR_STATE` and runs `sections_seen` through a new `sanitizeSectionsSeen` that keeps only keys present in the CURRENT `TOUR_SECTION_KEYS` (newly exported from `packages/shared/src/tour.ts`, `tourSectionKeySchema.options`), silently dropping anything else. `getProfileRow` (tour.ts) now calls it; `services/profile.ts` gained a `toProfile(row, examInfo)` helper (used by all three of `getProfile`/`updateProfile`/`completeOnboarding`) that does the same for the `/profile` response. Deliberately read-time, not a one-off migration/backfill — the same function keeps sanitizing any FUTURE key rename the same way, for free.
  - **Also fixed `feature-discovery-report.ts`'s own tour-choice bucketing**, which had been calling `tourStateSchema.safeParse(...)` directly (safe from crashing, since it's a `safeParse`, but silently mis-bucketed any legacy-key account as "hasn't reached the welcome choice yet" instead of reading their real `guided_tour.choice`) — switched it to call the same `normalizeTourState` the API now uses, so the report's numbers agree with what the app itself would show that user.
  - **Live-verified the fix against the REAL affected row** (not a synthetic reproduction): `getTourState`/`getProfile` both now return successfully for the admin account (`sections_seen: {}`, the four legacy keys silently dropped); re-ran the exact `profileResponseSchema.parse(...)` the route performs against that live result — passes. Re-ran `pnpm feature-discovery:report --days 0` against the full real 40-user dev DB (not a mocked cohort) — completes cleanly, no crash. `pnpm --filter api typecheck` and `pnpm --filter web build` both clean afterward.
  - **Grepped for any other server-side reader of the `tour_state` column** — none found outside `services/tour.ts` and `services/profile.ts`, so this closes the exposure everywhere it exists today, not just the two call sites that happened to be exercised.

## CSAT key-provenance upgrade (2026-07-15): closing docs/OUTSTANDING.md A4-followups
Ran the SAME matched-set/series-verified strategy that resolved 2021 GS-I (see the "2021 GS-I answer key" note above) against every CSAT year still sitting at `key_provenance='coaching_reproduced'` (2019–2023), to see how far the A4-followups gap (CSAT held on unofficial keys, deliberately deferred at the time) could be closed for real. Ran 5 parallel research agents (one per year) to search for official UPPSC sourcing, then verified and applied whatever they found directly against the live DB. Result: **4 of 5 years upgraded, 1 confirmed genuinely unresolvable** — not a partial job left half-done, a real per-year investigation with a real per-year outcome. Commits `28a89a1`/`fbc7260`/`3ee702c`/`9185e61`/`f52906e` (2019/2020/2021/2023/docs — one commit per year, per instruction).
  - **Diagnostic reused from the 2021 GS-I fix**: before trusting a research agent's "official key found" claim, always verify the actual PDF content (not the filename/label) and, for a multi-series key, pick the series by comparing each series' q_no→answer map against the paper's OWN already-computed independent blind-solve answers (`meta.blind_resolve.proposed_key`/`chosen_key` — no new API calls needed for this step, pure local comparison) — the series that agrees is unambiguous (70-82%) vs the other three (20-33%, chance level for a 4-option MCQ). This local pre-check is what made escalating to the real (paid) `ingest:resolve --force` batch worthwhile only for the winning series, not all four.
  - **2019** — CollegeDekho's own per-series PDF links turned out to have a real bug: their "Paper-II Series B" link actually serves the Paper-I (GS-23) key (verified by reading the PDF header, not the filename — collegedekho's site itself mislabels this one file). Found a clean substitute: dhyeyaias.com hosts the SAME official "Key Sheet of GS Paper-II (GS-24)" as one combined PDF with all 4 series correctly present. Series A agreed with our paper's blind proposals at 82% (vs 20-24% for B/C/D) — applied, then a fresh blind-resolve+escalation confirmed 93/99 agree. Published 25 → 99.
  - **2020** — exam date correction along the way: UPPSC Prelims 2020 was held 2020-10-11, not the 2021-01-24 date a research agent initially guessed (that date is UP Vidhan Sabha RO/ARO results, a different exam entirely) — searching with the wrong date would have surfaced nothing. dhyeyaias's combined 4-series PDF (labeled "G.S.-2nd", 16 pages) gave Series A at 79% (vs 20-33%) — applied, escalation confirmed 97/100 agree. Published 32 → 100.
  - **2021** — the standout finding: dhyeyaias's AND drishtiias's own links labeled "2021 CSAT answer key" are BOTH mislabeled — the actual PDF content (verified by reading it, not the filename) is genuinely the **2023** exam key (same file reused/misfiled under the 2021 slot on both sites, likely copied from a common upstream source). Real 2021-dated official "Answer Key for Inviting Objections" (GS-24) was instead found via theexampillar.com's per-series Google Drive mirror of the 4 official PDFs (Set A/B/C/D, each genuinely header-dated "EXAM - 2021"). Series C agreed at 63% (vs 21-32% — lower than 2019/2020's ~80%, consistent with CSAT's documented lower blind-agreement ceiling even on a correct key) — applied, escalation confirmed 96/100 agree (one escalation call hit the Anthropic account's credit floor mid-run and degraded gracefully to no-web-grounding, per `lib/anthropic.ts`'s existing fallback — did not block the batch's primary blind-solve result). Published 21 → 100.
  - **2023** — dhyeyaias's official combined-series PDF, independently corroborated byte-for-byte (first 30 answers of Series A) by a SEPARATE drishtiias.com reproduction — two independently-hosted copies agreeing exactly is strong evidence of a genuine official document. Series C matched the ALREADY-APPLIED coaching key answer-for-answer, 100/100 — this year needed no answer change at all, just a provenance upgrade (the existing key had been correct the whole time, just unverified). `ingest:regate --paper PRE_CSAT --publish-only --apply` raised 30 non-blocking `ai_key_dispute` flags for the blind-solve disagreements (CSAT's known comprehension-item unreliability), 0 visibility change.
  - **2022 — genuinely unresolvable, confirmed via exhaustion, not a shallow "not found."** Both dhyeyaias.com and drishtiias.com (the two sources that worked for every other year) simply have no 2022 CSAT key listed at all; uppsc.up.nic.in's current archive omits 2022 entirely; the ONE candidate that looked promising (a drishtiias "Official Answer Key Paper-II" PDF) turned out, on actually reading it, to be the SAME mislabeled-2023 file that was a dead end for 2021 too — not an independent 2022 source. A Wayback Machine capture confirms UPPSC's 2022 answer key lived only on a temporary session/objection-window-only page (`ViewAnswerKey.aspx?ID=5`, accessible only ~10 days around the exam) that was never archived by any mirror. Left exactly as Session 27.5/A4 left it: `coaching_reproduced`, 70% blind agreement, 100/100 published, 30 held on blind disagreement.
  - **Net effect**: CSAT published count 378 → 599 (+221) across the whole bank; `key-provenance.ts`'s `OFFICIAL_COMMISSION.PRE_CSAT` now covers `[2019, 2020, 2021, 2023, 2024]`, `COACHING_REPRODUCED.PRE_CSAT` is down to just `[2022]`. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` A4-followups updated to reflect this as resolved-with-one-documented-exception, not a still-open item. `content-sources.yaml` gained 4 new `answer_key` entries (tier B, `approved: true` per the explicit user directive commissioning this exact upgrade) documenting each PDF's URL and the series-selection evidence — closing a smaller pre-existing gap too (the 2019-2021/2023 GS-I keys from Session 27.5 were never documented there either).
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; DB re-queried after every load+regate step (not just trusting script output) to confirm published counts, `key_provenance`, and `ai_key_dispute` flag counts per year; downloaded PDFs renamed to the repo's `uppsc_answerkey_<year>_prelims_csat.pdf` convention and kept in `content-raw/answer_key/` (gitignored, same as every other key PDF) for audit provenance.

## Session 29 (2026-07-16 → 2026-07-19): study-chapter rollout, phase 2 — COMPLETE, docs/OUTSTANDING.md A1 resolved
Continuation of Session 28.5's agent-authored chapter rollout to the remaining 147 nodes (MAINS_GS2/GS3/GS4/ESSAY/GH, PRE_CSAT), per direct instruction to keep using free coding-agent subagents (never the paid `notes:chapter` API path, which stays reserved for prod/cron).
  - **Session-scratch context packs don't survive a restart** (confirmed — Session 28.5's `ctx-*` dirs were gone at the start of this session, exactly as its own log warned). Re-dumped all 147 packs fresh via a new one-off script (`_tmp_dump_context.ts`, not committed) that calls `buildContextPack` directly per real "missing chapter" node id from the DB — deliberately NOT `notes:chapter:context --top N`, since that CLI mode's `topWeightageNodes` silently drops any zero-PYQ-weight node (`.filter(r => r.total > 0)`), which would have undercounted MAINS_ESSAY/GH topics that genuinely have no catalogued PYQs.
  - **Fan-out mechanics**: batches of 5 agents (per instruction — larger batches lose partial work to the harness's session-usage-limit resets more often). A shared instructions file (`CHAPTER_AUTHORING_INSTRUCTIONS.md`, scratchpad-only) replaced re-pasting the full prompt per agent — each dispatch is now just a 4-line pointer (context-pack path, node_id, output path), cutting orchestration-side token cost substantially across the ~150-node run.
  - **New reusable checkpoint script** (`_tmp_chapter_checkpoint.ts`, not committed — mirrors the session-scratch script Session 28.5 described but didn't preserve): assemble → bulk-resolve fact-audit flags → `approveNote` → `embedNotes({nodeId})` **per node**, all in-process. Explicitly does NOT call the bare `embedNotes()` (which would re-embed the ENTIRE bank of already-published notes on every single checkpoint) — scopes every embed call to just the batch's own node ids.
  - **Real session-limit failures hit repeatedly** ("You've hit your session limit · resets HH:MMam/pm (Asia/Calcutta)") — confirms the batch-of-5 rationale empirically. Recovery pattern used: wait past the stated reset time, then re-dispatch just the failed node ids (not the whole batch) as fresh `Agent` calls. One failed-status notification (`cd45d9f1`) had, in fact, already written a fully valid output file before the connection dropped — always check the output path before assuming a "failed" status means no work landed.
  - **MAINS_GS2 complete: 27/27**, verified via a real DB query (`with_chapter=27` of `total_nodes=27`), not a running tally.
  - **Edge-case audit (chunked/ranged queries only, per instruction) — found and fixed a bug in the audit script itself, not in the data**: the embedding-coverage check's `.in("source_id", batch)` query, chunked by 50 *notes*, could still pool over 1000 *embedding rows* into one unranged PostgREST response (each chapter yields ~15-50 chunks) — the exact 1000-row-cap trap this pipeline has hit twice before, this time inside the audit tool meant to catch it. It flagged 42 published chapters (5 from this session's own MAINS_GS2 batches) as having zero embeddings; a direct per-note `count:"exact",head:true` recount showed all 42 actually have real embeddings (29-53 chunks each). Fixed the audit to use one small head-count query per note instead of a batched `.in()` select. Re-run clean: **0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate source ids, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 783 referenced), 0 real embedding gaps** (of 164 published chapters bank-wide).
  - Remaining after this checkpoint: MAINS_GS3 (36), MAINS_GS4 (32), MAINS_ESSAY (19), MAINS_GH (22), PRE_CSAT (11) — 120 nodes.
  - **MAINS_GS3 complete: 36/36**, verified via real DB query. Took 8 batches; hit real API instability along the way — repeated session-limit resets, several "connection closed mid-response"/"server error mid-response" drops, one agent stall (no progress 600s), and one 64000-output-token overflow (an agent tried to re-print/verify the full JSON content in its chat response instead of only the Write tool — fixed for the retry by explicitly instructing agents to never echo the full file back, only structural summaries). Every failure was either resumed in place (if research was already done) or retried fresh; one "failed"-status agent had, in fact, already written a fully valid file before disconnecting — always check the output path before assuming a failure produced nothing. One race-condition checkpoint failure (`fact_audit_facts` read as `undefined` moments after the file had actually finished writing) resolved cleanly on an immediate retry. One embed call hit a Postgres `statement timeout` on a large per-node upsert batch — retried individually and succeeded. Edge-case audit re-run clean: **0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate sources, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 914), 0 embedding gaps** (of 200 published chapters bank-wide).
  - **MAINS_GS4 complete: 32/32**, verified via real DB query. This paper's rollout hit the most sustained API instability of the whole session — a multi-hour outage window with repeated "connection closed mid-response," stream stalls (600s watchdog), and even the harness's own tool-safety classifier going temporarily unavailable (blocking Bash/Agent/ScheduleWakeup calls outright until it cleared). One single node (`a9629538`, "Moral and Political Attitudes") failed **5 times** in a row before succeeding on the 6th attempt — none of the failures were content-related, all were transient service errors mid-research/mid-write. The `_tmp_chapter_checkpoint.ts` script's batched `embedNotes` step also hit repeated multi-minute timeouts under this instability (each individual `embedNotes({nodeId})` call taking 1-4 min instead of seconds) — worked around by dropping to one-node-at-a-time embed calls with longer timeouts until all fell through. Lesson applied going forward: when a single node fails repeatedly, check for a genuinely stuck/malformed context pack first, but usually it's just bad luck against a transient outage window — keep retrying rather than assuming the node itself is the problem. Edge-case audit re-run clean: **0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate sources, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 1061), 0 embedding gaps** (of 232 published chapters bank-wide).
  - **MAINS_ESSAY complete: 19/19**, verified via real DB query. Essay-paper nodes needed a framing adjustment (agents were told their "pyqs" may be past essay TOPICS, not GS questions/MCQs, and to write chapters as essay-approach guidance — structure, angles, an examples/quotes bank — rather than GS-content chapters), which worked cleanly across every node. **Real data-quality find, unrelated to this rollout**: one node's context pack (International Relations, under Section C) rolled up two `CURRENT_AFFAIRS`-sourced, `source:'generated'` MCQs via the normal (correct) parent-child PYQ-subtree aggregation, both claiming Ayatollah Khamenei died/was buried in 2024 — false. Multiple independent authoring agents caught this via their own fact-audit web search and correctly excluded the items rather than building content around them; flagged here as a genuine CA-pipeline content-accuracy issue for separate follow-up (see docs/OUTSTANDING.md), not a chapter-authoring bug. Also hit the same session-limit/stall pattern as MAINS_GS4 — one node (`defbe7bd`, "Social Issues and Human Values") took 4 attempts before landing. Edge-case audit clean: **0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate sources, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 1148), 0 embedding gaps** (of 251 published chapters bank-wide).
  - **MAINS_GH complete: 22/22**, verified via real DB query. This paper's own topics needed per-node framing since General Hindi is a language-skill paper, not GS content — each dispatch explicitly told the agent the node's genre (letter-writing formats, translation direction, grammar drill, comprehension technique, poetics) and instructed that the Hindi side be authored as natural, authoritative exam-register prose (not a literal translation of English), which every agent honored (`machine_translated:false` on the letter-writing/office-drafting nodes where the brief called for it). Content highlights: full worked Devanagari sample letters/notings/drafts, a worked unseen-comprehension passage with model answers, the complete eight-karak/ने-rule/vibhakti grammar system, and a fact-audited navras/chhand poetics chapter (29/29 verified).
    - **Stray-duplicate handling reached a new case this session: a stray was sometimes MORE complete than the already-published version**, not just redundant. Node `808054e5` ("Error Correction") first published thin (1 section/0 facts, an early/rushed run) — two independently-arriving late duplicates were each substantially richer (7-8 sections, PYQ-grounded worked examples from real year-by-year solution pages); rather than reflexively discarding every stray per the established pattern, each was diffed against the currently-published version's section/fact/PYQ counts first, and the richer one was assembled through the checkpoint pipeline to upgrade the note in place (`chapter_version` climbed v1→v2→v3 as richer versions landed) — `persistChapter`'s upsert-by-node-id semantics made this a safe, idempotent operation. A third, still-richer 808054e5 arrival after v3 was judged not to add enough over v3 to justify a fourth upgrade cycle and was discarded — a deliberate stopping point, not exhaustive perfectionism. Node `6e1ed8e4`/`6f0dfdfc` conversely had strays checked and found NO richer than the published version (fewer PYQ refs) — correctly discarded without upgrading. One 6f0dfdfc stray also raised a real fact discrepancy (does UPPSC General Hindi's 150 marks count toward merit, or is the paper qualifying-only?) against the published version's "merit-counted" claim; a THIRD independent arrival for the same node ran its own fresh web verification and confirmed "merit-counted, 150 marks" — settling the question in favor of what was already published, with no code/data change needed.
    - Two session-limit failures ("resets 3:20am Asia/Calcutta") on the final batch's last 2 nodes were, per the by-now-established pattern, false negatives — both agents had fully written valid output files before the connection dropped; checked and used directly rather than re-run.
    - Edge-case audit clean: **0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate sources, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 1203), 0 embedding gaps** (of 273 published chapters bank-wide).
  - **PRE_CSAT complete: 11/11**, verified via real DB query. CSAT nodes are aptitude/reasoning skill topics, not GS content — instructions per-node emphasized worked PROBLEMS with step-by-step solutions (formulas, techniques) over "decisive facts" in the GS sense, and every agent was told to frame around what the node's OWN real PYQs actually show rather than the generic syllabus label, since several turned out to test something narrower/different than their title implied: "General Mental Ability"'s 5 real PYQs are all NCERT-XI Psychology fill-in-the-blanks (memory/attribution/attribution theory), not numeric reasoning; "English Language Comprehension Skills"'s real PYQs are standalone word/grammar items (synonyms/antonyms/idioms/spelling), not passage comprehension like its "Comprehension" sibling. Every agent independently hand-verified its own worked-example arithmetic/logic before writing (e.g. recomputing series formulas, coding-cipher sums, set-difference chains) rather than asserting answers from memory. One per-node decision surfaced honestly rather than assumed: whether UPPSC's CSAT follows UPSC's convention of zero negative marking on "Decision Making" questions — no official UPPSC-specific source confirms this, so the published chapter states it as unconfirmed and tells students to prepare under the standard penalty assumption.
    - **Stray-duplicate handling reached a new case this session: sometimes a stray is MORE complete than the already-published version, not just redundant.** Established a lightweight decision rule going forward: before discarding a stray per the usual pattern, diff its section/fact/PYQ-reference counts against the currently-published version; if genuinely richer, upgrade the note in place through the checkpoint pipeline (safe and idempotent — `persistChapter` upserts by node id, `chapter_version` just climbs). Applied 3 times this session on node `808054e5` (MAINS_GH, "Error Correction") — v1 (1 section/0 facts, an early rushed run) → v2 (7/12, PYQ-grounded) → v3 (8/23, sourced from real year-by-year official-solution pages) — with a 4th still-richer arrival judged not to add enough over v3 to justify another cycle and discarded, a deliberate stopping point rather than perfectionism. Conversely, checked-and-correctly-discarded strays for `6e1ed8e4`/`6f0dfdfc` (already-published versions had MORE PYQ refs, not fewer) — the rule cuts both ways, it's not "always upgrade," it's "check, then decide."
    - Two more session-limit false-negatives (resets 3:20am Asia/Calcutta) and 3 connection-drop/stall retries hit across the PRE_CSAT batches — all resolved via the by-now-standard pattern (check the output path before assuming a failure produced nothing; retry fresh with a leaner "write directly, don't re-verify by printing" instruction after a node fails twice).
    - **Final edge-case audit across the WHOLE bank, all 6 papers from this session included: 0 gate leaks, 0 duplicate source ids, 0 dangling pyq_id refs (of 1320 unique referenced), 0 embedding gaps** — of **284 published chapters**.
  - **FULL SYLLABUS-TREE COVERAGE ACHIEVED: 284/284 nodes across all 10 papers now have a complete, fact-audited study chapter** (verified via a real DB query across every paper, not a running tally): PRE_GS1 30/30, PRE_CSAT 11/11, MAINS_GS1 43/43, MAINS_GS2 27/27, MAINS_GS3 36/36, MAINS_GS4 32/32, MAINS_GS5 38/38, MAINS_GS6 26/26, MAINS_ESSAY 19/19, MAINS_GH 22/22. Zero digest-only nodes remain anywhere in the tree. `/learn`'s `chapters_published_count`/`notes_published_count` (`getPaperSummaries`, `apps/api/src/services/syllabus.ts`) are computed LIVE from the DB per request — no code change was needed for the papers grid to reflect 100% coverage, it already does automatically.
  - **Mentor RAG retrieval spot-checked end-to-end (credit-free, `scripts/prove-chapter-retrieval.ts`) on two freshly-generated chapters from this session**: a CSAT Data-Interpretation doubt ("How do I approach a DI set with a pie chart?") retrieved that chapter's own "Chart & Graph Types" section at **rank 1, sim 0.582**; a GS4-ethics doubt ("What are moral and political attitudes and why do they matter for ethics?") retrieved that chapter's own section at **rank 1, sim 0.779** — both would resolve to a `[n]` mentor citation deep-linking to `/learn/<paper>/<node>?tab=notes`, confirming the per-section embedding pipeline (Session 28) works identically for this session's newly-authored content, no different from the original 7.
  - **Total cost: $0 of app Anthropic API spend for authoring** — all 284 chapters (147 from this session, 137 from Sessions 28/28.5) were authored by free coding-agent subagents with real WebSearch/WebFetch fact-verification, recorded honestly via `model:'claude-code-agent'`/`meta.authored_by` on every row (`cost:report` will show $0 for this work, as intended — the paid `notes:chapter` API path was never invoked, reserved for prod/cron per instruction). **No longer literally true for all 284 as of 2026-07-23** — one node (PRE_CSAT "Interpersonal Skills including Communication Skills") was later regenerated via the real API path for an unrelated caching investigation (see that session's note) and now has `model:'claude-sonnet-5'`, `meta.authored_by:'api'`, real cost $1.83. Everything else stated here still holds for the other 283.
  - **docs/OUTSTANDING.md A1 marked RESOLVED** — see that file for the closing note.

## Trial + pricing (2026-07-19): 7-day Pro free-trial + expanded plan ladder
Migration `0075_trial_and_pricing.sql`, `services/entitlements.ts`, `services/trial.ts`, billing UI. Verified end-to-end against the cloud DB (throwaway auth users, real entitlements logic, cleaned up after).
  - **Plan ladder repriced + expanded** to four tiers with a transparent monotonic per-month discount toward yearly: monthly ₹399 (39900) · quarterly ₹999 (≈₹333/mo, `interval='month'`/`interval_count=3`) · half-yearly ₹1,799 (≈₹300/mo, count=6) · yearly ₹2,499 (≈₹208/mo, `is_intro=true` = flagged best-value). Multi-month tiers use the EXISTING `interval_count` column (month×N) — no schema change; `addInterval` in `billing.ts` already honors it, so the webhook sets the right period end. **DISCREPANCY the user asked me to surface + confirmed before applying:** the brief said "monthly stays ₹399" but `0057`'s last-decided seed was actually ₹299 (and yearly ₹1,499, not ₹2,499) — asked, user confirmed the raise. `pricing.tsx` now renders 4 cards (`sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4`), the correct period suffix per `interval_count` (the old code hardcoded `/month` for anything non-yearly → would have mislabeled ₹999 as "/month"), and a plain "≈ ₹X/mo" effective sub-label on multi-month tiers (the transparent-pricing point, shown not implied).
  - **Trial REUSES existing state — deliberately no parallel state machine** (per the brief): `handle_new_user()` (0052) now also sets `plan='pro'`, `plan_expires_at = now()+7d`, `has_used_trial=true` at profile provisioning. The EXISTING `getPlanFor` lazy-downgrade flips a lapsed trial back to `'free'` exactly as it does a lapsed paid Pro — no second downgrade path (verified: the DB row durably downgrades + the free 3-lifetime-eval floor is intact afterward). `has_used_trial` is the ONLY new column (anti-replay). **Existing users are NOT retro-granted a trial** — the trigger fires only on `auth.users` INSERT and the column defaults false (confirmed choice, per brief #4).
  - **Trial vs paid Pro is read-time, no `is_trial` column:** both have `plan='pro'`; `entitlements.isOnTrial` = `pro` + an expiry + NO active `subscriptions` row (paid subs are webhook-created). `getTrialContext` runs `hasActiveSubscription` only when `plan='pro'` (short-circuited for free). Trial gets full Pro FEATURES (OCR/mocks/drills/all-notes — `assertPro` still passes on `plan='pro'`) but tighter DAILY caps: `LIMITS.trial = { evaluationsPerDay: 2, mentorPerDay: 15 }`. Evaluations needed a NEW per-day counter (existing Pro count is monthly) — `countEvaluations` now takes a `since` bound (unset=lifetime free floor, month start=paid Pro, IST-day start=trial), reusing `istDayRangeUtc`/`istToday`. `is_on_trial` added to the shared `entitlementsSchema` + `getEntitlements`.
  - **Paywall/quota copy made trial-aware** (brief #6 — three distinct eval states): the eval quota chip shows "Unlimited" only for PAID Pro (`plan==='pro' && !is_on_trial`), the real "N left today" for a trial; `assertEvaluationCredit` throws a trial-specific "resets at midnight IST — or go Pro" 402 (distinct from free's "upgrade" and paid's "this month" message); `PaywallModal`'s eval path branches free/trial/proCap and, for a PAID Pro at the monthly fair-use cap, drops the upgrade CTA + bullets entirely (nothing to sell — just "Got it").
  - **Trial countdown surfaced so lapse is never silent** (brief #6): `PlanBanner` (Profile) shows a "Pro trial active · N days left" variant with a "Keep Pro" CTA; a compact `TrialCountdownChip` sits in the dashboard greeting (coral on the final day). Honest onboarding copy — "7 days of full Pro, free — no card needed" — on the onboarding wizard's final step + the welcome choice screen. **No auto-charge language anywhere** (no card is collected).
  - **Abuse SIGNAL, not enforcement** (brief #5): `trial_starts` (coarse salted IP hash — `sha256(TRIAL_IP_SALT:ip)` truncated, never a raw IP at rest; service-role-only RLS), written best-effort at onboarding completion (the reliable one-shot point every new signup passes through). `pnpm trial-abuse:report [--days N] [--window-hours H] [--min-accounts K]` surfaces IP-hash clusters for MANUAL review — nothing auto-restricts (false positives from shared hostel/CGNAT IPs are worse than the abuse at this scale).
  - **Migration applied via the IPv4 transaction pooler** (`aws-1-ap-southeast-1`, port 6543, user `postgres.<ref>`) — this machine had no IPv6 egress this session, so the direct `db.<ref>.supabase.co` IPv6 host was unreachable; recorded the pooler fallback + region in [[supabase-headless-migrations]] so future sessions skip the region hunt.
  - **Decision recorded in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §7:** pricing psychology is **transparent single pricing**, deliberately chosen over discount-theater (fake strikethrough MRPs / countdown urgency) — reopen only with explicit discussion. Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `pnpm --filter web build` clean; the full behavioral matrix (plans, fresh-signup trial grant, 2/day cap + IST reset + yesterday-exclusion, trial-start hashing, lazy downgrade, paid-sub-unaffected) passed against the live DB.

### Edge-case audit, same day (2026-07-19): two isOnTrial misclassifications fixed
Adversarial pass over the trial work — the risk surface is `isOnTrial` wrongly capping a non-trial user at the 2-eval/day trial limit. The first cut defined it as `plan='pro' + expiry + no ACTIVE subscription`, which was wrong two ways:
  - **BUG 1 (production, real): a paid Pro who CANCELLED auto-renew but is still inside their paid period.** `cancel()` sets `subscriptions.status='cancelled'` and keeps `plan='pro'` + a future `plan_expires_at` (access continues until period end). The `status='active'`-only check saw "no active subscription" → classified them as a trial → they'd silently drop from 60/mo to 2/day. Fixed by keying on **`started_at is not null`** (set only by the webhook's `activate()`, and never cleared by `cancel()`) — i.e. "has this user EVER had a paid subscription", which stays true through cancellation-in-period and through a trial→paid conversion.
  - **BUG 2 (manual/demo grants): a Pro granted directly (not via a paid webhook).** The `demo:seed` account sets `plan='pro'` + a 365-day expiry with no subscription; created via the signup trigger it also carries `has_used_trial=true` → it read as a **365-day trial** (2 evals/day) instead of Pro. Fixed by (a) requiring **`has_used_trial=true`** in `isOnTrial` (a future admin/comp grant leaves the flag false → never a trial), and (b) making `seed-demo-account.ts` insert a representative **active subscription** (`started_at` set) so the demo models a real paid subscriber. `getPlanFor` now also returns `hasUsedTrial`. Final: `isOnTrial = plan==='pro' && expiry && has_used_trial && !hasPaidSubscription`.
  - Re-verified against the cloud DB with four new cases added to the harness (converted trial→paid, cancelled-but-in-period, manual grant with `has_used_trial=false`, plus the originals) — all keep the correct 60/mo cap; the genuine trial still resolves to 2/day. **FOLLOW-UP (data, not code):** any demo account already seeded in the DB before this fix still has the misclassifying shape until `demo:seed --reset` is re-run (entangled with the pending D10 domain re-seed) — flagged, not re-seeded here.

## UI polish (2026-07-19): single-arc Rubric Dial, Study Plan on Dashboard, revision quick-add reachability, raw-float score display
Four small, user-flagged UX fixes, verified live via a temporary Playwright pass (installed, used, removed — same convention as prior sessions) against a running dev server signed in as a real account (`demo@prayasup.app`), both light+dark and 1440px/390px.
  - **`ScoreGauge` (`components/ui-x/score-gauge.tsx`) redesigned to read as ONE arc, not two stacked ones.** The old implementation drew a full pale 3-color band (22% opacity) UNDER a second, full-opacity value arc covering `0-clamped` at the identical radius/stroke-width — for any score not exactly at a band boundary, this rendered as a solid arc abruptly cutting into a faded multi-color remainder, which read as two overlapping charts rather than one gauge (flagged by the user against the landing-page hero mock, `value=78`). Fixed to the standard analog-gauge pattern: the coral→marigold→tulsi band is now ALWAYS fully visible at fixed high opacity (0.85) — a constant reference scale, like a speedometer's colored zones — and a single needle (the existing dot marker) points at the current value; the redundant covering arc was deleted entirely. Tick marks switched from `var(--border)` to a `var(--card)`-colored notch (so they read as cut-ins against the now-vivid band rather than disappearing against it). Score-band color feedback is preserved via the needle's fill color and the numeral text color, so "am I in the green zone" glanceability is unchanged. Same public API (`value`/`label`/`size`), so all 6 call sites (landing hero, evaluation score hero, practice result hero, dashboard answer-spotlight, evaluation dimensions, community shared-answer) needed no changes. Matches the frontend-design skill's own description of the Rubric Dial ("reads like an exam-hall meter, not a fitness ring") more literally than the old implementation did.
  - **AI Study Plan moved from Profile to Dashboard for daily visibility.** `components/profile/study-plan-card.tsx` → `components/dashboard/study-plan-card.tsx` (file moved, not just re-exported — matches the codebase's by-section component organization), removed from `profile.tsx`'s "Grow your writing" section (now just `MicroDrillsCard`), inserted into `dashboard.tsx` directly after the Continue/Today grid and before `WeeklyDigestCard` — a daily planning tool now sits where it's seen every day, rather than buried at the bottom of a settings-heavy Profile page a user visits far less often. No hook/query changes; `useActivePlan`/`useStudyPlanStream` are plain TanStack Query hooks, freely importable from either route.
  - **Revision's two one-tap seed buttons ("Add my wrong answers" / "Add key facts from notes I've read") were only reachable from the empty-deck state** — the moment `stats.total_cards > 0`, `RevisionEmptyState` (and the buttons with it) stopped rendering forever, since the Review tab switches to the stats+start-review view. A user who'd added even one manual card lost the only UI path to bulk-seed from real wrong-answers/read-notes data (both mutations are idempotent server-side — a re-run just reports "already added" for existing ones, per Session 16 — so there was no reason they needed to disappear). Extracted the buttons+result-message into a shared `components/revision/seed-buttons.tsx` (`SeedButtons`), used by `RevisionEmptyState` (unchanged behavior) AND newly added to `ManageCardList`'s toolbar as a "Quick add" row (new `Revision.quickAddLabel` key, en+hi) — reachable regardless of deck size from now on. Live-verified: clicking "Add my wrong answers" from the Manage tab on a 22-card deck actually added a new question-sourced card to the list.
  - **Raw floating-point scores rendered ugly, e.g. "-0.44/40.61999999999999"** on the practice result hero — UPPSC's one-third negative marking produces fractional per-question marks whose sums carry IEEE-754 noise, and `attempt.score`/`attempt.total`/`test.total_marks`/`test.best_score` were interpolated straight into JSX/i18n with no rounding. Added `lib/format-score.ts` (`formatScoreValue`: `Math.round(value*100)/100` then `String()`, so a whole number still prints as "40" and a fractional one as "40.62", never a raw float) and applied it at the three raw-render sites: `result-score-hero.tsx`'s score/total fraction, and `test-card.tsx`'s `bestScore`/`marks` i18n interpolations. `cutoff-comparison.tsx` already rounded its own derived percentages and needed no change.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter web build` and `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; live Playwright pass — landing-page gauge (single clean banded arc, needle at 78%), signed-in dashboard (Study Plan card rendering between Continue/Today and Weekly Digest, both 1440px and 390px, dark mode), Profile (Study Plan section confirmed gone, only Micro-drills remains under "Grow your writing"), Revision Manage tab (Quick-add row present and functional on a non-empty deck), and a real result page's gauge + score fraction in both light and dark — zero console errors throughout. Playwright was a temporary devDependency for this session only (installed, used, removed — `pnpm-lock.yaml`/`package.json` confirmed clean afterward).

## Landing-page auth nav + domain-portability sweep (2026-07-19)
Two independent fixes, kept as separate commits.
  - **Redundant top-nav auth buttons.** `routes/landing.tsx`'s header always rendered BOTH "Sign in" (ghost, hidden on mobile only) and the primary "Start free"/"Go to dashboard" button — a signed-in visitor on desktop (≥640px) saw "Sign in" and "Go to dashboard" simultaneously, redundant and confusing about whether they're the same action. Made the nav control state-dependent instead of always-both: logged-out shows only "Sign in" (→ `/auth`), logged-in shows only "Go to dashboard" (→ `/dashboard`, reusing the existing `Landing.goToApp` key, whose English text is already literally "Go to dashboard") — exactly one button in the nav at a time, on every viewport. The hero's large CTA (unchanged) remains the primary conversion path and already read fine in both states (`startFree`/`goToApp`), so no copy change was needed there. Verified against real signed-in/signed-out sessions (a throwaway Supabase auth user created via the admin API, session seeded into `localStorage`'s `sb-<ref>-auth-token` key — same pattern as prior sessions' RLS/community verification) with a temporary Playwright devDependency (installed, used, removed): both locales, 1440px and 390px, header shows exactly one button in every combination, zero console errors.
  - **Domain-portability sweep.** Root cause: the original prayasup→neev brand rename swept the repo for `prayasup` case-insensitive, which does NOT match `prayas.pages.dev` as a substring (missing the "up") — a hardcoded reference to a retired Cloudflare Pages preview domain could have survived undetected the same way. The one instance actually found in the wild (an auth-redirect callback) turned out to live in Supabase's dashboard Site URL setting, not the repo, and was already corrected there, not via a code change. This session's sweep was for OTHER leftovers actually in the codebase — and found several: the domain has in fact moved a second time since the CLAUDE.md Branding note was last updated (`prayasup.app` → `neev.app` → **`neevstudy.com`**, the current production domain), and `neev.app` was still hardcoded in `apps/web/src/components/seo/page-seo.tsx` (the `SITE_URL` fallback default backing canonical/hreflang/OG tags on every public page), `apps/web/public/sitemap.xml` (8 literal occurrences) and `robots.txt`'s `Sitemap:` line, `apps/api/scripts/seed-demo-account.ts`'s default `--email`, and as example text in `render.yaml`/`apps/api/.env.example`/`docs/operations.md`. Fixed every one — `page-seo.tsx` still reads `VITE_SITE_URL` first (unchanged, already the config-driven pattern) and only its hardcoded *fallback* moved to `neevstudy.com`; the static `sitemap.xml`/`robots.txt` necessarily still contain a literal absolute URL (the sitemap protocol requires one — there's no runtime templating for `public/` assets in this Vite SPA) but now point at the current domain; every doc/example string was updated to `neevstudy.com` too. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`'s D10 (demo account still under an old-domain email — that's DB data, not code, unchanged here) updated to note the domain has moved twice, not once. `CLAUDE.md`'s own Branding note (above) updated to name `neevstudy.com` as current. Historical session-log prose (this file, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`'s stale-log-entries table) deliberately keeps `prayasup.app`/`neev.app` as a factual changelog, per the pre-existing "Deliberately left as-is" convention — not touched.
  - **CI guard extended, not just the strings fixed** — the whole point of this sweep is that the SAME class of bug (a rename that doesn't catch every literal, `pages.dev`-shaped or otherwise) will happen again at the next domain move. `scripts/check-portable-paths.mjs` (previously path-only) now also fails on `prayasup.app`, `neev.app`, `prayas.pages.dev` by name, plus the whole `*.pages.dev` shape generically (a bare Cloudflare Pages auto-domain should never be a literal in source — it belongs in `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`/`ALLOWED_ORIGIN_SUFFIXES`/`VITE_SITE_URL` env config). `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` are exempted from the new domain checks only (still fully scanned for hardcoded paths) since both are permanent changelogs that are supposed to keep old domain names as a factual record — every other tracked file must be current. Genuinely explanatory doc/`.env.example` text that shows a `<project-name>.pages.dev`-style placeholder gets the same `portable-paths-allow` escape hatch already used for path examples. Live-verified the guard actually catches a reintroduced leftover: staged a throwaway file containing `https://prayas.pages.dev/callback`, confirmed the guard fails and names both the specific and generic pattern, then removed it — `pnpm check:paths` is clean (670 tracked files) with the real fixes in place.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter web build` (production build) and `apps/api`'s `tsc --noEmit` both clean; grepped the built `dist/` for `neev.app`/`prayasup.app`/`prayas.pages.dev`/`pages.dev` — zero hits; `dist/sitemap.xml`/`dist/robots.txt` confirmed to contain `neevstudy.com`, not the old domain. Full-repo grep (via `git ls-files`, every tracked file, every extension) confirms the only remaining `neev.app`/`prayasup.app` mentions anywhere in the tree are inside `CLAUDE.md`/`docs/OUTSTANDING.md`'s historical prose, and the only remaining `pages.dev` mentions are the escape-hatched doc/`.env.example` placeholder examples.

### Edge-case audit, same day (2026-07-19): the UI-polish gauge/score fixes above had real gaps
Asked to "check all edge cases" right after the UI-polish commit. Found and fixed real problems rather than just re-confirming the happy path already screenshotted.
  - **`ScoreGauge`'s always-vivid band lost the "no score yet" signal.** The redesign made the coral→marigold→tulsi band permanently 0.85-opacity, but `value === null` is a real, reachable state (not hypothetical) at three unguarded call sites — `result-score-hero.tsx` (`score_pct: z.number().nullable()`), `dashboard/answer-spotlight-card.tsx` (`pct` is null whenever an evaluation lacks `overall_score`/`max_score`), and `profile-drill.tsx` (`overall_pct: z.number().nullable()`) — none of which guard with `value !== null &&` before rendering (unlike the three Community call sites, which do). A vivid full-color band with no needle and only small "—" text below it doesn't read as "empty" at a glance. Fixed by conditionally dimming the band back to ~0.25 opacity when `value === null`, restoring the original empty-state legibility while keeping the single-arc fix for every real value.
  - **The new `SeedButtons` (Manage-tab quick-add) used `size="sm"` (32px tall), a regression under the design skill's ">=44px tap target" floor** — the original empty-state buttons and the adjacent "New card" button are both default size (36px). Not fully 44px either, but shrinking it further specifically in the extracted component was an avoidable regression; reverted both buttons to default size for consistency with the toolbar they now sit in.
  - **The `formatScoreValue` fix from the UI-polish commit only touched 3 of ~13 raw-float render sites.** Dispatched a research agent to grep every remaining place a summed/weighted score interpolates into JSX or an i18next string unrounded, given this app's UPPSC negative-marking sums are the exact "irrational-looking decimal" the user's original screenshot flagged. Confirmed-safe (already rounded, skipped): `cutoff-comparison.tsx`, `board-table.tsx`, `dimension-bests-panel.tsx`, `evaluation-score-hero.tsx` (`.toFixed(1)`), `weekly-digest-card.tsx`. Fixed 9 more real gaps, all wrapped in the same `formatScoreValue`: `practice/attempt-history-list.tsx` (`item.score`/`item.total`, the Practice History list), `practice/test-instructions.tsx` (`Practice.marks` — the SAME i18n key already fixed in `test-card.tsx` but missed here — and the negative-marking percentage, `Math.abs(negative_marking*100)`, which can render e.g. `33.33000000000001`), `routes/answers-session-result.tsx` (both the session total `result.total_score/total_max_score` and the per-question `s.overall_score/s.max_score`), `answers/submission-history-list.tsx` (`item.overall_score/item.max_score`), `answers/daily-answer-set.tsx` (`Answers.dailySetScore`), `answers/evaluation-dimensions.tsx` (the per-dimension gauge's visible label text, `d.score/10`), `routes/profile-drill.tsx` (micro-drill per-item `item.score/10`), and `practice/result-review-list.tsx` (`marks_awarded`, the single closest field to the original bug report — a raw per-question negative-marking value like `-0.33`). Left `item.marks`/`Answers.dailySetMarks`-style fields alone where the schema shows a static admin/ingest-set integer-ish value rather than a computed sum — a different category of number, not exhibiting the float-noise pattern.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter web build` + `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean after every batch of fixes. Live-verified against the real running app (temporary Playwright pass, same install-use-remove convention, signed in as `demo@prayasup.app`) hitting genuinely negative-marked real data: the Practice History list rendered `191.53/270`, `24.7/38`, `141.48/200`, `10.68/16`, `6.02/14`, `153.95/267`, `30.04/46`, `16.02/24`, `38.08/70` — all clean 2-decimal values, confirming this exact list was a live (not theoretical) instance of the bug; `submission-history-list.tsx` rendered `7.08/8`, `6.64/8`, `34.5/50`, `7.92/12`, `8.1/12` cleanly; a full evaluation page showed all 6 dimension gauges as single clean arcs with clean labels and a clean `7.1/8` overall fraction. **NOTE:** mid-audit, a concurrent process landed two unrelated commits (`ab563ed` domain-portability sweep, `11c19ba` landing-page auth-nav fix) in this same working tree — confirmed fully disjoint from this session's file list via `git status`/`git log` before staging, and this session's own files were staged and committed by explicit name, never `git add -A`.

## CA-pipeline prompt caching investigated and REJECTED on evidence (2026-07-22)
Asked to add prompt caching to the CA pipeline's triage/enrich/mains-qgen calls — the only LLM calls with zero caching despite being the highest-frequency loop (one per RSS item, every `ca:run`). **Outcome: no caching added; the measured cost of the only layout that actually caches is a ~1/3 loss of kept CA content.** The commit is a 25-line comment in `ca/prompts.ts` documenting the finding so it isn't re-attempted; zero executable change.
  - **Three premises in the brief were wrong, all caught by measuring rather than assuming.** (1) The brief said to mirror "the proven split already working in this file's `generateMcqs` sibling stages" — `generateMcqs` has a plain-string system prompt with **no caching at all**; the proven `PromptSegment[]`/`cache:true` split lives in `evaluation/prompts.ts` (Session 13), not here.
