  - **Time Attack: a real UPPSC content leak found and fixed, the most concrete bleed of this whole audit.** `getTimeAttackTopics` had **zero server-side exam check** — since its schema only accepts the two bare UPPSC codes, ANY signed-in user regardless of `target_exam` could call it directly and get real UPPSC topic titles/counts back, content otherwise fully inaccessible to them anywhere else in the app. Live-confirmed: a `target_exam='upsc'` account got 8 real UPPSC topics with a 200. `startTimeAttack` compounded it — it validated the paper code shape but not that the node's exam matched the caller's, and was only rescued downstream by `startAttempt`'s own (already-fixed) exam check, but only AFTER creating real `tests`/`test_questions` rows, leaving an orphaned UPPSC test behind on every blocked cross-exam attempt (confirmed live, cleaned up). Fixed: `getTimeAttackTopics` now returns an honest empty list for a non-UPPSC caller; `startTimeAttack` now fetches the node's exam up front and 404s before any write, matching the established "exam match, not just paper match" convention used elsewhere. Re-verified live post-fix: 0 topics + clean 404 + 0 rows created for a mismatched exam, unchanged 8-topic/201-start behavior for a real UPPSC caller.
  - **Confirmed NOT bugs, checked live rather than assumed** (recorded so they aren't re-investigated): the switcher's `queryClient.clear()` is called only inside the mutation's `onSuccess` — no `onError`/`onSettled` exists to fire it on failure, confirmed via a Playwright network-interception test (forced 500 → dialog stays open with the real error text, target_exam server-side unchanged, and a request-count probe over the following 2.5s showed exactly the one failed PATCH, no cache-wide refetch burst); milestones are correctly GLOBAL/one-time by design (matching the SRS-deck precedent), not per-exam achievements; ghost battle's ownership check plus the already-fixed `startAttempt` exam check together block a cross-exam ghost race with a clean 404 and zero orphaned rows; every `scoreboard.ts` function not already covered by M5-M9 already enforces exam match correctly.
  - Both audits ran against the same live cloud DB with a THIRD, unrelated concurrent process active in the tree throughout (an ingest/seed data-import stream touching `apps/api/src/ingest/_shared.ts`/`syllabus.ts`/`seed/` + both `package.json`s) — confirmed fully disjoint via `git status` before every commit and left completely untouched; every file staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + build, `pnpm --filter web build` (full production build), `pnpm --filter web lint` at **40 warnings — baseline unchanged**, `pnpm check:paths` — all clean, run once more against the combined diff from both audits before committing. Files touched this pass: `apps/api/src/services/{profile,tests,dashboard,profile-analytics,digest,time-attack}.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/onboarding.tsx`.

## U5 (2026-07-30/31): UPSC PYQ ingest — 72 papers, and a live production bug it uncovered
The full UPSC Civil Services PYQ bank, ingested through the documented two-step flow. **2,791 questions across 72 papers (2016-2026), 2,760 visible**, every source file from the official `www.upsc.gov.in` host; **22/22 official answer keys, Set A confirmed**; 5,670 embeddings; 927 `mv_node_weightage` rows. `upsc` is still **`is_live: false`** — content is not a launch. Full actionable index in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **§8i**; multi-exam write-up in `docs/multi-exam.md` **§3h**. Commits `eab7e56`, `9c03da1`, `f3aac32`, `af67aa7`, `e13e7d2`, `17770f9`.
  - **M23 FIXED FIRST, before the first `ingest:pyq` run**, exactly as `docs/multi-exam.md` §0a prescribed. `classifyPyqId` mapped `upsc_prelims_2024_gs1` to a **bare `PRE_GS1`** and `pyq-load.ts`'s `resolveSyllabusId` resolved it with **no exam filter** — so UPSC PYQs would have attached to UPPSC's tree silently, with no constraint violation to notice. **The bug and its own concealment were the same line.** The missing decision (provenance→product exam) is now `productExamForProvenance` in `ingest/_shared.ts`: a provenance exam that IS a product exam ingests into itself with exam-prefixed codes; a provenance-ONLY exam (`up_ro_aro`/`upsssc_pet`/`other`) keeps mapping onto the DEFAULT exam's shared prelims tree — the pre-existing deliberate overlap, not a fallback invented here. **Byte-identity proven over all 70 pre-existing manifest ids** (their prefix is `""`), so UPPSC is untouched. A paper the exam does not have (`upsc_mains_*_gs5`) now returns null loudly instead of minting a code with no syllabus behind it. `pyq.ts` also stopped hardcoding the product exam — `cls.productExam` is threaded as a REQUIRED parameter into every prompt path (the M24 lesson). Live after loading: **0 UPSC questions on a UPPSC node, 0 UPPSC on a UPSC node, 0 questions on a `UPSC_*` code with a non-`upsc` exam.**
  - **THREE silent content-loss bugs in the extractor, ALL caught by one check: summing `marks` per paper.** A UPSC Mains GS paper is always 250, so the total is a free invariant — and it was the *only* thing that saw any of them. Every structural check (counts, q_no gaps, duplicates, bilingual completeness, option keys) passed on all three. (1) **Sub-parts collide on `q_no`**: 2020 GS-IV prints Section A as separately-scored `1.(a)`/`1.(b)` sharing one integer `q_no`, and `extractWhole` merged with a blind `byNo.set()` (last write wins) → **one sub-part of every Q1-6 discarded**, the paper loading as a clean-looking 12 questions summing to 180. (2) **Section-restarted numbering**: 2017 Essay numbers Section A 1-4 then Section B 1-4 → collided → **4 of 8 topics discarded**. (3) **`marks` declared `integer`** while UPSC Mains 2016 is 20 × **12.5** = 250 → rounded to 260/240 (`questions.marks` has been `numeric(6,2)` since 0062, so the DB was never the constraint). Fixes: the prompt states the sub-part contract and requires `q_no` unique across the whole paper; `marks` is `number`; `extractWhole` keeps the MORE COMPLETE copy on a collision, matching `extractByPages`. **All 50 Mains papers re-verified — every GS paper sums to exactly 250, zero dropped-sub-part signatures. KEEP THE MARKS-TOTAL CHECK; it is the only thing that has ever seen this bug class.**
  - **⚑ A LIVE PRODUCTION BUG, on the LIVE exam, that this ingest only happened to uncover: the mentor was answering UNGROUNDED.** Every `match_embeddings` call through PostgREST failed with `57014 statement timeout` at ~8s — every filter, even `match_count=1` — while the identical SQL on a direct connection ran in ~100ms. `retrieveGrounding` degrades gracefully on an RPC error, so it returned **0 chunks silently** instead of erroring, which is exactly why nothing had ever surfaced it. **Root cause: a `language sql` function is INLINED into the caller's statement.** PostgREST runs RPCs as prepared statements, so Postgres switched to a GENERIC plan where `query_embedding` is an unknown parameter — and a generic plan cannot use the HNSW index for the ORDER BY, falling back to a seq scan + sort over 28k × 1536-dim vectors (measured **20.6s** with a 60s function-level timeout; the 8s `statement_timeout` on `authenticator` was only the messenger). Migration **`0113`** converts both vector RPCs to **plpgsql + dynamic EXECUTE**, making the body opaque to inlining. **The negative results are the useful part, recorded so nobody re-derives them:** `pg_stat_activity` showed **ZERO active backends** during an 8s failure (ruling out "the DB is slow"); RLS was excluded (`service_role` has `bypassrls`, and the same query under that role via `EXECUTE` was fast); and `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET plan_cache_mode='force_custom_plan'` did **NOT** help — itself the confirmation, since a SET clause attaches to function *execution* and an inlined SQL function never executes as a function at all. After: `retrieveGrounding` returns **8 exam-correct passages for both exams**.
  - **`0113` fixes a SECOND, quieter bug in the same place: HNSW silently returned FEWER rows than asked.** With a post-filter, plain HNSW keeps only those of its `ef_search` (40) candidates that survive, so a selective filter under-returns — `filter_exam_code='upsc'` requested 8 and got **5**, i.e. the mentor grounding on 5 passages while believing it had 8. **This bites the smallest partition hardest, which is always the newest exam.** Fixed with pgvector 0.8 `iterative_scan`. The two RPCs pick **different modes on purpose**: `match_embeddings` → `relaxed_order` (callers consume the whole top-k, so membership matters and intra-k order does not; 101-225ms vs a 4.1s cold spike for strict), `match_doubt_faq` → `strict_order` (its TOP-1 similarity drives hard 0.95/0.86 cache thresholds). `match_doubt_faq` was converted too — identical shape, fast today only because that table holds ~40 rows. Applied via `set_config(..., is_local => true)` inside the body, because this DB's migration role is not superuser and `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET hnsw.iterative_scan` is rejected `42501`; `is_local` scopes it to the transaction so it cannot leak onto a pooled connection (verified: reads `off` after the call). **RESIDUAL to watch: the HNSW index is 221 MB against 224 MB of `shared_buffers`**, so the first call for a new query vector can still spike to ~1.5-4.7s (cold page faults) — no longer a timeout, but the headroom shrinks as the bank grows.
  - **A cross-exam embedding leak this ingest itself introduced, then closed.** `collectQuestionChunks` derived an embedding's exam from the question's syllabus node and fell back to the DEFAULT exam when there was none — under a comment reading *"every such row is UPPSC"*, true when written and false the moment UPSC PYQs landed. `ingest:embed`'s own warning moved 40 → 97; the extra ~57 are UPSC questions, and `match_embeddings` filters on exactly that column, so they would have surfaced in **UPPSC users'** grounding. Now falls back to `paper_code` (globally unique across exams per §0, exam-prefixed by M23) — deliberately **not** `questions.exam_code`, which is provenance and includes exams nobody can select. Verified **5,670/5,670 correctly stamped, 0 reverse leaks**.
  - **Answer keys — 22/22 official, Set A confirmed, 0 mismatches on every paper.** **Three naming families, none guessable:** 2018-2023 `AnsKey-CSP-…`; 2024 `AnsKey-CivilServicesPExam-2024-…`; 2025 `AnsKeyCivilServicesP-Exam-2025-…`; and **2016 and earlier use `CSP_16_GS_Paper_I.pdf`, containing no `anskey`/`answer`/`key` token at all** — so a Wayback CDX filter on `anskey` provably cannot find it and the filename is indistinguishable from a question paper. 2026's filename contains a literal **EN DASH (U+2013)** (`%E2%80%93`). **UPSC's own answer-key pages render ZERO rows**, so these URLs are linked from nowhere and cannot be rediscovered by crawling — hence pinned in `content-sources.yaml`.
  - **`loadAnswerKey` is now SERIES-AWARE — the trust gate this task actually turned on.** Every official UPSC key is a 4-page scan carrying **all four booklet series, one per page (p1=A)**. The old prompt just said "extract the answer map", which on such a document is ambiguous and can silently return another series' answers — the exact failure mode behind this repo's 2021 GS-I incident. It now takes the paper's OWN detected series, asks for that series only, makes the model **name** the series it read from, and **discards the key on a mismatch** (→ blind re-solve). Non-option tokens are dropped in prompt and again in code: `X` marks a dropped question, tables are padded with `0`, and neither may become a "verified" answer. Result: all 22 papers report `series A / key series A / aligned`, and the per-paper key-verified counts (98, 99, 79, 99 …) match the sourcing report's dropped-question counts **exactly** — an independent confirmation of the `X`-omission logic.
  - **2026 uses a PROVISIONAL key and is deliberately held to a HIGHER bar** (founder-confirmed). This overturned the starting assumption that no 2026 key could exist: UPSC published one **two days after the exam** (27-05-2026), a change in practice from 2017-2025 where keys appeared about a year later. It self-identifies as `[Prov. Ans. Key]` and can still change, so 2026 is **deliberately absent from `OFFICIAL_COMMISSION`**: listing it would grant gate (A) — publish on the key ALONE — which is the wrong bar. Omitting it routes 2026 through gate (B): the provisional key IS applied, but a question publishes only where an independent blind re-solve agrees. **167 of 180 published, 13 held.** **Re-check for a FINAL 2026 key and promote it then.**
  - **Sources are reproducible now, not hand-dropped.** Every one of the 72 papers had its original official URL recovered from **its own embedded PDF Title metadata**, verified to return `application/pdf` (72/72), and registered tier A. Also fixed a pre-existing entry recorded as *"unreachable from ingest machine"* — **the host was never the problem, the missing `www.` was**: the bare host 307s to the homepage and, followed, returns `200 + text/html`, a failure that masquerades as success. Zero bare-host `upsc.gov.in` URLs remain. **Measured, worth not re-testing: these are IMAGE-ONLY scans** — re-fetching the pristine originals was tried and does NOT recover a text layer (UPSC simply publishes scans), so UPSC extraction is ~100% vision, unlike UPPSC where English extracted cleanly and only Hindi was mojibake. Sole exception: `upsc_prelims_2023_gs1`. The booklets ARE bilingual, so vision reads authentic exam Hindi — **100% bilingual, 0 machine-translated across all 2,791**.
  - **Cost visibility was missing entirely, and is now fixed:** `structuredJson` writes an `llm_calls` row only when `purpose` is set, and the PYQ extract/classify/key/translate calls had none — so **the entire UPPSC bank's extraction is absent from `cost:report`** and there was no per-paper baseline for any exam. Five purposes added. **This session: $61.56** (extraction $41.17 over 72 papers, 2026 blind-resolve escalation $18.07, keys/classify/series $1.93).
  - **`0113` edge-case audit — 15/16** (the one failure was my probe guessing a return shape; re-run correctly after): every filter combination honoured (`locale`, `source_type`, `source_id`, `exam_code`) and combined; `match_count` and its default; an exam with no rows returns empty rather than erroring; similarity in range and non-increasing; **three SQL-injection probes return 0 rows with the table intact** (all values are bound via `USING`, never interpolated); the GUC does not leak to the session; and the real `retrieveGrounding` returns 8 exam-correct passages for BOTH exams. Retrieval isolation re-verified 3 probes × 2 exams with every hit resolved back to its actual `exam_code`; the unfiltered control returns `uppsc=7 upsc=1`, proving the filter is load-bearing rather than cosmetic; a UP-only probe (ODOP) returns **0 hits for `upsc`**. `mv_node_weightage` is sane, not flat: **Polity 203 > Economy 185 > History 175** in Prelims GS-I.
  - **TWO corrections I had to make to my own reporting, same root cause both times:** intermediate status figures (question counts, then spend) were understated because the query hit **PostgREST's 1000-row cap** — it returned exactly 1000, which is the tell. This file warns about that trap repeatedly and it still caught me twice in one session. **Any count over a few hundred rows needs `selectAll`/`.range()` paging or an exact `head:true` count.**
  - **Scope note + a non-defect worth not re-investigating:** the brief said 2018-2025; files existed for 2016/2017/2026 too, flagged rather than silently widened or dropped, and ingested on explicit approval. 2016/2017 Mains GS-IV have 14 questions and 2019 has 19 where other years have 12 — **not a defect**: UPSC genuinely restructures that paper by year and every one still sums to 250. Essay papers sum to 1000 because all 8 topics carry 125 marks and a candidate attempts 2 → 250.
  - **Still open after U5:** **M21** (chapter `pyq_ids` validated for existence, not exam) gates a UPSC chapter rollout, and **U6** gates every model-facing path — U5 needed only **6** of U6's 74 slots, authored per-slot from directly observed evidence (UPSC prints a large standalone series letter AND a separate T.B.C. code whose own letters mislead — a booklet coded `HGY-D-LKUV` is Series **A**), so the other 68 are untouched and still throw. `ingest:tests` was deliberately NOT run for UPSC: it would hit `requireAuthored(...pyqTestTitlePrefix)` and throw, which is U6 working as designed.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` + `build`, `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` (**128 byte-identical**), `pnpm check:paths` — all clean. `pg` was a temporary devDependency for the direct-connection diagnostics and was removed (`package.json`/lockfile confirmed clean). A concurrent process was building a PYQ-archive feature in the same tree throughout; every commit staged only this session's files by explicit name, never `git add -A`.

### Second edge-case pass on U5, same day (2026-07-31): three question reads the ingest silently opened up
Asked the sharpest question available after publishing 2,791 questions — **does any user-facing read now surface them to a UPPSC user?** Three did; all measured live before fixing. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **§8j**. Commit `6569807`.
  - **`getTodaysQuestion()` took NO exam at all.** It picks a descriptive question by `istDayNumber() % count` over the whole published pool, which went from entirely UPPSC to **811 of 2,052 UPSC (40%)** — so a UPPSC user's "today's practice question" on the Answers hub would have been a **UPSC** question on ~40% of days. Both the COUNT and the pick are now scoped, and **they must stay in step**: scoping one and not the other makes the modulo index into a different population than it selects from.
  - **`listQuestions()` had no product-exam scoping** — confirmed live, `?year=2024` returned a page of **11 UPSC + 9 UPPSC**. `?node=`/`?paper=` were already safe (they scope through globally-unique codes); the unfiltered, `?type=` and `?year=` paths were not, i.e. exactly the PYQ browse and search. **`getQuestionById()`** took an untrusted id with no exam check (M7 class) — now 404s a foreign question (404 not 403 per convention). That one is not merely browsing: it is the hydration path for the Writing Room's `?question=`, so without it a user could write and have evaluated an answer to **another exam's question under their own exam's rubric**.
  - **A REAL FLAW IN MY OWN FIRST FIX, caught by the verification rather than by review.** I blanket-added `CURRENT_AFFAIRS` to every exam's paper set, reasoning from the dashboard's `validPaperCodes` precedent — **but that precedent does not transfer.** There it attributes a user's OWN graded answers; here it decides what exists in your bank. Every CA question is generated for and stamped with ONE exam, so including the code for all exams leaked UPPSC's current affairs into UPSC (the first page still came back 20/20 UPPSC). Replaced with `questionExamScopeFilter` in `lib/exams.ts`, which admits a CA question only for its generating exam. **Verified both ways: all 1,183 live CA questions still reach UPPSC users, zero reach UPSC.**
  - Scoped by **paper code**, never by `questions.exam_code` — that is PROVENANCE, and its domain deliberately includes exams we ingest from but never sell (`up_ro_aro`, `upsssc_pet`) whose papers ARE legitimately part of the default exam's bank; filtering on it would wrongly exclude them. `examCode` is a **required** parameter on all three so the compiler forced every call site to decide (M24).
  - **The generalisable lesson, worth applying after ANY second-exam content ingest:** publishing content does not just add rows — it converts every *unscoped* read from "correct by accident" into a live leak, and none of those reads changed. The ones to audit are those with **no node and no paper filter**: a whole-table `count`/modulo, a browse filtered only by `type`/`year`, and any by-id fetch of an untrusted id.
  - Verified **16/16** live as a real user of each exam (four filter shapes each, today's-question, cross-exam id 404 vs same-exam success, ids-mode both ways, foreign `?paper=`); totals land at **4,292 uppsc / 2,760 upsc** against 7,052 unscoped. `typecheck`, `prompts:snapshot` (128 byte-identical) and `check:paths` all clean.

## PYQ Archive page for UPSC (2026-07-31): a real UI over U5's data, plus a shared bug found auditing it
The public-facing UPSC "Previous Year Questions" archive — `/:locale/pyq-archive` — filterable by paper and year, reading U5's real ingested bank directly (no mock/placeholder data). Ran CONCURRENTLY with the "second edge-case pass on U5" session above (same tree, `services/questions.ts` touched by both) — every commit staged only this session's own files by explicit name, never `git add -A`; the one line this session added to that file (`key_provenance` in `QUESTION_COLUMNS`) landed inside the *other* session's own commit `6569807` because it was sitting uncommitted in the shared working tree when that session staged the file — a real, disclosed entanglement, not silently absorbed. Commits `1be8f22`, `31afd7c`.
  - **Premise-checked before building anything.** The brief claimed "U5 already produced" real UPSC PYQ rows with official Set-A keys and populated `mv_node_weightage` — CLAUDE.md's session log at the time only showed U5 as *future, gated* work (M23 still open), so this was verified against the live cloud DB before trusting it: **2,791 real `exam_code='upsc'` questions**, MCQs correctly `key_provenance='official_commission'`/`meta.answer_key_verified=true` for the vast majority, Mains descriptive rows correctly `correct_option_key: null`/`key_provenance: 'none'` (UPSC genuinely publishes no Mains key), **927 real `mv_node_weightage` rows**. The premise held — U5 had in fact landed mid-session, just after this file's context was last loaded — so the build proceeded on verified fact, not the brief's unverified claim.
  - **Reuses UPPSC's PYQ browse UI as a structural model only, never by modifying it** — `pyq-list.tsx`, `learn-node.tsx`, `learn-paper.tsx`, `learn-trends.tsx` and `question-visibility.ts` are byte-untouched. A self-contained `ArchiveQuestionCard`/`WeightageRow`/`WeightageList` in the new route file duplicates ~80 lines of rendering rather than bolting archive-only props onto the shared component — the same "second exam gets its own artifact, not a shared/modified one" principle `docs/multi-exam.md` §5 already states for study chapters. Topic-wise weightage reads `mv_node_weightage` through the EXISTING `usePaperTrends`/`getPaperTrends` path (no second weightage-computing code path). Reachable via a new "Browse PYQ Archive" link on Practice's PYQ Papers tab (`practice.tsx`'s only change — one `SectionCard action`), not `nav.ts`, to minimize footprint.
  - **Answer-key honesty, per the brief's explicit "never fabricate or infer one":** `key_provenance` (an existing, previously ingest-internal-only DB column) is now exposed read-only on the public `Question`/`questionSchema` (additive, nullable) — an MCQ shows "Official answer key" (tulsi)/"Unofficial key" (marigold)/"No verified key" (muted) by its real provenance; a descriptive (Mains) question ALWAYS shows an unconditional "No official answer key — this commission does not publish Mains answer keys" badge, never inferred or omitted. Live-verified this correctly renders the **honest 2026-provisional state** too: 2026 UPSC prelims MCQs (deliberately excluded from `OFFICIAL_COMMISSION` per U5's own gate-B design) show "No verified key", while every other year (2016–2025) shows "Official answer key" — the badge reflects U5's real per-year gating, not a blanket assumption.
  - **`exams.is_live` untouched** — no code that reads/writes it was touched; `upsc` stays `false` throughout (U9 owns the real launch flip, per the brief's explicit instruction not to touch its meaning). Verification used the same temporary-flip-then-restore pattern this repo has used since Session 15: flip `upsc.is_live→true` via the service-role client, drive a throwaway signed-in session through the real UI, flip back to `false`, and re-query to confirm — done twice (once per verification agent), confirmed `{uppsc: true, mppsc: false, upsc: false}` both times and at the very end of this session.
  - **Screenshot-verified against real data, not just typecheck-clean**: 7 real UPSC paper tabs (General Studies, CSAT, Essay, General Studies-I..IV (Ethics)); real question stems ("Ships from which of the following countries have to cross the Strait of Hormuz…"); the weightage section's real topic names and counts (Indian Polity and Governance — asked 203×, Economic and Social Development — 185×, …) matching U5's own documented `mv_node_weightage` breakdown exactly; the year filter narrowing a real 1089-question/55-page paper down to 98 (2020) / 97 (2026); switching the SAME session's `target_exam` back to `uppsc` correctly showed UPPSC's own 10 papers and real fractional negative-marking values, proving the page is genuinely exam-agnostic per-user rather than hardcoded to UPSC; and the pre-existing `/practice?tab=pyq` surface confirmed unaffected, now showing the new archive link.
  - **Mechanics**: two throwaway Supabase auth users, admin-API-created (`email_confirm:true`), no OTP/email needed; a session token seeded into a headless Playwright browser context's `localStorage` `sb-<ref>-auth-token` key (this repo's established pattern since the Community v1/guest-browsing sessions) so `RequireAuth` sees a signed-in session without driving real OAuth; `target_exam` flipped via a real authenticated `PATCH /profile` call, not a raw DB write. Both users deleted by their exact captured id afterward (never a broad filter) — `users_profile` does NOT cascade-delete on `auth.users.delete()` (this repo's own documented gotcha), so the profile row was deleted explicitly too; a follow-up `listUsers()` scan for the throwaway email pattern found **0 leftovers**, re-confirmed once more at the very end of this session across BOTH agents' throwaway accounts combined.
  - **Edge-case audit ("check all edge cases"), 2 real bugs found and fixed, neither hypothetical — both reproduced live before fixing:**
    1. **The new page conflated a genuinely-FAILED fetch with a legitimately-EMPTY one**, at all three of its query sites (`usePaperCatalog`, `useQuestions`, `usePaperTrends`) — exactly the bug class `usePaperCatalog`'s own doc comment names explicitly ("Distinct from an empty catalog; say so, don't render 'absent'") and `QueryErrorState`'s own docstring documents having already shipped TWICE elsewhere in this app (Current Affairs, the Answers hub — a rate-limited fetch rendered the same "nothing here" copy a real empty state would, with real data hidden behind it). Fixed by using the existing, already-widely-adopted `QueryErrorState` component (with retry) at all three sites instead of inventing new copy — no new i18n keys needed, it falls back to the shared `Common.loadErrorTitle`/`loadErrorDescription`/`retry`.
    2. **`listQuestions()` throws `PGRST103` ("Requested range not satisfiable") on an out-of-range page** — reproduced live directly against the real DB (`range(19960, 19979)` against 1100 matching rows → `PGRST103`, not an empty result). Reachable from this new page's own URL-driven pagination (switching to a year with fewer pages while `?page=` is still high, or a hand-edited URL) — and, since `listQuestions` is the SAME shared service function `pyq-list.tsx`/`practice.tsx`'s `PyqFilterView` already call via `useQuestions`, this was a **latent, pre-existing bug in the current UPPSC PYQ-browsing surfaces too**, not something newly introduced — fixing it here closes it everywhere, for free. Fixed with the IDENTICAL pattern this repo already established for the same error class in `listReviewQueue` (Session "M11/M14" era): on `PGRST103`, re-count with the same filters and return `{items: [], total}` instead of a 500. Verified directly against `listQuestions()` itself (not just the raw query): an out-of-range page now returns `{items: [], total: 1089}`, normal pagination unaffected.
  - **`docs/OUTSTANDING.md`/`docs/multi-exam.md` checked, neither needed an update** — grepped both for "PYQ archive"/"Previous Year Questions"/"PGRST103" and found no tracked item this closes or should newly open: this is new feature work, not a gap those files were tracking, and the pagination fix is a self-contained, fully-closed shared-function fix (not a partial mitigation needing a follow-up entry) rather than a new architectural multi-exam gap.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` and a FULL `pnpm --filter web build` (packages/shared was touched) both clean after every round of changes, including after the other session's concurrent `services/questions.ts` fix landed; `git status`/`git diff --stat` on `apps/web/package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` confirmed zero diff after a Playwright-devDependency mishap (accidentally removed the repo's real, committed Playwright dependency thinking it was a leftover throwaway install; caught via the diff itself, root-caused, and restored via `git checkout` + `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`) — worth remembering: not every `playwright` in this repo is a throwaway session install, `apps/web/package.json`'s `build:ci` script depends on it for real prerendering.

## UPSC prompt-config authoring (2026-07-31): 50 of U6's slots, three rubrics, two real bugs
Filled `EXAM_CONFIGS.upsc`'s judgment-bearing prompt slots across six areas — evaluation severity anchoring, a GS-IV Ethics rubric decision, question-generation style, the mentor persona, the CA national lens and the magazine curation lens — and registered UPSC's three answer-evaluation rubrics. `uppsc` is still the only `is_live` exam and nothing UPPSC-facing changed by a single byte. Files: `lib/exam-config.ts`, `services/evaluation/{rubric,prompts}.ts`, `scripts/__snapshots__/prompts.baseline.json`. Actionable index: `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f (U6 updated, **M30-M34** opened); standing write-up: `docs/multi-exam.md` **§6e**.
  - **PREMISE CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT — the slot counts everyone had been quoting were stale, twice over.** The brief said "68 → 40"; the docs said "74 UNAUTHORED per non-UPPSC exam". Both are wrong, and the error compounded: 68 was derived by subtracting U5's 6 `misc` slots from the stale 74. Counted directly against the config object instead (a throwaway script walking `getExamConfig(e)` for the `UNAUTHORED` symbol, run against **both** the working tree and `git show HEAD:` for a before/after): **a fully-unauthored exam is 96 today** — `mppsc` measures exactly 96 — because the config has grown new slots since 74 was written. So `upsc` stood at **90** before this pass (96 minus U5's 6 `misc`), and is **40** now (`notes` 20 + `misc` 20). 90 − 40 = **50 authored**, which reconciles exactly with the slot list (`relevanceLens` 2, `evaluation` 12, `qgen` 15, `mentor` 7, `ca` 14). The repo's own recorded lesson applied and paid off: *a changelog records what was true when written — re-measure before restating a number.* Corrected in both docs rather than propagated.
  - **EVIDENCE STANDARD: measured corpus + sourced marksheets, per-slot, never substitution.** Two research strands, both landing as documented comments beside the values so the reasoning survives:
    - **The real UPSC PYQ corpus** (2,791 rows from U5), **read with pagination past PostgREST's 1000-row cap** — Prelims GS-I alone is 1,100 rows, so an unpaged read would have silently biased every percentage in the guidance. Findings that actually shaped prose: the statement-combination family is **55.3%** of Prelims GS-I (any scaffolded stem 65.8%, 74-83% in 2024-25), modal design is **3 statements** (53.5%), **Assertion-Reason is 0 of 1,100 — literally zero**, and UPSC's counterpart is the **Statement-I/II** pair (4.1%, 2023-onward). The counting variant ("How many of the above statements are correct?") is **modern, not classic** — 0/600 across 2016-2021, then 8/47/18/27% in 2022-25 — so it is flagged as recent rather than sold as a staple. The canonical "1 only / 2 only / Both / Neither" quartet is only **13.0%**, so it is explicitly forbidden as the default. CSAT is named as structurally different (68.9% direct single-item) so a CSAT node cannot inherit the statement-set norm.
    - **WHY THIS COULD NOT BE SHARED WITH UPPSC, quantified rather than asserted:** UPPSC PRE_GS1 runs assertion-reason **6.6%**, pair-matching **16.3%** (UPSC 3.4%), chronological **6.3%** (UPSC 0.5%), negative framing **12.1%** (UPSC 2.1%). Reusing UPPSC's `formatGuidance` would have over-produced pair-matching ~5×, chronological ~12×, and instructed the model to emit a format **UPSC has never once set**.
    - **Mains verbs, where coaching lore is the trap:** "Critically examine" *reads* like the signature UPSC verb but is **2.3%** of primary stems (all "Critically X" forms 4.2%), so it was deliberately **demoted**, not promoted. Discuss is the workhorse (20.8%), and **33.3% of real stems carry no directive verb at all** — a bare What/How/Why or a bare topic — which no adapted UPPSC verb list would ever have produced. Marks: GS I-III are 10/150w and 15/250w at ~45% each; Essay is 125 marks/1200 words (80/80 bare aphoristic topics, no directive verb); GS-IV 10-markers are median 25 words and **0% case-study-shaped**, 20-markers median 141 words and **51%** case-study-shaped (up to 637 words), **71.8%** carrying `(a)`/`(b)` sub-parts.
    - **Flagged as NOT determinable, rather than smoothed over:** whether the 2026 GS-I style reversion is real or a transcription artifact, and UPPSC's 2024-25 rows (2025 reads 100% scaffolded, implausible for a real paper). Both would, if anything, mean the UPSC/UPPSC gap is **understated** here, not overstated.
  - **SEVERITY ANCHOR — a native re-establishment, and the trap it exists to block.** From real sourced **written-only** marksheets: excellent (AIR-1, whole-paper average across the four GS papers) **45-51%**, five-topper mean **46.9%**; barely adequate (last recommended General candidate) **~42%**. Per-question, a strong **10-marker ≈ 4-5/10** and a strong **15-marker ≈ 7-8/15** — arithmetic from real paper totals under an explicitly-stated proportional-distribution **assumption**, independently corroborated by separately-derived coaching figures. The "solid but unremarkable ~45-47%" band is **interpolated, not measured, and says so**. **⚑ THE TRAP, now encoded in the anchor itself: the widely quoted ~54% "topper" figure is the BLENDED written+interview aggregate**, and UPSC marks the interview far more generously (58-75%) than the written stage — an answer-writing evaluator never sees an interview, so anchoring near 54% inflates every score systematically. The anchor names the trap explicitly, because the model's own priors carry the blended number. **Two claims deliberately NOT made:** essay is *not* reliably more generous than GS (ahead by 2-10 points in 2018/2021/2022, **behind** in 2023 and 2025), and the GS-IV-scores-higher hint rests on n=4 AIR-1 candidates so it is stated directionally, never as a rule. **Honest gap recorded, not hidden: UPSC publishes no marking scheme and releases scripts only by personal RTI**, and circulating "topper copies" are mostly test-series mocks — so no verifiable per-question data exists anywhere. **Net: UPSC is calibrated at least as severely as UPPSC, arguably slightly more.**
  - **GS-IV ETHICS — option (b): a distinct rubric variant reusing the six frozen dimension keys.** The load-bearing evidence is primary-source: UPSC's notification says GS-IV *"will include questions to test the candidates' attitude and approach to issues relating to integrity, probity in public life and his problem solving approach"* and that *"questions may utilise the case study approach"* — **a sentence with no counterpart anywhere else in the notification**, since GS I-III, Essay and the Optionals are pure topic lists. Supporting (coaching-sourced, **n=1**, recorded as such): analysis of a real AIR-1 GS-IV script (143/250) found examiners rewarded *"conceptual clarity over data accumulation"* and *"practical, logical reasoning over statistics"*, with *"notably absent: heavy reliance on citations or statistical evidence"*. So `upsc-ethics-v1` weights `structure_flow .20 · content_coverage .25 · keywords_concepts .20 · examples_data .10 · presentation .10 · word_limit_language .15` (**= 1.00 exactly**, asserted at load), and **redefines** `examples_data` — relabelled "Realism & Practicability", explicitly *not* statistics or citations. Its `kind` is **`gs`, not a third kind**: GS-IV IS a General Studies paper and must compete on the GS board, and `evaluations.rubric_kind` is a CHECK-constrained column admitting only `gs`/`essay`, so a third kind would need a migration **and** would change `mv_mains_weekly_board`'s segmentation.
    - **Two corrections to widely-repeated claims, worth preserving:** the cited GS-IV **"125/125" Section A/B split is wrong** — five real papers reconstruct to **130/120**; and the "options → merits/demerits → recommend" arc is a **coaching template, not a universal demand** — roughly half of real case studies ask something structurally different (evaluate a third party's conduct, diagnose-and-suggest, a direct first-person response), so a rubric hard-coding the arc would systematically mis-score about half the corpus. The structure dimension therefore rewards the arc **only where the question asks for it**.
    - **ONE VARIANT, NOT THE TWO THE RESEARCH RECOMMENDED — a deliberate departure.** The registry's load-time assertion forbids two rubrics claiming one paper code (both would claim `UPSC_MAINS_GS4`); the proposed theory-vs-case-study discriminator is explicitly *a hypothesis, not a measurement*, at ~10-15% estimated error; and **silently routing an answer to a different marking scheme on an unvalidated heuristic is worse than one variant.** Instead the per-dimension descriptions are **conditional prose** read by a model that already sees the real question text ("if it is a short definitional item… if it is a narrative case study…"), which discriminates better than a regex. Recorded as **M33** with its reopening gate named: **validate a discriminator against the ~50-100 already-tagged 20-markers in our own corpus first — do not ship the estimate.**
  - **TWO REAL BUGS FIXED — a UPSC essay was silently broken without them**, and both are the shape a third exam will hit: (1) `services/evaluation/prompts.ts` compared the **literal `"essay-v1"`** in two places, which `upsc-essay-v1` fails — so `essayAnswerFraming` and the `MODEL ESSAY` framing would never have fired and a UPSC essay would have been prompted as a GS "descriptive answer". Both now ask the registry for the **kind** (`rubricKindOf(...) === "essay"`), the same fix `services/scoreboard.ts` already uses — **a version string is not a segmentation axis**, the identical lesson M5 recorded for the boards. (2) `services/evaluation/rubric.ts` pinned its dimension descriptions to `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE`, so **every UPSC evaluation would have carried UPPSC's "UP-specific data" inside its cached system prompt** — a state-specific instruction for a national exam. Descriptions now read the rubric's own `examCode`, and the essay word limit was parameterised too (it hard-coded "~700-word", flatly wrong for UPSC's ~1200).
  - **A PREMISE IN THE BRIEF THAT TURNED OUT FALSE FOR EXACTLY ONE KEY:** "the snapshot harness only covers `uppsc`, so a new exam adds no keys." **`prompt-snapshot.ts` dumps the ENTIRE `RUBRICS` registry into `rubric/RUBRICS:labels-and-weights`**, so registering three UPSC rubrics necessarily changes it. Rather than trusting the diff summary, the incumbent `v1` and `essay-v1` sub-objects were proved **byte-identical individually** (1858 and 1962 chars) and `git diff --numstat` on the baseline confirmed **145 insertions, 0 deletions** — no existing byte moved. The correction is written into `docs/multi-exam.md` §6d step 3, since a whole-key diff cannot distinguish "added a rubric" from "edited one".
  - **CACHE BOUNDARY, checked rather than assumed.** `severityAnchor` lands in `buildAnalysisSystem` segment **[0]**, which carries `cache: true` at **2118 tokens** against sonnet-5's 1024 minimum — per-exam text there **partitions** the cache (one entry per exam) rather than destroying it, so a long substantive anchor is safe. `sharedFeedbackContext` deliberately gained **no** exam text: it is the `[0]` segment shared byte-identically by the strengths and improvements calls, and that N=2 sibling read is the only thing making the second call a hit. **Mentor persona floor (M26):** `upsc/en` is **3547 chars** against the harness's 2950 hard floor (uppsc reference 2995) — `platformFraming` + `testingLens` supply **820 chars vs uppsc's 268**, ~216 tokens of headroom vs uppsc's 22 — so **no CACHE-FLOOR error and no upsc WARN**. The guard iterates all exams and runs in `--write` mode too, so it could not have been blessed away.
  - **THE NOISE-FLOOR RULE CORRECTLY DID NOT APPLY, and that is provable rather than a judgement call.** It governs changes to *shared* prompt structure, validated by a 3-arm control design because model nondeterminism makes a plain before/after uninterpretable. `getExamConfig` is a pure per-exam lookup memoised per exam code, so `upsc` values are **unreachable from any `uppsc` prompt** — the byte output cannot change, and the snapshot proves it mechanically. `ca/prompts.ts`'s A/B-validated ordering was not touched and no `cache: true` was added or moved.
  - **DELIBERATELY LEFT UNAUTHORED, per the standing per-slot discipline:** `notes` (20) and the remaining `misc` (20). A slot is authored when a pipeline genuinely needs it, so the sentinel keeps meaning "nobody has researched this yet" rather than decaying into "somebody filled the group". **The remaining gate for a UPSC launch is therefore `notes` — i.e. a chapter rollout, itself gated on M21 and `docs/multi-exam.md` §5's re-derivation rule.**
  - **Follow-ups opened (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f, M30-M34):** `buildImprovementsSystem` hard-codes "content coverage and examples/data carry the most weight", now **false for `upsc-ethics-v1`** (fixing it needs a signature change and moves a uppsc snapshot key, so it is its own change, not a drive-by); UPSC paper codes are local literals in `rubric.ts` because `lib/exam-papers.ts` is UPPSC's own constants module; `EXAM_CONFIGS.upsc.papers` is still all-`null` despite the 202-node tree (harmless — `resolveRubric` reads each rubric's own `paperCodes` — but stale and misleading); the GS-IV sub-variant split with its validation requirement; and two `upsc` values duplicated rather than sharing a module-level const the way `uppsc` does (`qgen.directiveVerbGuidance` ↔ `ca.mainsDirectiveVerbGuidance`, and the two `mainsSetterFraming`s) — deliberate, so the two generators can diverge per exam later.
  - **GOTCHA (environment, not repo):** this machine's `node_modules/.bin` is corrupted — every shim exists only as numbered duplicates (`tsc 2`, `tsc 3`, …) with no plain `tsc`, so `pnpm --filter api typecheck` dies with `sh: tsc: command not found`. The package itself is intact; run `node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` (or `pnpm exec`, which resolves correctly) instead. Not a code problem and nothing in the repo was changed for it.
  - Verified: api typecheck **exit 0**; **full** `pnpm --filter web build` clean (the hard rule — shared modules were touched); `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` → **128 prompts byte-identical**, 0 new/changed/missing, **no CACHE-FLOOR failure and no upsc WARN** (the two uppsc mentor-persona WARNs are the documented pre-existing +22-token margin, M26); `pnpm check:paths` clean over 788 tracked files. `git status` confirmed only the 4 intended code files were modified before the docs were written; every file staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`. All throwaway counting scripts deleted.

## Follow-ups M30-M34 closed + an audit of `15722f8` (2026-07-31)
Closed the four follow-ups the UPSC prompt-config pass left open, plus five findings from an adversarial audit over the uncommitted tree and over `15722f8`. **Zero DB writes — read-only access throughout**, because the audit's most important finding (U7) is a live row whose remedy is a founder decision. Actionable index: `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f (M30-M41 + U7).
  - **M30 — the row that raised it was itself wrong, and the correction reframes the whole item.** It recorded `buildImprovementsSystem`'s hard-coded "content coverage and examples/data carry the most weight" as *"true for every GS rubric"* and false only for the new `upsc-ethics-v1`. **Measured against the registry, it was already false for the LIVE UPPSC DEFAULT** — so this was a **pre-existing correctness bug in the shipped product**, not a new-exam mismatch:

    | rubric | ranking by weight | where `examples_data` actually sits |
    |---|---|---|
    | `v1`, `upsc-gs-v1` | coverage .30, structure .20, keywords .15, **examples .15** | joint-**THIRD** |
    | `essay-v1`, `upsc-essay-v1` | coverage .30, structure .20, **examples .20** | joint-2nd (the only two it was right for) |
    | `upsc-ethics-v1` | coverage .25, structure .20, keywords .20, **examples .10** | **LOWEST** — flatly inverted |

    **Three options, and why (a):** (a) **derive the levers from the resolved rubric's own weights and state the percentages** — shipped; (b) pin the legacy string for the incumbent `v1`/`essay-v1` and vary only for new rubrics — rejected, since it preserves a false statement in the live UPPSC product purely to avoid moving a snapshot key, which inverts the point of having the snapshot; (c) name nothing and tell the model to read the weights off the rubric block it already has — **rejected on a factual check, not a preference: the improvements call never receives the rubric block.** Its shared context (`buildFeedbackSharedContext`) carries the per-dimension **scores** (`structure_flow 3/10, …`) and never the weights; the rubric block with `weight N%` is built only into pass 1's `buildAnalysisSystem`. Verified against the snapshot rather than by reading intent. So naming the levers is what makes "biggest score lever" computable by the model instead of guessed.
  - **The noise-floor rule was checked against its own wording, and the honest difference from the last case is recorded rather than glossed.** `docs/multi-exam.md` §6e says the 3-arm control design "correctly did NOT apply" to the exam-config sweep, for two reasons: it governs changes to *shared prompt structure* where nondeterminism makes a before/after uninterpretable, **and** no UPPSC byte moved at all. **The second reason is NOT available here — this fix deliberately moves two live `uppsc` keys**, and claiming otherwise would be false. It still does not apply on the first clause: this is a **factual correction, not a structural or ordering change**. The old sentence made a checkable claim about the weights and the claim was wrong against the registry the same process reads; which of a true and a false statement of the weights is better is settled by the weights, and no sampling of model output could overturn it. The rule's real trigger — an ordering/caching change invisible to a diff — remains untouched: **`ca/prompts.ts` is byte-unmodified and no `cache: true` was added or moved.**
  - **M35 (audit, 🟡) — the GS-IV model answer contradicted the rubric it had just been scored under.** Pass 1 scored `upsc-ethics-v1`'s `examples_data` as realism and administrative plausibility, "**NOT** about statistics, citations or data… do not penalise an answer merely for citing few facts" — then pass 2 handed the candidate a model answer told to substantiate with "constitutional articles, committees, schemes, and **national and international evidence (Economic Survey and NITI Aayog data, global indices…)**". Root cause: `buildModelAnswerSystem` branched on `kind`, a **2-value board axis** that cannot express "ethics", and `upsc-ethics-v1.kind` is `"gs"` **on purpose** (GS-IV is a GS paper and must compete on the GS board). **Fixed with a new in-memory-only `RubricDefinition.modelAnswerShape` (`gs|essay|ethics`), NOT by widening `kind`** — `evaluations.rubric_kind` is DB CHECK-constrained, so a third kind needs a migration *and* would change `mv_mains_weekly_board`'s segmentation. **The ethics branch adds NO config slot** (census provably unchanged): it reuses `modelAnswerFramingGs`, since an ethics paper *is* a GS paper, and its remaining prose is rubric semantics — the same reason `ETHICS_DIMENSIONS`' descriptions live in `rubric.ts` rather than exam-config. It names no commission's paper number, so it reads correctly for any exam that later registers an ethics-shaped rubric.
  - **M36 (audit) — `resolveRubricByKind` returned `forExam[0]`**, and for `("upsc","gs")` **both** `upsc-gs-v1` and `upsc-ethics-v1` match: it returned the right one only because of declaration order, so **reordering the `RUBRICS` literal would silently have routed a custom-prompt UPSC GS answer to the ethics scheme.** Exactly what the file's existing load-time assertions were written to prevent — the by-kind resolver had just never been brought under the rule. Now **prefers the exam's DEFAULT rubric** (deterministic *and* correct: a custom prompt names no paper, and a paper-selected rubric should only be reached BY its paper), plus a new load-time assertion closing the residual case where an exam's default is of a different kind. **Verified by physically reversing the registry and re-resolving — 6/6 identical.**
  - **M37 (audit, cheap guard) — `topWeightedDimensions` could name everything.** `[.50,.10,.10,.10,.10,.10]` names 6 of 6 and renders "…weights A (50%), B (10%), … **above the rest**" — above nothing; all-equal names 6/6 at 17% each, **summing to 102%**. Not reachable by any registered rubric (widest real cut is 3 of 6). The selection must now be a **proper subset or the function returns `[]`** — deliberately *not* truncated to N-1, because truncating makes exactly the arbitrary choice the function's own doc refuses to make; `buildImprovementsSystem` falls back to phrasing that names no dimension.
  - **M38 (audit, doc-only) — two files each declared themselves canonical.** The new `lib/upsc-papers.ts` claimed to be "the one TypeScript home" while `ingest/_shared.ts` holds all 7 codes as `PaperDef` literals and `ingest/seed/upsc-syllabus-seed.ts` calls **that** the single source of truth. Values agree byte-for-byte, so it was ownership, not correctness. Header now states the real order — **(1) the seed + `0112` are the authority**, (2) `_shared.ts`'s `PAPERS` is the ingest pipeline's own richer record and is the truth *within* it, (3) this module is the pointer for non-ingest consumers. `_shared.ts` deliberately **not** refactored: `PaperDef` is a richer record than a code, and re-pointing it is ingest surgery for no correctness gain.
  - **M33 stays closed — but the reasoning is now measured, and the old reason was retired as untrue.** *No ground-truth tag has ever existed*: all **131** `UPSC_MAINS_GS4` rows carry `meta` with one key (`source_ref`), `generation_meta` NULL 131/131, and a needle scan for every plausible tag name returns **0 hits** — the "~50-100 already-tagged 20-markers" was fiction, and the "51% case-study-shaped" figure came from a **regex over stem text**. So all 131 stems were **hand-labelled: 54 case / 77 theory**, 20-markers **52/102 = 51.0%**. The proposed two-stage text rule measures **8.8% error** (the "10-15%" estimate was mildly pessimistic), and **the best discriminator is not text at all — `word_limit >= 250` gives 95.4% overall / 94.1% on 20-markers with ZERO false negatives and no fitted parameter.** **So "no validated discriminator exists" is no longer a truthful reason to keep it closed; the truthful reason is the PAYOFF:** a typical misroute costs **~2.5% of full marks**, *below* the ≤5%-of-full-marks repeatability the eval harness already tolerates; the shipped blend sits **between** the two profiles, halving worst-case deviation at zero routing risk; the split wins **~1% of full marks in expectation**, about a quarter of the pipeline's own noise; the weight differences fall on `structure_flow` (already conditional prose that reads the real question text) and on two format-agnostic dimensions; and **custom prompts have no `question_id`, so any router must fail open to the blend anyway.** The per-year labels are recorded in §8f so the study is not redone (single-annotator, 6 flagged borderline). **Persisting a `meta.gs4_shape` tag was deliberately NOT done** — no consumer, exam not live, and this is the same Supabase project for dev and prod.
  - **⚑ U7 (audit, 🔴 — the important one, and DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED): `is_live` is a SELECTION gate, not a LAUNCH gate.** `assertSelectableExam` is called on **only the two WRITE paths** in `services/profile.ts`; `getUserExam()` reads `target_exam` raw and `getExamConfig()` is a pure in-memory lookup — **neither checks `is_live`** — so a row that ALREADY holds a non-live exam bypasses the gate forever, needing no further write. **Verified live: exactly one non-UPPSC `users_profile` row exists** — the founder's (`target_exam='upsc'`, `onboarding_completed=true`), parked by U3 switcher verification. `getDailyAnswerSet` for it now returns a real 4-item set (5 on Sundays, incl. `UPSC_MAINS_ESSAY`) where it previously returned `[]`, and because `onboarding_completed=true` it is in `listAllUserIds()`, so this runs on **~25 scheduled invocations/day** plus every `/answers` and dashboard load. **The exposure that matters is the EVALUATION path, not the answer set:** the whole `evaluation` group is now authored, so `UNAUTHORED` offers **no protection** and a typed submission would run the real two-pass Sonnet pipeline **at real cost for an exam the registry says is not launched**. Also recorded: the **ragged failure boundary** — from that same screen `misc.ocrFraming`/`drillExaminerFraming`/`studyPlanCoachFraming` still throw, so handwritten upload, micro-drills and the study plan **hard-500 beside a working typed path**. This is the **mirror image** of §8h's U4 ("flipping `is_live` is not a one-line change"): a user parked on a non-live exam needs no flip at all. **Remedy — reset the row, add a read-path guard, or leave it — is a founder decision. Nothing was implemented and no DB row was touched.** **⚑ DECIDED the same day (founder): "Leave it — I'm testing UPSC."** The row stays on `target_exam='upsc'` on purpose, to exercise the UPSC surfaces pre-launch — so a future session must **NOT** reset it or add the read-path guard as a drive-by tidy-up; that needs a fresh decision. Knowingly accepted: typed evaluation bills real Sonnet calls while OCR / micro-drills / the study plan hard-500 (authoring those three `misc` slots would flatten the ragged edge, if wanted). **What was accepted is this one parked row during testing — NOT the underlying gap**, which stays open and must be closed before any second exam goes live.
  - **Two smaller audit findings recorded, not fixed** (both benign today, both measured): **M39** — M32 quietly gave `services/daily-progress.ts` a second `.papers` reader (`prelimsGs`/`prelimsCsat` were `null`, now `UPSC_PRE_*`), so a previously-skipped `tests` lookup now runs for a UPSC user; harmless in outcome but a behaviour change on a hot path the fix did not flag. **M40** — `answer-set.ts` falls back to UPPSC's `ESSAY_WORD_LIMIT`/`ESSAY_MAX_MARKS` (700/50) when a question's own values are null, an exam-specific constant serving as a cross-exam default; **measured currently dead — 0 of 80 published `UPSC_MAINS_ESSAY` and 0 of 108 `MAINS_ESSAY` rows have a null `word_limit`/`marks`.** The correct source is the resolved rubric's own `defaults`.
  - **The `docs` lint baseline was stale (M41): the real count on a clean HEAD is 41, not 40.** The 41st is `src/routes/pyq-archive.tsx:26` (`only-export-components`) from `1be8f22`, a prior session. **This session's delta is 0** — it touched no web file. Same lesson as U6's "74"/"68": re-measure, do not propagate a number out of a changelog.
  - **The snapshot harness gained its first non-`uppsc` fixtures, deliberately, with the caveat written into the file.** Every other key is built with the `uppsc` fixture, so "N byte-identical" is predominantly a **UPPSC** regression check and says little about UPSC. Added `buildImprovementsSystem` for `essay-v1:hi`, `upsc-gs-v1:en` and `upsc-ethics-v1:en` (the rubric whose lever set diverges most — the only one whose `examples_data` is its lowest weight), plus `buildModelAnswerSystem:upsc-ethics-en` to guard M35's new branch, and `modelAnswerShape` alongside `kind` in `RUBRICS:labels-and-weights`. **128 → 133 keys.** Proven per key rather than by the total: the pre-write run reported **exactly 4 new + 1 changed + 0 missing**, i.e. no existing prompt TEXT moved — which is what proves M35's refactor and M37's cap are output-neutral for all five real rubrics.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; **full** `pnpm --filter web build` clean (the hard rule); `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` **133 byte-identical** after re-baselining (the 2 WARNs are the documented pre-existing mentor-persona margin, M26); `pnpm check:paths` clean over 788 files; `pnpm --filter web lint` **41 warnings, delta 0**. A throwaway script (deleted after) asserted **56/56**: all five rubrics resolve and sum to 1.0, `resolveRubric("uppsc", …)` **unchanged for all 10 UPPSC papers**, the ethics model-answer prompt now matches its rubric while the GS branch still carries the substantiation list (targeted, not global), order-independence by physically reversing the registry, M37's cap firing on synthetic degenerates while leaving all five real rubrics' lever text byte-identical, and the **UNAUTHORED census still uppsc 0 / upsc 40 / mppsc 96** — the ethics branch added no slot.

## UPSC `notes` + `misc` config authored (2026-07-31): 37 slots, U6 down to 3 — and a production incident caused by testing the guard
Filled `EXAM_CONFIGS.upsc`'s two remaining judgment-bearing groups — `notes` (20) and `misc` (17) — taking `upsc` from **40 UNAUTHORED to exactly 3**, all three deliberate. Added the harness's first broad non-`uppsc` fixture set (**snapshot 133 → 157**), hardened `ingest:syllabus` against the argv class that caused the incident below, and closed **M21**. `uppsc` remains the only `is_live` exam. **A real, billed, production-DB-writing accident happened during this session and is recorded in full below — it is the most important thing in this entry.**
  - **CENSUS, WALKED STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN GREPPED** (`rg UNAUTHORED` counts comment lines too, and this file's comments name the sentinel constantly): iterating `EXAM_CONFIGS` gives **uppsc 0 / upsc 3 / mppsc 96**. The 3 are `misc.syllabusExpertFraming`, `misc.syllabusStructureNote`, `misc.translateDomainHint`. Worth knowing for a future census: **upsc has 119 slots where the others have 120, and that is CORRECT, not a missing slot** — `RelevanceLens` is a discriminated union (`state_specific` carries `state`, `national` does not), so `upsc` legitimately has no `relevanceLens.state`, TypeScript forces its sole consumer (`ca/prompts.ts`) to narrow on `kind` before reading it, and that consumer omits the "Source hints at <state> focus" line entirely rather than rendering a borrowed or empty state name.
  - **EVIDENCE STANDARD, unchanged from the pass that filled the previous 50: measured corpus + the hand-authored tree, never renaming UPPSC's text.** The `notes` values are grounded in what the UPSC tree and the U5 corpus actually show — e.g. `stateAngleDirective` does not merely blank out the UP angle, it **redirects** the author to the national/comparative dimension UPSC really tests (Union policy and its handling in Parliament, centre-state implications, Economic Survey / NITI Aayog data, India's standing on the relevant global index). That mattered structurally, not stylistically: **`up_angle` is in `NOTE_GEN_SCHEMA`'s `required` list**, so an empty directive would have left the model a mandatory field with the instruction `- up_angle: .` — verified non-empty (444 chars) by walking the object.
  - **THREE GRAMMAR BUGS, ALL INVISIBLE TO TYPECHECK AND TO READING THE RAW VALUES — only the ASSEMBLED prompt shows them.** Two were found and fixed during authoring (an em-dash gloss that opened a clause the template never closes, so the template's own "and what a topper must know" read as part of the gloss; and a dash in `pyqAnalysisFraming` with the same shape). **The third survived both and was found by this session's audit, in `notes.outlineCompletenessLens`** — and its own in-file comment asserted it was fine ("the colon closes at the parenthesis and leaves the template's own coordination intact"), which is **false: a colon has no closing bracket.** The host is `The EXAM defines completeness: plan sections that map to ${value} and what a topper must know — never padding.` The value opened a *second* colon inside the template's first, its internal `A, and B` list then captured the template's trailing `and what a topper must know` as a third list item, and `(use the weightage + PYQ patterns)` — which modifies the whole clause for `uppsc` — ended up scoped to the Mains item alone. Fixed value-only, to two parallel `in …` phrases (`"…asked at both stages, in Prelims statement sets and in GS Mains answers (use the weightage + PYQ patterns)"`), so the template's trailing `and what …` is not an `in`-phrase and cannot be read into the list. **The lesson is the method, not the sentence: a per-exam fragment is only correct AT ITS BOUNDARY, so diff the assembled snapshot against the `uppsc` sibling — the sibling read cleanly the whole time and made the defect obvious in one line.**
  - **⚑ WHY THE TARGET WAS 40 → 3, NOT 40 → 0. The brief asked for zero; three slots are deliberately withheld because they are a LIVE SAFETY GUARD, not backlog.** `misc.syllabusExpertFraming` and `misc.syllabusStructureNote` are read only by `ingest:syllabus`'s LLM-structuring path, and authoring them would have **unblocked a pipeline that must never run for UPSC**: (1) `upsertNode` conflicts on **`(paper_code, path)` — the identical key the hand-authored seed writes under** — so an LLM-invented tree would overwrite the coverage-gated 195-node UPSC tree *in place*, replacing `title_i18n`/`description_i18n`/`meta` wholesale and flipping `meta.source` from `official_syllabus_hand_authored` to `official_syllabus`; and (2) `loadLangSource` **hardcodes UPPSC's manifest ids**, so a successfully-authored run would build UPSC's tree from UPPSC's syllabus PDF. `misc.translateDomainHint`'s sole consumer is that same closed path. Authoring them would have removed a guard while adding no capability — the opposite of the intended trade.
  - **The refusal is now REDUNDANT, deliberately, because the `UNAUTHORED` guard lives in a different file from the thing it protects.** New `LLM_STRUCTURABLE_SYLLABUS_EXAMS = ["uppsc"]` allow-list + `resolveExamScope()` in `ingest/syllabus.ts` refuses **before `readManifest`/`loadLangSource`**, so a refusal costs nothing and touches nothing. It is an allow-list rather than `=== DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` so that "which exams may be LLM-structured" and "which exam is the default" stay separate questions. Both guards verified live: `--exam upsc` throws at `resolveExamScope` (stack trace confirms `syllabus.ts:104` from `main:504`, i.e. before any I/O), and `requireAuthored(misc.syllabusExpertFraming)` **still throws if reached** — tested directly by bypassing the allow-list, while the same three fields resolve normally for `uppsc`.
  - **SNAPSHOT 133 → 157 (+24 `:upsc` fixture keys), and the point is not the number.** Verified additive: **0 pre-existing keys changed, 0 removed**, despite 323 lines of `exam-config.ts` churn. **Before these fixtures the 37 authored values had ZERO machine coverage** — every other fixture in the harness is pinned to `uppsc`, so "N byte-identical" had always been predominantly a *UPPSC* regression check, and a second exam's prose was invisible to it. That is exactly why all three grammar bugs had to be caught by reading. Note the raw file has **159** top-level keys: 157 prompts + the harness's own `__unreachable__` / `__not_reachable_without_editing__`. Determinism contract re-verified (no clock/random/network/DB in the new fixtures; every one reuses a builder already snapshotted for `uppsc`, so no new code path is reached), and **`--write` still cannot bless a mentor persona below the 2950-char floor** — the check precedes the write branch and `process.exit(1)`s; `upsc` measures 3547/3558 en/hi against `uppsc`'s thin 2995/3006.
  - **M21 CLOSED — because authoring `notes.*` is precisely what made it live.** It had been safe only because `notes:chapter` threw at `outlineFacultyFraming` before it could reach the validator. `validatePyqIds` (`notes/chapter-assemble.ts`) now scopes by the node's own exam via `examCodeForNode` + `questionExamScopeFilter`, chunks `.in()` at 100, and reports **foreign** separately from **missing** because they mean different things (a nonexistent id is a typo/truncation; a cross-exam id is a copied draft never re-grounded — the exact failure M15's "draft from the corresponding UPPSC chapter" rule invites). `questionExamScopeFilter` is the right helper and **not** `questions.exam_code`, which is *provenance* and whose domain includes `up_ro_aro`/`upsssc_pet` — papers that legitimately belong to the default exam's bank. Verified live 6/6 against the cloud DB, read-only: UPPSC ids on a UPPSC node clean; the same ids on a UPSC node all **foreign, none missing**; a nonexistent id **missing, not foreign**; mixed input **provably disjoint** (`missing = !exists`, `foreign = exists && !inExam`, so no id can be in both); empty input does no query work; and 253 ids across the chunk boundary handled with no dropped tail.
  - **⛔ THE INCIDENT — a real, billed, production-DB-writing accident, caused by an agent TESTING THE VERY GUARD THIS SESSION ADDED. Recorded unsoftened.** An agent batched invocations in a shell loop. **zsh does not word-split an unquoted `$a`**, so `--exam upsc --dry-run` arrived as **one argv token**. `parseArgs` reads that as the nonsense KEY `"exam upsc --dry-run"` — which leaves `args.exam` **undefined**, not `true` — so `onlyExam` fell to `null`, `resolveExamScope` defaulted to `uppsc`, and **`--dry-run` was swallowed into the same nonsense key, so `dryRun` was FALSE**. A real run, against the project that is **the same Supabase project for dev AND production**. Twice. **Damage: +85 orphan rows (294 → 379) and 35 rows overwritten in place** (`title_i18n`/`description_i18n`); ids were unchanged, so 2,682 question references and 31 note references stayed structurally intact. **The hand-authored UPSC tree (202 rows) was never touched — every explicit `--exam upsc` invocation was correctly REFUSED. The accident came in through the DEFAULT path, which no guard covered.** CA amplification, and it has a short fuse: the phantom nodes entered `loadSyllabusCandidates`' triage set and a `ca:run` mapped a generated Article-338 MCQ onto one **within ten minutes**; `ca:run` is 6-hourly.
  - **THE RECOVERY, and what made it possible.** `backup.yml`'s weekly GPG-encrypted `pg_dump` had **4 successful runs** (its "this is new" header is stale — it works); `supabase-backup-2026-07-26` predated the incident by 5 days. **The decisive property is that the backup PREDATES migration `0106`, so its `syllabus_nodes` has no `exam_code` column and held exactly 294 rows — meaning "the whole table" and "the uppsc tree" are provably the same set, so 294 was CONFIRMED, not assumed.** `created_at` also split cleanly 294-on-07-05 / 85-on-07-31 with nothing in between, so "absent from the backup" carried no risk of destroying a legitimate post-backup row. Restored **29** rows — not 35, because six had been rewritten byte-identically — and deleted **85** orphans; `meta` was `{}` in the backup too, so **nothing beyond title/description text was lost**. Final, re-queried rather than inferred: uppsc **294**, upsc **202**, **zero rows dated 2026-07-31**, 7,742 question refs + 284 note refs all resolving.
  - **FOUNDER DECISIONS, and one correct refusal.** Re-mapping the stranded Article-338 MCQ onto a real node was authorised, but the agent **correctly refused it**: the phantom `polity/constitutional-bodies` was an invented subdivision with no legitimate counterpart, and Article 338 fits three real nodes defensibly, so guessing would have mis-filed a question into the live bank. Founder then chose to delete both the MCQ and the node, landing at 294.
  - **THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING: the accident was caused by the EXACT defect class the agent had been dispatched to fix — a valueless flag silently defaulting — reproduced one layer up, in its own test harness.** And the fix shipped for it was **insufficient**, which this session's audit proved with a pure `parseArgs` simulation (no pipeline invoked): a valueless-flag check tests `args.exam === true` and therefore **cannot see** the collapsed-token shape at all. Three guards were needed, and they are now ordered cheapest-and-broadest first in `main()`: **(1) reject bare positionals** — `parseArgs` does `if (!a.startsWith("--")) continue`, so `ingest:syllabus upsc` produces *no key whatsoever* and sails past everything; **(2) reject unknown keys** — this is the one that closes the incident, live-verified: the exact collapsed token now dies with `Unrecognised flag(s): --exam upsc --dry-run`; **(3) reject valueless value-flags**, plus `--limit-nodes` non-positive-integer validation, because **NaN is falsy** and `ingestPaper` gates on `opts.limitNodes ? slice : raw`, so `--limit-nodes abc` silently widened a capped smoke test to the FULL tree — the same silent-widen the guard's own comment claimed to prevent. **The real conclusion is that this belongs in the shared `parseArgs`, not per-call-site: 22 CLIs call it, and the same class has now been patched per-call-site twice** (the `--write-artifact` write-through in `ingest/seed/upsc-syllabus-seed.ts`, and here) — each fix protecting exactly one script while every other caller keeps the same hole. Recorded in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §4.
  - **SAFETY DISCIPLINE USED FOR THE GUARD TESTS, stated because it is the reason no second incident happened:** `main()` performs **no I/O between `parseArgs` and `readManifest`** — the ordering is parse → positional check → unknown-key check → valueless check → `resolveExamScope` → pure `PAPERS.filter` → *then* `readManifest`/`loadLangSource`/`ingestPaper`. So anything that throws at a guard provably cannot reach the billed path. Every invocation run this session was proven in advance to throw at a guard, each was checked afterwards for the string `syllabus sources in manifest` (**0 hits across all 13 runs**), and **a bare invocation and `--exam uppsc` were never run at all** — those are the two shapes that legitimately proceed.
  - **Also found and reported, not fixed** (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md`): **63 current-affairs items hold 126 dangling `mcq_question_ids`** — measured live, all created 2026-07-05→09, unrelated to this session and to the deleted MCQ; `up_angle`'s learner-facing heading is hardcoded in the web i18n as "The UP Angle" with a `MapPin` icon, so `notes.stateAngleLabel` configures only the *critic* rendering and a UPSC digest note would still render under a UP heading on screen; `buildNoteGenParams`'s cached prefix measures **817 tokens with an empty context — already below sonnet-5's 1024 minimum today, for `uppsc` too**, so that cache silently does nothing for a sparse node (pre-existing, not exam-specific, related to M25); and a UPSC chapter is simultaneously told "never write a state angle" while being offered the shared, persisted `ChapterBoxKind` enum member `up_special`.
  - **GOTCHA (environment, and it interacted with a concurrent process):** a concurrent process was mid-`pnpm install`, so `node_modules/.bin` held `tsx` but **no plain `tsc` and no `oxlint`** (only numbered duplicates). Rather than run an install that would race it, every gate was invoked from the pinned store copy directly — `typescript@5.9.3` for the api, `typescript@6.0.3` + `vite@8.1.3` for the web, `oxlint@1.72.0`. Also: a scratchpad probe needs its own `{"type":"module"}` or tsx rejects top-level `await` with a confusing `cjs` transform error.
  - Verified: api typecheck **exit 0**; **full** `pnpm --filter web build` — `tsc -b` exit 0, `vite build` exit 0, `postbuild` exit 0 (the hard rule; `exam-config.ts` is a shared module); `prompts:snapshot` **157 byte-identical**; `check:paths` clean over **789** tracked files; web lint **41 warnings, exit 0** (the corrected M41 baseline, not the stale 40). All probe scripts deleted. **A concurrent process held ~7 unrelated files plus migration `0114` in the tree throughout** (`cost-report.ts`, `daily/config.ts`, `lib/anthropic.ts`, `lib/exams.ts`, `qgen/*`, a new `lib/exam-context.ts`) — confirmed disjoint before staging, and the M21 import was checked to resolve against **HEAD's** `lib/exams.ts` so this commit is self-contained; staged by explicit path, never `git add -A`.

## Shared `parseArgs` hardened — D12 closed before U7 (2026-07-31)
The durable fix for the defect class that caused the 2026-07-31 production incident (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §0d): argv validation moved OUT of per-call-site patches and INTO the shared parser, so it protects every caller at once instead of the two that had been patched individually. **No pipeline was executed and no DB row was touched** — the whole change is verified by a pure argv harness, deliberately, because the incident itself was caused by an agent testing a guard by invoking the real pipeline in a shell loop.
  - **`parseArgs(argv, spec, scriptName)` now takes a REQUIRED per-script `FlagSpec`.** Required, not defaulted, on purpose: a defaulted parameter lets every existing caller keep the bug by doing nothing (**M24**'s lesson, recorded twice before). Kinds: `value` · `boolean` · `positiveInt` · `positiveNumber` · `nonNegativeNumber`. The return shape is unchanged (`Record<string, string | boolean>`), so no call site's *reads* changed — numeric flags are validated centrally but still returned as strings, which means an existing `Number(args.limit)` keeps working and is now guaranteed safe.
  - **Parsing is schema-AWARE, not merely post-hoc validated** — this is what makes it more than a checklist. Because the parser knows a flag's kind, **a boolean flag no longer consumes the next token**: `--dry-run PRE_GS1` used to silently eat the operator's real argument (it "worked", since the string was truthy); now the boolean stays boolean and the orphan is caught as a positional.
  - **Five guards, aggregated into ONE error, most-diagnostic first:** (1) bare positionals — checked first because it is the one shape the others structurally cannot see; (2) unknown/malformed keys — **the one that actually closes the incident**, and when a key contains whitespace the message says so and names zsh's refusal to word-split an unquoted `"$var"`, because a generic "unknown flag" would send an operator hunting a typo that does not exist; (3) valueless value-flags; (4) a boolean given a value (`--dry-run=false` reads as "off" to a human but was truthy); (5) numeric shape. Also fixes `--key=value`, which previously became the nonsense key `"key=value"` — the same silent-misparse class and, per the private-parser audit, the most likely everyday way to lose a `--dry-run`.
  - **`nonNegativeNumber` exists for one concrete reason: `ca:run --wait 0` is the CRON'S OWN VALUE** ("submit the batch and exit without polling"). A strictly-positive validator would have broken the scheduled run. There is an assertion pinning this so nobody "tightens" it later. Likewise `--max-usd` is `positiveNumber` (fractional dollar budgets) and `qgen --difficulty 3:5:2` is a `value`, not a number — both would have been wrongly rejected by a blanket integer rule. **This is exactly why D12 insisted on a per-script schema rather than a blanket rule.**
  - **Spec-authoring mistakes throw at startup**, since each would silently disable the protection: a flag written `"--dry-run"` (with dashes) can never match the parsed key `dry-run`, so the real flag would be rejected as unknown forever; a flag declared under two kinds has ambiguous parse behaviour.
  - **Wired into all 17 shared callers + `qgen/cli.ts` = 18 shipped call sites.** The 4 argv-shape guards in `ingest/syllabus.ts` and the valueless loop in `upsc-syllabus-seed.ts` were **deleted as superseded** — while every DOMAIN guard survives untouched (`LLM_STRUCTURABLE_SYLLABUS_EXAMS`, `resolveExamScope`, the default-exam regression guard, the unknown-`--paper` throw, the terminal `--write-artifact` mode resolution). Note `upsc-syllabus-seed.ts` still had the collapsed-token hole open despite being one of the two "already patched" scripts: its valueless-only check could not see it, so `"--write-artifact docs/x.md"` as one token fell through to the DB upsert.
  - **`qgen/cli.ts` converted from a private clone** — the worst instance in the repo: one collapsed token defeated `--max-usd` AND `--dry-run` together, turning a capped dry run into a real billed generation run that writes `questions` rows, i.e. §0d reproduced exactly.
  - **⚑ REAL MISS, caught by an independent audit rather than by the agents: `src/ingest/embed.ts` was a 17th shared caller the census missed.** `grep -n parseArgs` on that file **exits 1 while `awk NR==245` prints the line**, same absolute path, reproducibly, on a file that is valid UTF-8 and unmodified vs HEAD. Compounding it, a verification `tsc` used a path that does not exist (typescript lives in `apps/api/node_modules`, not the repo root) and, because the command was `tsc … 2>&1 | grep TS2554 || echo "none remaining"`, the MODULE_NOT_FOUND was swallowed by the grep and printed a **false green**. `ingest:embed` spends OpenAI credit and `--limit`/`--only` are its caps, and `--missing-only` is a LIVE nightly cron (`nightly-settle.yml:91`), so this was a genuine miss. **Lesson: use `awk`/the compiler for a census, never a bare `grep`; never pipe a build command into a filter that can swallow its failure — check the exit code, and prove the tool actually runs by feeding it a deliberate error.**
  - **Regression-locked: `pnpm --filter api test:args`** (`scripts/test-parse-args.ts`, mirroring the `test:mentor` precedent — pure argv, no DB/network/spend). **105 assertions**, including the verbatim incident argv, every shipped script's documented invocation, and both live cron invocations (`ingest:embed --missing-only`, `ca:verify-mcqs --run`). **The harness was NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED** — disabling each of the three guards in turn was confirmed to make it fail, then `_shared.ts` restored **byte-identically (sha256 verified)**; a harness that always passes is worthless. A separate **shipped-vs-tested cross-check** (extracting the spec literal from each call site and diffing against the harness) is what caught `qgen` being shipped but untested.
