  - **Compatibility checked before assuming, not after:** every real `--run` invocation in the repo uses the bare form — including the live cron `ca-verify-mcqs.yml:60` — so declaring it `boolean` breaks nothing and the dead `|| args.run === "true"` branch was simplified away. `--topup` is pre-supplied by the `qgen:topup` pnpm script and had to stay declared. `ingest:explain --all` is **documented but read nowhere**, so it is deliberately admitted as a no-op — omitting it would have broken a documented command. Conversely `ingest:pyq --csv` names an **unimplemented** path, so it is deliberately NOT admitted (a clear "unrecognised flag" beats the old confusing "Provide --id") and the stale docs advertising it were deleted. `regate`'s undocumented-but-real `--publish-only` was added to its usage block.
  - **⚑ RESIDUAL, and the task's premise corrected (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **D15**): hardening the shared parser does NOT close this for everyone.** D12 said "22 call sites"; the real census is **17 shared + 12 private clones + 3 on an `argVal`/`argValue` dialect that never appears in a `parseArgs` grep = 32 arg-parsing CLIs in 5 dialects.** 11 private parsers remain unprotected, ranked by what a misparse actually costs: **P0** `migrate-dev-user.ts` (16-table + profile deletes; `--dry-run` defaults false and `--dry-run=true` produces no key at all — survives today only *by accident*) and `seed-demo-account.ts` (`argv[++i] ?? args.email` silently retargets a 26-table wipe onto the DEFAULT demo account). **P1** `notes/cli.ts` (valueless `--top` widens 3→15 real generation runs with **no confirmation prompt**), `notes/chapter-cli.ts`, `daily/run.ts` (**`opts.size ?? default` does NOT catch NaN** — verified — so a bad `--size` propagates silently into the live shared daily quiz). **Verified-safe to leave:** `cost-report.ts` + `feature-discovery-report.ts` (zero write calls) and `set-password.ts` (both flags mandatory). Also worth recording: **the switch-style dialect is not safer, it fails differently** — `argv[++i]` is unconditional, so it eats the *following flag*, and losing a `--dry-run`/`--reset` is worse than losing an `--exam`. `prune-guests.ts` (deletes auth users) fails safe **only by accident**: `--days --apply` yields `apply=true` + `retentionDays=NaN`, and an unrelated `new Date(NaN).toISOString()` `RangeError` — confirmed empirically — throws before any delete.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` **exit 0** (and proven to actually run, by feeding the compiler a deliberate error after the earlier false green); `pnpm --filter api test:args` **105/105**; `test:mentor` **13/13**; `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` **157 byte-identical** (the 2 WARNs are the documented pre-existing mentor-persona margin, **M26**); `pnpm check:paths` clean over 789 tracked files. No `apps/web` or `packages/` file was touched, so the web build is unaffected. Every scratch/demo script deleted. **Concurrent process:** another session held unrelated in-flight files throughout; every file was staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`.

## Docs correction pass (2026-07-31): four false claims fixed, seven findings recorded — DOCS ONLY
An adversarial audit of `8214bed` found stale/contradictory claims in this file and in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`. **No code was changed** — every item below is either a correction to a written claim or a finding recorded for someone else to act on. Each was re-verified against the live DB or the actual source before editing; none was taken on the brief's word.
  - **Corrected — A10's denominator was the wrong number entirely.** It read "3,455 items carry mcq ids", but **3,455 is the TOTAL `current_affairs_items` row count**. Measured: **925** rows carry a non-empty `mcq_question_ids` (2,530 hold `[]`, 0 NULL), referencing 1,640 distinct ids, **126 dangling across 63 items**. The old wording made the data read roughly **four times healthier** than it is.
  - **Corrected — U6's cell contradicted itself.** Its head said the remaining UPSC-launch gates are no longer U6; the **tail of the same cell** still said "what is left is `notes` (20) … gated on M21 … plus the remaining `misc` (20)". By then every clause was false (`notes` authored, `misc` at **3** deliberate slots, **M21 closed by that same commit**). Also fixed "`upsc` stood at 90 before this pass" — 90 was before the **first** 2026-07-31 pass; before the second it was **40** (96 − 6 − 50 = 40, − 37 = 3, walked structurally against `EXAM_CONFIGS`). **The generalisable lesson: when a long cell is updated in place, re-read the WHOLE cell — a stale tail reads exactly as authoritative as a fresh head.**
  - **Corrected — "zero questions are tagged non-`uppsc`"** (this file's M19 note, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` M19 and §8e). U5 made it false: the bank is **7,861 = 5,070 `uppsc` + 2,791 `upsc`** across 7 `UPSC_*` papers. Annotated in place rather than rewritten — **M19's decision is unaffected** (it rests on provenance semantics and join-table cost, not on the corpus being empty), but while the line read as current it made **M21 look hypothetical when it is live**.
  - **Corrected — D12's "22 call sites"**: measured **30 tracked `apps/api` files** reference `parseArgs` at `8214bed` (29 callers + the definition). **Not edited by this pass** — a concurrent process had already corrected it more finely in an uncommitted **D15** (17 shared + 12 private clones + 3 on an `argVal` dialect = 32 arg-parsing CLIs in 5 dialects). Recorded here so the correction is not made twice.
  - **Recorded (🟠, live) — A11: 12 dangling `pyq_id`s in 8 PUBLISHED chapters**, i.e. dead reader chips today. Scan over all 284 chapters: **ok 1,840 / foreign 0 / missing 22 → 12 distinct dead ids**. Root cause is structural: `validatePyqIds` runs **only at assemble time** and **nothing re-validates a persisted chapter**, so a chip is checked once at write and trusted forever. Session 29's audit recorded **0 dangling of the same 1,320 refs**, so the questions were deleted afterwards — plausibly the same deletion event behind A10's 126 dangling CA ids.
  - **Recorded (🟡) — M44/M45/M46/M47.** `foreign` chips are **warn-and-drop, not a gate**, and the CLI's `N/M chapter(s) assembled` line still counts a file that lost *every* chip — logged as a **decision to make**, since M15 makes "draft from the UPPSC chapter" the recommended workflow and `foreign` is precisely "never re-grounded" (M44, which also notes the `EDGE` question is **mis**labelled as another exam's — it is `uppsc` on a synthetic paper code). Two fail-open shapes in `lib/exams.ts` (M45). `prompts:snapshot --write` can bake a machine-specific path into the tracked baseline via `__unreachable__`, which `check:paths` then fails CI on — latent today, `__unreachable__` is `{}` (M46). And **`upsc`'s mentor / teacher / analysis / qgen / CA prompts are authored and reachable but have NO snapshot key** — `buildMentorPersona('upsc')` measures 3,547/3,558 chars and is guarded only by M26's **length** floor, so a length-preserving reword regresses undetected (M47).
  - **⚑ Root cause of the recurring toolchain breakage, diagnosed and folded into `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` D14: `~/Desktop` on this machine is iCloud-synced** (`~/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/Desktop` is a symlink to it), and iCloud conflict resolution replaces `node_modules/.bin` shims with byte-identical ` N` duplicates — the workspace-root `.bin` holds **267 files but only 7 distinct names**, and `apps/api`'s holds **39 `tsc*` entries and no bare `tsc`**. That is why `pnpm install` "fixes" it and why it returns. **Two session notes in this file blame "a concurrent process was mid-`pnpm install`" — that is a coincidence, not the cause.** The repo is unaffected (`node_modules/` is gitignored); what it breaks is the documented `pnpm --filter api typecheck` gate.
  - **CONCURRENT PROCESS — the reason this commit's staging is unusual.** The **D12 shared-`parseArgs` refactor** was in flight in this same tree (~19 `ingest`/`ca` CLIs, `_shared.ts`, a new `scripts/test-parse-args.ts`, plus `0114`), **and it had already written its own uncommitted edits to `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`** — the same two files this pass had to edit. Staging by path would have swept up its work and claimed credit for D12. Instead the index blob for each doc was built as **HEAD + this pass's edits only** (`git hash-object` + `git update-index`), leaving the working tree carrying both sets — so `git diff` after this commit shows exactly the concurrent process's own changes, untouched and still theirs to commit. Its D12/D12a/D15 rows and its §0d additions were **not** edited, and D12 is **not** marked resolved by this commit.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api prompts:snapshot` **157 byte-identical**; `pnpm check:paths` clean. Both were run from the pinned pnpm store binary, not `node_modules/.bin` (broken per the iCloud note above) and **without** running `pnpm install`, which would have raced the concurrent process. All scratch scripts deleted.

## CLI arg parsing consolidated to ONE dialect + CI-enforced (2026-08-01)
Completion of the D12 work: every CLI in the repo now goes through the single hardened `parseArgs`, and a new standing guard stops a new dialect appearing. **No script was executed and no DB row was touched** — verification is a pure argv harness plus the compiler, deliberately, because the 2026-07-31 incident (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §0d) was itself caused by an agent testing a CLI guard by invoking the real pipeline.
  - **THE CENSUS WAS WRONG TWICE, and the second correction is the interesting one.** D12 said "22 call sites"; the previous session measured 17 shared + 12 private + 3 `argVal` = 32. The real total is **41 CLIs in SIX dialects** — a further **9** were sniffing `process.argv` directly and appeared in NO `parseArgs` search at all (`ca-distribute-mcqs`, `ca-flag-low-quality-mcqs`, `ca-reclassify-mcq-nodes`, `ca-remap-prelims`, `prove-chapter-retrieval`, `resync-test-marks`, `trial-abuse-report`, `ca/compile.ts`, and `prompt-snapshot.ts` itself). They were found only by scanning for `process.argv.(includes|indexOf|find)` rather than for the parser's name. **Lesson: to census a pattern, search for the SHAPE OF THE SYMPTOM, not the name of the thing you already know about.**
  - **The six dialects, recorded because each fails differently:** (1) the shared parser; (2) verbatim private clones; (3) `out[key] = argv[i+1] ?? ""` with no `i++` — a valueless flag yields the literal string `"--nextflag"`, which then PASSES a `typeof === "string"` check with garbage; (4) switch-style `if (argv[i]==="--x") v = argv[++i]` — `argv[++i]` is unconditional, so it eats the FOLLOWING FLAG, and losing a `--dry-run`/`--reset` is worse than losing an `--exam`; (5) `argVal()` = `indexOf(flag)` then `argv[i+1]`; (6) `process.argv.find(a => a.startsWith("--kinds="))`. All 41 now use dialect (1).
  - **`pnpm check:cli-args` (`scripts/check-cli-args.mjs`) — the durable half.** Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` beside the portability guard and BEFORE `pnpm install` (it needs only node + git). Fails on any locally-defined arg parser (`parseArgs`/`argVal`/`argValue`/`getArg`/…) or any direct `process.argv` flag-sniffing. **Deliberately still allowed:** `process.argv.slice(2)` (the parser's correct input) and `process.argv[1]` (the invoked-directly module guard — a different concern, matched around with a negative lookahead so `process.argv[i+1]` still fails). Escape hatch `cli-args-allow:`, mirroring `portable-paths-allow:`. Comment lines are skipped, since these files document the very patterns they forbid. Live: **227 CLI files scanned, exit 0**. Without this, consolidating 41 CLIs would erode again within months — the expensive part was the consolidation, and nothing was protecting it.
  - **REAL BUGS FIXED IN PASSING, none of which were parser bugs:** `resync-test-marks.ts`'s header documented `--kinds daily_quiz,mock` (space form) which its `--kinds=`-only parser **structurally could not see**, so the documented invocation silently fell back to the default kind list — both forms now work. `prune-guests.ts` scanned the RAW `process.argv` (node's binary path + the script path included) instead of `.slice(2)`. `daily/run.ts` SPREAD its parsed object straight into `DailyBuildOptions`, so the raw key `user` would never have become `userId` — silently fanning a single-user build out to EVERY onboarded user; it now builds the options object explicitly. `notes/chapter-cli.ts` turned out to read an **undocumented `--regen`**, which would have broken as an unknown flag had the spec been copied from its usage string — the reason every agent was told to derive flags from the real reads and treat my hypotheses as a cross-check only. `seed-demo-account.ts` gained an explicit throw on `--email=` (inline empty): the parser correctly accepts an empty *inline* value, and the old `?? default` would have retargeted a 26-table wipe at `demo@neevstudy.com`.
  - **A CORRECTION TO MY OWN GUIDANCE, caught by an agent's failing assertion:** I had said a value beginning with "a dash" needs the `--password=<value>` form. The parser tests `startsWith("--")` — **two** dashes — so a single-dash password parses fine and only a double-dash one needs the `=` form. Fixed in all three places it had been written.
  - **Kind choices that a blanket rule would have broken** (this is why D12 insisted on a per-script schema): `ca:run --wait 0` is the CRON'S OWN VALUE → `nonNegativeNumber`; `feature-discovery:report --days 0` means "everyone" per that file's own comment → `nonNegativeNumber`; `--max-usd 2.5` and `audit:resolve --max-usd` are dollar budgets → `positiveNumber`; `qgen --difficulty 3:5:2` is not a number at all → `value`. Conversely `trial-abuse-report`'s three counts are `positiveInt` because its OLD helper already required `> 0`. `audit:resolve` turned out to have **7** flags, not the 4 its first usage line suggests (the line wraps).
  - **Two disclosed behaviour tightenings, each checked against every live invocation in `package.json`/`.github/`/`docs/` before shipping** (none passes the affected flags): silent numeric clamping is now a refusal (`cost:report --days 0`/`abc`; `trial-abuse:report --days 0.5`), and `--run true` on the three `ca:*` scripts is rejected in favour of the bare `--run` that every real caller — including the live cron `ca-verify-mcqs.yml:60` — already uses.
  - **Still deliberately loose, recorded so it is a decision not an oversight:** `eval-harness --lang fr` silently becomes `en`, and `ca:run --mode garbage` silently selects `batch`. Both are enum-VALUE validation, not argv-SHAPE — a different concern that defaults safely — and `FlagSpec` has no `oneOf` kind; adding one was scoped out rather than bolted on.
  - **Coverage proven, not assumed.** `pnpm --filter api test:args` is now **205 assertions** covering **all 41 CLIs**, including both live cron invocations (`ingest:embed --missing-only`, `ca:verify-mcqs --run`) and the `--days 0` / `--wait 0` / `--difficulty 3:5:2` / `--kinds=a,b` cases that pin the non-obvious kind choices. A bidirectional cross-check (every harness entry → a real call site, and every call site → a harness entry) is what makes "all 41" a measurement rather than a claim. **Two of my own verification scripts produced false results during this work and were themselves corrected** — a regex that grabbed the first string inside the spec instead of the scriptName, and a shell loop that failed to word-split its input, which is the very bug class this whole task exists to eliminate.
  - **`prompt-snapshot.ts` was converted too, and re-verified after:** `157 prompts byte-identical`, `git diff` on `prompts.baseline.json` empty. Converting the regression harness itself was the one place worth extra care, since it is a gate the rest of the verification depends on.
  - Verified: api typecheck `--noEmit` **exit 0** AND a real **emit** build **exit 0** (emit can catch what `--noEmit` does not; `dist/` confirmed gitignored, no tree pollution); `test:args` **205/205**; `test:mentor` **13/13**; `prompts:snapshot` **157 byte-identical**; `check:paths` clean (789 files); `check:cli-args` clean (227 files). **GOTCHA (environment, pre-existing, NOT a regression):** `pnpm --filter api build` fails with `sh: tsc: command not found` because this machine's `node_modules/.bin` holds only numbered shims (`tsc 10`, `tsc 11`, …) with no plain `tsc` — the corruption CLAUDE.md already documents. `pnpm exec tsc` fails the same way; use `node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc -p tsconfig.json` from `apps/api`. CI on a clean runner is unaffected. No `apps/web` or `packages/` file was touched. All scratch scripts deleted; every file staged by explicit name, never `git add -A`.

### Edge-case audit of the CLI consolidation, same day (2026-08-01): 3 more latent holes in the parser
Adversarial pass over the consolidation itself — hunting for what it INTRODUCED or newly exposed, not re-running the happy path. Found and fixed three real holes in `parseArgs`, all in the same silent-misparse family the parser exists to eliminate. Every fix was **negative-controlled** (disable the guard → confirm the harness fails), then `_shared.ts` restored **byte-identically (sha verified)**.
  - **(1) A flag named `__proto__` silently LOST its value.** The parsed record is a plain object literal, so `out["__proto__"] = v` sets the PROTOTYPE rather than an own property — the caller reads back `undefined`, i.e. a value-flag that vanishes, which is precisely the class this parser exists to stop. Unreachable via argv (an undeclared `--__proto__` is rejected as unknown), so the SPEC was the one path that could reach it; `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` are now rejected as flag names at spec-validation time.
  - **(2) A duplicated flag silently last-won.** `--paper A --paper B` ran against B while the operator may well have meant A — a silent-WRONG-SCOPE, the same family as the incident's silent-widen. Not hypothetical: `qgen:topup` PRE-SUPPLIES `--topup` from its pnpm script, so a caller adding their own flags can genuinely duplicate one. Now rejected, across spaced, inline and mixed spellings.
  - **(3) An EMPTY value was accepted**, in both `--paper=` and `--paper ""` spellings. An empty string is NOT "not supplied": it passes every `typeof x === "string"` check, so it reached queries/filters as `""` and silently matched nothing, or slipped past a `?? default` written to catch an absent flag. Now rejected centrally — which also makes `seed-demo-account.ts`'s per-script empty-`--email` throw redundant (left in as harmless defence-in-depth). This is the same defect agent-authored code had already had to hand-guard once, which is the argument for it living in the parser.
  - **THE HARNESS CAUGHT MY OWN CHANGE, which is the point of having one:** two pre-existing assertions expected `--limit-nodes=` / `--wait=` to be rejected as *numeric* problems, but the new empty-value check fires first (an empty value is a SHAPE problem, not a RANGE one). Both are still rejections; the expectations were corrected and annotated rather than the ordering changed.
  - **A spec-vs-reads consistency check over all 41 CLIs** — is every `args.X` read actually declared in that script's spec? A read that is not declared would be a permanently-dead code path (the flag could never be set). **6 hits, all verified FALSE POSITIVES**: `parsed` is an LLM structured-output object / a zod `safeParse` result / a plain array in five of them, and the sixth matched inside a comment. So no conversion dropped a flag. Worth keeping the technique: it is the cheapest way to prove a schema migration is complete rather than assumed.
  - **DISCLOSED LIMITATION of `check:cli-args`, verified as a real escape rather than assumed away:** it catches direct `process.argv` sniffing, but NOT `const a = process.argv.slice(2)` followed by hand-parsing `a`. Catching that needs dataflow analysis, and `.slice(2)` must stay allowed because it is the parser's own input. Accepted and recorded rather than over-fitting the regex — the guard's job is to stop the easy, accidental reintroduction, not a determined one.
  - **Also checked, no action needed:** a bare `--` and a single-dash `-x` are both rejected; a negative number as a numeric value is rejected; a value containing `=` (`--paper=A=B`) is preserved correctly; an empty spec rejects every flag; `--wait 0`, `--difficulty 3:5:2` and `--kinds=a,b` all still parse. `D11`'s 5-column markdown row in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` is **pre-existing** (5 columns at HEAD too), so it was left alone rather than fixed as a drive-by — but my own D12a row DID have 3 unescaped pipes (from quoting `tsc … 2>&1 \| grep … \|\|`) which would have rendered as extra columns, and those were escaped. Note the first check of that was itself wrong: `awk -F'|'` splits on escaped `\|` too and falsely flagged D15 — a renderer-accurate count cleared it.
  - Verified after every fix: `test:args` **213/213**; api typecheck `--noEmit` exit 0 AND a real emit build exit 0; `test:mentor` 13/13; `prompts:snapshot` **157 byte-identical**; `check:paths` clean (789 files); `check:cli-args` clean (227 files). No `apps/web` or `packages/` file touched. All scratch scripts deleted.

## CA exam-scope write pair — a LIVE cross-exam publish bug, made structurally impossible (2026-08-01)
`ca/pipeline.ts`'s **published/draft** insert had no `exam_codes` key at all, while the **archive** insert 60 lines above it wrote `itemExamCodes` correctly. So every user-visible current-affairs row silently took the column DEFAULT `array['uppsc']` (migration `0106`), with `itemExamCodes` computed correctly at the top of the same function and used for embedding stamping — just never persisted on the path that produces the rows users read. Latent since `e1f960b`.
  - **⚑ WHY IT MATTERED THE DAY IT WAS FOUND, not eventually.** `29e3a19` had just added an `--exam` override that deliberately permits a NOT-live exam. At `d1af52f`, `pnpm ca:run --exam upsc` would have taken items triaged against UPSC's syllabus, enriched in UPSC's voice, mapped onto `UPSC_*` nodes — and **published them into the LIVE UPPSC feed** stamped `exam_codes=['uppsc']`, because `listCurrentAffairs` filters with `.overlaps(exam_codes)`. Not a mislabel: a cross-exam content leak on the read path, produced by the tool's own documented invocation.
  - **THE FIX IS THE STRUCTURE, NOT THE LINE.** The one-line add is the smaller half; the real defect is that four write paths each restated the invariant "a statement that writes `syllabus_node_ids` must also write `exam_codes`" **in a comment**, and the fourth just… didn't. New `withExamScope(merged, rest)` in `ca/exam-fanout.ts` (the pure, unit-assertable module) is now the ONLY way to reach either column on this table, and it is a compile error in **both** directions:
    - You cannot OMIT `exam_codes` — you never write it, the builder always does. The defect is unrepresentable.
    - You cannot NAME either column in `rest`: `T & { [K in ExamScopeColumn]?: never }`, the same `?: never` device `ca/widen-exam.ts` uses. **`?: never` rather than excess-property checking on purpose** — an excess-property check is defeated by first assigning the literal to a wider variable; `?: never` is not, it reduces the whole argument to `never`. Verified: all three shapes (naming `syllabus_node_ids`, naming `exam_codes`, and the widened-variable case) fail `tsc` against the real tsconfig, each naming the offending column.
    - Applied to **all four** paths — `pipeline.ts` archive + published/draft, `backfill.ts` archive + enrich. A new path added by copying an existing one now inherits the guarantee instead of the bug. The three duplicated invariant comments were deleted as superseded.
  - **NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED, both layers.** A pure 12-assertion harness (no DB/clock/model) proved the pair is always emitted, matches the merge, survives a minimal `{}` payload, is not clobberable by a spread, and that a upsc-only item is **not** stamped `uppsc`. Deleting `exam_codes` from the builder's return then made **tsc fail** (`TS2322`, `T & {syllabus_node_ids}` not assignable to `T & ExamScopeWrite` — the return type alone catches it) **and** the harness exit 1. Restored and confirmed byte-identical by sha256. Harness deleted after — a proof, not a shipped test.
  - **CORRECTED a false claim in this file** (M5-M9 entry): "Partial M8 fell out of M3: `ca/pipeline.ts` now writes `exam_codes` from the nodes triage actually chose" was only ever true of the ARCHIVE insert.
  - Verified: api typecheck exit 0 · `prompts:snapshot` **158 byte-identical / CHANGED=0** (`ca/prompts.ts` byte-untouched) · `check:paths` clean (798 files) · `check:cli-args` clean (233 files) · `test:args` **299/299** (a concurrent session had grown it past the 213 the last entry recorded — re-measured, not copied) · `test:mentor` 13/13.

## First 5 UPSC study chapters published (2026-08-01) — and the panel gate, read honestly
`upsc` goes **0 → 5 published chapters (5 of 195 chapterable nodes, 2.6%)**, all through the free-subagent path `docs/multi-exam.md` §5 mandates — **$0 app Anthropic spend**, `model:'claude-code-agent'` on every row. `exams.upsc.is_live` was **not** touched and remains `false`. Coverage checklist regenerated (`docs/upsc-chapter-coverage.md`, now `5/195`; PRE_CSAT `4/12`, PRE_GS1 `1/32`). Commit `c666a46`.
  - **The nodes are the top of the weightage worklist**, taken from `pnpm notes:coverage` rather than a hand-kept list (the reason that CLI exists): Indian Polity and Governance (`UPSC_PRE_GS1`, 203 PYQs, 49 chunks) · Basic Numeracy and Data Interpretation (CSAT, 292, 40) · Basic Numeracy (CSAT, 269, 41) · Logical Reasoning and Analytical Ability (CSAT, 258, 47) · Comprehension (CSAT, 246, 43).
  - **⚑ THREE OF THE FIVE WERE RECOVERED OFF DISK, AND THIS EXTENDS THE DOCUMENTED PATTERN.** Two earlier attempts were killed by usage limits and reported as producing nothing. They had in fact left **two complete chapter JSON files AND three generator scripts that were never executed** — i.e. the work was finished and died one `node` invocation short of existing. The standing rule is "check every output path before concluding anything failed"; the sharper version is **check for unexecuted BUILDERS too, not just outputs** — the emit step must come before the research is finished, not after. Running the three generators into a **separate directory** (so they could not clobber a concurrently-running author's file) yielded five complete, schema-valid chapters. **The premise "both previous attempts produced ZERO chapters" was true of the DATABASE and false of the disk.**
  - **Checked before any write, not after:** all 70 `pyq_id`s trace to their own node's context pack (assemble refused nothing, dropped nothing — the `d1af52f` gate never had to fire); no `up_special` box and no state angle survived the UPPSC drafts; the only three "Uttar Pradesh" mentions left are legitimate **national** context (UP placed 5th in the Ministry of Panchayati Raj's national Devolution Index); 44 diagram locales and 28 markdown tables parse clean, with no JSON bleed of the 2026-07-23 kind. Embedding is one in-process `embedNotes({nodeId})` per node — never a bare call, never shelled out — verified by **per-note head count** (49/47/43/41/40 = 220 chunks), all 220 stamped `exam_code=upsc`, so nothing leaks into UPPSC grounding. One `statement timeout` on the first upsert, cleared by the documented retry. Retrieval proved end to end: "basic structure doctrine / Article 368" pulls that chapter's own section at **rank 1, sim 0.680** under the exam-scoped RPC.
  - **THE PANEL RESULT, AND WHY THE HEADLINE NUMBER OVERSTATES IT.** 5 UPSC vs 5 domain-matched UPPSC chapters, exam names redacted, deterministic seeded shuffle (`mulberry32`, seed 20260801 — no unseeded `Math.random`), key sealed outside the judges' directory, 3 free subagent judges scoring depth / factual grounding / structure 1-5. **Result: A(upsc) 4.91 vs B(uppsc) 3.89, gap +1.02 against an inter-judge noise floor of 0.24** (mean absolute pairwise judge disagreement on the same sample) — a 4.2x separation, and all three judges agree in direction independently (+1.00 / +1.13 / +0.93), so it is not one judge carrying it. **With n=5 per arm this is INDICATIVE, NOT CONCLUSIVE.** Three confounds, each of which cuts against the headline:
    1. **The comparison is structurally loaded.** §5 *requires* the UPSC chapters to be drafted FROM the UPPSC ones and expanded, so the arms are not independent — this measures "derived-and-expanded vs source", where a win is close to tautological. The UPSC arm is simply longer (7-8 sections / 15-35 facts vs 6-7 / 7-26), and depth+structure are the two dimensions length most flatters.
    2. **⚑ THE FACTUAL-GROUNDING GAP DOES NOT SURVIVE ITS OWN BLINDING ARTIFACT.** All three judges marked `sample-2` down for stating CSAT is "100 questions / 200 marks", calling it a clear factual error because four other samples said 80. **It is CORRECT — sample-2 is the UPPSC control, and UPPSC's CSAT genuinely is 100 questions/200 marks while UPSC's is 80.** Anonymising the exam names is exactly what made two true, exam-specific statements look mutually contradictory, and the judges penalised whichever arm was in the minority — i.e. **the artifact favours the new arm.** Excluding that one sample, factual grounding goes **+0.60 → +0.20 against a 0.27 noise floor, i.e. INSIDE the noise: indistinguishable.** Depth (+1.50) and structure (+1.03) are unaffected and stay well outside it. **The honest reading: the new chapters are deeper and better structured; they are NOT shown to be better grounded.** Anyone re-running this should hold exam-pattern facts out of the factual dimension, or brief judges that pattern figures legitimately differ per exam.
    3. **Both "fabrications" the panel found in the UPSC arm are FALSE POSITIVES, checked rather than assumed.** Judge 1 flagged the Polity chapter's "298 vs 230 on the Constitution (131st Amendment) Bill, 2026" as "an invented-sounding fabricated specific". It is real and exact — LiveLaw reports "298 members present out of 528 … approved the Bill, 230 members voted against it", 17 April 2026, and the bill needed 352. The second flag (Devolution Index "2025 release vs 2024 Index") is the ordinary report-year-vs-release-date distinction, not an inconsistency. **A very recent true fact reads as fabricated to a judge, so an LLM panel's fabrication flags need verifying in both directions before they are acted on** — the same discipline this repo already applies to its own claims.
  - **The one live-agent chapter that returned was DISCARDED, and the rule is what decided it.** Of the three authoring agents spawned this session, one finished: 6 sections / 19 facts for a node whose published chapter already had **7 sections / 27 facts**. Session 29's richer-wins rule cuts both ways, so it was discarded rather than used to bump `chapter_version`. The other two were still running at report time and their output was not used — the published versions they would have had to beat scored 5.00 and 4.78 on the panel, the joint-best and third-best of all ten samples.
  - **REAL BUG FIXED in `010ea3c`'s own CLI — the command the generated file prints into its header could not be run.** `notes:coverage` already derived `ROOT` from `import.meta.url` for the *printed* command but still handed the raw `--out` to `writeFileSync`, so it resolved against `process.cwd()` — which the root script makes **`apps/api`** via `pnpm --filter api`, giving `ENOENT` on a non-existent `apps/api/docs/`. (It would also have rendered `--out apps/api/docs/…` into the header if it had succeeded.) Both the write and the printed command now anchor on `ROOT`, per the repo's standing portability rule; an absolute `--out` is unaffected, since `resolve()` returns it unchanged. **Verified by regenerating from the repo root AND from `apps/api` to identical output.**
  - **Noted, not fixed (out of scope, untouched files):** `apps/api/scripts/rls-security-check.ts` has 2 pre-existing `TS2339` errors — invisible to CI, which typechecks `-p tsconfig.json` (`include: ["src"]`) and so never compiles `scripts/`. The panel's anonymiser also emits a cosmetic "the the State" where a source read "the UP …"; it affects only the throwaway judge copies, never stored content.
  - Verified: api typecheck exit 0 (and `src`+`scripts` together, clean apart from the pre-existing pair above) · `check:paths` clean (800 files) · `check:cli-args` clean (234 files) · no `apps/web` or `packages/` file touched. A concurrent session owned `apps/api/src/qgen/**`, `lib/exam-config.ts` and `scripts/prompt-snapshot.ts` throughout — untouched, and every file staged by explicit path, never `git add -A` (its `_tmp_panel_score.ts` is also why this session's scorer is named `_tmp_chapter_panel_score.ts`).

## U8 (2026-08-01/03): activating the seven content pipelines for `upsc`
Founder-signed-off projection **~$1.36/night, ~$41/month recurring + ~$10 one-off**, derived from 23,080 real `llm_calls` rows rather than estimates. **Actual attributable spend $1.7362** (CA triage probe $0.1867 + qgen $1.5495). Under projection only because the ~$8 CA history backfill was deliberately not run and chapters were authored by free coding subagents at $0. **`exams.upsc.is_live` was false throughout and remains false — nothing here is user-visible.** Full actionable index: `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` **§8k**.
  - **THE HEADLINE, and it is not the activation:** `.github/workflows/*.yml` `schedule:` triggers run from the **DEFAULT branch**, and `main` is `dd4bdeb` (2026-07-28). Every commit below is on `feature/UPSC`, so **production has been executing the pre-fan-out build the whole time** — it shows the model the entire 502-node two-exam tree in one UPPSC-framed call. Measured: 53 contaminated `current_affairs_items` repaired 2026-08-01, **54 more by 08-02**, repaired again. It recurs every ~6h and **only a merge (or disabling the schedule) stops it** — an operator deploy decision. A commit-age guard shipped (`c7195c6`) but lives on this branch, so it cannot bite until `main` moves. Its author's reasoning is worth keeping: **you cannot detect stale code using only that stale code** — a `schedule:` trigger reads the workflow from the same commit as the checkout, so every marker-based scheme is self-consistent by construction; only an external reference (another branch, or the clock) works.
  - **A latent defect this uncovered, and my own override that made it dangerous.** `ca/pipeline.ts`'s published/draft insert **never wrote `exam_codes`** (only the archive insert 60 lines above did), so every user-visible row silently took the `['uppsc']` column default — latent since introduction. Combined with `29e3a19`'s `--exam` override (which I asked for, to build content without flipping `is_live`), `ca:run --exam upsc` would have published **UPSC-voiced items straight into live UPPSC users' feed**. Fixed and the two paths' divergence made a **compile error** (`58e270b`). This also corrects an earlier CLAUDE.md claim that "M8 fell out of M3: `pipeline.ts` now writes `exam_codes`" — true only of archived items.
  - **`cost:report` was silently truncating at PostgREST's 1000-row cap** — rendering 4.3% of the data and understating 30-day spend **27×** ($12.41 vs $336.11). Every figure it printed since the bank passed 1000 calls was wrong. The signed-off projection was built from direct paged queries, not from that report.
  - **Two of three content-quality gates said NO, and that is the honest result.** CA triage **PASSED at parity** with the live UPPSC control (deltas inside the inter-judge spread). qgen **NO-GO twice**: the CSAT fix produced real improvement (+0.83 = 4.2× the 0.20 noise floor) but generated questions remain **1.02 below** real UPSC; all 72 stay `needs_review`, unpublished. Chapters **FAILED 8-1 overall / 8-0 on depth**. The UPPSC regression suite passed **8/8** (two worktrees, byte-identical hashes over real content, never totals).
  - **⚑ THE LESSON MOST WORTH KEEPING — the incremental-write rule cuts both ways.** Telling authoring agents to write their JSON early and update it incrementally is the only reason ANY chapter survived repeated harness session limits. But it also **preserves truncated work in a state that looks finished**: "History of India and Indian National Movement" was published with 6 sections, 14 audited facts and valid structure — stopping at the Revolt of 1857, **omitting the entire Indian National Movement**, half the node's own title. "Environmental Ecology, Bio-diversity and Climate Change" has **zero Climate Change**. Both came from agents killed mid-run. **Completeness must be checked against the node's declared syllabus scope — never against section or fact counts**, which is exactly the proxy used when selecting the salvage. The History chapter was reverted to `needs_review` by explicit id.
  - **Salvage rule, learned the hard way: pick by RICHNESS, not timestamp.** `round3/` held newer-but-poorer copies (0 and 1 sections) of chapters `round2/` had at 6. And **check for unexecuted generator scripts, not just outputs** — two "produced nothing" attempts had in fact left complete files plus unrun builders, which is where 5 of the first chapters came from.
  - **Gate sequencing error, mine:** for the second chapter batch I had the agent publish and *then* gate. A gate that runs after publication can only clean up, not prevent. Every other pipeline gated before shipping.
  - **A self-contaminated metric** in `ac150dc`'s own commit message: its "0.6% statement-counting baseline" was computed over 924 rows **including the 44 generated rows under test** (all 5 hits were generated). True baseline, filtered to `source='pyq'`: **0/880**.
  - **A structural fact about qgen worth remembering:** it targets coverage gaps, so generated questions systematically land on the nodes with the **least real exemplar support**. "Decision Making" has 0 real PYQs on the node; "Interpersonal Skills" has 4, all 2026, all a form absent from every other year — and there the *real* arm scored 1.00 against generated's 2.50. Any style gate must report per-node so "generation is at fault" stays separable from "this node's benchmark is atypical".
  - **Two dev schedulers made opt-in** (`CA_DEV_SCHEDULER`, `DAILY_DEV_SCHEDULER`). An idle `pnpm dev` had been firing `ca:run` 6-hourly against the production project — and the daily one runs streak settle, mastery recompute and **real web-push sends** to real devices. Neither is inferred from `NODE_ENV` (unset under `pnpm dev`) or the DB URL (one project serves dev and prod by design); both log loudly on either branch.
  - **M20b revised from "defer as debt" to "genuinely required for a UPSC Mains magazine"** on evidence: **2,478 placements** would be inherited from UPPSC triage into a UPSC edition against 70 structurally blocked, because the shared `GS1..GS4`/`ESSAY` namespace collides while the syllabi do not (UPPSC has 6 Mains GS papers to UPSC's 4). Not urgent — 0 rows carry >1 `exam_code` today.
  - **Chapters: 8 of 195 authored, 7 published, 1 `needs_review`** — reported precisely because "activated seven pipelines" would otherwise imply content that does not exist. `pnpm notes:coverage` + `docs/upsc-chapter-coverage.md` are the committed, resumable checklist.

## UPSC study-chapter rollout continued (2026-08-07): rounds 23-30, 55 → 78 of 195, and the rollout's structural completion point
Direct continuation of U8's chapter work, run as a `/loop` across many iterations — same standing rules throughout: FREE coding-agent subagents only (never `pnpm notes:chapter`, the paid API path), `exams.upsc.is_live` never touched, no `notes` row ever deleted, every agent's self-report independently re-verified against the live DB/actual file content before being trusted or published (this rule was proven necessary twice this arc, below), commits staged by explicit path (never `git add -A`). Commits `6dc565c` (57) through `c786b50` (78); `docs/upsc-chapter-coverage.md` regenerated and `uppsc` re-confirmed unchanged at **284/284** after every single round. Full round-by-round detail lives in each commit's own message — this entry is the cross-round findings, patterns and incidents worth keeping.

  - **A real, live overlap defect on an ALREADY-PUBLISHED chapter, found and remediated (round 24, `ffdfb74`).** The brief for "Resources of South Asia and the Indian Sub-continent" checked overlap against "Indian and World Geography" and "Geography of India" (both Prelims) but never named the correct GS1 Mains sibling, "Natural Resources and Industrial Location" — which turned out to have an **identically-titled section** ("India's Physiographic Resource Base: the Deccan Trap, the Gondwana Paradox and the Coastline"). A post-publish 7-word n-gram check (`re.findall(r'\w+', text.lower())`, sliding-window tuples, exact method used throughout this arc) found **51 overlapping n-grams** concentrated entirely in that one section, closely paraphrasing the sibling's Gondwanaland-mineral-inheritance/mining-GDP-share passage. Remediated in two passes: a fix agent explicitly denied sight of the sibling's actual text rewrote the passage from independently-researched facts (51→11), then a direct one-sentence edit removed a residual template-convergent transitional phrase (11→8, all confirmed unavoidable "500,000 sq km Deccan Trap" geological measurement). Every new claim in the rewrite (Odisha's ~50%+ iron-ore/~73-76% bauxite/~98% chromite output shares; mining's 2-2.5% direct/10-11% downstream GDP contribution; MMDR Amendment Act 2023's six-mineral list; National Critical Mineral Mission's ₹34,300cr/1,200-project figures) was independently re-verified via live web search before being accepted, replacing two now-stale fact_audit entries; a genuine 2.5%-vs-5%-by-2030 GDP-target source disagreement was surfaced and honestly disclosed rather than presented as settled. **Lesson, applied for the rest of the arc: before dispatching a resource/geography/history-themed node, check ALL same-paper depth-1 siblings for a same-theme collision, not just the 1-2 most obviously titled ones — a title match this close can exist even when the parent node's own title looks unrelated.**
  - **A second, subtler content-restatement found the same round**, on "Significant Provisions, Amendments and Basic Structure" (the deliberate, explicit split from the already-published "Indian Constitution" — verified by quoting that parent's own reservation sentence, "the fuller line of judgments building on Kesavananda and Minerva Mills… belong to the dedicated chapter", directly to the authoring agent before dispatch). n-gram check found only 21 overlapping n-grams (well below violation range) but one 8-gram cluster in the Minerva Mills section read as genuine sentence-level template convergence with the parent's own "the precise answer to whether Parliament can…" framing — fixed directly (English + Hindi) by restructuring the passage around Section 55 instead, reducing residual overlap to 14, all confirmed unavoidable legal terminology/case names. **This established the calibration used for the rest of the arc: <~15 total n-grams, spread thin with no single cluster above ~8, is the acceptable range; a concentrated cluster of 8+ on one passage warrants inspection regardless of the total.**
  - **The redundancy-scan methodology, built round 25 (`b929727`) after the round-24 incident, became the arc's primary tool.** A short Node script queries every UPSC syllabus node + every published chapter's section headings, normalizes both (lowercased, hyphens→spaces, 4+-letter words only), and scores each unauthored node's title against its best-matching sibling section by word overlap. Rebuilt fresh before every round (`/tmp/redundancy_scan{N}.json`, always deleted after use — never committed, always re-derivable). **A real bug in the scan itself was found and fixed mid-arc**: the first version stripped hyphens WITHOUT inserting a space, so "Decision-Making Items" normalized to "decisionmaking items" — one word, not two — silently defeating the match for every hyphenated section heading (this is why "Decision Making"/"Problem Solving" scored a false 0% in one early scan despite being confirmed redundant by direct reading). Fixed by replacing hyphens with spaces before stripping punctuation. **The scan is a first-pass filter only, never a verdict** — every round's dispatch decisions were made after reading the actual published section text, not the score alone; the score under- and over-flags in both directions (a >=50% score can still be an unavoidable proper-noun collision worth ignoring; a 0% score can still hide a real thematic overlap the title-word heuristic can't see, as the South Asia Resources incident itself proved).
  - **A structural finding this arc made explicit and load-bearing for every subsequent round: comprehensive depth-1 Mains chapters absorb nearly all of their depth-2 children's real content.** Once a weighty depth-1 node is authored comprehensively (as every one in this rollout has been), its section titles very often turn out to be near-verbatim restatements of the OFFICIAL SYLLABUS's own sub-clauses — which are also the titles of separate, still-unauthored depth-2 nodes. Confirmed exhaustively, not asserted: by the end of round 30, a full sweep of all 117 then-remaining unauthored nodes across GS1-GS4, Prelims GS1 and CSAT found every single one matched an exact or near-exact section title already published — e.g. "Concept of Public Service and Philosophical Basis of Probity", "Dimensions of Ethics", "Ethics in Private and Public Relationships" are *literal* section headings already live in "Probity in Governance"/"Ethics and Human Interface"; "Salient Aspects of Indian Art Forms" is explicitly named as covered by "Indian Heritage and Culture"'s own text ("named in the syllabus's 'Salient Aspects of Indian Art Forms' line"); "Physical Geography"/"Social Geography" are the literal titles of two of "Indian and World Geography"'s nine sections. Prelims CSAT's three remaining nodes (Decision Making, Data Interpretation, Problem Solving) were confirmed redundant the same way early in the arc — their parent chapters already have dedicated sections carrying the identical names. **This is why 78/195 is reported as a genuine structural completion point, not an arbitrary stopping place — continuing would mean either duplicating an existing chapter or padding a thin, weakly-PYQ-grounded fragment, exactly what this rollout's own discipline exists to prevent.**
  - **A real data-integrity bug caught before it reached the DB (round 26, `d639a4d`), found only because a test dispatch's self-report was independently checked rather than trusted.** A batched dispatch hit a real session-limit error before writing any output; per the standing "test before assuming it blocks work" practice, one single test dispatch (Concise Writing) was sent to confirm the limit had actually cleared — it had, and succeeded, but set `machine_translated: false`, reasoning that hand-authored natural-register Hindi shouldn't count as "machine translated." This is a real misreading of the flag: `apps/api/src/notes/chapter-generate.ts`, the canonical real-API chapter pipeline, hardcodes `machine_translated: true` whenever Hindi is DERIVED by translating English (section authoring is English-only; a single batched translate pass fills Hindi) — the flag tracks derivation method, not translation quality; `false` is reserved for content genuinely composed independently in Hindi, not translated from the English at all (the narrower Session-28.5 letter-writing-format precedent). Fixed to `true` before publish; every subsequent round's briefs were updated with an explicit correction quoting the exact file/line reasoning to prevent recurrence, and a spot-check confirmed no earlier round had actually shipped the wrong value (General Mental Ability, checked directly, correctly carried `true`).
  - **The self-verification brief pattern, adopted from round 27 onward (`d0d27d3`) and used for the rest of the arc:** every dispatch brief requires the agent to itself query the DB for EVERY published sibling under the same `paper_code` (not just the 1-2 pre-identified by the orchestrator) before writing anything, and to run its own programmatic n-gram check before reporting done. This measurably worked — round 27's "Information Sharing, Transparency and RTI" agent found and correctly excluded a PYQ the brief hadn't flagged (already fully claimed by "Probity in Governance"'s own Synthesis section, independently re-confirmed against the live DB); round 26's "Nation-Building and Internal Challenges" agent self-declined after finding all 3 of its real PYQs already cited by exact id inside "Post-Independence Consolidation" (independently re-verified, all 3 confirmed exact matches); round 29's "Development Processes and the Development Industry" agent declined with a PYQ-by-PYQ table showing 4 of 5 already substantively answered elsewhere (3 of 4 spot-checked and confirmed exact pyq_id matches). **Every self-reported decline or exclusion this arc was independently re-checked against the live DB before being accepted — this is not a formality: it is the same discipline that caught the South Asia Resources and machine_translated bugs, applied symmetrically to "nothing to do here" claims, not just "here is a finished chapter" claims.**
  - **Two genuine content improvements found and closed mid-verification, not just overlap fixes (round 27, `d0d27d3`).** "Achievements of Indians in Science and Technology" scoped correctly against its "Science and Technology" sibling (policy/mission-level content) by building a people-and-history angle instead — but its own DB description named four domains (space, atomic energy, medicine, **computing**) and the agent's 2-PYQ-grounded draft had nothing on computing. Caught by running the scope gate (`notes:chapter:checkpoint --stage scope`) against the DB's own `description_i18n`, not just trusting the agent's section list; closed with a concise, independently-verified addition (C-DAC/PARAM 8000, 1991, Vijay Bhatkar — the classic technology-denial-breeds-indigenous-capability arc mirroring the chapter's nuclear and space stories), re-ran the scope gate to confirm PASS before publishing. The same chapter's fact-audit correction — the "God particle" nickname does NOT honour S.N. Bose, despite some Indian government science-outreach material implying it does; it traces to Leon Lederman's 1993 book title, softened by his publisher from "The Goddamn Particle" — was independently re-verified via fresh web search before being accepted as a genuine, valuable correction rather than an agent's own confusion.
  - **The scope gate's false-positive rate stayed high all arc, and the discipline for handling it never changed: grep the actual prose for synonymous coverage before treating a literal-term FAIL as a real defect.** Recorded so the pattern isn't relearned every round — a DB description's summary phrase almost never appears verbatim in well-written prose (e.g. "effects" absent but "impact"(14)/"consequence"(9) present; "colonial" absent but "Government of India Act, 1935" — the actual colonial statute — named 14 times; "answerability" absent but "accountab-" present 22 times, the chapter's own title theme). A genuine, expected exception this arc: a depth-1 PARENT deliberately did NOT restate "geophysical phenomena" (its own title's first half) because that half is the exclusive territory of its already-published depth-2 CHILD ("Earthquakes, Tsunami, Volcanic Activity and Cyclones") — a correct architectural split, not a gap, confirmed by design rather than treated as a false positive to explain away.
  - **The highest-risk node this arc successfully authored (round 29, `3c56c15`): "Geophysical Phenomena and Geographical Features", the depth-1 PARENT of the already-published disaster-event child.** Two prior dispatch attempts failed with a real, transient service-side stall ("no progress for 600s") before writing any output (confirmed via `git status` — no stray files either); a clean retry succeeded. The retry checked all four relevant siblings' full stored text against all 14 real PYQs before writing anything, and found 9 already fully claimed elsewhere (7 in the earthquake/cyclone child, 2 in the "Flora and Fauna Changes" child) — every one of the 9 exclusions was independently re-verified directly against the live DB and confirmed an exact pyq_id match. Built the chapter around the 5 genuinely uncovered PYQs (Himalayan glacier retreat, grounded in ICIMOD's real HKH Glacier Outlook 2026 released 21 March 2026 — independently re-verified via fresh search, confirmed accurate to the specific published figures; desertification; sea-level rise on island nations; why India is a subcontinent; physical geography's role in Ancient India). n-gram overlap against all four checked siblings, independently re-verified: **0/0/0/0**. The same round's discovery that two of the node's own already-existing depth-2 CHILDREN ("Geographical Features and their Location", "Changes in Critical Geographical Features") turned out to share **100% of their real PYQs** with what this parent chapter had just used — confirmed by direct id comparison — meant both children were struck from the worklist as pre-emptively redundant before ever being dispatched, saving a wasted round.
  - **A subtle near-collision correctly resolved by reading the actual PYQ stem text, not just titles or vibes (round 30, `c786b50`).** "Challenges of Corruption" was dispatched with 3 of its 6 real PYQs pre-excluded (already confirmed claimed by "Probity in Governance"); the agent flagged that its remaining "senior officer with road-project insider knowledge" case study superficially resembled "Case Studies and Problem-Solving Approach"'s own "Subash" road-project scenario. Independently re-verified by pulling BOTH PYQs' actual stem text directly from the `questions` table: the sibling's Subash case is a son wanting insider knowledge with a Minister's nephew as an interested tender bidder; this node's PYQ is a Minister pressuring for road realignment near his farmhouse plus land purchased in the officer's own wife's name — genuinely distinct scenarios despite the shared "road project" surface similarity, confirmed by the sibling's own pyq_ids not containing this PYQ's id. The same round's "Accountability, Ethical Governance and Strengthening Moral Values" was dispatched as an explicit strong-decline candidate (only 2 real PYQs, heavy title overlap with a crowded GS4 territory) but the agent proceeded after finding a genuine, real overlap risk — not in a section literally called "Synthesis" as the agent's own report loosely characterized it, but (independently located by pulling all of "Attitude, Integrity…"'s sections and grepping each for "accountab-") inside its "Public/Civil Service Values and Ethics in Public Administration" section, which carries a real ~150-word passage explicitly titled "Accountability and ethical governance; strengthening ethical values" (documented paper trail, independent audit/vigilance, the Citizen's Charter's 24 May 1997 adoption) — and wrote around it rather than declining or restating; independently re-verified by pulling that exact passage from the live DB and confirming the new chapter contains ZERO of its specific content (no "Citizen's Charter", no "24 May 1997", no "paper trail", no "independent audit"). **Both of these were nodes the orchestrator expected might reasonably decline; both turned out to have genuine additive ground once actually investigated — a reminder that "looks redundant from the title" and "is redundant" are different questions, answered only by reading the real sibling text.**
  - **Net this arc: 23 chapters published across rounds 23-30 (55→78), 2 nodes correctly declined within this arc's own direct verification (Nation-Building and Internal Challenges, round 26; Development Processes and the Development Industry, round 29) — plus Welfare Schemes, declined in round 22 just before this arc began (commit `686ac18`, referenced above for context, not independently re-verified in this entry) — 1 real content-overlap defect found on an already-published chapter and fully remediated, 1 real data-integrity bug (a mis-set provenance flag) caught before it reached the DB, 1 real content gap (a named-but-uncovered syllabus domain) found and closed, 1 genuine misconception correction independently verified (the God-particle/Bose case, round 27) alongside a recurring pattern of honestly-disclosed genuine uncertainties (a Ninth Schedule entry-count disagreement, a legal citation traceable to only one secondary source, a contested GDP-target figure) resolved by disclosure rather than false certainty, and a comprehensive final sweep confirming the rollout's remaining ~117 nodes are structurally exhausted.** `uppsc` regression (284/284 chapterable nodes) re-confirmed unchanged after every single round — zero UPPSC content was ever at risk. `exams.upsc.is_live` remained `false` throughout; every chapter published is real, fact-audited, independently-verified content with **$0 of app Anthropic API spend** (free coding-agent subagents throughout, per the standing instruction reserving the paid `notes:chapter` path for production/cron).

## Model-switch regression test on the UPSC chapters — RULED OUT, plus an independent coverage audit (2026-08-07)
Investigated whether the Claude Code default-model change degraded the later half of the UPSC chapter rollout, and re-derived the rollout's own "78 is the structural ceiling" claim from scratch. **Answer: no regression, and the premise that motivated the question was itself wrong.** Full numbers, methodology and every named finding in **`docs/upsc-chapter-panel-verdict.md`**. **Zero chapters modified, zero DB writes, $0 of Anthropic API spend** (all 8 agents were free coding-agent subagents; `pnpm notes:chapter`, the paid path, was never invoked). `exams.upsc.is_live` untouched.
  - **The trailer break is real, and the arms were derived mechanically.** All 11 chapter-publish commits ≤ `74bc3ea` carry `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5` (2026-08-02→08-05); all 19 from `3d03596` to `c786b50` carry none (2026-08-06). Arm membership came from **diffing `docs/upsc-chapter-coverage.md`'s published node_ids between consecutive commits**, not from commit prose — which is how the **LATE arm turned out to be 54 chapters, not the 52 the task assumed** (78 − 24, confirmed node-for-node, no chapter dropping out in between). **Caveat worth keeping: the trailer records the ORCHESTRATOR's model, not the free subagent that actually authored each chapter** — a subagent inherits the parent model when none is specified, so propagation is plausible but the trailer is circumstantial, not proof.
  - **⚑ THE FINDING THAT MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE NULL RESULT: both original panels ran on chapters that PREDATE the switch entirely, so it could never have explained their disagreement.** `docs/OUTSTANDING.md:478` records panel 2 as **`| 5 chapters | ... FAIL 8-1 overall, 8-0 on depth |`** — so **"8-1"/"8-0" are WIN TALLIES, not a sample size, and panel 2 was 5 chapters, not the 8 the task premise stated.** U8t records the chapter count at that time as **"8 of 195 authored, 7 published"**, all authored 2026-08-01/03; panel 1 took 5 of them and panel 2 took 5 of them, and the Aug-5→Aug-6 break is later than both. Three concrete methodological differences, not a real contradiction: (1) **incomparable metrics** — panel 1 reported a mean-score gap (4.91 vs 3.89, +1.02 against a 0.24 floor), panel 2 reported win tallies, and neither was ever converted into the other; (2) **a 25%-defective population** — U8m records that **2 of those 8 chapters were TRUNCATED** (History stopped at 1857 omitting the entire INM; Environmental Ecology had zero Climate Change), so a 5-draw very likely included one, which explains "8-0 on depth" far better than a model change; (3) **panel 1's own already-documented blinding artifact**, which dropped its grounding gap from +0.60 to +0.20, inside its own 0.27 floor. Panel 1's depth/structure findings survived its artifact; panel 2's depth failure is explained by the truncated chapters. **Read that way the two panels never disagreed — they measured different things, with different metrics, on a population that changed underneath them.**
  - **PART 1 — the panel says NO REGRESSION.** Same methodology as the originals so the results are comparable: 6 chapters per arm, each paired with a domain-matched published UPPSC control, exam names redacted (verified **0** `UPSC`/`UPPSC` occurrences across 24 files, ~92k words), deterministic `mulberry32` shuffle, **seed `20260807`**, 3 free subagent judges scoring depth/grounding/structure 1-5. **EARLY − LATE: depth +0.00, grounding +0.11, structure +0.06, overall +0.06 — against an inter-judge noise floor of 0.28/0.31/0.25/0.23.** Every difference is far inside noise and **depth, the dimension specifically suspected, is exactly zero**. Both arms beat their controls by margins that DO exceed the floor (EARLY +0.96, LATE +0.83 overall). **The judges do not even agree on the sign: J1 rated the suspected-degraded LATE arm HIGHER** (+1.00 vs +0.89), J2 and J3 the other way. Two corroborating signals run against the hypothesis: **factual-error flags are higher in EARLY (7 vs 4)**, and **the severe completeness defect is more common in EARLY (8.3% vs 5.6%)**.
  - **TWO ARTIFACTS, one of them mine.** (1) **The documented exam-format class recurred** — all three judges flagged the UPPSC control's claim about its own essay paper ("two essays from two of four sections"), and judges 1 and 3 "corrected" it using **UPSC's** format; the four themes it names match UPPSC's four `MAINS_ESSAY` depth-1 nodes exactly. Excluded from the grounding dimension per precedent, moving LATE's grounding gap +0.50 → **+0.27** (inside the 0.29 floor); **the EARLY−LATE comparison is unchanged, because the artifact sits in a control** — and note it penalised the CONTROL, i.e. **inflated** the UPSC arm, the same direction as the original CSAT-question-count artifact. (2) **An export artifact of my own making, and the sharper lesson**: all three judges independently reported "empty `[pyq_inline]` placeholder boxes" as a real content hole — **all 29 such boxes in the entire bank carry real `pyq_ids` (1-6 each)**; the box text is a lead-in and the reader UI renders the questions from those ids, but **my markdown export dropped the PYQ chips**. Three independent judges were confidently wrong about the same thing because the harness misrepresented the artefact. **Verify a panel's flags in BOTH directions — a unanimous flag is not a confirmed defect.**
  - **PART 2 — NOT TRIGGERED, and the no-op was PROVEN rather than asserted.** No chapter was read for rewriting, re-authored, superseded or modified; no `chapter_version` incremented. **Verification method: every script this session ran was scanned for Supabase write verbs — 12 `.select(` calls and ZERO `.insert(`/`.update(`/`.upsert(`/`.delete(`.** So `uppsc`'s 284 and `upsc`'s 78 are untouched *by construction*, not merely unchanged as far as a re-count could tell; `git status` shows no tracked file modified by the analysis. **One verification could not be completed and is disclosed rather than skipped:** a closing re-read of live chapter counts failed on a transient sandbox network failure (node `fetch` → `ENOTFOUND` while `nslookup` resolved the host fine, and `curl` to unrelated hosts also failed). Not load-bearing given the write-verb proof; `docs/upsc-chapter-coverage.md` (2026-08-06, 78/195) needed no regeneration because nothing changed.
  - **A REAL structural difference that is NOT a quality difference — recorded so it isn't mistaken for the regression it isn't.** Across all 78 chapters: **total length is identical (EARLY 3906 vs LATE 3902 English words)**, but LATE writes **fewer, longer sections** (6.0 vs 7.7 sections; 657 vs 512 words/section) with fewer study aids (boxes 9.2 vs 13.8, diagrams 1.7 vs 3.3, PYQ refs 9.4 vs 12.3, facts 22.1 vs 25.8). EARLY's minimum is 5 sections; **LATE has 7 chapters at ≤4**, thinnest being `Historical Underpinnings, Evolution and Features` at 2 sections/2 PYQ refs. Fewer aids is a legitimate product concern, but it is not depth — and depth measured exactly equal. **0 unresolved fact-audit flags in either arm** (the publish gate works).
  - **BONUS — completeness audit of all 78 against each node's OWN declared syllabus scope (the U8m class), never against section/word counts: 68 complete · 5 thin · 5 truncated.** TRUNCATED: **Political System** (EARLY — "Vice-President" appears once, in a heading; no Art. 63-66), **Economic and Social Development** (EARLY — no MGNREGA/PDS/PM-JAY/PM-KISAN anywhere), **Indian National Movement** (LATE — scope says "from the early nationalists"; 0 hits for 1885/Swadeshi/Tilak/Gokhale, jumps 1931→1947), **Historical Underpinnings, Evolution and Features** (LATE — Constituent Assembly reduced to a sub-clause), **Appointment to Constitutional Posts and Constitutional Bodies** (LATE — "powers and functions", the description's whole second half, absent). THIN: **History of the World** (colonization as a topic), **Natural Resources and Industrial Location** (tertiary-sector location factors), **Resources of South Asia** (soil resources), **General Mental Ability** (series/analogies/classification), **Sustainable Development** (the SDG framework itself). The two historically-defective U8m chapters both now pass. The auditor **corrected itself in both directions** — overturning two false positives (`Challenges of Corruption` teaches institutional response via the PC Act and CCS Conduct Rules despite zero Lokpal/CVC/CBI mentions: **literal-term absence ≠ component absence**) and upgrading one verdict from `thin` to `truncated`. Several `complete` verdicts rest on an **explicit** deferral to a named sibling; the five truncated ones differ precisely in that their gaps are **silent**.
  - **PART 3 — the "78 is the structural ceiling" claim is DIRECTIONALLY RIGHT BUT NOT EXACT, and must not be quoted as "everything is covered".** Four fresh auditors (none authored any chapter) walked **all 117 unauthored nodes** against the **actual published body text** of all 78 chapters — a `redundant` verdict required naming the covering chapter/section **and quoting a verbatim ≥2-sentence teaching passage**, with a matching heading explicitly ruled insufficient, and cross-paper coverage in scope. **101 redundant · 16 partial · 0 uncovered**, all 117 classified, **0 redundant verdicts resting on a weak quote** (every evidence quote ≥20 words, verified mechanically). So no node is *wholly* uncovered and stopping at 78 was defensible — but **16 nodes have real, named gaps confirmed by corpus-wide zero-hit searches the auditors re-ran themselves**, carrying **143 of the 786 unauthored PYQ weight (18%)**. The 16, by weight: **Data Interpretation** (23 — charts and graphs, 2 of 4 named forms, no worked item anywhere), **World Geography** (23 — continents, whole treatment is one sentence), **Rights Issues** (20 — human rights as a concept; NCSC/NCST/NCBC/Art. 338 all 0 hits), **Environmental Pollution and Degradation** (15 — soil pollution; municipal solid waste), **Statutory, Regulatory and Quasi-Judicial Bodies** (13 — the regulatory limb; TRAI 0 hits), **Structure and Functioning of the Executive and the Judiciary** (10 — ministries and departments; Art. 77 0 hits), **Salient Aspects of Indian Art Forms** (8 — folk traditions and theatre), **Representation of People's Act** (7 — qualification and election disputes), **Salient Aspects of Indian Architecture** (5 — modern, and Delhi Sultanate), **Poverty** (5 — alleviation programmes as an architecture), **Inclusion** (5 — regional balance), **Demographics** (4 — age structure and demographic dividend), **World Wars and the Redrawal of National Boundaries** (2 — the Second World War itself; 0 hits for Yalta/Potsdam/Hiroshima), **Colonization and Decolonization** (2 — the colonization half), **Indian Economy: Planning and Mobilization of Resources** (1 — savings), **Indigenization of Technology** (0 — technology transfer). Most are a missing *section* inside an already-published chapter, so the fix is the established richer-supersedes `chapter_version` move, not 16 new chapters; the top four by weight account for 81 of the 143.
  - **⚑ THE DEMOGRAPHICS HEADING-SCAN TRAP — keep this.** `Demographics` is "covered" by a parent chapter containing a section titled literally **"Demographics and Human Development"** whose body teaches only HDI and PLFS and never touches age structure or the demographic dividend. **A heading-based or index-based coverage scan scores that node as fully covered.** This is why the audit demanded verbatim body-text quotes, and it is exactly the failure mode **the rollout's own redundancy scan (title-word overlap against section headings) is vulnerable to** — the scan is a first-pass filter, never a verdict. A matching heading is not evidence of coverage.
  - **If chapter quality is ever re-tested, fix these three things first** (they are what made the original two panels look contradictory): report ONE metric consistently, exclude or stratify on chapters with known completeness defects, and render `pyq_inline` boxes' actual questions into the judged text.

## Remediating the panel verdict's 26 findings (2026-08-07): 6 chapters fixed, and a wrong premise caught by the agents
Acting on `docs/upsc-chapter-panel-verdict.md`'s three defect lists — 5 truncated chapters, 5 thin, 16 unauthored nodes with corpus-verified partial gaps. **Priority 1 (the truncated chapters, i.e. live shipped content) is complete except one, which is in flight.** All authoring by free coding-agent subagents or in the main loop; **$0 of app Anthropic spend** (`pnpm notes:chapter`, the paid path, was never invoked). `exams.upsc.is_live` untouched. uppsc verified at **284/284 with a byte-identical `study_content` fingerprint after every single publish** (`924eb8e7…`).
  - **⚑ THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST — THREE OF FIVE PRIORITY-1 BRIEFS WERE WRONG, AND THE AGENTS CAUGHT IT.** The audit asked *"does this chapter cover its own description?"* — a correct question that does **NOT** establish the content is missing from the **BANK**. Checking each gap against a dump of all 78 published chapters before writing: **Indian National Movement**'s parent teaches 1885-1946 in **17 sections** including Quit India; **Economic and Social Development**'s "missing" schemes (MGNREGA, PM-JAY, PM-KISAN, Ayushman, PDS, Jan Dhan, Aspirational Districts) are ALL taught elsewhere, several in a **Prelims sibling**; **Historical Underpinnings**' "missing" Constituent Assembly has a dedicated section in its own parent. Writing what those briefs asked would have manufactured the exact duplication defect the round exists to remove. **Corpus-verify every audit gap before authoring — the audit's verdict is a hypothesis about a chapter, not a measurement of the bank.**
  - **THE DEFERRAL RULE, from the verdict doc's own criterion: *"`complete` verdicts rest on an EXPLICIT deferral to a named sibling; the truncated ones' gaps are SILENT."* THE DEFECT IS OFTEN THE SILENCE, NOT THE ABSENCE.** INM was fixed by adding an orientation section that names the parent chapter and carries a chronological bridge table, plus only the four topics confirmed absent bank-wide (Eka Movement, the 1922 Great Trial, the Swaraj Party, the 7-August/Swadeshi link) — **n-gram overlap against that parent: ZERO**, which duplication could never have achieved. This is now the standing pattern for a child chapter whose parent is comprehensive.
  - **NEW TOOL, committed: `notes:chapter:checkpoint --stage dump`** (`60dede8`) — the missing half of superseding a published chapter. `persistChapter` upserts on `syllabus_node_id` and bumps `chapter_version`, so a richer version replaces a poorer one in place; but the assemble input is the WHOLE chapter, so a supersede needs the current sections back in editable form or an agent re-authors prose that was already correct. Every prior supersede in this rollout hand-rebuilt that read-back. It parses against the schema BEFORE writing (a dump that cannot re-assemble is worse than none — the failure would surface only after an agent had edited it) and is **verified lossless**: dumped sections/facts/sources are byte-identical to the persisted jsonb; a zod re-parse differs only in KEY ORDER, which jsonb does not preserve. **Its header also records the operational hazard: `assemble` resets status to `needs_review` AND deletes the note's embeddings, so a chapter is off the live site and out of retrieval between `assemble` and `publish`. Finish the cycle per chapter; never park a batch mid-way.**
  - **PUBLISHED (each: assemble → publish → embed → verify → scope, one commit, uppsc fingerprint re-checked):** Political System v2 `6ef9f18` (S1's heading promised "The President **and Vice-President**" and the body mentioned the office ZERO times; `Article 63`/`65` were ZERO **corpus-wide**, `Article 66` in one Mains chapter only) · Appointment to Constitutional Posts v3 `f5af1d6` (all 7 sections were appointment/removal; its description's whole second half, "**and the powers and functions of those Bodies**", was silently dropped) · Economic and Social Development v2 `23de638` (**four findings in one chapter** — the node's own gap plus Poverty, Inclusion and Demographics, three children with no chapter of their own) · Indian National Movement v2 `691dab0` · General Mental Ability v2 `2689646` (its S8 was literally titled "…Named in the Syllabus, Not Yet in This Bank" — the one case where substantially REWRITING a section was correct, since it existed only to say the topic was untaught) · History of the World v2 `778da34` (**three findings** — the `thin` verdict and the Colonization gap are the SAME finding).
  - **⚑ A UPPSC/UPSC IDENTICALLY-NAMED-NODE TRAP THAT CAUGHT ME.** I briefed the General Mental Ability agent that "this node's five real PYQs are NCERT-XI Psychology fill-in-the-blanks on memory/attribution" — quoting **Session 29's PRE_CSAT note above, which is about UPPSC's node**. UPSC's identically-named node has **11 PYQs, all classic aptitude puzzles** (calendars, direction/distance, clock angle, mirror images, cube painting, ball bounce, alphabet coding), zero psychology. The agent contradicted the brief, and was right. **Both exams have same-titled nodes across CSAT and GS; a note in one exam's session log does not transfer — re-measure against the node id.**
  - **RESOLVING A FLAGGED FACT MEANS SETTLING IT, NOT WAIVING THE GATE.** History of the World's authoring pass correctly flagged a 21m-vs-20m disagreement on the Treaty of Nanking indemnity and — the right call — kept the number out of the body prose. Rather than resolve to force a publish, the treaty text was fetched: Arts. IV/V/VI give 6m + 3m + 12m and **Art. VII states the total as twenty-one million** with an instalment schedule; the 20m figure is simply wrong. Both languages now state 21m with the article breakdown. Same discipline elsewhere: the **Lakdawala date is genuinely contested** (government listings 1989 = constitution, most writing 1993 = report), so the chapter states BOTH and says which is which rather than picking one; and the **Finance Commission's income-distance criterion is taught WITHOUT a numeric weight** deliberately, since weights change each Commission and quoting one would date the chapter.
  - **A NUMBER I COULD NOT REPRODUCE, RECORDED AS SUCH.** The GMA chapter claims fifteen "find the missing term" items across the 880 real CSAT questions, one in every year 2018-2025. Re-checking: denominators reproduce EXACTLY (924 rows, 880 `source='pyq'`) and the every-year claim is **independently confirmed**; but "fifteen" is not reproducible — a narrow classifier gives 4, a broad one 65. It rests on the authoring pass's own hand-tuned boundary, and `f16`'s evidence now carries both bracketing counts so a reviewer who redraws the line knows why their number differs.
  - **⚑ COST/THROUGHPUT LESSON — DO NOT DISPATCH 9 AGENTS AT ONCE.** Nine concurrent authoring agents exhausted the session budget with **literally nothing written** (verified by checking every output path, per the standing rule — not assumed). A second round of seven died the same way. The **only** salvage across both was a heading narrowed *before* the section it presupposed, which was **reverted rather than shipped** — half-finished work that looks finished is the defect this round exists to remove. Four chapters were then authored directly in the main loop instead. Batches of **3-4** survived; the reset cadence (~4h) is the real constraint.
  - **METHOD NOTES worth reusing.** A shared `SUPERSEDE_INSTRUCTIONS.md` plus a one-file corpus dump of all 78 chapters kept per-agent dispatches to a short pointer. The n-gram calibration held: **under ~15 total, thinly spread, no cluster above ~8**, with verbatim constitutional/legal text counting as unavoidable (Political System's 9 hits were all Constitution wording; Appointment's 1; Econ+Social, INM, GMA and History of the World effectively 0). **Short acronyms need `grep -w`** — a plain `grep -i TRAI` matches "cons**trai**nt" and "s**trai**t" and reported it present in 65 files when the true count is ZERO.
  - **REMAINING (briefs corpus-verified and written to a scratch file; concurrency conflicts noted — Natural Resources and Resources of South Asia must NEVER run together, that pair produced a real overlap defect in round 24):** Historical Underpinnings and Indian Heritage in flight; then Natural Resources, Resources of South Asia, Sustainable Development, Basic Numeracy/Data Interpretation, Indian and World Geography, Indian Polity and Governance (Rights Issues), Bio diversity/Environment (soil pollution + MSW), Indian Polity GS2 (three gaps: regulators, ministries, RPA), Indian Economy (savings) and Science and Technology (technology transfer).

### Edge-case audit of that remediation, same day: 30 dead citations found in my own work
Adversarial pass over the seven chapters I had just published — hunting for what I INTRODUCED, not re-running the happy path. One live user-visible defect, one over-strict guard of my own caught before it shipped, and a measurement technique that was wrong twice.
  - **⚑ THE LIVE DEFECT (mine, shipped hours earlier, now fixed + gated — commit `260f06c`).** `chapter-markdown.tsx` splits body text on the literal `/\[S\d+\]/` — **UPPERCASE ONLY** — then resolves with **strict** `sources.find((s) => s.id === id)`. So there are two silent failure modes and neither errors: a marker written `[s12]` is not recognised as a citation at all and renders as literal text; a marker `[S12]` against a source stored as `s12` fails the `===` and degrades to a **plain superscript with no link**, indistinguishable to a reader from a source deliberately grounded in our own bank. History of the World shipped with **30 uppercase markers against lowercase `s1..s49` ids — every citation in its two new sections was dead.** Economic and Social Development had the mirror-image latent version. Fixed by canonicalising to `S<n>` and repointing 69 fact `source_ref`s; **454/454 upsc markers now resolve.** `uppsc` was and remains clean (1195 markers over 50 chapters), and the pre-existing HotW sections carried no markers, so the defect was entirely introduced that night.
