  - **⚑ MY FIRST VERSION OF THE GATE WAS TOO STRICT, AND MEASURING IS WHAT CAUGHT IT.** It also rejected any source id not matching `S<n>`. Measured against the live bank before shipping: that would have **REFUSED 16 already-published chapters** on their next re-assemble (3 uppsc, 13 upsc) for two legitimate shapes — the deliberate `S11b`/`S7b`/`S2b` sub-source convention, and chapters with lowercase ids carrying **no marker at all**, which render perfectly. **Blocking a future supersede of a healthy chapter is a worse defect than the latent risk it guards.** Non-canonical ids are now a WARNING, emitted only when the chapter actually carries markers. Final gate refuses **0 of 284 uppsc and 0 of 78 upsc**, while still catching the exact bug that shipped (7/7 negative controls, including an unresolved marker in a body, in a box, and in **Hindi only**).
  - **⚑ A MEASUREMENT TECHNIQUE THAT WAS WRONG TWICE — do not reuse it naively.** A regex sweep for "risky mermaid labels" reported **99** hits, then **182** after a "correction". Both were false-positive floods: (1) a label containing a comma is perfectly safe **when quoted**, which is exactly how the shipped diagrams do it; and (2) the naive pattern `[\[(]([^\]\)]*)[\])]` **truncates at the first `)`**, so `"Response (search, rescue, relief)"` gets cut mid-label and the fragment looks unquoted. Strip quoted spans FIRST, then test. Correctly scoped to the sections actually authored: only **3 of 16** carry a diagram, and all three are clean (`graph TD`, every comma-bearing label quoted, both languages, captions present).
  - **⚑ A FOURTH SCOPE-STAGE FAILURE MODE, found by publishing Indian Heritage — and this one was in THE TOOL.** `--stage scope` reported Yakshagana / Nautanki / Tamasha as METADATA-ONLY, the truncation signature, on a chapter that teaches all three in a `Form | Region | What distinguishes it` markdown table (Hindi variant carrying the Devanagari names). PROSE was defined as headings + `body_md_i18n` only, so **`boxes[].content_i18n` and a `table`-kind diagram's `source_i18n` were both invisible**. That is the DANGEROUS direction of error — a false "this topic is missing" invites the next person to fix a chapter by DUPLICATING content it already has, i.e. the exact overlap defect this pipeline keeps repairing — and the blast radius was bank-wide: **all 362 published chapters have boxes and 151 use a table diagram, so every scope run in this rollout was under-reading.** PROSE now means everything the reader reads; a `mermaid` diagram is deliberately NOT prose (a node label summarises something taught elsewhere) and gets its own `DIAGRAM-ONLY` verdict so the operator judges. Negative-controlled: nonexistent terms still come back ABSENT with SCOPE: FAIL, and every chapter scoped earlier still PASSES.
  - **Four flagged facts across the session were SETTLED, never waived** — the gate fired four times and held each time: the Treaty of Nanking indemnity (fetched the treaty; Art. VII states twenty-one million, so the 21m-vs-20m hedge became a sourced figure), s.93's High Court exception (primary text genuinely unreachable, so the CLAIM was narrowed to what is established and the body's "reported" hedge left in place), the Lakdawala date (contested, so the chapter states both years and says which is which), and the Lodi double dome (corroborated well beyond the single source the agent had, and the chapter's own wording is weaker than the sources support). **The tool's own rule — "there is deliberately NO resolve-all; judge each flag, then name it" — is what forced each of these to be a real investigation rather than a rubber stamp.**
  - **Clean on everything else** across the 7 chapters, checked rather than assumed: no duplicate section/source/fact ids; `toc` mirrors `sections` in order; no empty body or heading in either language; no ragged markdown-table column counts in either language; every fact's `section_id` and `source_ref` resolves; the digest (Quick Revision layer) preserved bilingual. **`A11` (the verdict doc's "12 dangling `pyq_ids` in 8 published chapters") now measures 0 dangling of 739 unique referenced ids** — most likely because `assembleChapter`'s warn-and-drop removed dead ids during the re-publishes, a side effect I did not verify at the time, so treat A11 as *re-measured clean* rather than *deliberately closed*.

## Session UA3 (2026-08-07): redundant-node coverage links — findability, not content
`docs/upsc-chapter-panel-verdict.md`'s Part 3 confirmed all 117 of `upsc`'s currently-unchaptered nodes are `redundant` (101) or `partial` (16) — their content already lives inside a sibling's published chapter, with zero wholly uncovered. But `notes-view.tsx` gated purely on `hasChapter(...)` with no fallback, so a student navigating directly to one of the 117 hit a bare "check back soon" dead end despite the content existing one click away. This closes that gap — a link, not new content.
  - **Source of the mapping: the still-live scratch RESULT_{A,B,C,D}.json + PUBLISHED_INDEX.json** the panel session left in `/tmp/coverage_audit/` (its own doc calls these "not committed" but they'd survived). Each of the 117 audit entries names its primary `covering_files[0]`; `PUBLISHED_INDEX.json` maps every published chapter's filename to its real `node_id`/`paper_code`. Joined mechanically (Python), not re-derived by re-reading 78 chapters — **117/117 resolved cleanly, 0 unresolved, 0 duplicate node_ids, 0 self-reference risk** (the "no chapter" set and the "has chapter" set are disjoint by construction).
  - **New column, not a new table: `syllabus_nodes.covered_by_node_id`** (migration `0115`, `references syllabus_nodes(id) on delete set null`). A one-time backfill from a specific reviewed audit, not a computed/recurring value — the migration's own comment says so and tells a future chapter-rollout round to NULL it out for a node once it gets its own chapter. Every backfill row is asserted at apply time: both ids must exist, both must be `exam_code='upsc'` (this migration is upsc-only — `uppsc`'s rollout is chaptered 1:1 and must never get a pointer), no node points at itself, exactly 117 rows land, and every pointer resolves to a node with an actual published chapter (`notes.status='published' and chapter_version>=1`). Applied via `db push --db-url` over the direct IPv6 host (no pooler fallback needed this time); live-verified post-apply: 117 pointed, 0 non-upsc, and — the thing the task asked to confirm — **0 of 294 `uppsc` rows carry the column**.
  - **API**: `SyllabusNodeDetail` (`packages/shared/src/learn.ts`) gained `covered_by: {node_id, paper_code, title_i18n} | null`. `getNodeDetail` fetches the covering node's title/paper_code in the same `Promise.all` batch as everything else (conditional on `node.covered_by_node_id` being set — most nodes have none, so this is a no-op query for the common case). Reused `getNodeDetail`'s existing "public reference" pattern (§0a — no exam-match gate on an untrusted node id) rather than adding a new one.
  - **Web**: `learn-node.tsx` (which already fetches `SyllabusNodeDetail` via `useSyllabusNode`) passes `node.covered_by` into `NotesView` as a new optional prop. `NotesView`'s empty-state branch now distinguishes a genuine fetch **error** (unchanged bare empty state) from a legitimate "no note" response that IS `coveredBy` (new state: `EmptyState` with the existing `action` slot — a real UPPSC-and-UPSC-established `Link` to `/${locale}/learn/${coveredBy.paper_code}/${coveredBy.node_id}`, i.e. cross-paper-aware, matching 6 of the 117 real cross-paper mappings). New bilingual copy (`Notes.coveredByTitle/Description/Action`, en+hi) rather than reusing the "no notes yet" strings, since the two states mean different things to a student.
  - **Verified live, not just typechecked**: a throwaway Supabase auth user (admin-API-created, this session's own onboarding + welcome-gate flow driven for real through `/profile/onboarding` and `PATCH /tour {welcome_seen:true}` rather than skipped) navigated a real signed-in session (seeded into `localStorage`'s `sb-<ref>-auth-token` — supabase-js v2's actual storage shape is the raw `Session` object, NOT the older `{currentSession,...}` wrapper my first attempt used and which silently bounced to `/auth`) to a real one-of-117 node's page. Confirmed via Playwright screenshot at 1440px/en and 390px/hi: title, description naming the real chapter ("Indian Society"), the action button, correct Devanagari at 390px with **no horizontal overflow**, **zero console errors** either viewport. Clicked the link — landed on the real covering node's URL, which rendered its actual published chapter (section list, PYQ count, "Study" tab) confirming the link isn't just plausible-looking, it's live content. `exams.upsc.is_live` was never touched (`getNodeDetail` needs no exam-match, so the throwaway user's default `target_exam='uppsc'` was sufficient — no production-DB `is_live` flip was needed or attempted, unlike prior UPSC verification sessions). Throwaway user + its `users_profile` row deleted by exact captured id afterward (per this repo's own documented gotcha that the profile doesn't cascade-delete) — 0 leftovers confirmed.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; `apps/web` `tsc -b` clean; **full `pnpm --filter web build`** clean (shared module touched, hard rule); `oxlint` **41 warnings — unchanged from the last recorded baseline**, exit 0; `pnpm check:paths` clean (802 tracked files). All scratch scripts and screenshots deleted; dev servers stopped.

### Edge-case audit, same day (2026-08-08): a real stale-pointer gap in `getNodeDetail`, found and fixed
Asked to "check all edge cases" over `2d777a3`. Hunted for what the commit introduced or newly exposed, not the happy path already screenshotted.
  - **REAL GAP FOUND: `getNodeDetail`'s `covered_by` field trusted the one-time migration `0115` backfill blindly, with no live check.** `covered_by_node_id` is a snapshot from a specific reviewed audit, not a recomputed invariant — nothing clears it if the covering chapter is later unpublished/reverted to draft/deleted-and-recreated, and nothing clears it if the SOURCE node itself later grows its own chapter (the migration's own comment explicitly asks a future rollout to remember to NULL it out, with no enforcement). Two real drift scenarios follow: (1) a regressed covering chapter → the API keeps serving a "Go to that chapter" link that dead-ends on ANOTHER empty state; (2) a source node that grows its own chapter → the API keeps returning stale `covered_by` data forever (harmless today only because `NotesView`'s one real call site happens to ignore `covered_by` whenever its own `note` is non-null — a future second consumer of this field would have no such protection). **Fixed**: `getNodeDetail` now checks BOTH conditions live, in one additional lightweight query gated behind the (usually null) `covered_by_node_id` — `.from("notes").select(...).in("syllabus_node_id", [node.id, node.covered_by_node_id])`, reusing the exact defensive to-one-embed-as-object-or-array pattern already established by `getPaperSummaries` in the same file. If the source node already has a live published chapter, `covered_by` is now suppressed (never redirect away from real content); if the covering node's chapter isn't currently live, `covered_by` is suppressed too (never send a student to a broken link).
  - **Verified the fix's own logic against real DB rows, not just typecheck** — both branches simulated with genuine node ids (no writes): the "source already has its own chapter" self-heal, confirmed via two real chaptered nodes returning 2 rows and the code correctly nulling `covered_by`; the normal case, re-confirmed via the object-shaped (not array) embed PostgREST actually returns. Then re-verified end-to-end for real: 15/117 pointers spot-checked through the live (fixed) API with zero mismatches; a `uppsc` node re-confirmed to short-circuit the whole branch (`covered_by: null`, no extra query); and — not yet browser-tested in the original commit — a genuine **cross-paper** case (`UPSC_MAINS_GS1` node linking to a `UPSC_PRE_GS1` chapter) driven through a real signed-in browser session end to end: empty state renders with the correct (different-paper) chapter title, click-through lands on the real URL, and the landed page's own breadcrumb correctly reads "Prelims — General Studies Paper I" — confirming the paper-code-aware link construction works across papers, not just within one.
  - **Confirmed NOT bugs, checked rather than assumed**: `NotesView` has exactly one real call site (a second file-content hit was a comment, not a second render path needing the new prop); no `select("*")` anywhere in the API touches `syllabus_nodes`, so the new column can't silently leak into an unrelated response shape; zero application write path anywhere touches `covered_by_node_id` outside the migration itself (repo-wide grep), so the "no ongoing same-exam / no-cycle guard" risk is purely theoretical — and even reasoned through, a hypothetical self-reference degrades safely to `covered_by: null` either way, no special-casing needed; none of the 33 real covering chapter titles contain a quote character, so the `"…"`-wrapped description copy can't break; `UserNotesGroup` (rendered above `NotesView` in the same card) is fully self-contained and renders nothing when the user has no personal notes — no interaction risk; no stale-data flash on node-to-node navigation (`useSyllabusNode`/`useNoteForNode` are both correctly keyed by `nodeId`, neither sets `keepPreviousData`/`placeholderData`, and no global `QueryClient` default does either); migration `0115` itself has no assertion guarding against backfilling onto a node that already had its own chapter — a real gap in the migration's OWN self-verification, manually confirmed absent at actual apply time, and now fully compensated for at a better layer (the new read-time self-heal, which protects against this regardless of cause or timing) rather than requiring the already-applied migration to be edited; no migration-numbering collision (`0115` used exactly once).
  - **Concurrent process, mid-audit**: another session was actively mid-edit on `ca/exam-fanout.ts` (a separate CA per-exam-curation change), which left that file in a syntactically broken state — confirmed via `git diff --stat` that `syllabus.ts` (this fix's only touched file) has zero overlap, and an isolated post-fix typecheck confirms `syllabus.ts` itself has zero errors while every error in the combined `tsc` run resolves to their in-flight files. Staged by explicit path, never `git add -A`.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean (isolated from the concurrent breakage); `apps/web` `tsc -b` clean; full `pnpm --filter web build` clean; `oxlint` **41 warnings, exit 0 — unchanged**; `pnpm check:paths` clean (803 tracked files). Second throwaway user + its `users_profile` row deleted by exact captured id afterward — 0 leftovers. Dev servers stopped, all scratch scripts and screenshots removed.

## Generation-quality audit across all six generating surfaces (2026-08-08)
Asked to diagnose qgen's twice-failed quality panel and, more importantly, to establish for **every** generating surface whether it has EVER been panel-gated — and to gate the ones that had not. **Four surfaces had never been gated; all four were gated this session** with 12 free coding-agent subagent judges (3 per panel, blind, seeded shuffle, key sealed outside the judges' directory), **$0 of app Anthropic spend for judging**; the only paid calls were three small controlled before/after harnesses (~24 calls, all synthetic rows deleted by exact purpose tag). `exams.upsc.is_live` untouched. Commits `aeb2d2c`, `5948253`, `ad862c8` — one per surface, per this repo's convention.

| # | Surface | Prior gate status | This session's result | Root cause | Fix applied | New result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **qgen MCQ** (`qgen_*`, kind=mcq) | ✅ Gated twice (`ac150dc`), **NO-GO** both — **but CSAT-only** | Not re-panelled. Measured instead: the format cause `ac150dc` diagnosed **is fixed** — `statement_counting` went **20.7% → 0%** of generated CSAT across the fix boundary | Format-concentrated (GS statement-set norm bleeding into an aptitude paper). **Already fixed.** Residual is node-concentrated (U8o) | none needed this session | Format cause closed; residual gap still open |
| 2 | **Mains descriptive** (`qgen_*`, kind=descriptive) | ❌ **NEVER** — the prior panel judged "generated UPSC **CSAT MCQs**"; 28 descriptive rows had no gate at all | ❌ **NO-GO.** Authenticity **2.50 vs 4.75** real UPSC = **2.25 gap = 8.7× the 0.26 noise floor**; power control passed (12×); all 3 judges agreed in direction | **Targeting**: 21 of 28 rows aimed at nodes that are *marking criteria*, not topics, so the coverage floor and the critic's in-syllabus check are both vacuous | `qgen.skillDimensionPapers` + `topup.ts` skips such papers and **logs** it (`aeb2d2c`) | ✅ Verified via the read-only planner: `UPSC_MAINS_ESSAY` now excluded (29 nodes across GS1-4); **uppsc byte-identical at 54 nodes/8 papers**. ⚑ **Necessary, NOT sufficient** — see below |
| 3 | **CA enrichment** (`ca_enrich`) | ❌ **NEVER** — the U8 CA gate was on **`ca_triage`**, a different call | ❌ **FAIL.** Faithfulness **3.04/5** (noise 0.36); **8/15 items carry a claim their own summary does not support**; 8/15 ≤2.5 on exam-worthiness | The **only** instruction relating output to source is a *copyright* rule ("write in your own words"). Nothing requires a claim to be **supported**, while the prompt demands "crisp who/what/when/how-much facts anchored on a NAMED entity" | GROUNDING block: no unstated specific, keep attribution, don't sharpen a general statement (`5948253`) | ⚠️ **Unvalidated** — controlled test showed OLD 2 unsupported specifics / 6 runs → NEW 0, but n=2 events is directional only |
| 4 | **Magazine curation** | n/a | ⚪ **Nothing generative to gate** — `services/magazine.ts` and the in-flight `services/magazine-curation.ts` make **zero LLM calls**; curation is deterministic weightage ranking | — | none | ⚑ But `magazine_deepdive` (`ca/deepdive.ts`) **is** a live generating surface — 4 deep dives, 5 calls ever — and **has never been gated**. Not gated here either |
| 5 | **Model answer** (`answer_eval_model`) | ❌ **NEVER** — `eval:answers` gates the SCORE's ranking/repeatability and merely *counts* `modelAnswerChars` | ⚠️ **MIXED.** Structure 4.79 / coverage 4.85 ≈ ceiling, but **accuracy 4.40** and **2/16 answers ≤3.5**; 7/16 exceed their word limit | (a) prompt demands substantiation with no rule for uncertainty; (b) **no verification stage exists at all** — one `streamText`, streamed live AND cached for replay | `FACTUAL_PRECISION` on all 3 answer shapes (`ad862c8`) | ⚠️ **Unvalidated** (targeted re-run reproduced the error in *neither* arm). **(b) NOT fixed — the larger half** |
| 6 | **Mentor** (`mentor_doubt`) | ❌ **NEVER** — `test:mentor` gates *pure text heuristics*; `0113` fixed retrieval, not quality | ⚠️ **MIXED.** Accuracy **4.09** (noise 0.27), answers-the-question 4.20, usefulness 3.98; **3/15 ≤3.5**; **5/15 uncited by ≥2 judges** | Not diagnosed to a single cause — see the four findings below | none this session | ❌ **Not fixed** — diagnosed and recorded only |

  - **⚑ THE HEADLINE CORRECTION TO THE BRIEF, measured against the DB before any work started.** The task described "the 72 needs_review generated questions" as the qgen MCQ population and pointed at the statement-combination family (55.3% of real Prelims **GS-I**). Both are wrong: the 72 rows are **44 MCQ + 28 descriptive**, **all 44 MCQs are `UPSC_PRE_CSAT`**, and **zero GS MCQs have ever been generated** — so the hypothesised format fix aimed at a paper with no generated questions at all. The 28 descriptive rows are 21 Essay + 7 GS4 and were an *ungated* surface, not one inheriting MCQ's verdict.
  - **The categorisation the brief asked for, answered per half rather than once.** MCQ: **format-concentrated, and already fixed** — measured across the `ac150dc` boundary, `statement_counting` fell 20.7% → 0% while a passage-based item appeared, exactly as that commit intended. Descriptive: **targeting**, not style — but the panel then showed the targeting fix cannot be the whole story, because **the gap is not concentrated in the Essay paper at all**: generated scored **2.50 on both** papers against **4.63** real Essay and **5.00** real GS4, so **GS4 — whose nodes ARE genuine topics — fails just as hard**. There is a second, evenly-spread descriptive quality problem that none of this session's commits touch.
  - **Two judges found the targeting defect's fingerprint without being told the cause**, which is the strongest single piece of evidence here: both independently reported near-duplicate topic clusters in the blinded set — four essays on brevity/verbosity, four on attitude theory — "a degree of motif repetition no single real paper would show". That is exactly what generating 8 topics against a node named *Concise Writing* produces.
  - **⚑ A REAL BLINDING ARTIFACT IN MY OWN HARNESS, disclosed rather than buried.** All three Panel-A judges said they used **marks/word-limit metadata** as a tell, and they were right to: only `125m/1200w` is shared between the generated and real-target arms (generated also used 125m/**1000w**, 10m/150w, 15m/250w; real used 20m/250w, 20m/150w). Restricted to the one shared slot the gap is **2.03 = 7.8× noise** — lower, still decisive. The arm most inflated by the artifact is the **power control** (UPPSC's 50m/700w and 8m/125w slots are unmistakable), so "the instrument works, 12×" is weaker than it reads. **Design lesson for the next panel: normalise or withhold metadata that differs by arm.**
  - **⚑ THE DEEPEST FINDING, and it is a data-retention gap, not a prompt gap: CA enrichment's evidence is discarded at write time.** `current_affairs_items` has **no raw-snippet column** — `enrichParams` reads `row.payload.snippet` off the in-flight triage-batch row, and `summary_i18n` is the model's **own** paraphrase. Two consequences, both live: **no reviewer can ever check a shipped fact against its source**, and **no before/after test of any enrichment prompt change can reproduce the original conditions.** My first A/B did exactly that wrong — fed `summary_i18n` back in as the source and reproduced **0 of 16** flagged inventions in **either** arm. That is an invalid harness, not a passing result, and it also means Panel D's own reference was the derived summary: the honest reading of FAI 3.04 is *"the model's facts exceed or contradict its own summary"*, plus several claims false regardless of source (a fabricated "Indian Zoo Association"; corruption placed under the IPC rather than the PC Act, 1988; 1,200 MW for a ~500 MW project). **Fidelity to the original news text is unmeasured and stays unmeasurable until the snippet is retained.**
  - **⚑ THE ASYMMETRY WORTH FIXING NEXT: every sibling generator has a verification stage except the one labelled to the student as the gold standard.** qgen has a critic **and** a blind verify; CA MCQs have `ca_mcq_verify`; chapters have a fact audit with a publish gate. `answer_eval_model` is a single `streamText` that streams straight to the candidate **and is cached into `question_model_answers` for replay to every future student on that question** — so a wrong specific is authoritative *and* durable. That is why the ~12% error rate matters more here than the same rate would elsewhere, and why the prompt tweak shipped in `ad862c8` is explicitly the smaller half.
  - **Mentor (surface 6) — four findings, recorded not fixed.** (1) **A hallucination wearing a citation**: all three judges flagged an invented "Battle of Sarnul (1544), Humayun vs Maldev" (the real 1544 Marwar battle was Sher Shah Suri's), and one judge found the tell — *the reply's own cited source code contains Samugarh, not Sarnul*, i.e. the model substituted a fact while citing a source that says otherwise. (2) **A mnemonic that teaches the error it warns about**: "SHWJH-GAS" expands to 7 of the 8 NAPCC missions, dropping Enhanced Energy Efficiency, inside a paragraph warning about the "which is NOT a mission" trap. (3) **Stale constitutional fact**: Lok Sabha maximum given as 552 (550 since the 104th Amendment, 2020). (4) **Cross-answer contradiction from one corpus**: two replies disagree on the highest-weightage Prelims area (50 vs 57 questions over the identical 2022-24 window) and on the exam's own Mains structure. Also: **5/15 replies assert case names, article numbers and vote splits with zero citation markers** — the single most accurate reply in the set was among them.
  - **A methodological result that matters more than either unvalidated fix: two consecutive controlled A/Bs were UNINFORMATIVE, for different reasons** — the CA one because the source input is not retained, the model-answer one because the error needs the real pipeline's answer + grounding + pass-1 context. **These are low-base-rate defects that do not reproduce on demand at small n.** Any future gate for these surfaces must either run the real pipeline end-to-end on a sample large enough to see a ~12% rate, or skip prompt-tuning entirely and add the verification stage, which does not depend on reproducing the error. Both fixes were shipped on the strength of the defect being independently evidenced by three judges on real shipped data, plus each change being purely restrictive — **not** on the strength of their A/Bs, and each commit says so.
  - **Harness artifact affecting Panels B/C/D, disclosed:** I redacted exam/state names uniformly across all four packs, but B/C/D are ABSOLUTE panels with no arm to hide, so the redaction added noise for nothing — judges saw "the State" mid-sentence and correctly identified it as an anonymisation artifact rather than an authoring fault (each said so, and said they did not penalise for it). **Redact only where an arm can leak.**
  - **⚑ MY COMMIT `5428376` IS MISLABELLED — it also carries a concurrent session's 15-file M20b work** (migration `0116`, `ca/curation-scope.ts`, the CA/magazine per-exam changes, and that session's own CLAUDE.md + OUTSTANDING rows). That session recorded it from its side in `1b16268`. **Staging by explicit path did NOT prevent it, and that is the durable lesson: `git commit` commits the INDEX, not the paths you named** — the other session ran its own `git add` inside the window between my `git add` and my `git commit`. `git status` before staging is too early to catch it. **The mitigation is `git commit -- <paths>`, which commits only the named paths regardless of index state, or checking `git diff --cached --name-only` immediately before committing.** Not split: nothing is lost or pushed, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` genuinely interleaves both sessions' rows, and rewriting history over a running session is the riskier option. **I then made it worse before making it better** — an amend intended for `5428376` landed on that session's errata commit because it had committed between my message-read and my amend; restored verbatim, message and tree both verified byte-identical.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` exit 0 after every commit; `prompts:snapshot` **163 keys**, with exactly the intended keys changed per commit and **0 others** — in particular `ca/triageParams` is untouched, preserving the A/B-validated item-text-first ordering the 2026-07-22 caching investigation established as load-bearing; `check:paths` (803 files) and `check:cli-args` (235 files) clean. **Concurrent process:** another session was mid-flight on CA per-exam curation (`magazine-curation.ts`, `ca/curation-scope.ts`, migration `0116`, 6 web files) throughout — confirmed disjoint (`exam-config.ts`'s diff is **purely additive, 0 deletions**) and every file staged by explicit path, never `git add -A`. All 30 synthetic `llm_calls` rows deleted by exact purpose tag (`ca_enrich_ab`, `ca_enrich_grounding_ab`, `eval_model_precision_ab`), 0 remain; all probe scripts deleted.

## CA per-exam curation — M20b shipped (2026-08-08): migration `0116`, and a UPSC Mains edition built end to end
Closed the last piece of M8/M20: `current_affairs_items`' two UPPSC-shaped curation columns are now per-exam, so a second exam's Mains magazine can no longer inherit UPPSC's paper placements. `uppsc` remains the only `is_live` exam and **`exams.is_live` was not touched**. Actionable index: `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8b M8 / §8c M20b (+ new M20c, M20d).
  - **THE DEFECT, AND WHY IT IS SILENT.** `current_affairs_items` is deliberately ONE row across several `exam_codes` (0106 §11 — a national story maps into several trees so it is not duplicated and its two most expensive LLM calls are not re-paid). But `gs_papers text[]` and `is_up_specific boolean` are a single commission's VERDICT, and `mergeExamTriages` folded N of them onto that row by UNION and OR. Lossless at N=1, lossy at N>1 — and wrong, because **`GS1..GS4`/`ESSAY` is a SHARED NAMESPACE over syllabi that differ**: UPPSC sets six Mains GS papers, UPSC four; UPPSC GS2 carries UP-specific governance, UPSC GS1 carries World History. So "UPPSC says GS2" would render under UPSC's GS2, a different paper. `gsPapersFor` (M20a) constrains which HEADINGS render, never which items land under them.
  - **⚑ THE PREMISE WAS STALE, AND RE-MEASURING IS WHAT SIZED THE FIX.** M20b recorded 2,478 inheritable placements. Re-measured on the live corpus at implementation time: **3,224 placements over 2,244 published mains-life items, only 101 state-labelled ⇒ 3,123 shared-namespace**. Also corrected: the brief's item 3 said the five `current_affairs_items` reads in `services/magazine.ts` were unscoped — **all five already carried `.overlaps("exam_codes")`**. That filter decides WHICH ROWS a reader sees; it does nothing about which PAPER a shared row's verdict places it under. The real work of item 3 was making those reads resolve per-exam.
  - **⚑ AND THE PROMPT RESHAPE M8 CALLED FOR TURNED OUT TO BE UNNECESSARY.** M8 said to do this "in the same change as the triage prompt + JSON schema reshape". Not needed: triage already runs **per exam** (`ca/exam-fanout.ts`'s fan-out), each call with its own `gsPapersFor(examCode)` schema enum and its own `curationDirective` — so each verdict was already correctly exam-scoped **at generation time**. The loss was entirely at the MERGE. `ca/prompts.ts` is byte-untouched and `prompts:snapshot` is **163 byte-identical with ZERO new keys** (verified with a concurrent session's in-flight `prompts.ts` edit temporarily reverted, so the result isolates this change).
  - **MIGRATION `0116` — additive, nullable, ZERO backfill.** `gs_papers_by_exam jsonb` + `state_focus text[]`. No `UPDATE`, no `ALTER` of an existing column, no index change, so **not one existing row is touched or reshaped**; the file asserts exactly that (`raise exception` if any row is non-null) rather than claiming it. **NULL IS LOAD-BEARING on both columns** — it means "pre-0116, use the legacy fallback", and a `not null default '{}'` would have been indistinguishable from a genuine "no paper / no state focus" and silently blanked all 4,459 rows' curation. Legacy columns are NOT dropped or deprecated; they stay written and stay the fallback.
  - **THE ATTRIBUTION RULE IS THE FIX.** A pre-0116 row's legacy value is attributed to `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` **and to no other exam** — so a widened row simply does not appear in a second exam's Mains sections until that exam's own triage runs (an honest absence, not a wrong placement). Two things justify pinning it to the default exam rather than the row's own `exam_codes[0]`: **measured** — all 4,459 rows are `{uppsc}` and 0 carry >1 code, so the rules agree on every row that exists; and **SQL-expressible** — the feed's `lens=up` tab paginates in the database, so a post-hoc JS filter would return short pages, and `stateFocusFilterFor` can express "state_focus contains my code OR (null AND is_up_specific)" but cannot express "…AND exam_codes[1] = me". A rule the read path can only half-enforce is worse than a stricter rule both paths enforce identically. `state_focus` is an ARRAY, not a scalar code, because it generalises a boolean `mergeExamTriages` already ORs — a scalar would force that merge to pick one winner, reintroducing the same lossy collapse one level down (a UP-MP river dispute is honestly `{UP,MP}`).
  - **WRITES ARE COMPILE-ENFORCED.** `withExamScope`'s enforced set grew from 2 columns to 4, for the same reason it was created after the published/draft insert was found silently taking the `exam_codes` default: a path that wrote `gs_papers` but forgot the companions would leave per-exam curation NULL, i.e. silently re-create the inheritance, on a row that looks perfectly well-formed. All four write paths (pipeline insert ×2, backfill update ×2) inherit it. `ca:widen-exam` adds both to `ForbiddenWidenColumn` — widening does not re-triage, so leaving the new exam ABSENT from the map is correct.
  - **`applyStateBoost` DELETED, not threaded.** `magazine-curation.ts` took the raw flag AND a separate lens gate; resolution subsumes the gate (`hasStateFocusForExam` returns false for a national exam before consulting either column), and one resolved input cannot be got wrong the way two independent ones could. It also does what the gate structurally could not: distinguish TWO state exams, so a UP story earns no boost for an MP aspirant.
  - **A LATENT LEAK CLOSED IN PASSING:** the CA feed card's "UP-specific" chip (`item-card.tsx`, `item-detail-sheet.tsx`) rendered **ungated** off the raw column — a national-exam reader would have seen another commission's state verdict on any widened row. Both `CurrentAffairsItem` and `MagazineIssueBrief` now carry `state_focus: boolean` **resolved server-side**, and `gs_papers` on the wire is this exam's own placement. That deleted the client-side lens gate in `magazine-mains.tsx` (a workaround, with a registry-loading race it had to guard) rather than adding two more.
  - **VERIFICATION.** (1) **35/35 pure assertions** over `curation-scope.ts` + the merge — **negative-controlled**: reverting the fallback to the pre-fix behaviour drops it to 32/35 naming exactly the three inheritance cases, then `curation-scope.ts` restored byte-identically (sha256 verified). (2) **UPPSC byte-identical**, via a git worktree at HEAD compiling the same editions against the same DB: index counts, both editions, every section, ORDER, `weightage_pct`, `editors_pick`, caps, and the state-flag VALUES — **44,773 bytes identical across both months**, then **re-run after 60 post-0116 rows entered the corpus and still identical**, with the two snapshots differing from each other (143 lines) so it is provably a live comparison and not a stale no-op. (3) **The inheritance proved on real data, read-only, zero writes** — simulating the widening over all 2,262 real mains-life rows: **OLD rule 3,152 placements across 2,238 items → NEW rule 0**. (4) A real bounded `ca:run --exam upsc` (23 items published, $0.77) then a real UPSC edition: national lens, **10/10 leak checks pass** (no state lead, no `up_special` section, no `GS5_UP`/`GS6_UP`, every rendered paper in UPSC's own list, every rendered placement === that item's own upsc verdict), same rank+cap mechanism (`weightage_pct`, editor's-pick ★, per-paper caps).
  - **CONTENT REVIEW — and a selection error of mine, corrected.** I first handed the judge the highest-`weightage_pct` item and told it that was the edition's top issue. **It was not**: sections are score-ordered and the relevance tier (1000) dominates weightage (≤400), so the true lead is a different, relevance-3 item. The judge's harshest point rested on that false premise. Re-judged the actual lead (a real PIL on police facial recognition, which the reviewer independently web-verified): **`PUBLISHABLE WITH EDITS`**, GS2 placement correct, **no state framing leaked**. Both reviews agree on what this change is about (zero leakage, correct national placement) and both locate the weakness in enrichment DEPTH — recorded as **M20d**, pre-existing and exam-neutral, not a M20b regression. Also worth not re-deriving: the UPSC pool is 20 mains-life items for the month against UPPSC's 513, so `weightage_pct = 100` there means "most weighted of 20", not "excellent".
  - **ALSO FOUND, recorded not fixed:** `ca/sources.ts` is a flat exam-agnostic list with a UP-specific `isUpSource` hint, 2 of 6 feeds UP-regional — so a national run pays to triage feeds it discards (**M20c**; measured: 0 of the 23 UPSC keeps came from either, so cost not correctness). The blocking pending-batch guard also had to be drained first via the documented `--mode sync` remedy (sync submits no new batch, so it does not re-block).
  - Verified: api typecheck clean · **full `vite build`** clean (shared modules touched — the hard rule) · web `tsc -b` clean · `oxlint` **41 warnings, delta 0** against the corrected baseline · `check:paths` (803 files) and `check:cli-args` (235 files) clean · `prompts:snapshot` **163 byte-identical**.
  - **⚑ COMMIT-ATTRIBUTION ACCIDENT, RECORDED RATHER THAN REWRITTEN. All 16 files of this M20b change live in commit `5428376`, whose message describes something else entirely** ("Fix two defects the audit found in my own generation-quality commits" — a concurrent session's `ca/prompts.ts` + `qgen/topup.ts` + `prompts.baseline.json` work). This work was staged by explicit path, as the convention requires; the concurrent session then committed with the shared index still holding it, sweeping all of it in. **So a future reader looking for M20b's commit will not find it by message — it is `5428376`, together with that session's three files.** Deliberately NOT fixed with `git reset --soft` (the repo's own Session-17 precedent for a co-stage): that session was mid-run at the time, and disrupting another session's in-flight history is worse than a mislabelled LOCAL, unpushed commit. The branch is unpushed, so the split is still available if wanted. The real lesson is upstream of the convention: **staging by explicit path does not protect you when another process commits the shared index** — only committing promptly after staging, or not leaving work staged, does.

### Edge-case audit of M20b, same day (2026-08-08): a missed surface, a non-replayable migration of my own, and a stale claim disproved
Adversarial pass over the M20b change — hunting for what it INTRODUCED or newly EXPOSED. Four real fixes; the UPPSC control and the UPSC leak checks were re-run against all of them.
  - **MISSED SURFACE — `ca/deepdive.ts` wrote the raw cross-exam UNION onto a deep dive.** The same defect M20b closes, one table over, on a magazine surface. It already derives its exam PER ISSUE (`examCodeForNode` on the primary node) and frames the prompt with it, but at insert time stamped `gs_papers: issue.item.gs_papers` — so a dive written FOR upsc would carry UPPSC's paper union, including the state-only `GS5_UP`/`GS6_UP` labels UPSC does not set, and `magazine-review-panel.tsx` + the Mains edition render those as chips. Latent (0 non-uppsc dives exist), live on the next `ca:deepdive` for a second exam. Now resolved through `gsPapersForExam`. **The typechecker caught my first attempt**: I reached for `r.examCode` inside the insert loop, where `r` is the batch RESULT — the request is `requests[i]`. Two different `r`s live in that function; noted at the line.
  - **⚑ MY OWN MIGRATION WAS NOT REPLAYABLE, which is the standard M14 sets for every migration here.** `0116`'s assertion counted rows and demanded ZERO non-null ones — correct exactly once, at first apply, and false from the moment the pipeline wrote its first row. **Verified by replaying the file against the live DB inside a rolled-back transaction: it failed with "74 row(s) are non-null".** Replaced with a SCHEMA assertion — both columns must be nullable with NO default — which is what actually guarantees "no existing row was reshaped" (a `not null default '{}'` would have given every pre-existing row a value and destroyed the NULL sentinel) and is true on first apply AND on every replay. **Negative-controlled properly**: the obvious control (edit the file to add `not null default '{}'`, replay) PASSED misleadingly, because `add column if not exists` skips the bad ADD when the column already exists — the control only bites on a DB that has never seen 0116, so the harness now DROPs both columns inside the transaction first. With that, the good file applies on a fresh DB (nullable, no default) and replays on the live one, and the bad file is REFUSED naming the offending column. All inside rolled-back transactions; live schema and all 74 populated rows confirmed untouched afterward.
    - Editing an applied migration creates file-vs-ledger drift, and `0116`'s ledger row carries real statements (it was applied by `db push`, unlike the hand-inserted rows §0c describes) — so this is exactly the case CLAUDE.md warns not to generalise to. Closed properly with `supabase migration repair --status applied 0116`, which re-parses the local file: verified afterwards that the ledger records the NEW schema-based assertion and no longer contains the old row-count text, and `db push --dry-run` reports "Remote database is up to date".
  - **A STALE CLAIM DISPROVED — in a comment I had kept.** `magazine.ts` asserted that CA-generated questions "leave `exam_code` at the column default ('uppsc'), so this filter can only ever match uppsc rows". **Measured across every CA question generated by a real uppsc AND a real upsc run: 17/17 carry the exam of their own syllabus node, 0 mismatches** — the 3 UPSC MCQs are `exam_code=upsc` on `UPSC_PRE_GS1`. The filter is genuinely per-exam now. Corrected in both `loadWorkbook` and `loadModelQuestions`. (A second exam's workbook still reads 0, for the honest reason that CA MCQs are inserted `needs_review` and the query selects `approved`.) **This was the check most worth running: had it gone the other way, my own `ca:run --exam upsc` would have injected 3 UPSC MCQs into UPPSC's live workbook and weekly quiz.**
  - **`ca-strip-foreign-exam-nodes.ts`'s forbidden-column list** did not cover the two new columns — the same gap already closed in `widen-exam.ts`. Extended, with the reasoning that it deliberately does NOT prune an exam's map entry when stripping that exam's nodes: the entry records what that exam's triage said, which stays true either way.
  - **CHECKED AND CONFIRMED NOT DEFECTS** (recorded so they are not re-flagged): **(a)** the raw PostgREST `.or()` I had never exercised live executes correctly for all three exams, ANDs with `.overlaps`/`.eq`, tolerates being stacked twice (`lens=up` + legacy `up_only`), and **returns EXACTLY the set the old `.eq("is_up_specific", true)` returned for uppsc — 363/363, 0 only-old, 0 only-new**. **(b)** 10 post-0116 rows carry `exam_codes {uppsc}` while their map names only `upsc` — that is `mergeExamTriages`' documented archive-path placeholder, **all 10 are `status='archived'` and every read filters `status='published'`**, and they resolve to `[]`/`false` for every exam anyway; the invariant that does hold is that every PUBLISHED row has a map entry for each of its `exam_codes` (42/42). My first assertion was too strict, not the data wrong — written into `curation-scope.ts` so it is not re-investigated. **(c)** the N=1 identity holds on all 74 real post-0116 rows (0 state_focus mismatches, 0 map-vs-legacy mismatches). **(d)** no raw `is_up_specific` reader remains on a curation path — the survivors are the `sourceIsUp` triage HINT (widen/backfill), the legacy writes, and the wire-stripping destructure.
  - Re-verified after every fix: **35/35** pure assertions · UPPSC editions **byte-identical against the pre-M20b commit on the same DB, now including deep-dive `gs_papers`** (44,487 bytes) · UPSC **11/11 leak checks** · api typecheck · full `vite build` · `prompts:snapshot` **163 byte-identical** · `check:paths` (805) · `check:cli-args` (236) · oxlint **41, delta 0**.

## U7 closed + UPSC pre-launch verification under a REAL is_live flip (2026-08-08)
Closed `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f's **U7** — `is_live` had only ever gated the two WRITE paths (`assertSelectableExam`, onboarding + the U3 switcher); nothing stopped a `users_profile` row that ALREADY held a non-live `target_exam` from reading as that exam forever, no further write needed — then ran real pre-launch verification against UPPSC's own bar, under an ACTUAL temporary `is_live=true` flip rather than a simulated one. **`exams.upsc.is_live` is left `false`** — the permanent flip is explicitly gated on the founder reviewing the new `docs/launch-checklist-upsc.md` and confirming. Nothing was pushed.
  - **THE FIX: `apps/api/src/lib/exams.ts`'s `getUserExam()`** — the single function ~27 files call to resolve "what exam should this user's reads be scoped to" — now checks `is_live` (a 60s-TTL module cache mirroring the file's existing `paperCodesForExam` pattern, so no extra DB round trip lands on hot paths like the dashboard) and falls back to `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` whenever the resolved `target_exam` names a non-live exam. **Read-time only — never writes `target_exam`.** `getExamConfig()` itself was deliberately left untouched (it's a pure, synchronous, in-memory, never-throws lookup by design — its own doc comment explains why: taking down a paid evaluation because a row carries an unexpected exam code is worse than serving the default framing); instead every one of its ~35 call sites was traced to confirm none reaches it with a user-facing exam code that bypasses `getUserExam()` — evaluation's custom-prompt path, mentor, study-plan, answer-set, micro-drills, OCR, user-notes all confirmed to derive their `examCode` from `getUserExam(userId)`. Content-pipeline paths (`examCodeForNode`, a CLI's `--exam` override) are deliberately NOT gated — stocking a not-yet-live exam's content is the whole point of those paths, per `resolveTargetExams`'s own pre-existing doc comment citing this exact U7 confusion. One residual, recorded rather than additionally gated: `evaluate.ts`'s catalogued-question path derives its exam from the QUESTION's own syllabus node, not the submitter's — a user who knows a specific non-live-exam `question_id` could still get it evaluated under that exam's rubric, matching the pre-existing "public reference content" precedent (§8h's U4b), not reachable through any scoped browse/search UI.

Measured live: **0 of 163 profiles currently carry `target_exam='upsc'`** — every one is `uppsc`. So the tension the task asked me to navigate carefully (does the fix silently break active testing?) was not live. I did not create, reset, or touch any profile row to arrive at this — it's a direct measurement, reported rather than assumed away. If that account exists under a different id or was reset outside this session, worth a quick check on the founder's end.
  - **Verified with throwaway accounts only, fresh-process reads (the module cache means a single long-lived process can lag ≤60s behind a flip — confirmed as the expected, documented tradeoff, not a bug, when a same-process check hit it):** a profile parked on `target_exam:'upsc'` resolves to `uppsc` while `is_live=false`, resolves to `upsc` the moment `is_live` flips `true` with **zero write to the row**, and reverts to `uppsc` the moment it flips back — the row itself never touched across the whole sequence.
