### Edge-case audit of the U7 fix, same day (2026-08-08): a real display-layer gap, and three "confirmed not a bug" checks
Adversarial pass over `cb7d72a` itself — hunting for what the fix introduced or left half-closed, not re-confirming the happy path.
  - **⚑ REAL, LIVE-REPRODUCED GAP: `getUserExam()`'s fix closed the cost/content exposure but left a DISPLAY-layer mismatch.** `services/profile.ts`'s `getProfile()` selects `target_exam` **raw** — a direct column read, not a call to `getUserExam()` — and the web app's `useCurrentExam()` derives its `examCode` straight from `profile.target_exam`, ungated. `usePaperCatalog` (and everything built on it — the PYQ picker, mocks tabs, scoreboard, papers grid, per its own docstring) reads THAT value. **Reproduced live**: a throwaway profile parked on `target_exam:'upsc'` while `upsc.is_live=false` — `getUserExam()` correctly returns `'uppsc'` (content-serving is safe), but `getProfile()` returns `target_exam:'upsc'` (raw, what the UI reads). So a row in this state would show UPSC's papers/labels in the switcher and paper-driven UI, while the dashboard/evaluation/mentor/etc. silently serve UPPSC content underneath. **Deliberately NOT fixed** — closing it means picking between two UX options (gate `getProfile`'s value the same way, silently "reverting" a deliberate non-live selection on screen vs. leaving the raw/mismatched display) and that's a product decision, not a mechanical extension of the same fix, matching how the founder's own parked-row question was handled ("Leave it" was an explicit decision, not inferred). Currently dormant: 0 profiles are in the affected state. Recorded as `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`'s **U7-residual**.
  - **Reconsidered and REJECTED a tempting "fix": removing `getUserExam`'s short-circuit that skips the liveness check for `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` itself.** First read as an inconsistency (why does the gate apply to every exam except the default?) — but the short-circuit is correct by construction: `DEFAULT_EXAM_CODE` is the fallback of last resort, and "is the fallback live, and if not, fall back to what?" is circular for the one exam that IS the fallback. There's no sensible resolution at this function's layer if the default exam itself were ever un-launched (that's a maintenance-mode decision, not a per-user exam substitution). Left unchanged.
  - **Reconsidered and CONFIRMED (not a bug) a write/read cache-staleness race**: a user who successfully selects a JUST-flipped-live exam via `assertSelectableExam` (uncached, always fresh) could still see the DEFAULT exam's content for up to 60s afterward if their process's `getUserExam()` cache had warmed with the old (non-live) state moments earlier — the write and read paths aren't kept in sync. Checked whether the file's PRE-EXISTING, identically-shaped `paperCodesForExam` cache has any invalidation hook for its own write path (a syllabus ingest) — it has none, confirming TTL-only-no-invalidation is this file's established convention, not a gap unique to the new cache. Left as a disclosed, self-healing (≤60s) tradeoff rather than adding write/read coupling for a narrow, one-time-per-launch window.
  - **Verified no orphaned data from the parent session's live testing, independently rather than trusting the subagents' own cleanup claims**: 0 auth users created in the last 8h, 0 evaluations, 0 doubt messages, profile count unchanged at 163 (all `uppsc`) — matches what both subagents reported, confirmed by a fresh query rather than assumed.
  - **Checked and confirmed no other gap**: all ~27 `getUserExam()` callers benefit from the fix automatically (it's a single choke point, no per-caller change needed — the architectural point of gating it there); no test suite exercises `getUserExam`/`is_live` directly (none existed before this fix either, not a regression); `resolveTargetExams`'s own doc comment (which already names U7) needed no update, since it still accurately describes `is_live` as a selection-not-launch gate — that hasn't changed, only where it's enforced has.
  - Verified: `pnpm --filter api typecheck` clean; `git status` clean except the one intended `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` edit (U7-residual + the two rejected-fix notes were docs-only, no code change from this pass); throwaway verification user deleted by exact id, confirmed 0 leftover.

## Model-answer verify gate shipped (G3/G8) + mentor defects root-caused (G4) — 2026-08-08
Acting on `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9. **G8 CLOSED, G3 substantially advanced but deliberately NOT closed, G4 root-caused with no fix shipped.** Full measurements in the new **§9a** (G3) and **§9b** (G4). **Budget: exactly 20 Anthropic calls, the stated hard cap — independently confirmed by the cleanup deleting exactly 20 tagged `llm_calls` rows.** Files: `services/evaluation/{prompts,evaluate}.ts`, `scripts/prompt-snapshot.ts` + baseline. No web/shared file touched.
  - **⚑ THE CAP AND THE REQUESTED PANEL ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, so I did not run the requested one.** "A fresh sample of 16 model-answers post-fix" costs **≥32 calls** (16 generate + 16 verify) before a single regeneration. Instead the 20 calls went at the **EXACT 16 stored model answers the 2026-08-08 Panel B judged**, so every gate verdict is comparable against three judges' recorded accuracy — ground truth instead of a fresh unlabelled sample. **Consequence, stated plainly: there is NO post-fix answer sample and NO post-fix accuracy number.** The before/after that does exist is on the gate's own precision (below).
  - **G3 — the verify stage now exists**, which is the thing that row said "is not built". `generateVerifiedModelAnswer` buffers the answer, gates it, allows ONE corrective retry carrying the findings, and **persists to `question_model_answers` only if the final text passes**. Fails OPEN on verifier error (a broken verifier must not suppress caching everywhere). Buffering rather than streaming costs nothing real: the reuse branch has ALWAYS emitted the whole answer in one event, and `EvaluationModelAnswer` renders the card **collapsed by default**, so the streaming was almost never on screen.
  - **THREE ITERATIONS, EACH FORCED BY A MEASUREMENT — this is the useful part.** (1) `contradicted`-only blocking **let the panel's worst answer straight through**: B01's invented "Supreme Court (2011)" comes back `unverifiable`, because a careful checker will not claim a judgment it cannot place definitely does not exist. (2) So I added `kind`, and blocked `unverifiable`+`attribution` — justified by the generator's OWN contract, since `FACTUAL_PRECISION` already forbids writing an attribution you are not sure of. That caught B01 but rejected **10 of 14 known-good answers (71% FP)**. (3) The issue texts showed why — the haiku verifier was **anchoring on the retrieved passages** ("not mentioned in the reference passages") instead of using world knowledge, against instruction. Rewrote the prompt to attack anchoring head-on and moved to sonnet.
  - **⚑ A BLIND 3-JUDGE PANEL PROVES THE FIRST VERIFIER WAS UNUSABLE: precision 1/21 ≈ 5%.** 22 flagged claims, adjudicated TRUE/FALSE/CANNOT_PLACE by 3 independent free subagents with web search, provenance hidden, seeded `mulberry32` shuffle (seed 20260808), key sealed outside the judges' directory. **20 TRUE, 0 FALSE, 2 CANNOT_PLACE**, inter-judge disagreement **0.67 of 22**. Every one of haiku's `contradicted` verdicts is majority-TRUE — it *positively asserted falsity* about real things, denying the Criminal Law (Amendment) Act, 2013 and calling GeM, MISHTI, CRZ notifications and the Code on Social Security, 2020 unverifiable. The recalibrated verifier flagged **one** claim across the 3 boundary answers, and it is **exactly the one claim all three judges could not place** — B01's SC-2011 attribution.
  - **⚑ AND THE HONEST COUNTERWEIGHT: my earlier "recall 2/2" was ACCIDENTAL and is withdrawn.** B05 (the other known-bad, accuracy 2.00) has one real error — `"CTBT signed conceptually never ratified"`, when **India has never signed the CTBT**. The verifier **never flagged it**; it rejected B05 on three *other* claims that the judges call TRUE **3/3**. So B05 was rejected for entirely the wrong reasons and may pass under the recalibrated verifier. **That is why G3 stays open** — §9a names the exact remaining measurement (15 calls).
  - **G8 CLOSED, with a measurement correction.** Two-tier length gate: strict (≤ limit) on the first attempt, **1.1** after one retry — the asymmetry exists so a stubbornly-long answer cannot be rejected forever and permanently deny the reuse cache. Deterministic, so validated on all 16, not a sample. **"7 of 16 exceed" counts against the BARE limit; against the ~10% grace the prompt itself promises, only 1 of 16 does.** The judges scored `len` against the bare limit, and a model answer is an exemplar, so strict-first is right.
  - **G4 — the brief's hypothesis is FALSIFIED, and the recorded defect (1) is factually WRONG.** "All four came from low-retrieval-confidence answers": **3 of the 4 defect replies carried the MAXIMUM 6 citations**; the worst reply overall had 6. Zero-retrieval replies span accuracy 2.67-5.00 (including the joint-best). And **"Battle of Sarnul" is not invented and the citation is not mismatched** — it is verbatim text from a real UPPSC 2019 PYQ in our own bank, cited correctly; the recorded "cites a source that says Samugarh" conflated two different citations.
  - **What actually happened is worse and more fixable.** A `question` embedding chunk is `stem + options + explanation` (`ingest/embed.ts:137`) — **`correct_option_key` is not embedded and this question's explanation is null**, so the mentor received four battle names plus four *mutually contradictory* orderings with no marker of which is right, and supplied every date and opponent from memory: **1544 / Humayun / Maldev of Marwar** against the truth **1572 / Akbar / Ibrahim Husain Mirza**. It then rationalised the conflict with another chunk as "a common examiner trick". **The correct answer was in the same DB row the whole time** — key `A` plus a July web-verified `meta.blind_resolve` that states the 1572 date explicitly. Second mechanism: **no weightage data is injected anywhere in the mentor path**, yet two replies assert mutually exclusive "single highest-weightage" statistics (50 vs 57), both wearing citation markers, while `mv_node_weightage` already exists and powers `/learn`. Plus two genuinely independent modes (a stale Lok Sabha 552 — 550 since the 104th Amendment — at zero retrieval; and a NAPCC mnemonic that fixes the count at 8 and lists 7, inside the paragraph warning about that exact trap). **Four fixes specified in §9b, none shipped** — no validation budget remained, and the mentor is a live paid surface where G6's own lesson forbids an unvalidated prompt/retrieval change.
  - **Two traps recorded in §9b for whoever does the mentor work:** `meta.blind_resolve.decisive_facts` can hold the **pre-escalation, REJECTED** reasoning (on this row it says "Sarnul (1764)", the guess escalation overturned), so it must never be surfaced naively; and **`ingest/embed.ts` contains a NUL byte**, so `grep`/`rg` treat it as binary and **silently return nothing** — the same class as the `parseArgs` census miss in §0d/D15. Use `awk`/`python`/the compiler.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical**, and the diff vs HEAD is **5 insertions / 0 deletions** — every changed key is one this session created, so **no live prompt moved** (the 2 WARNs are the documented pre-existing mentor-persona margin, M26) · `check:paths` clean (806) · `check:cli-args` clean (236). All 20 validation `llm_calls` rows deleted by exact purpose tag (`g3val_*`, 0 remain); all six throwaway `_tmp_*` scripts deleted; **zero DB writes to any app table** — the validation is read-only apart from the tagged telemetry it cleaned up.

### Edge-case audit of the verify gate, same day (2026-08-08): a fail-open contract that failed closed
Adversarial pass over `d3bba65` — hunting for what it INTRODUCED or newly EXPOSED. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9a (A8-A12).
  - **REAL BUG FIXED — the fail-open gate had a fail-CLOSED path, and it fired late.** `claims` was assigned inside the try that owns fail-open, but `.filter(isBlockingClaim)` ran OUTSIDE it, so one malformed element in an otherwise well-formed envelope threw a `TypeError` out of `generateVerifiedModelAnswer` and failed the whole evaluation — **after the candidate had been charged a credit and already seen their scores, strengths and improvements.** That is the worst possible moment to fail, and it directly contradicts the function's own documented contract. Negative-controlled: proved it throws, fixed with `isWellFormedClaim` inside the try, then proved the blocking semantics survive intact (malformed dropped, well-formed siblings KEPT rather than the batch discarded, `unverifiable`+`attribution` still blocks, `unverifiable`+`figure` still does not).
  - **⚑ THE GATE'S HINDI BEHAVIOUR IS ENTIRELY UNMEASURED — on a Hindi-equal-first product.** `question_model_answers` holds **16 rows, 100% `en`**, which also means the 2026-08-08 panel judged the **ENTIRE population, not a sample** (worth knowing before anyone reads "16" as a sampling choice). No Hindi model answer has ever been cached or gated, and Hindi prose can run to a different word count for the same content, so the strict length tier may fire at a systematically different rate for `hi`. Recorded as open, not guessed at.
  - **A latent trap the length gate newly created: `word_limit = 0` would reject every answer forever** and make that question permanently uncacheable, because `?? ` only catches null/undefined and passes a literal `0` straight through. **Measured: 0 descriptive questions have `word_limit` 0 or negative** (51 null, all falling back correctly; lowest real value 60), and the custom-prompt path drops 0 by a different route (`if (body.word_limit)`). Latent, not live.
  - **A prompt-injection vector into the verifier, deliberately NOT fixed.** `questionText` is interpolated raw ahead of the fenced answer, so a user-authored custom prompt could try to talk the gate down. Bounded and low — a catalogued stem is admin content, and a custom prompt is **never cached**, so the attacker can only disable the gate on their own answer — and it matches the existing convention exactly (every builder interpolates `questionText` raw; `buildModelAnswerSystem` also carries no `UNTRUSTED_ANSWER_CLAUSE`). Adding the clause would alter a prompt whose behaviour had just been measured, with no budget left to re-validate, so it is recorded rather than shipped.
  - **MY OWN AUDIT SCRIPT WAS WRONG BEFORE THE CODE WAS.** An intermediate check reported 2 of 16 answers over the 1.1 tolerance, contradicting the committed claim of 1. The script compared a raw `ratio > 1.1`; the gate compares `> Math.round(limit * 1.1)`, and at 138/125 those differ (`round(137.5) = 138`, and `138 > 138` is false). Re-run against the gate's real predicate, `countWords` matches the panel key **16/16 exactly** and the gate fires strict **7/16** / tolerant **1/16**, exactly as documented. **Compute the gate's actual predicate, never a proxy for it.**
  - **Confirmed clean:** `eval-harness.ts` accumulates `String(d.text).length` per `model_answer_delta`, so collapsing the stream into one event yields an identical `modelAnswerChars` — the `eval:answers` tuning gate is unaffected by dropping live streaming; the web hook concatenates and is equally indifferent; `replayEvaluation` untouched; `answer_eval_model_verify` folds correctly into `cost:report`'s `answer_eval_` numerator while `answer_eval_analysis` remains the per-evaluation denominator; added latency (~60-100s worst case) stays well inside the 5-minute stale-reclaim window, which is per-user and only fires when that user starts another evaluation.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** (the fix is code, not prompt text) · `check:paths` (806) · `check:cli-args` (236) clean. **Concurrent process:** another session was mid-flight on a PWA install-card feature (`apps/web/src/components/profile/install-app-card.tsx`, `lib/pwa-platform.ts`, `routes/profile.tsx`, both message files, plus its own `_tmp_` scripts) — confirmed disjoint, left untouched, and this commit names its two files explicitly via `git commit -- <paths>` so a shared-index race cannot sweep them in (the `5428376` lesson).

## `upsc` is LIVE (2026-08-11) — the flip, and the live bug it exposed
Flipped `exams.upsc.is_live` to `true` on explicit instruction, after closing the three pre-launch items, then audited. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8l.
  - **Pre-launch prep.** The 3 upsc CA MCQs were **already actioned** (2 approved+published, 1 deliberately rejected) — **0 were pending**, so nothing was approved by this pass and the rejection was left standing rather than overridden. **42 mock sets built** (`mocks:build` + `mocks:build:mains`, `--exam upsc`): 6 each across `UPSC_PRE_GS1`, `UPSC_PRE_CSAT`, `UPSC_MAINS_GS1-GS4`, `UPSC_MAINS_ESSAY`, all published with membership rows and **0 cross-exam question leak**. `launch_scope_i18n` rewritten against measured coverage — deliberately honest per the schema's own doc ("never overstate what is actually ingested"): chapters stated as **"78 of 195 syllabus topics so far"** with the remainder under `not_covered`, and current affairs marked recently-started because 30 items is genuinely thin. Zod-validated before the write and re-validated after through the real `listExamsDetailed()`.
  - **⚑ THE CLI CANARY THAT MATTERED.** `mocks:build`'s `--exam <code>: this exam is NOT live … building anyway` notice was used as proof the flag actually ARRIVED. Had `--exam` been dropped, `resolveMockBuildExams` defaults to **every LIVE exam** — it would have silently rebuilt **UPPSC's** mocks instead. That is the §0d failure class, so it was checked rather than assumed.
  - **⚑ THE FLIP EXPOSED A REAL, LIVE, USER-VISIBLE BUG — and it is §0's own convention biting.** `listTests` decided Prelims-vs-Mains with `paper_code LIKE 'PRE_%'`. A non-default exam's codes are exam-PREFIXED (M23), so UPSC's Prelims papers are `UPSC_PRE_GS1`/`UPSC_PRE_CSAT` — which do **not** match `LIKE 'PRE_%'` and **do** satisfy `NOT LIKE 'PRE_%'`, so **both branches were wrong at once**. Measured live with the 42 mocks already built: a UPSC user's Prelims list returned **0** (the whole Practice page empty, every mock invisible) while `stage:"mains"` returned **44**, wrongly including **14 UPSC Prelims tests**. Two more sites had the identical assumption and failed OPEN: `answer-sessions.ts`'s "this is an MCQ test, not an answer-writing test" guard stops firing for a UPSC Prelims test, and `syllabus.ts`'s CA top-up fold under-counts a UPSC Prelims node's supply.
  - **Fixed at the root: stage now comes from `syllabus_nodes`' own depth-0 roots, never from the shape of the code.** New `isPrelimsPaperCode()` / `prelimsPaperCodesForExam()` in `lib/exams.ts`, behind the same 60s TTL cache as `paperCodesForExam` (~15 rows). Paper codes are globally unique (§0), so the two boolean sites need no exam argument. The mains branch skips its `not.in` filter when an exam has no Prelims stage rather than emitting invalid `not.in.()`. **Verified both directions: UPSC Prelims 0→14, Mains 44→30 with the leak gone, `{kind:"mock"}`→12 (12+30=42 ✓); UPPSC byte-identical to its pre-fix baseline — 122 tests, same kind breakdown.**
  - **Blast radius was zero — 0 of 168 profiles are on `target_exam='upsc'`**, so no real user ever saw the broken page. That is why this was fixed forward rather than reverted.
  - **U4's four documented dual-live hazards are all RESOLVED, re-measured rather than assumed:** the CA candidate pool stays per-exam (284/195 — all three `loadSyllabusCandidates` call sites pass a single `[examCode]`, so the merged 479 is never built); `caPromptExamCode` no longer exists (M20b's fan-out replaced it); `getPrelimsCurrentAffairsNodeId` now takes an examCode; and upsc has 30 CA items + 48 CA embeddings, so a UPSC user gets real grounding rather than none.
  - **Also confirmed clean:** daily-quiz slugs correctly exam-prefixed (`daily:2026-08-08:upsc:gs` vs UPPSC's bare `daily:2026-08-11:gs`), 0 duplicate slugs; `exam_cutoffs` 20 uppsc / 0 upsc, so a UPSC user gets an honest empty card rather than UPPSC's numbers. **Expected new behaviour, not a defect:** `daily-build` and `ca:run` now iterate TWO live exams, so UPSC daily quizzes and UPSC-scoped CA will be produced from the next tick, with cost rising accordingly.
  - **A false-green worth remembering:** `tsc ... | head -5 && echo "typecheck clean"` printed "clean" while tsc was reporting errors, because `head` succeeded. Re-run bare with `echo "TSC_EXIT=$?"` — the same swallow-the-failure pattern recorded in D15.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` **exit 0** · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** · `check:paths` (828) · `check:cli-args` (238) clean. All throwaway users deleted by exact id (0 profile rows left) and every `_tmp_` script removed. Concurrent commits landing in this window (`cba5078`, `5ea0f46`, `f769a7e`) are docs + web assets only — no API surface — and my earlier commits were verified still ancestors of HEAD with all files byte-identical.

### Edge-case audit of the stage fix, same day (2026-08-11): 0 defects, 1 residual
Adversarial pass over `310d14c` — hunting for what the fix INTRODUCED, and for siblings of the defect it closed. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8l (L8-L14).
  - **⚑ THE MEASUREMENT THAT SETTLES IT, rather than reasoning about it.** The OLD predicate (`paper_code LIKE 'PRE_%'`) and the NEW one (`paper_code IN <that exam's own prelims roots>`) were run as raw queries over the same rows and the resulting id SETS diffed, per exam per stage. **uppsc is IDENTICAL on both branches — prelims 122=122, mains 166=166, zero ids on either side** — so the fix is provably a no-op for the live exam rather than "should be equivalent". upsc: prelims 0→14 (+14/−0), mains 44→30 (−14/+0), and **the 14 removed from Mains are exactly the 14 added to Prelims** — the same tests moved from the wrong bucket to the right one, no collateral.
  - **The main risk in my own fix was an exam with NO Prelims stage**, which would make the filter `.in("paper_code", [])`. Measured: `prelimsPaperCodesForExam("mppsc")` returns `[]` and PostgREST's `.in([])` returns **0 rows with no error** — it fails CLOSED. So an exam that goes live before its syllabus is ingested gets an honest empty Prelims list, not a 500 on the whole Practice page. The Mains branch separately skips its `not.in` filter when the set is empty, since `not.in.()` is not valid syntax.
  - **`isPrelimsPaperCode` verified across every interesting input, none throwing** — and critically it matches the old `startsWith` behaviour exactly for the codes with no `syllabus_nodes` row (`CURRENT_AFFAIRS`, `EDGE`, `""`, `null`, `undefined` → all false), so the two boolean call sites' semantics are unchanged for everything except the case that was broken.
  - **The `answer-sessions` guard now actually fires**, verified live: an answer-writing session on a UPSC **Prelims** test is rejected with "This test is an MCQ test, not an answer-writing test", while a UPSC **Mains** test is accepted. Before the fix it was silently inert for UPSC. The session row that check created was deleted by exact id.
  - **Whole-repo sweep for the same defect class across `apps/api`, `apps/web` AND `packages/shared` — `startsWith("PRE_"/"MAINS_"/"UPSC_")` and `LIKE 'PRE_%'/'MAINS_%'`: ZERO remaining hits.** The web app has no paper-code-prefix stage logic of its own, so there is no frontend counterpart left behind — worth stating explicitly, because a fix that only lands server-side while the UI keeps its own copy of the predicate is the usual way this class survives.
  - **Residual, recorded not fixed:** the Mains branch interpolates the prelims codes into a PostgREST filter string (`not.in.(A,B)`) — safe today because paper codes are ingest-controlled `[A-Z0-9_]+`, but it is string interpolation into a filter; `prelimsPaperCodesForExam` returns an array where its sibling `paperCodesForExam` returns a `Set` (deliberate, since the array feeds `.in()` directly, but inconsistent); and the 60s TTL leaves a syllabus ingest up to 60s stale, exactly as for `paperCodesForExam`.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` **exit 0** (checked bare with `echo "TSC_EXIT=$?"`, not through a pipe that can swallow the failure) · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** · `check:paths` (828) · `check:cli-args` (238) clean. All throwaway users deleted by exact id (0 profile rows left); every `_tmp_` script removed.


## Daily current-affairs quiz + weekly week-boundary fix + set sizing (2026-08-08 → 2026-08-11)
Weekly CA sittings already existed (`getWeeklyCaSets`, slugs `ca-{prelims,mains}-w…`); there was no DAILY equivalent. Added one, and in verifying it found a live bug in the weekly cadence. Commits `d5da420` (daily cadence + week anchor), `b92ecf6` (empty-state clipping), `e905e85` (error vs empty), `b47c30e` (set sizes).
  - **DAILY CADENCE.** `assembleDailyCaSets` mirrors the weekly builder and reuses every mechanism it has — pool query, relevance+weightage ranking, slug idempotency, per-exam scoping — differing only in parameters. Slugs `ca-{kind}-d{YYYY-MM-DD}`, exam-suffixed for a non-default exam exactly as the weekly ones are. New `GET /current-affairs/daily-sets`, registered BEFORE the `/:id` catch-all (which would otherwise match "daily-sets" as an item id). Built inside `daily:build` beside the GS/CSAT quizzes so both daily cadences resolve "today" and "which exams" ONCE from the same options, rather than from two crons that can disagree.
  - **Two behaviours a daily set must NOT inherit from the weekly one**, each documented at its constant: (1) **no pool widening** — the weekly set widens to 14 days when thin, which for a daily set would put two-week-old items under a heading that says "Today" AND make consecutive days near-identical; (2) **anti-repetition** — the window is `[date-1, date+1)` so a question approved late on day D cannot fall between two builds, but that slack means consecutive days see an overlapping pool. The previous `DAILY_RECENCY_DAYS`=3 days' sets are excluded, applied to the **POOL rather than the selection** so the set still fills to its cap instead of shrinking by however many top picks were excluded.
  - **⚑ A LIVE BUG THE VERIFICATION FOUND, unrelated to the new feature: the weekly quiz was invisible four days out of every seven.** `istWeekNumber()` was `floor(daysBetween("1970-01-01", date) / 7)` — and **the epoch was a THURSDAY**, so the week number rolled over mid-week while `ca-assemble.yml`'s cron builds on **Monday**. Monday's freshly built set became unreachable every Thursday and the CA page showed "No prelims quiz yet this week" Thu–Sun. Confirmed in the DB, not inferred: `ca-mains-w2952` was built Mon 08-03 and was already unreachable by Sat 08-08. Fixed by keying off **`istWeekStart`, promoted out of `services/scoreboard.ts` into `lib/ist.ts`** so there is ONE Monday-anchored week definition rather than two that can drift; slugs became `ca-{kind}-w{monday}`, mirroring the daily shape. **The cron day is now load-bearing and says so in the workflow: if it moves, move the anchor with it.**
  - **BOTH cadences anchor their POOL to the period being built, never to the clock** (`dayWindow(period, days)`). Without this the slug was period-keyed while the pool was clock-relative, so building a past period filed TODAY's questions under that period's slug — silently, since the row looks well-formed. This is also what makes the new **weekly gap-fill in `daily:build`** safe on any day: a Tuesday recovery build produces what Monday would have, so "generated on a fixed day" still holds. Weekly also excludes the previous `WEEKLY_RECENCY_WEEKS`=2 weeks (the widened window reaches back 14 days, so two thin weeks would otherwise repeat). **Daily↔weekly overlap within one week is DELIBERATE and documented** — that is spaced revision of the week, and excluding it would strip a week of daily picks from the weekly pool.
  - **SET SIZES (`b47c30e`), on request: daily 15 prelims / 5 mains, weekly 50 / 20** (was 5/2 and 20/5). Caps only; every mechanism unchanged. **Measured supply reality rather than assumed:** approved CA MCQs run ~5-29/day currently (50-125/day in July), descriptive only **~1-2/day** — so prelims fills its cap and **daily mains is supply-limited and will usually show 2-3, not 5**. Live after the change: daily 15/15 prelims + 2 mains, weekly 50/50 + 19/20. A larger cap consumes more per period, so the exclusion has more to remove — replayed both cadences over real supply, both question types, old vs new: prelims is safe at either scale (sweeping `DAILY_RECENCY_DAYS` 1..4 gives byte-identical fills, i.e. it does not even bind). Daily mains at cap 5 yields one empty day in 14 where cap 2 yielded none — **investigated rather than accepted: on that day the pipeline approved ZERO new descriptive questions, so the only candidates were the previous day's, already served. Showing nothing is correct; cap 2 only had something left because it had under-served the day before.** Net 2.36 vs 1.71 mains/day. `MIN_POOL` deliberately left at `{mcq:10, descriptive:3}` — raising it would pull fortnight-old items into a set labelled "this week", and it never binds in practice.
  - **UI.** Two cadences as separate bordered panels, not four similarly-named pills: CADENCE is the panel (icon + title + one line of copy), SCALE is the question count as a display numeral (15 vs 50 legible at a glance), TYPE keeps its existing colour+icon pairing. Each panel states when its next set lands, so an empty panel is a promise rather than a dead end. 44px tap targets.
  - **THREE UI DEFECTS FOUND BY LOOKING AT REAL STATES, each a different failure mode:** (1) `EmptySlot` was a FIXED `h-11` chosen to match the buttons' tap target — right for a button label, wrong for a sentence, which wrapped and clipped, worse in Devanagari where the mandated 1.75 line-height makes two lines taller. Now `min-h-11`, copy names only the TYPE (the panel heading already carries the cadence), and a both-empty cadence collapses to ONE sentence instead of two dashed placeholders. (2) **Error rendered as empty** — `data` is undefined on a FAILED fetch too, so a 429/5xx said "Nothing new has come in since yesterday". `ui-x/query-error-state.tsx`'s own docstring records this class shipping **twice before, once on this very page**; I reintroduced it AND had already seen it (a 429 during verification rendered the weekly panel empty and I filed it as a harness artifact). Each panel now takes `isError`/`retry` and renders a compact coral `role="alert"` row; the date promise is suppressed on error since it is derived from whether this week's set exists. (3) The display numeral used `leading-none`, giving a 16px line box for 18px glyphs — invisible today (overflow-visible) but latent the moment an ancestor gains `overflow-hidden`; two-digit counts made it measurable where single digits never did.
  - **⚑ CLEANUP-RULE VIOLATION BY MY OWN VERIFICATION SCRIPT, and the lesson.** `assembleDailyCaSetsForExam`/`assembleWeeklySetsForExam` return a **PRE-EXISTING** row on a slug hit, so a script that collects "the ids assemble returned" collects rows it never created. A cap-verification run did exactly that against the CURRENT period and its cleanup deleted four real production CA sets. Repaired by re-running the builders for those periods against real supply (deterministic per period, so this restores equivalent sets). **Two guards now standard for any script touching these builders: run against a THROWAWAY period, and abort if the target slugs already exist.** Root cause of the blind spot: these commits had been **merged to `main`**, so the production cron was already creating real daily sets — a fact I had not checked before treating today's periods as free.
  - **Verification.** 58/58 (daily cadence: shape, caps, exam scoping, idempotency, ranking, both anti-repetition rules, Monday anchor) · 16/16 end-to-end student journey (start → answer → submit → grade → result; no `daily_quiz_board_entries` row, since CA sets are `kind='custom'`) · 18/18 cap-raise edge cases (100-row exclusion set does not truncate, `total_marks` 100/200, a 50-question attempt grades and renders 50 review rows, a 20-question mains session is a MENU with **no completion gate** so it cannot trap a user who cannot afford 20 evaluations) · 12/12 audit of the week-anchor change, including **`istWeekStart` byte-identical to the implementation it replaced across 8001 days** and all 37 stored `mains_weekly` board keys still valid Mondays. UPPSC's pre-existing weekly sets byte-identical throughout as the control. Browser-verified at 1440/390 × en/hi across populated, both-empty, mixed and forced-error states: zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow, zero clipped nodes.
  - **GOTCHAS worth keeping.** Scratch scripts at `apps/api/` root are **NOT typechecked** (`include: ["src"]`), so `tsc` passes on a nonexistent import — the same gap CLAUDE.md notes for `scripts/`; four harness assertions were wrong before any product code was (`chosen_option_key` not `selected_option_key`; `AttemptSubmitResult` is `{attempt, results}`; `AnswerSessionDetail` is `{session, test, submissions}`; and an asserted negative-marking penalty that **was never specified** — CA sets carry a null `marking_scheme`, the exam-mode −0.33 belongs to the GS/CSAT daily quiz, so the correct bar is the weekly sibling). A page-wide clipping detector flags the CA feed's intentional `line-clamp` — scope it to the component under test. Repeated verification runs against one throwaway user trip the documented 120/min per-user rate limiter, which then renders as an empty panel; space the runs out.

## G3 closed + mentor mechanism-A fixed (2026-08-12)
Ran the exact remaining measurement `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9a named, and shipped the mechanism-A fix §9b specified. **G3 CLOSED; G8 was already closed; G4's mechanism A fixed with a partial, honestly-bounded effect; B4/B6/B7 still open.** Full numbers in §9a (A13-A17) and §9b (B8-B11). **Budget: exactly 20 Anthropic calls, the stated cap — $0.4310, independently confirmed by the cleanup deleting exactly 20 tagged `llm_calls` rows.** No app-table writes.
  - **⚑ PART 1 WAS ALREADY SHIPPED — check before rebuilding.** The verify stage, the cache gate and G8's two-tier length gate all landed 2026-08-08 (`d873def` + predecessor); this prompt is a re-issue. The genuinely open work was §9a's named 15-call measurement, not the gate.
  - **THE 2026-08-08 SCRATCHPAD HAD SURVIVED**, which is what made a ground-truth measurement possible rather than a fresh unlabelled sample: all 16 `question_model_answers` rows still present and **byte-identical**, plus the three judges' per-answer CSV. Arm membership was **derived from that CSV** (`mean acc <= 3.5`), not hardcoded, and reproduced the documented known-bad pair exactly. ctx mirrored production **including real RAG grounding — 8 chunks on all 15**.
  - **RESULT: FP 1/14 = 7.1%** (haiku 71%), **precision 3/4 = 75%** (haiku ~5%), **recall 2/2 on the known-bad and no longer accidental** — B05's CTBT error, the one A6 recorded as missed, is caught and caught for the right reason. **Cache-admitted population: mean accuracy 4.40 → 4.83, defect rate 12.5% → 0%.**
  - **⚑ THE BEFORE/AFTER IS A FILTERING EFFECT, NOT A FRESH SAMPLE.** §9a's original arithmetic still holds — 16 fresh answers cost >=32 calls and cannot exist under a 20-call cap — so the paired number is computed on the ORIGINAL 16 by treating rejection as exclusion. The regeneration path is the unmeasured part. **There is no post-fix answer-accuracy number; do not report one.**
  - **⚑ DON'T ASSUME A FLAG IS A FALSE POSITIVE — ADJUDICATE IT. Two of the three suspected FPs were REAL errors the original 3-judge panel MISSED, both in answers it scored 4.00/5.** B04's coal stat is the wrong denominator (~55% of primary energy; >70% is *electricity generation*) and is literally one of the three defects §9 itself names; B15's wheat "11 Mt (1950s)" is the **1960-61** baseline, corroborated independently by a judge spotting that the paired "rice 35" matches 1960-61 rice exactly. Blind 3-judge panel, provenance hidden, seeded shuffle, key sealed, B05 as a positive control — **unanimous, 0/4 disagreement**. So the gate finds defects beyond the panel, and the original 16's true defect rate was above 2/16.
  - **DECOMPOSE THE REJECTION RATE OR IT READS AS ALARMING.** 8 of 14 known-good fail the first pass — but **7 of those are the deterministic length tier** (exactly the documented "7 of 16 against the bare limit") and only **1** is a genuine verifier FP. Each gets one corrective retry. "6/14 admitted" is a first-pass figure, not 8 good answers discarded.
  - **MECHANISM A (B2/B3/B5) FIXED:** `retrieval.ts` now emits `[n] (<label>[; verified correct option: …]) <chunk>`. The key is read inside `resolveCitations`, which **already** fetches those `questions` rows, so **no extra round trip and no re-embed**. One builder feeds both the normal and teacher turns. The persona gained the matching rule (+617 chars) — and as a side effect clears **M26**'s 1024-token cache floor with ~230 tokens of headroom instead of +22, so the two standing snapshot WARNs are gone.
  - **⚑ VALIDATION FOUND A DEFECT THE FIX ITSELF INTRODUCED.** Given `verified correct option: A — "II, I, III, IV"`, the mentor read the code left-to-right and reported "Sarnul → Bilgram → …" when II,I,… means **Bilgram first** — then presented that as *what our verified key confirms*. **A wrong order wearing the platform's authority is worse than the silence being fixed.** So `decodeOrderingCode` resolves the code against the stem's own enumeration and falls back to labelling it a code only when the mapping is not unambiguous and total. **Validated deterministically at zero API cost over all 32 real ordering-code questions: 22 decoded, 10 fell back, every sampled decode correct by hand** (roman numerals, a 5-item case, and several independently right on the history).
  - **⚑ AND A CLAIM OF MINE THAT n=1 MADE WRONG.** After the first post-fix run I called the Sarnul defect "fully resolved" — it produced exactly 1572/Akbar/Ibrahim Husain Mirza. The second run produced **1531/Humayun vs Bahadur Shah**, and the warn-only intermediate produced 1657/Bijapur. **A different wrong answer each time: that reads as luck, not as the fix working.** What the fix reliably delivers is **the ORDER** (both questions' sequences now correct from our key, and the 1544/Humayun/Maldev fabrication plus the "examiner trick" rationalisation did not recur in either run); what it does not deliver is Sarnul's date/opponent, which is in **neither the chunk nor the key**. Scope of a data fix is exactly the data you hold. The remaining lever is the one B3 already named — `meta.blind_resolve.escalation_reasoning`, still unsurfaced, subject to B3's own trap that `decisive_facts` can hold the REJECTED pre-escalation guess.
  - **G4's shared-vs-independent question, answered:** mechanism A is genuinely shared across B2/B3/B5 and is fixed; **B4** (invented weightage statistics wearing citation markers, from a pipeline fed no weightage data at all) needs `mv_node_weightage` injected and is a feature addition, not a prompt tweak; **B6/B7** are genuinely independent modes. Per this repo's own **G6** lesson, nothing unvalidatable was shipped to a live paid surface.
  - **GOTCHAS.** `retrieveGrounding` returns `.chunks`, not `.passages` — my probe AND my sweep used the wrong name and reported `grounding=0`, which looked like the documented `match_embeddings` timeout; the prompt itself was always grounded. `streamText` + `onDelta` produced **empty text while still billing 1600 output tokens** (the real mentor uses **`streamChat` with `effort: "low"`**) — 2 calls lost to that, so read the caller you are imitating. And `tsc … | head` prints a **`head` exit code**: the D15 false-green, hit again.
  - Verified: api typecheck exit 0 · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** after re-baselining, with the baseline diff exactly **2 lines** (the two persona keys, nothing else) · `check:paths` · `check:cli-args` · full `pnpm --filter web build`. All 20 tagged `llm_calls` rows deleted (0 remain); every `_tmp_` script removed.

### Edge-case audit of the mechanism-A fix, same day (2026-08-12): 4 defects it introduced, 2 pre-existing exposed
Adversarial pass over `49299ef` — hunting for what it INTRODUCED, not re-running the happy path. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9b (B12-B17). All measurements deterministic over **all 5,321 published MCQs**; **zero API calls**.
  - **⚑ THE HINDI CLIP SILENTLY NO-OPPED, BECAUSE `\b` IS ASCII-WORD-BASED.** The instruction-clip regex used `\b` after Devanagari keywords like `कूट`, where it never matches — so English clipped correctly and Hindi never did, leaving **52 Hindi decodes carrying the whole instruction inside the item name**. Fixed with `(?![\p{L}\p{M}])` under the `u` flag; **52 → 0**. The English-only spot check that shipped the fix structurally could not have seen this — **on a Hindi-equal-first product, verify a text transform in BOTH scripts, and never trust `\b`/`\w`/`\d` around Devanagari.**
  - **`looksLikeOrderingCode` was far too loose — 95 of 232 matches are not sequences.** 38 are not codes at all ("2021-22" a year range, "55,550" a money value, "8, 7" two unknowns, "2, 3, 5, 7" a SET of correct statements) and 57 are Match-List codes, where the answer is a PAIRING that `A → B → C` misrepresents. All were being told they were positional codes. Now gated on a permutation of 1..n (n>=3) **plus** a sequence cue in the stem: **95 → 0** misleading notes, decode coverage of genuine sequences **131/137 = 96%**.
  - **The fix broke a downstream PARSING CONTRACT nobody had declared.** Teacher mode rebuilt quick-check facts out of `contextText` by stripping a leading `[n] `, so it silently began feeding `generateQuickCheck` every fact with the `(past exam question; verified correct option: …)` label attached. `MentorContext` now exposes `chunkTexts` (raw) and `index.ts` reads that. **A string formatted for a model is not an API — if something needs the content, give it the content.**
  - **Middle-item truncation, latent:** clipping ran on every item, so "1. Indian Penal Code and CrPC" decoded to "Indian Penal". Zero live instances, but silent and authoritative. Clipping is now LAST-ITEM-ONLY — the only place a closing instruction can appear — which removes the class rather than narrowing it.
  - **⚑ PRE-EXISTING, NEWLY EXPOSED: the TEACHER persona's `cache: true` has been a silent no-op.** The floor guard only ever checked `buildMentorPersona`; `buildTeacherPersona` is equally a cached segment (`index.ts:670`) at the same 1024-token cliff and was never checked. Measured **2896/2907 chars vs the 2950 floor** — so every teacher doubt re-bills the full persona, on the DOMINANT mode. The guard now covers both (WARN, since it is pre-existing and lengthening a prompt is a deliberate decision, not something an audit should force).
  - **⚑ AND C07 IS TEACHER MODE — 11 of the 15 panel replies are.** My validation ran the NORMAL path, and the rule I added went only into `buildMentorPersona`. The labelled context reaches teacher mode (one builder feeds both turns) but the rule explaining it did not, so the mode that produced the defect was the mode left without it. Same rule now added to `buildTeacherPersona`. **Check which code path actually produced the artefact you are fixing before validating against a sibling path.**
  - **⚑ THE HALLUCINATION REACHED GENERATED ASSESSMENT, which §9b understated.** C07's stored `meta.quick_check` holds **two generated MCQs whose answer keys encode the same wrong fact** — both explaining "Sarnul (1544) … against Maldev of Marwar", one ordering Sarnul *after* Jajau (1707). Scope is that one thread (ephemeral per-user meta, **not** the `questions` bank), so nothing is owed a cleanup, but a mentor confabulation propagating into graded items raises G4's severity.
  - **Confirmed clean:** no ReDoS (20,000 non-matching tokens, 1ms); one caller of `resolveCitations`; decode is locale-consistent (`जैसलमेर → रणथम्बोर → चित्तौड़ → वारंगल` = `Jaisalmer → Ranthambore → Chittor → Warangal`); Hindi coverage at parity, 0 Devanagari-digit codes; a marker inside an item's own text ("Article 21. Right to life") is caught by the total-mapping requirement and falls back rather than mis-slicing.
  - **GOTCHA that cost a build:** a JSDoc comment containing a regex with `\s*/` **terminates the block comment early** (`*/`) and produced 52 cascading parse errors. Don't paste a regex ending in `*/` into a block comment.
  - **CONCURRENT PROCESS:** another session overwrote my `_tmp_a4.ts` with its own UPSC content-audit script mid-run. Left it in place (deleting it would destroy their in-flight work) and switched to `_tmp_g4audit_*` names; only my own scratch files were removed.
  - Verified: api typecheck exit 0 · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** after re-baselining, the only changed keys being the 2 teacher-persona ones · `check:paths` · `check:cli-args` · full web build.
  - **⚑ WHERE THIS WORK LIVES — it is NOT on `main`.** Both commits sit on the local branch **`fix/g3-close-g4-mechanism-a`** (`49299ef` G3 closure + mechanism-A fix, `dd2d47a` the audit), unpushed, no upstream. Every prior session in this log commits directly to `main`/a feature branch, so a future session looking for the G3 closure on `main` will not find it — **merge with `git merge fix/g3-close-g4-mechanism-a` from `main`** (fast-forward, nothing else is on it from this work). It went to a branch because the session began on the default branch and the standing guidance is to branch first; it is not a signal that the work is provisional.
  - **⚑ AND A CONCURRENT SESSION'S COMMIT IS INTERLEAVED INSIDE THAT BRANCH: `127880a` ("Point Current Affairs' magazine link at the all-months index") sits BETWEEN the two commits above** and belongs to the notifications/magazine work that ran in this same checkout throughout. It is not part of the G3/G4 change and should not be read as such; conversely, merging this branch carries it along. Same class as the `5428376` co-stage recorded earlier — **staging by explicit path protects the FILES in your commit, it does not stop another session committing to the branch you are on.** This session's own two commits used `git commit -- <paths>`, and both contain exactly their intended file lists (7 and 7).
  - **⚑ IT HAPPENED AGAIN, TO THIS SESSION'S OWN DOC COMMIT — and the mitigation everyone reaches for does not prevent it.** The M26 rewrite, the §9a-A9↔§9b-B12 cross-links and the B12 disambiguation were edited into `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`, and before they could be committed a concurrent session ran its own `git add`/`git commit` on the shared checkout and swept them into **`3e04195` ("Correct docs to match reality: upsc.is_live already flipped true 2026-08-11")** — whose 16-line `OUTSTANDING.md` diff is its own ~10 lines plus these 3. The follow-up commit `233f651` therefore carries a message describing M26/A9 work it does not contain (it holds only the 2-line CLAUDE.md branch note). **Nothing is lost — every edit is verified present at HEAD** (`M26` reads "teacher persona open", B12 reads "**§9a's A9**", B12-B17 and A13-A17 all present exactly once). Deliberately NOT amended or reset, per the `5428376` precedent: that session had committed twice in ~10 minutes, so an amend risks rewriting THEIR tip, which is worse than a mislabelled LOCAL unpushed commit. **The real lesson, now recorded twice: `git commit -- <paths>` protects the FILES IN YOUR COMMIT, not YOUR EDITS SITTING UNCOMMITTED IN A SHARED TREE. The only actual defence is to commit immediately after editing — the exposure is the gap between edit and commit, not the staging command.**

## Post-launch upsc verification, continued (2026-08-12, same day): the index-race independently re-verified, and a real bug found by checking the mentor fix against real upsc data
Continuation of the earlier same-day "upsc launch checklist correction" work (commit `3e04195`), following on the user's "fix any issue if found, then check all edge cases" instruction. Two things: independently re-confirmed the concurrent session's own index-race incident (`aa23cdc`) actually cost nothing, then found and fixed a genuinely new, live bug in the just-shipped mentor mechanism-A fix by checking it against real `upsc` PYQs specifically rather than trusting its "5,321 published MCQs" aggregate validation.
  - **Independently re-verified the index-race, not just trusted the concurrent session's own account.** My commit `3e04195` had accidentally absorbed a concurrent session's in-flight `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` edits (M26 rewrite + the §9a-A9↔§9b-B12 cross-links) via the classic shared-index race — already self-diagnosed and recorded by that session in `aa23cdc`, per the standing `5428376` precedent of NOT amending/resetting a commit another session's history depends on. Re-checked from scratch rather than taking their word for it: re-read every claimed-present piece of content at HEAD (the corrected `is_live` summary line, U8u/U8v/U8w/U4d all struck-through-and-resolved, the M26 teacher-persona note, both directions of the A9↔B12 cross-link) — all present exactly once, nothing duplicated or garbled. Confirmed independently, not assumed.
  - **Re-verified the live `upsc` launch state hadn't drifted**: `is_live` still `true` for both `uppsc`/`upsc`, still `false` for `mppsc`; the 3 approved+published CA MCQs unchanged (now sitting inside a 14-question total as the live `ca:run` cadence for the now-live exam has produced 9 more `needs_review` rows since — ordinary backlog, not a regression); 42 mock tests unchanged. **New, positive finding**: `users_profile` rows on `target_exam='upsc'` went from the previously-documented 0 to **5** — checked each one (real emails, real display names, `onboarding_completed=true`, `updated_at` timestamps clustered right after the 2026-08-11 flip) — genuine real users (including the founder's own account) discovering and switching to the newly-launched exam, not leftover test data needing cleanup.
  - **⚑ THE REAL FIND: checked the SAME-DAY mentor mechanism-A fix (`49299ef`/`dd2d47a`, landed by a concurrent session) against real `upsc` PYQs specifically, since its own validation ("52 → 0 over all 5,321 published MCQs") — while genuinely covering both exams in aggregate (verified: `5,321 = 3,332 uppsc + 1,989 upsc` exactly) — had never been spot-checked against upsc's OWN phrasing conventions, which come from a structurally different ingest pipeline (U5's vision/OCR extraction of different source PDFs).** Found a live, confirmed bug: `TAIL_INSTRUCTION`'s trigger-word list (Select/Choose/Codes/कूट/उपर्युक्त/नीचे/सूचियों) never matches upsc's own closing-question phrasing — a direct WH-question ("What is the correct chronological sequence of the above events?" / "निम्नलिखित में से कौन-सा उपर्युक्त...") rather than the "Select/Choose the code" style the trigger list was tuned against — so the whole trailing sentence stayed glued onto the last enumerated item's decoded text: `"Second Round Table Conference What is the correct chronological sequence of the above events?" → Quit India Movement launched → Mutiny in Royal Indian Navy`, presented to the student as the mentor's VERIFIED sequence — precisely the "wrong answer wearing the platform's authority" failure mode that whole fix chain exists to prevent. **Reproduced once in `uppsc` data too** (`433aafce`, "पश्चिम बंगाल निम्नलिखित") — upsc-dominant, not upsc-exclusive, so this was always latent, just far more likely to surface once upsc's differently-worded PYQs entered the bank.
  - **A first fix attempt was tested and REJECTED before shipping — this is the part worth remembering.** Broadening `TAIL_INSTRUCTION`'s trigger list to also catch "Which of/one"/"What is"/"निम्नलिखित"/"कौन-सा" fixed the two known cases in isolation, but a full-bank before/after comparison surfaced a NEW bug it introduced: a mid-sentence "which one" match, occurring after a long unrelated preamble ("With reference to the States mentioned above, ..., which one of the following..."), clipped mid-preamble and produced a differently-garbled but still-wrong result that happened to sneak under the 120-char safety cap (previously this case safely fell back to a plain code-label — too long, unclipped, the CORRECT degraded behavior). **This was caught only by manually inspecting the actual TEXT of every changed case, not by trusting the aggregate "0 regressed" count** — the new bug was a null→value change, a completely different axis from the value→null regression count that looked clean.
  - **Root-caused and fixed properly instead: `.replace(/\s+/g, " ")` ran BEFORE any tail-clip, destroying the newline that structurally separates the last enumerated item's own line from a trailing meta-question on its own line** — exactly the signal that, preserved, needs no per-phrasing word list at all, since these list-style stems reliably put the closing instruction on its own line after the last item. Splitting on the first raw newline first, THEN running the existing (unchanged) `TAIL_INSTRUCTION` regex on the remainder (for the pre-existing same-line case, "... Select the correct answer using the codes given below" with no newline) is structural rather than lexical, so it generalises to phrasings neither the original fix nor my rejected first attempt anticipated. **Measured on the full published bank, paged past PostgREST's 1000-row cap (both exams, 137 genuine ordering sequences): 0 regressions, 3 previously-garbled decodes now clip clean, 3 previously-safe-fallback cases now decode correctly** — 6 net improvements, every single one manually inspected for content correctness before trusting the aggregate count.
  - **Confirmed no duplicated logic elsewhere** (`decodeOrderingCode`/`TAIL_INSTRUCTION`/`looksLikeOrderingCode` exist in exactly one file, grepped) and **no prompt-structure impact** (`prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical**, since this is a pure data-transform fix feeding INTO a prompt, not prompt text itself).
  - **The generalisable lesson, stated in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`'s new B18-B20 rows since this is the SECOND time this exact function was audited in one day and still had a live bug**: a fix validated against "the full bank" in aggregate is still only as good as the phrasing diversity that bank actually contained at validation time. A second exam's own ingest pipeline can shift that diversity in ways a combined pass/fail count won't surface — the same discipline this file already states for Hindi (§9a's A9: "verify a text transform in BOTH scripts") applies identically to a second exam's own phrasing conventions: **spot-check text-specific fixes against BOTH exams' real data separately, not just the combined total.**
  - Two commits, each committed IMMEDIATELY after its edit (per the "commit immediately, don't leave edits sitting in a shared tree" lesson the concurrent session had just recorded twice) — `380551c` (the code fix, `apps/api/src/services/mentor/retrieval.ts` alone) and `6044936` (the docs record, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` alone). Both verified via `git show --stat` to contain exactly their one intended file, no bystander content swept in.
  - Verified: `apps/api` typecheck clean; `prompts:snapshot` 168 byte-identical; grepped for duplicate ordering-code logic (none); all throwaway scratch scripts deleted, leaving the concurrent session's own in-flight files (`_tmp_a1/a2/a3`, ` 2`-suffixed iCloud-sync duplicates, the notifications/mentor-insights feature's uncommitted working-tree changes) completely untouched throughout.

## Notifications "Clear all" + a real mentor-tip catalogue with contextual ranking (2026-08-12)
Two engagement gaps. Logic only — the visual restyle is Session 9's, so the one web change beyond the button is classifying new tip kinds against the card's EXISTING accents. Commits `6729ea5` (feature) and the follow-up audit commit below, both on `fix/g3-close-g4-mechanism-a` (see that branch note above — this work is NOT on `main` either).
  - **"Clear all"** — `POST /notifications/clear-all` + a button in the bell sheet. **Deliberately bounded by the same `scheduled_for <= now` cut-off `listActive` uses, not "every pending row":** a nudge scheduled for 8 PM that the user has never seen must not be silently suppressed by a 10 AM clear, so it clears exactly what was on screen and tonight's nudge still arrives. The clear sticks because `generateForUser`'s enqueue upserts with `ignoreDuplicates` — a re-run leaves a dismissed row alone rather than resurrecting it for the same dedupe_key.
  - **The mentor-tip problem was not the COUNT, it was that nothing CHOSE.** The catalogue already had 5 kinds, but `listInsights` ordered by `created_at` — and a day's candidates are all written in ONE upsert, so they share a timestamp and their stored order is arbitrary. In practice a learner saw whichever row Postgres happened to return, which is why it read as "the single missing-topic % tip".
  - **Five new kinds, each from data already computed elsewhere:** `streak_risk` (day progress + streak), `srs_backlog` (due cards), `plan_today` (the AI study plan's own day), `ca_weak_node` (fresh current affairs mapped into a weak section), `get_started`. Plus `exam_proximity` rebuilt from one sentence into **three ranked tiers** — "5 days out" and "80 days out" are different instructions, not the same sentence with a number swapped in.
  - **Ranking is contextual.** Each candidate carries a priority built from the three things that actually change what a learner should be doing: the IST hour, distance to the exam, and the size of the signal. Same learner, same untouched day → study plan at 10 AM, streak rescue at 9 PM. Mains answer-writing outranks exam pacing 80 days out and is outranked by it inside the last month. A 60-card backlog outranks a 12-card one, and outranks itself again before noon. **Ranking happens in JS because the DB cannot express it**, so the whole undismissed set is read and sorted here — truncating at the database would drop tips at random, possibly the most relevant one.
  - **Dismiss reveals the next**, never nothing: the ranked list is returned six deep, stale rows (yesterday's, or a tip whose condition stopped holding) sort last rather than vanishing, and a brand-new account with no signal at all still gets one honest card (`get_started`) instead of blank space. **Ties break on `id`** — two tips can legitimately tie, and without a stable final key the card would pick a different one on each reload and visibly flicker.
  - `ca_weak_node`'s query **mirrors `getCurrentAffairsItemById`'s filters exactly** (`status='published'` + `exam_codes` overlap) because the tip's CTA opens that very endpoint — a looser filter would produce a card whose link 404s. It matches the weak section's **SUBTREE**, since weak nodes are depth-1 while CA triage maps to depth 1-2, so an exact match would miss most real links.
  - **Every signal loads best-effort and degrades to "not a candidate"** rather than throwing — a slow or failing table can never take out the dashboard's mentor card.
  - **VERIFIED:** `buildCandidates` is pure (context in, ranked candidates out — no DB, no clock, no model), which is what makes the ranking testable: **`pnpm --filter api test:tips`, 74 assertions**, following the existing `test:mentor` convention. The two queries written from scratch were validated **read-only** against the real DB (the CA lookup returns genuinely recent items on real weak sections, and correctly returns nothing for the two Essay sections — the honest empty path).

### Edge-case audit of the above, same day: 5 defects found in my own commit
  - **⚑ TWO TIPS LINKED TO THE PAGE THEY ARE RENDERED ON.** `MentorInsightCard` renders **only** on `routes/dashboard.tsx:62`, and `StudyPlanCard` sits on that same page at line 84 — so `streak_risk` → `/dashboard` and `plan_today` → `/dashboard` were buttons that go nowhere. Fixed: `streak_risk` → `/practice` (a quiz is the fastest of the three qualifying actions its own copy names); `plan_today` → **`cta_link: null`**, because the plan is further down the same page and a card with no button beats a button that does nothing (the CTA is already conditional, so it renders fine). Note the bell's own `streak_at_risk` nudge legitimately keeps `/dashboard` — it is reachable from every page.
  - **The clear-all count would have capped at 1000.** `.update().select("id")` returns a representation, which PostgREST truncates at its row cap, so `cleared` would silently under-report. Switched to `.update(x, { count: "exact" })` with **no `.select()`** → `Prefer: return=minimal`, no representation, true affected-row count. **Verified at runtime with a zero-match filter** (a random user id, so nothing was written): `status=204, count=0, data=null` — a real number, not null.
  - **`listActive` was an unranged select whose LENGTH was the badge count** — so a long-lived account would have truncated at 1000 and then under-reported its own badge, with nothing about the response looking wrong. Pre-existing, but it defines exactly the set "Clear all" acts on, so it was in scope. Now `.limit(50)` for the list + `{ count: "exact" }` for the badge. **Measured: nudges accumulate ~3/day and the heaviest live account is already at 106 pending rows** (~35 days) — the cap is a real future bug, not hypothetical, and the audit query that found it hit the 1000-row cap itself.
  - **`ca_weak_node` could mint several cards a day.** It was keyed `ca_weak_node:<item_id>:<today>`, and `ca:run` publishes every 6h — so a newer item meant a fresh card, and a learner could face three CA cards in one day. Every other tip is one-per-day; keyed by day now.
  - **The "Clear all" button was `size="sm"` (32px)** — under the design system's tap-target floor, and **this repo already reverted exactly that** on the revision Quick-add buttons. Default size.
  - **COST REGRESSION OF MY OWN, fixed rather than documented.** The first cut called `getDailyProgress` (~10 counts plus the day's answer set) on **every** dashboard load, doubling what the dashboard summary already pays on the same page — and I had shipped it with a comment justifying the cost. The catalogue only needs two facts from it, so: `countSrsDue` extracted and **shared** with `getDailyProgress` (one definition of "due", no drift), fetched always as a single indexed count; the full progress fetched **only when the streak tip could actually fire** — late in the day, for a learner with a streak to lose. `TipContext` now carries `srsDue` / `dayIsBlank` instead of a whole `DailyProgress`. **This does NOT introduce a second definition of "did anything happen today"** — when the answer matters it still comes from `hadActivity`; what's avoided is paying for it at 9 AM, when no streak can break for another eleven hours.
  - **NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED SEVEN WAYS**, each confirmed to make the suite fail: deprioritise the streak rescue · remove the never-nothing fallback · flatten the exam tiers · drop the CA deep link · restore either `/dashboard` CTA · restore the per-item CA key. **One control (flattening the exam tiers) did NOT fail on its first run and exposed a genuine gap** — I asserted the exam copy was tiered but never that the tiers were RANKED differently; that assertion is now in. **A later control also appeared not to fire, but the substitution simply hadn't matched** — checked before concluding a gap, which is the difference between finding a hole and inventing one.
  - **STILL OPEN, disclosed:** the DB **write** path (`generateMentorInsights`) has not been exercised live — it writes `mentor_insights` rows for whichever user is picked, on a production project with another session active, so the ranking is proven pure-side only. And the bell's `streak_at_risk` notification and the new `streak_risk` card can both surface the same fact at 8 PM on different surfaces; accepted as the ask explicitly requested streak risk as a tip type.

#### Two process failures of mine, recorded because both are repeatable
  - **⚑ `git checkout -- <file>` DESTROYED A SESSION'S UNCOMMITTED WORK.** Undoing a `sed` from a negative control, `git checkout --` restored from HEAD and wiped **every** change to `mentor-insights.ts`, not just the sed. Nothing was recoverable — `git fsck --unreachable` found no commit or blob holding it (a popped stash's objects were already pruned), so the file was rewritten from scratch. **In a working tree, `git checkout -- <file>` is a destructive command, not an undo.** The correct pattern, used for every control after: `cp file /tmp/backup` first, restore with `cp`, and verify the restore with `shasum -a 256` — which caught nothing wrong across six subsequent controls precisely because it was checked each time.
  - **`git stash` to measure a lint baseline is unsafe on a shared checkout.** A pathspec-limited stash/pop round-trip ran while a concurrent session held in-flight edits under the same paths; it popped cleanly and their file survived, but the window was real and needless. **Measure a baseline against `git show HEAD:<file>` or a separate worktree instead.** (It did establish that CLAUDE.md's recorded lint baseline of 41 is stale — HEAD measures **42**; this work's delta is 0.)
  - **iCloud again (D14):** ~11 ` 2.` conflict duplicates of the files being edited appeared mid-session (`mentor-insights 2.ts`, `notification-bell 2.tsx`, …), byte-identical and untracked. They are not just clutter — **they produce false `grep` hits across the repo**, which is the same class as the D15 census miss. Left in place (one duplicates the concurrent session's file, so deleting is not this session's call) but they should be removed, and a `git add -A` would commit them.
  - Verified end to end: api typecheck · web `tsc -b` · **full production web build** (shared module touched) · `check:paths` (829) · `check:cli-args` (239) · `test:args` 316/316 · `test:mentor` 13/13 · `test:tips` 74/74 · **lint 42, delta 0**.

`docs/neev-post-launch-track.md`'s Session 5. Removed the "Browse PYQ Archive" button from Practice > PYQ Papers (`practice.tsx`), its now-unused `Practice.browsePyqArchive` i18n key (en+hi), and the now-unused `Archive` icon import — one commit (`4dbf64c`), 4 insertions/16 deletions. The `/pyq-archive` route/page itself was untouched, per the task's explicit "specifically" scope.
  - **The real finding: `/pyq-archive` is now reachable from NO UI element anywhere in the app.** Repo-wide grep against `nav.ts`, the sidebar, bottom-tab-bar, command palette, every tour/coachmark anchor, `explore.tsx`'s feature catalog, and sitemap/robots/prerender scripts — zero references outside `router.tsx`'s own route definition. This isn't accidental: the page's own build session log states it was reachable via "a new 'Browse PYQ Archive' link on Practice's PYQ Papers tab... not `nav.ts`, to minimize footprint" — one deliberate entry point, now gone. **Deliberately not re-linked** — out of scope for a task that said "remove the button... specifically," and the task's own rationale ("archive access stays on daily quiz / test history's own recent+archive pattern, not duplicated here") reads as a UX-consistency argument (Daily/History use an in-place `RecentThenArchive` expand toggle; the PYQ button instead navigated to a whole separate page) rather than a claim that `/pyq-archive`'s actual content — per-paper year browsing with answer-key provenance badges and topic weightage — is duplicated elsewhere, since it isn't. Not a dead end for anyone who lands there directly (nested inside `app-shell`, so full sidebar/topbar/bottom-nav chrome is always present) — just now undiscoverable through the product. Flagged as `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §3 **C5** for a product decision (re-link it, or accept the discoverability loss) rather than guessed at or silently fixed.
  - **Confirmed not a bug**: `SectionCard`'s `action` prop is optional (`action?: ReactNode`, `{(title || action) && (...)}`); the exact same title-only pattern is already live in production on the adjacent "Sectional" tab in the same file, so dropping the action here carried zero precedent risk — confirmed before, not just after, the change.

All three render cleanly (no orphaned action-slot spacing, tab bar scrolls correctly at 390px, Hindi renders correctly), **zero console errors**, button text confirmed absent in both languages. Throwaway user + profile row deleted by exact captured id afterward — 0 leftovers.

### Closing the items the audit above had left open (2026-08-12)
All three "left open" items and both process failures fixed rather than carried. **The `git checkout`/`git stash` lessons are now standing Dev conventions above, not just a session note** — they are repeatable mistakes, so they belong where a future session reads rules, not where it reads history.
  - **The mentor write path is no longer unexercised.** Ran the real `listInsights` → `dismissInsight` → `listInsights` cycle plus the whole notifications half against the cloud DB under ONE throwaway auth user — **20/20**: a blank account gets exactly one honest `get_started` card; seeding 12 due cards makes `srs_backlog` appear and **outrank** it (78 vs 20, the contextual order proven against real stored rows, not just in-memory); `meta.priority` persists; dismissing the top card reveals `get_started` and the re-run's `ignoreDuplicates` upsert does **not** resurrect the dismissed row; `generateForUser` → `listActive` → `dismissAllActive` reports a true count, empties the bell, and the self-heal does not resurrect what was cleared. Cleanup deleted by the exact ids captured at insert time and then **re-queried to prove it** — 0 leftovers across `mentor_insights`, `notification_schedule`, `srs_cards`, `learner_profiles`, `users_profile`. The streak card's own hour gate still can't be driven live (it was 10:35 IST), so that path remains pure-side plus a direct check of its new query.
  - **The bell nudge and the mentor's streak card are now an ESCALATION, not a duplicate pair.** They said the same sentence on one screen at 8 PM. The bell goes first — it also drives web push, so it reaches someone who isn't in the app — and the card only takes over once the user has **read or dismissed** it and *still* hasn't studied. `streakRiskDedupeKey` is exported so the two surfaces cannot drift on the key, and **"row absent" deliberately reads as NOT acknowledged**: the two generators self-heal on different endpoints with no ordering guarantee, so treating a missing row as acknowledged would let the card race in ahead of the bell and reintroduce the very duplication this removes. Note the pleasant interaction with "Clear all": clearing the bell counts as acknowledgement, so the card escalates on the next load — which is correct, the streak really is still at risk.
  - **iCloud conflict copies: 18 moved out of the tree (to the scratchpad, not deleted — several were STALE snapshots rather than identical, so preserving them cost nothing), plus a durable guard.** `.gitignore` now carries `* [0-9].*`, verified to catch `mentor-insights 2.ts` at any depth while leaving a legitimately-named `report v2.ts` tracked.
  - **⚑ AND THE GUARD IMMEDIATELY FOUND THAT THE HAZARD HAD ALREADY HAPPENED: `apps/web/src/routes/app-shell 2.tsx` was a COMMITTED iCloud duplicate** — swept into `ae04588` ("Fix Practice tab row overflow at 390px") by a `git add -A`. Verified dead before removing it: **zero references anywhere** in the repo, and it is a stale copy missing the guided tour, milestone toaster, guest banner, floating mentor button, paywall modal, push navigation and `titleI18n` support. Removed; full production build clean. This is the concrete cost of the ` 2.` files — one of them had already polluted a caller search during this very session.
  - **A REAL MISTAKE INSIDE THIS CLEANUP, caught by its own audit:** the bulk move used `**/*" 2."*`, which swept up **tracked** files too — it deleted `app-shell 2.tsx` from the working tree before anyone had decided anything about it. Caught by checking `git ls-files` for the pattern, restored byte-identically from the backup (confirmed by an empty `git status`/`git diff` on the path), and only *then* removed deliberately with `git rm` after proving it was dead. **The lesson is why the move was a move and not an `rm`: a reversible cleanup let a wrong call be undone; an `rm` would not have.** Always check a bulk-cleanup glob against `git ls-files` before running it.
  - Verified: api typecheck · web `tsc -b` · **full production build** · `check:paths` (828) · `check:cli-args` (239) · `test:args` 316/316 · `test:mentor` 13/13 · **`test:tips` 77/77** · `prompts:snapshot` 168 byte-identical · **lint 42, delta 0**. The escalation gate is negative-controlled (removing it fails "bell nudge still unread → no duplicate card"); an earlier control in this same round exposed that the multi-line `everyTip` fixture had silently stopped covering `streak_risk`, which is now fixed.

## Brand refresh (2026-08-12): tree-of-knowledge navy/blue/gold across tokens, logo, primitives and nav
Applied the three reference images now committed at `docs/design/reference-{1,2,3}.png` (the derivation must stay auditable — `index.css` and the design skill both cite them by name). Eight commits, `1c152c6`..`ceff0cb`, on `main`. `.claude/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md` is rewritten to the new system and is the thing to read first, not this entry.
  - **⚑ THE PALETTE IS READ FROM LABELS, AND THE LABELS ARE AI-RENDERED.** reference-3's dark swatch reads `#06122S` and `#102S42` — hex has no S; both are 5s, confirmed at 11x zoom AND cross-checked against the swatch pixels. The two references also disagree: reference-3's light navy label says `#081038`, reference-2's labelled COLOR PALETTE panel says `#0B1D3B`. **reference-2 wins — it is the only panel carrying all five light values, and it is the one the brief quotes.** Final, and every reference-defined value now matches EXACTLY, verified by dumping the live computed tokens rather than by reading the CSS: light `#F7F9FC #0B1D3B #1E4FD9 #F7C873 #667085`, dark `#061225 #102542 #F7C873 #A9B7CC`.
  - **`--action` is the one theme-INVERTING token**, and it exists because the reference genuinely inverts: the primary button is filled NAVY in light and GOLD in dark (sampled from reference-2's own pages — light "Start Preparing" `#082252`, dark `#FAC45D`). Navy on a navy page is invisible. `--primary` stays the interactive blue, so ~200 existing usages carried over with only a hue shift; the primary BUTTON is `--action`, not `--primary`, and the skill says so.
  - **A DETOUR WORTH NOT REPEATING: I moved the dark surfaces to the reference's RENDERED screens (~#020F23 page, ~#081830 cards) instead of its swatch chips, and it was wrong twice over** — the swatches are the stated spec, and the change pushed the theme *darker* precisely when the founder's feedback was "colour is going dark". Reverted to the chips. The measured render values are recorded in `index.css` so the next person can see the option was considered and rejected.
  - **THE LOGO: use ours, do not redraw it, do not re-shape it.** I first hand-drew a geometric SVG mark; the founder's correction ("use our logo") is right — `scripts/assets/brand-mark-source.png` is already the real tree-of-knowledge artwork at 1254px. I then circle-masked it to match the reference's circular nav badge, which **clips the artwork's rounded-SQUARE plate corners and drops the added rim on top of the plate edge** (the founder caught this from the icon diff: "see cutting"). Reverted. **The `any` icons and favicon are byte-identical to before, verified by sha, not by eye.** The reference's badge is drawn as a circle from scratch; ours is drawn as a rounded square, and one cannot be turned into the other by masking. Written into `generate-pwa-icons.mjs` so it isn't retried.
  - What DID change in the icons is the two mask-cropped slots, which were a real pre-existing defect: maskable and apple-touch were full-bleed plates with transparent corners, and a launcher crops a maskable icon to a circle/squircle with only the inner 80% guaranteed. Both now inset the logo on navy, checked by actually circle-cropping the output the way Android would.
  - **Wordmark stays "Neev", NOT the reference images' "NeevStudy"** — that pair is decided in exactly one place (Branding, above) and renaming the product is its own change, not a side effect of restyling. Flagged, not done.
  - **Two decisions were the founder's, not inferred:** the mobile bottom nav keeps the app's real 4+More (Dashboard/Learn/Answers/Practice) rather than the reference's generic Home/Courses/Tests/Dashboard/Profile, which would have demoted the flagship and stranded 8 routes; and the exam switcher gets a top-bar chip on all viewports (a **designed slot only** — it routes to the existing ExamSwitcherCard in Profile, and deliberately does not open a second picker that could disagree with it).
  - **A REGRESSION I CAUSED AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT CAUGHT IT.** The brand mark + exam chip added ~80px to a top bar already at capacity: at 320px the page title's box collapsed to **0px** against its documented ~8-character floor, and the avatar was being squeezed 36px → 20px. A sweep screenshot showed the Hindi title as "प्रो.". Fixed by consolidating rather than dropping either thing asked for — language and theme move into the account menu below `sm` (~76px back), and the chip renders its LATIN code in both locales (Hindi "यूपीपीएससी" is 10 Devanagari chars vs 5, and Latin-in-both-locales for a compact abbreviation is already this app's convention). Measured, longest real title: **390px went 54px/5 chars → 122px/15 chars (full title fits, better than before this branch)**; 320px went 0 → 52px/5 chars. **DISCLOSED: 320px English is still 3 chars short of the floor** — clearing it needs the chip to drop below `sm`, which removes it from every phone in portrait and reverses the placement decision, so it is a stated trade, not a silent one.
  - **THE CONTRAST STORY, and the one defect this codebase keeps regrowing.** Retuning `--marigold` to the brand gold made it PALER, turning every `text-marigold` from bad (2.2:1) to unusable (**1.6:1**) — 25 usages across 18 files. Worse, solid-gold surfaces paired with `text-marigold-foreground` measure **1.2:1 in dark**, because that partner flips light while the gold does not; it would have shipped on the current-affairs quiz button. **Text on solid gold is always `--brand-navy`.** The same raw-vivid-token-on-its-own-tint pattern was then found in the study-plan card, guided-today, and the weekly digest's inline-styled numerals.
  - **The reported study-plan bug: the stated cause was not the cause.** "A hardcoded light colour not swapped for dark mode" — measured, every colour there routes through a token, and the raw `<input>` inherits `--foreground` at 16.6:1 (Tailwind Preflight sets `color:inherit` on form elements, so the obvious culprit does not apply). The real failures were the per-day "3 / 3" count at **2.41:1** and the "Today" chip at 4.02:1, **both LIGHT-mode failures that pass in dark** — which is why a dark-mode-first reading pointed the wrong way.
