Reproducing them at all needed a seeded plan row, since the failing states only exist once a plan has days and a completed day; the empty dev account rendered none of them.
  - **⚑ MY OWN AUDIT HARNESS WAS WRONG BEFORE THE CODE WAS.** The first contrast sweep parsed computed colours with `/[\d.]+/g` — **Tailwind v4 emits `oklab(L a b / alpha)` and that regex drops the MINUS SIGNS on the a/b channels**, turning every pale tint into near-black and inventing four failures (it reported `text-muted-foreground` at 1.36:1). Colours must be resolved through a `<canvas>`, which asks the browser for the real sRGB value and is format-agnostic. Now recorded in the design skill.
  - **VERIFICATION.** Full sweep of 6 routes × 2 locales × 2 themes × 2 viewports = **24 combinations: 0 contrast failures, 0 horizontal overflow, 0 real console errors** (the 101 the run reports are all 429s from the harness hammering the per-user rate limiter — the sweep separates them so a real error cannot hide behind them, a trap an earlier pass fell into). Font cascade proven empirically on all five cases (`<h1>`→Poppins, `<h1 class="font-display">`→Inter+tabular-nums, `<p>`→Inter, `<p class="font-heading">`→Poppins). Gold active rules confirmed painting `#F7C873` in both themes. Account menu shows language/theme below `sm` and NOT above it (no duplication), and both work from there. Full production build, `check:paths` (836 files), **lint 42 — delta 0** against a git-worktree baseline.
  - **STILL OPEN, flagged not fixed:** (1) the satori share images (`apps/api/src/services/share-image.ts`) and the marketing OG generator (`apps/api/scripts/generate-marketing-og.ts`) still hardcode an even OLDER palette (`#12141C`, `#2B4EE0`, `#F2A93B`, `#1E9E6C`, `#E5484D`) — satori cannot read CSS vars, so a shared weekly digest or mastery map posts in pre-rebrand colours, and the social preview cards are stale. (2) **33 raw `text-tulsi` and 45 raw `text-coral`** elsewhere have the same shape as the bug above (`text-coral` is 3.7:1 on white); they predate this work and touch states this pass never rendered, so they were left rather than swept blind. (3) The landing hero still leads with a blue accent where the reference uses gold for the highlighted word.

## Session 9 / UD3 (2026-08-12): app UI — dashboard, notes, tests, profile, and the Progress Indicator
`docs/neev-post-launch-track.md`'s Session 9, built on Session 7's tokens/primitives (`.claude/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md` is the thing to read first, not this entry). Ten commits: `5aa4dde`, `bcb78ff`, `39aca63`, `f8f4d93`, `11a54ba`, `8ea1f38`, `2180464`, `030164a`, `2807cd2`, `4663d60` — one per surface, then the audit fixes. **Ran CONCURRENTLY with Session 8 (marketing pages) in the same checkout**; its eight commits are interleaved in the log and are NOT this session's (`3193d2e`, `6c1d45f`, `3438005`, `24c7411`, `78936d9`, `0d1a3d2`, `eb14b2c`, `64ca5de`, plus its audit `cf2070c`).
  - **THE PROGRESS INDICATOR IS `ProgressRing`, NOT A NEW COMPONENT.** reference-2's COMPONENTS panel specifies a circular "Progress Indicator"; the app already had exactly one circular progress primitive, the design skill names it, and three call sites import it — so a second would be a parallel system bought for a synonym. The reference's name is recorded in the docblock instead, where a grep for either term lands. **Do not "rename it to match the reference" later.** Verified there is no third: only `progress-ring.tsx` and `score-gauge.tsx` contain an SVG `<circle>` anywhere in the app, and every other `rounded-full` + percentage pairing is a linear bar.
  - **⚑ THE REFERENCE CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON THIS COMPONENT, AND THE LIGHT RENDERING IS THE CORRECT ONE.** Its dark COMPONENTS panel draws the ring as a gold→blue sweep; its light dashboard (reference-1 panel 6) draws the same ring plain blue with no gold at all. **The brand gold measures 1.5:1 on a white card**, so the light rendering is not a simplification, it is the accessible one. Resolved with an `--action` → `--primary` gradient: it reproduces the reference EXACTLY in dark (gold→blue) and stays legible in light (navy→blue), and reuses the one theme-inverting token that already fills `ProgressBar`, so the linear and circular indicators stay siblings. The reference also starts its arc at ~5 o'clock — an AI-mockup artifact, not a spec; this starts at 12. **Rendered at 8/34/68/92/100% in both themes off the built CSS and eyeballed** (the live screenshots could not confirm it: every ring on the demo account was at 0, so only the track was ever on screen).
  - **THE NAMED BUG WAS REAL AND ITS ROOT CAUSE WAS BIGGER THAN THE NAMED SITE.** Community's peer-review feed rendered the **Rubric Dial at 48-56px**. Both the Dial's numeral (`text-3xl`, 30px) and its pull-up (`-mt-7`, 28px) were **fixed px calibrated for the 168px default** — so at 48px the numeral was wider than its own container and the pull-up erased the entire arc. Fixed at both ends: those two list rows now use the ring, and the Dial's numeral/offset became ratios of `size` (30/168, 28/168), so the default renders byte-identical while the 120/132/140/196/200px call sites stop wearing a numeral sized for a different dial.
  - **DASHBOARD — the strip fixes a duplication rather than adding one.** The streak was on screen **three times** (TopBar's persistent flame, a second flame in the greeting action row, the freeze pips beside it); it is now one tile with banked freezes as its hint. Same reasoning moved the progress ring OUT of `GuidedTodayCard` — the reference's Today's Plan is a pure checklist, and two rings showing one fraction on one screen reads as a bug. Quick Actions deliberately surfaces **Revision / Current Affairs / My Notes / Mentor**, NOT the reference's literal set and not the app's four most-used routes: Dashboard/Learn/Answers/Practice are already one tap away in the mobile bottom bar, so repeating them buys a phone user nothing, while these four otherwise sit behind the "More" sheet. Checklist rows gained the reference's trailing checkbox, `aria-hidden` — the row is a Link and the item completes by doing the work, so it must not announce as a control.
  - **PROFILE — the badge case needed an endpoint that did not exist, and the reason is worth keeping.** `GET /milestones` returns **UNSEEN** milestones only: it drives the one-time toasts and is invalidated on every dismiss, so a badge case built on it would show a badge exactly once and lose it forever the moment the toast was dismissed. Added `listEarnedMilestones` + `GET /milestones/earned` **alongside** it rather than a flag on it, so the toast feed keeps its unseen-only contract. Its query key is deliberately a CHILD of the toast key (TanStack matches by prefix), so marking one seen refetches the case — correct, since `seen` is a field on those same rows.
  - **⚑ THE CONTRAST FINDING THAT DWARFED THE REST: `scoreBandColor` returns the raw vivid tokens and FOURTEEN sites were passing it to `color:`.** Every score numeral in practice history, test cards, the topic breakdown, the answers session result and OCR-confidence readout, the learn accuracy figures, improvement proof and micro-drills. **Measured, not asserted:** raw marigold **1.56:1** in light, tulsi **2.54**, coral **3.67**, against a 4.5:1 floor — and coral is **4.19 in DARK too**, so it is not purely a light-mode problem. The paired `-foreground` shades measure 8.06 / 7.63 / 8.02 light and 11.99 / 11.99 / 10.90 dark. Added `scoreBandTextColor`; **16 text sites** now use it, **4 genuine fill sites** keep `scoreBandColor`, and both are documented at the source so the distinction survives.
  - **AND THE DIAL KEPT ITS OWN PRIVATE COPY OF THAT TABLE.** Fixing `lib/score-band.ts` did not reach `score-gauge.tsx`, which duplicates the bands locally — caught live at **89% on the dashboard, 2.54:1**. Its numeral now reads `scoreBandTextColor`; **the arc deliberately keeps the raw tokens**, because it is the Dial's defined identity and its value is stated redundantly as the numeral directly beneath it.
  - **⚑ MY VERIFICATION HARNESS WAS WRONG TWICE, AND THE SECOND ONE IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** (1) It composited translucent backgrounds over **white regardless of theme**, so a near-white label on a `dark:bg-input/30` plate read as near-white-on-near-white and it invented a 1.18:1 "Retry" failure. Fixed by compositing the FULL ancestor background stack over the real root background — same class as the brand refresh's `oklab()` minus-sign bug, so the rule is now **resolve through a canvas AND composite over the real backdrop.** (2) Far worse: a run reported **0 failures across 64 page loads while 47 of those loads were BLANK** — the shared dev API's per-user rate limiter (120/min) had been exhausted by the sweep itself, and `RequireAuth` renders "Couldn't load your profile" on a failed profile fetch. **A blank page trivially passes every check.** Only a content assertion (`chars`, block count, error-copy regex) exposed it. **Any sweep of this app must assert the page actually rendered before believing a green result, and must pace itself against that limiter** — a per-context cooldown, since retrying a single already-failed page costs 60s and buys nothing.
  - **FIVE MORE FOUND BY READING MY OWN DIFF RATHER THAN BY THE SWEEP:** the test card wrapped block children in a `<span>` (invalid phrasing content); Community's score divides by a **nullable NUMERIC** `max_score` where 0 is representable and the old ScoreGauge silently clamped Infinity to 100 while a ring renders **"Infinity%"** (guarded at both call sites and again inside `ProgressRing`, since non-finite input there renders either no arc or a full one, both silently); `PlanBanner`'s expiry line used `--muted-foreground` on a `primary/10` plate at **4.02:1**; `ProgressRing`'s `aria-label` was `label ?? pct`, so **naming the ring removed the value from its accessible name entirely** — a screen reader heard "Today's plan" and never learned how much was done, because the visible numeral is `aria-hidden` to stop a double read; and the gradient id came straight from React 19's `useId()`, which returns **guillemet-wrapped ids («r0»)** interpolated into an SVG `url(#…)`. Chromium resolves that (verified by painting a guillemet-id gradient beside an ASCII control — 247,200,115 in both), **WebKit could not be tested here** (binary not installed) and the failure mode is a silently unpainted arc, so the id is now stripped to ASCII.
  - **STILL OPEN, measured not guessed: 58 raw `text-tulsi`/`text-coral`/`text-marigold` usages remain** app-wide (11 in routes, 11 mentor, 6 answers, 5 practice, 5 community, and a long tail). This is the brand refresh's own recorded backlog. **The first count of this was 58 and the claim beside it — that this session's own surfaces were clear — was NOT yet true**: a re-grep found seven left in practice and profile (including `text-coral` on its own `bg-coral/10` tint, the textbook shape), fixed in `0a19a7e` rather than reworded. The remaining 51 sit in states this session never rendered and were not swept blind.
  - **⚑ TWO COMMIT-HYGIENE INCIDENTS WITH THE CONCURRENT SESSION, AND THE FIRST EXTENDS A LESSON THIS FILE ALREADY RECORDS TWICE.** `git commit -- <paths>` protects you from the other session's **staging**; it does NOT protect you from their **edits to a file you also touched** — a pathspec commits the whole file, not your hunk. My 2-line band fix in `routes/learn.tsx` swept in their entire ~327-line Courses rebuild. Repaired by amending with a blob rebuilt as `HEAD~1 + only my change` (`git hash-object -w` + `git update-index --cacheinfo`), leaving their work uncommitted in the tree — verified afterwards that their 325-line diff was back and my fix was in HEAD. **The safe pattern for a file two sessions are editing is exactly that blob rebuild, not a pathspec.** Second: both sessions edit `messages/{en,hi}.json`, so **every** i18n addition this session made was staged the same way — HEAD plus only my own keys — which is why none of Session 8's Landing/Pricing/FAQ keys appear in any of my commits.
  - **⚑ A SECOND EDGE-CASE PASS FOUND A REAL REGRESSION I HAD CAUSED, plus a colour problem the reference could not have told me about.** (1) Removing the greeting's StreakFlame as a duplicate also removed Session 15's streak-advance reward — measured, `streak_incremented_today` had NO consumer left anywhere in the app. StatCard gained an opt-in `pop` carrying the same one-shot. (2) The ring's centred content had ZERO margin: "100%" at size 52 measured 42.1px against a 42px inner diameter, so the ratio moved 0.26 → 0.24 (5-6px clearance everywhere) — this task's own failure pattern, one size down. (3) The notification icon lookup could take the whole panel down, since the type comes off the wire and rendering an undefined component throws. (4) My blanket band-helper rewrite broke the OCR confidence badge, whose single value feeds BOTH the 15% tint and the label on it — fixed the text by muddying the tint into a brown wash, so it now uses the raw token for the fill and the paired shade for the label, which is what the pairing rule actually prescribes.
  - **⚑ THE COLOUR FINDING, and it is structural rather than cosmetic: BRAND GOLD CANNOT FORM A PLATE ON A WHITE CARD AT ANY ALPHA.** #F7C873 on #FFFFFF measures **1.042:1 at /10 and only 1.092 at /20** — light-on-light, so no alpha rescues it, which is exactly why gold accents read flat in light while looking fine in dark (marigold/20 is 1.601 there). Solved the way the reference itself handles gold on a pale ground — its "Important" badge is an OUTLINE: a `--marigold-foreground` ring at 25% takes the plate to **1.62:1 light / 2.95 dark**. Separately, EVERY icon plate used `/10`, which measures **1.169 light / 1.181 dark** — at or under the ~1.2:1 point where a tint stops reading as a plate at all; `/15` measures 1.261 / 1.291 and the glyph on top still clears (primary-on-primary/15 is 5.24 light, 4.42 dark). **Both numbers came from compositing the real mix, not from taste.**
  - **TINT BY MEANING, BUT ONLY WHERE THE ITEMS ARE DIFFERENT KINDS.** After the alpha pass nearly every plate in the app was the same blue, which spends a whole information channel on decoration. Quick actions and the profile stats rows now carry the app's existing semantic assignments (blue study/navigate, gold timely-and-worth-attention, green your-own-saved-material); the weakness radar and attempt history deliberately do NOT, because their rows are all one kind and their severity signal already lives in the banded bar — a rainbow there would collide with the score bands. The mentor keeps blue rather than minting an "AI" hue, which every other AI surface would then contradict.
  - **THE WEAKNESS RADAR HAD NO SERVER-SIDE CAP** — `weakness_radar` returns EVERY top-level node the user has answered in (13 on a four-week-old account, growing with every paper). As full reference-style rows that buried the dashboard, and a focus tool listing thirteen things to fix is not a focus tool. Now shows the four weakest behind a real toggle; the list was already sorted weakest-first, so those ARE the four to work on.
  - **A LAYOUT CHANGE I MEASURED AND THEN REJECTED, worth recording so it is not re-attempted:** a 2-up stat strip saves 122px of vertical space at 390px but **truncates a whole LABEL** ("Due for revision"), and a truncated label is worse than a taller card. Stacked stays. **The harness that first suggested otherwise was itself wrong** — it rendered a "hero + 2-up" variant whose `col-span-2` was not in the JIT bundle, so it silently measured a layout that never applied. Real-page measurement (`stripHeight: 354px`) is what settled it.
  - **⚑ A MOBILE-ONLY BUG CLASS WORTH KNOWING BY NAME: a fixed height LOSES to `flex-1` when the flex direction changes at a breakpoint.** Reported as "the CA quiz UI breaks on mobile, works great on laptop and tablet" — and the widths in the report were the whole diagnosis. `quiz-button.tsx`'s row is `flex flex-col gap-2 sm:flex-row`, and its buttons were `h-11 flex-1`. Above `sm` the main axis is horizontal, so `flex-1` sizes WIDTH and `h-11` governs height; below `sm` the main axis is VERTICAL and `flex-1` (`flex: 1 1 0%`) is a flex-basis ON THAT AXIS, which overrides `height`. **Measured: 44px at 1440px and 768px, 20px at 390px** — under half the tap floor, on exactly the budget phones this app targets. `min-height` is NOT overridden by flex-basis, which is why the `EmptySlot` sitting beside it (already `min-h-11`) never collapsed and looked right while the real button did not; the two rendering at visibly different heights in one row is what the report was seeing. The loading skeletons were worse — empty spans, so flex-basis 0 gave them height **0** and the loading state was invisible on a phone rather than merely short. **Use `min-h-*`, never `h-*`, on any flex child in a container whose `flex-direction` changes responsively.** Swept the app: this file was the only instance; `ui/accordion.tsx` already had it right.
  - **A SMALL RECURRING SYNTAX TRAP, hit twice in one session:** a `{/* … */}` comment cannot be the first thing inside a `cond && ( … )` JSX expression — it makes the parenthesised expression two expressions and produces a cascade of misleading "expected `)`" errors far from the real line. Put it above the `{cond &&`, or use a `//` comment inside the element's props.

## Session 8 / UD3-marketing (2026-08-12): static pages — homepage, browse, About, Pricing, Contact, FAQ, auth, 404
Rebuilt the eight static/marketing surfaces to `docs/design/reference-{1,3}.png`, on Session 7's tokens/primitives. Nine commits, one per page plus the audit: `3193d2e` (homepage), `3438005` (learn browse), `6c1d45f` (About), `24c7411` (Pricing), `78936d9` (Contact), `0d1a3d2` (FAQ), `eb14b2c` (auth), `64ca5de` (404), `cf2070c` (audit fixes). **Ran CONCURRENTLY with Session 9 (app UI) in the same checkout** — its commits are interleaved in the log and are NOT this session's.
  - **⚑ THREE THINGS THE MOCKUP ASKS FOR THAT WERE DELIBERATELY NOT COPIED, and the reasoning is the point.** (1) Its stat strip reads "50K+ Aspirants / 1000+ Courses / 5M+ Questions / **98% Success Stories**" — placeholder marketing claims this product cannot honestly make (a few hundred accounts, zero outcome data). `lib/content-stats.ts` carries four REAL measured counts instead — **7,400+ published+approved questions, 362 published chapters, 3,000+ CA briefs, 11 years (2016-2026)** — each with the query that produced it recorded beside it, and each rounded DOWN so it stays true as the bank grows. Same rule the brief states for prices, generalised. (2) Its gold headline word: **`#F7C873` on our light page measures 1.48:1**, the codebase's single most-repeated defect; `--marigold-foreground` reads gold in both themes at 7.6:1 / 14.6:1. (3) Its "Most Popular" pricing badge became **"Best value"** — the highlighted tier is flagged `is_intro` (cheapest per month, arithmetic), whereas "most popular" is a claim about what other people bought that we have no data for. Its Contact page's phone number and "Mon-Sat, 9 AM - 7 PM" hours are omitted for the same reason: we have neither, and publishing a support line that does not answer is worse than not listing one.
  - **THE GOLD RULE HAS A THIRD CASE, and all three appear in this session.** Raw `--brand-gold` as TEXT is correct on the auth page's fixed navy panel (**12:1**) and catastrophic on the light marketing page (**1.48:1**) — the panel never flips theme, so neither does text on it. For the nav's active underline neither works: on `--background` the gold bar is invisible, so it uses **`--action`**, matching what `ui/tabs.tsx`'s `underline` variant already does. The sidebar's raw-gold rule is not a counter-example — that bar sits on a filled active row, not on the page.
  - **THE MIXED-LANGUAGE HEADING BUG, and what it actually was.** The landing `<h1>` stacked the Hindi headline AND the English headline in **both** locales, so an English reader's primary heading opened in Devanagari and the element carried no single `lang`. Each locale now gets its own headline (`heroLine1` / `heroAccent` / `heroLine2`, a three-part split so the gold accent word works in either grammar). Hindi is not demoted — it is first-class in its own locale rather than bolted onto English.
  - **DE-UPPSC'ING WAS A CORRECTNESS FIX, NOT A TONE CHANGE.** `upsc.is_live` flipped 2026-08-11, so "Built for UPPSC (UP PCS)", "built only for UP PCS aspirants", "every UPPSC paper from 2018 to 2025" and FAQ q4's "UPPSC (UP PCS) only, today" were simply **false** on public, indexed pages. Exam names now come from the REGISTRY via `components/marketing/live-exam-chips.tsx`, so the next launch is the one-row `is_live` update it already is, not a copy edit in two languages. **That component renders NOTHING until `/exams` resolves and nothing if it fails** — deliberate on a prerendered page: the surrounding copy stands on its own, so a crawler sees complete prose rather than a dangling "Built for" label. **Never give it a hardcoded fallback list** — that reintroduces exactly the drift it removes. **~120 mentions remain under `Features.*` plus 4 in `Terms`/`Privacy`** — out of this session's scope, recorded as `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` C7.
  - **TWO STALE NUMBERS FOUND BY RE-MEASURING RATHER THAN COPYING FORWARD.** About's stat band said "284 chapters / 2,400+ questions"; live counts are **362** and **7,431**. The chapter figure was correct when written and quietly went stale. The landing pricing teaser's Pro price was a hardcoded **"Coming soon"** that went stale the day the ladder shipped; it now reads the real monthly plan from `GET /billing/plans` and links to `/pricing` rather than guessing when that has not loaded. It picks the cheapest **ONE-MONTH** tier, not the cheapest tier outright — the teaser prints "/month" and the ladder's multi-month tiers carry a whole-period price, so labelling ₹999-per-quarter as "/month" would overstate by 3x.
  - **THE COURSES PAGE MAPS ONTO REAL FIELDS OR NOT AT ALL.** The mockup offers "UPSC / State PCS / Optional / Foundation" as sibling checkboxes, an Enrolled/Completed status, and a bookmark per card. A user here prepares for exactly ONE exam (`target_exam`), so a cross-exam category filter is a control with one legal value; there is no enrolment, no completion flag, no paper bookmark. Reproduced against what exists: **category → exam stage, status → `answered_count > 0`, sort → syllabus/PYQs/coverage/accuracy, progress bar → real published chapter coverage.** Sorting by accuracy puts never-attempted papers **last** rather than treating null as 0% — "you haven't started" and "you scored zero" must not rank together. Filters live in the URL; an unknown value falls back to the permissive default instead of blanking the grid for a typo'd link.
  - **CONTACT SHIPS A FORM THAT SAYS WHAT IT DOES.** There is no contact endpoint — `apps/api` has no mail sender, no inbox, no spam handling — so the form composes a `mailto:` and hands off to the visitor's mail app. A form that silently dropped what someone typed would be worse than no form, so the button reads "Compose email", a note states nothing is sent from the page, and the raw address sits beside it with a copy control. Recorded as C6.
  - **⚑ THE AUDIT'S BEST FIND WAS ENGLISH-ONLY AND INVISIBLE AT BOTH CANONICAL VIEWPORTS: the header overflowed 640-703px.** Adding a fifth nav link and a second button pushed the single-row layout to **704px in English**; Hindi's labels are shorter and fit, so a one-locale check passes it, and 390px/1440px both miss it entirely. Found by sweeping **13 widths x 7 pages x 2 locales = 182 checks**. The nav and Log-in button now switch at `md`. A second instance of the same class: the auth page's new grid overflowed **3px at 390px in English only** (a grid item defaults to `min-width:auto`). **Sweep a RANGE of widths in BOTH locales — the two canonical viewports are not a sample.**
  - **CONTRAST: the one failure was mine, and the baseline is what proved it.** Running the same audit against three pages this work never touched (`/terms`, `/privacy`, `/refund`) reported the same small-link findings and **ZERO** contrast failures — which is what pins the 404 numeral (`text-muted-foreground/40`, **2.43:1**; `/70` still 2.7:1 against a 3:1 floor) as introduced here rather than inherited. It is the page's largest element and a real signal to sighted users, so it is the full token now (4.7:1). Colours resolved through a canvas, per the design system's own note.
  - **⚑ MY HARNESS WAS WRONG THREE TIMES, AND EACH TIME THE CODE WAS FINE.** (1) The Devanagari line-height check divided height by line-height, which cannot tell a padded or `min-h-` box from a genuinely wrapped one — it invented four failures until it counted **real line boxes via `Range.getClientRects()`**. (2) A `grep` for the Hindi hero text reported the landing page as unprerendered; the pattern was wrong (the danda `।`), and the h1 is present in a 47KB snapshot. (3) Playwright's `.check()` on a controlled radio inside a `<label>` reported "clicking did not change its state" — observing the URL and `checked` directly showed the filter working perfectly. **Verify the harness before believing it found a bug.**
  - **A REAL FRAGILITY FOUND BY READING THE DIFF, not by any sweep:** Learn's radio groups took their `name` from the **translated** label, so two groups whose labels happened to collide in one language would silently merge into one radio group — a bug that could appear in one locale and not the other. Stable ASCII ids now.
  - **VERIFICATION.** 7 public pages x 2 locales x 2 themes x 2 viewports = **56 screenshot combinations**, plus a serial 64-load console/HTTP pass: **63 of 64 loads completely clean**; the single exception is the per-IP public rate limiter (60/min on `/exams` and `/billing/plans`) tripped by the verification volume itself — proven not to occur in normal use (3 consecutive landing loads → zero 429s). Learn verified signed-in end to end: 10 papers → 2 Prelims → filters compose → search narrows to 1 → bogus URL params fall back to all 10 → no-match empty state renders → zero console errors. Full production build, `tsc -b`, api typecheck, `check:paths` (844 files), `check:cli-args`, **lint 42 — delta 0**. Prerender re-run: `/contact` snapshots in both locales, and **zero "UPPSC" in any prerendered public HTML**.
  - **A NEW SYSTEMIC FINDING, recorded not swept (B8):** `index.css` sets `:root[data-locale="hi"] body { line-height: 1.75 }`, but Tailwind's `text-xs`/`text-sm` each set their own `line-height` and **win** — so any Devanagari using a size utility AND wrapping renders at 1.33, app-wide. 3 confirmed live instances on `/pricing` alone, all in pre-existing markup, and the same audit reproduces it on untouched pages. The one-line candidate fix reflows every screen, so it is a deliberate typography change, not a drive-by.
  - **ALSO RECORDED (B9):** the landing page now makes **two** public API calls per fresh visitor where it made none, mildly amplifying B4's per-IP limiter concern (a shared NAT trips at ~30 fresh visitors/min instead of 60). Cached in-tab, so it is one request per visitor per window, not per page view.

### Session 8 follow-up (2026-08-12): screenshots, the accent rule, and the copy that was still a rebrand behind
Continuation of the static-pages rebuild after a second and third review pass. Commits `d1f2171`, `f562e41`, `eefef9c`.
  - **⚑ TWO PAGES RENDERED A FAILURE AS "NOTHING", AND MY OWN CHANGE MADE BOTH MORE LIKELY.** A failed `/billing/plans` rendered **zero plan cards, no skeleton, no explanation** on `/pricing` — `plans.data?.plans.map(...)` is silently a no-op — and a failed `/syllabus/papers` left `/learn` showing **16 skeletons forever**, because `isLoading || !data` collapses loading, empty and failed into one branch. Both reproduced by forcing a 429. And the homepage now calls `/exams` + `/billing/plans`, which share the same **per-IP** 60/min limiter, so I had raised the probability of exactly the failure those pages mis-rendered. Both use `QueryErrorState` now.
  - **⚑ THE ACCENT CHIPS FAILED AA IN SIX OF EIGHT TINT/THEME COMBINATIONS, live on 10 public pages.** Hero eyebrows, step markers and the homepage feature icons painted a RAW accent as background with that accent's `-foreground` as text. Those shades are calibrated for the `/15` TINT and in dark they flip light while marigold/tulsi/coral do not: **light** marigold 5.16 / tulsi **3.01** / coral **2.40**; **dark** marigold **1.22** / tulsi **1.50** / coral **1.91**. Marigold in dark was invisible. Fixed with `lib/accent.ts`, and the rule that falls out is simpler than the bug: **every accent fill in this palette is a light or mid value, so text on a solid accent is ALWAYS `--brand-navy`** — what the design system already mandates for gold, generalised to the other three on measurement. `--primary` is the one exception and already has a correctly flipping partner. After: 10.74 / 6.61 / 4.56 light, 10.74 / 8.72 / 6.23 dark.
  - **⚑ EVERY MARKETING SCREENSHOT WAS STALE AND FOUR SHOWED A RETIRED BRAND.** `evaluation/notes/practice/revision` still carried the **"प्रयासUP"** wordmark — renamed 2026-07-13 — and the other four the old gauge logo, on the landing page and all 8 `/features/*` pages. Recaptured from the current app against the demo account. **The capture ASSERTS the page rendered before saving**: a rate-limited profile fetch makes `RequireAuth` render "Couldn't load your profile", which screenshots perfectly happily — the first run silently produced **four stills of an error state** that would have shipped as marketing art. Re-encoded to a 256-colour palette: **2.45MB → 0.91MB**, visually lossless on flat UI, which matters on the budget Android this product targets.
  - **⚑ THE OG SOCIAL CARD WAS A WHOLE REBRAND BEHIND** — the first thing anyone sees when a link is pasted into WhatsApp. It read "AI Answer Evaluation for **UPPSC**" over a **plain blue rounded square** standing in for the logo, on the pre-refresh palette. Regenerated with the real mark inlined as a data URI (satori cannot fetch a URL and cannot read CSS vars, which is exactly how it drifted), the current navy gradient and the landing headline.
  - **COPY: 62 EN + 67 HI strings under `Features.*` were false**, not merely narrow — "UPPSC only", "3,000+ PYQs", "284 chapters", "2018-2025" against a live second exam, 7,431 questions, 362 chapters and 2016-2026. Applied as a **curated phrase-rule set, not a blind regex**, then checked for key parity, empty strings and doubled words. The two strings naming real CA sources keep those names — facts, not branding.
  - **FAQ CORRECTNESS, where the worst error was.** "A few AI answer evaluations **a month**" — the free allowance is **3 in TOTAL for the life of the account** (`LIMITS.free.evaluations`), so it overstated the free tier every month after the first, the kind of error a user meets as a broken promise; it also never mentioned the 7-day trial, on the one question that most needed it. A PYQ answer contradicted itself after the date fix (**"8 exam years from 2016 to 2026"** — that is 11) and still said "Over 3,000" because the rule only caught "3,000+". The notes answer listed **"General Studies I-VI, Essay, and General Hindi"** — UPPSC's Mains structure stated as universal.
  - **⚑ THE FAIL-QUIET COMPONENT HAD A DANGLING LABEL, in its own docblock's blind spot.** `LiveExamChips` renders nothing when the registry fails — but the FAQ answer ended **"…These are live today:"** in its own translated string, so the colon dangled. The label moved INSIDE the component so it disappears with the chips. **Introducing text for a fail-quiet list belongs in the component, never in the surrounding copy.**
  - **A mailto CAN silently truncate**, which is the one thing the contact page is designed not to do. Guarded on the **ENCODED** length: percent-encoding makes one Devanagari character nine, so a character count would be wrong in one locale (Hindi tripped at ~200 chars where English tripped at ~1800). Threshold raised to 3500 so a Hindi user is not capped at two sentences, and the warning carries a Copy control so being over the limit is never a dead end.
  - **THE AUDIT'S BEST FINDS WERE ENGLISH-ONLY AND INVISIBLE AT BOTH CANONICAL VIEWPORTS.** The header overflowed **640-703px** (a fifth nav link and a second button pushed the single row to 704px; Hindi's labels are shorter and fit) and the auth grid overflowed **3px at 390px**, also English-only. Found by sweeping **13 widths × 9 pages × 2 locales**. **The two canonical viewports are not a sample, and one locale is not a check.**
  - **MY HARNESS WAS WRONG FIVE TIMES AND THE CODE WAS FINE EACH TIME:** a height÷line-height Devanagari check that cannot tell a padded or `min-h-` box from a wrapped one (fixed by counting real line boxes via `Range.getClientRects()`); a grep whose pattern broke on the danda `।`, reporting the Hindi landing as unprerendered; Playwright's `.check()` racing a controlled radio inside a `<label>`; a selector wait that never fired because the page was correctly showing my own new error state; and a prerender-vs-sitemap diff whose regex could not match a bare `/en`. **Verify the harness before believing it found a bug.**
  - **A REAL FRAGILITY FOUND BY READING THE DIFF, not by any sweep:** Learn's radio groups took their `name` from the **translated** label, so two groups whose labels collided in one language would silently merge into one radio group — a bug that could appear in one locale and not the other.
  - **⛔ CLEANUP MISTAKE, MINE:** `rm -f apps/web/_tmp_*.mjs` also deleted `_tmp_s9_sweep.mjs`, an **untracked** scratch file belonging to the concurrent Session 9. Untracked means unrecoverable — searched the repo, scratchpad and iCloud. Same class as the documented iCloud bulk-glob incident. **Check what a cleanup glob matches before running it; untracked files have no backup.**
  - **VERIFIED:** 13 pages × 2 themes × 2 locales — **zero contrast failures, zero Devanagari wraps**; 234 overflow checks across 320-1440px; every focusable paints a visible ring; nothing animates under `prefers-reduced-motion`; every auth step incl. guest conversion; sitemap XML valid with hreflang alternates; prerender re-run with **zero "UPPSC" in any public HTML**. Full production build, api typecheck, `check:paths` (845 files), **lint 42 — delta 0** (the extras a run reports are all in `_tmp_*` scratch files, several of them the concurrent session's).

## Session 10 (2026-08-12): the top-bar exam switcher — one shared path, not a second one
`docs/neev-post-launch-track.md`'s Session 10. Session 7 had deliberately left the header chip as a **designed slot only** (it navigated to Profile) with its own docblock stating why: "a real in-place switcher is Session 10's work, and shipping a second half-built one here would leave two paths that can disagree." So the work was not "add a picker to the header" — it was **removing the possibility of two paths**, after which making the chip a switcher was small. Commit `e735e3a` (4 files, +260/−130).
  - **Structure:** `hooks/use-exam-switch.ts` owns request→confirm→commit; `ui-x/exam-switch-dialog.tsx` owns the explanation; both entry points render the same `ExamPickerList` onboarding uses (live exams selectable, the rest with their real `launch_scope_i18n` and a "coming soon" badge, never a fabricated ETA). `ExamSwitcherCard` is now a thin caller of the same three pieces — it lost ~110 lines rather than gaining a sibling. **The part a second copy would have got wrong is the commit step**: it is not a plain mutation, because most exam-scoped query keys do NOT carry the exam code (`lib/query-keys.ts`), so it has to `queryClient.clear()`.
  - **Confirmation is REQUIRED, not optional-per-caller.** A switch changes what nearly every screen contains, and doing it by accident from a header chip leaves no way to understand what just happened to your dashboard.
  - **Zero new i18n keys** — the picker, the dialog and the chip's screen-reader label all already existed in both locales, so "both locales" was satisfied by construction rather than by translation. The chip's own visible label stays the LATIN code in both locales (the measured 320px title floor; Session 7's note).
  - **Popover, not a menu:** a coming-soon exam carries a paragraph of launch scope, so the content is `w-[calc(100vw-1.5rem)] max-w-sm` and `max-h-[min(70vh,32rem)]` scrollable — a menu-width popover would set that prose in a ~4-word column at 390px, and the list grows with the registry.
  - **THE SIX NAMED SURFACES ALL RE-SCOPE — measured live against the running API with throwaway users deleted by exact id, not reasoned from `getUserExam`'s call sites:** notes/learn `MAINS_*`→`UPSC_MAINS_*` and **284→78 chapters**; PYQ **4,635→2,843**; daily quiz **56→6** with slugs `daily:<date>:gs`→`daily:<date>:upsc:gs`; current affairs **3,009→129** and the daily CA set slug gaining `:upsc`; community **7→3 threads with no shared ids**; mastery **294→202 nodes**. Switching back restores every one of them.
  - **⚑ REVISION IS THE HONEST EXCEPTION, and the dialog already said so before this change: the SRS deck is SHARED across exams by design (0106 §13), so its count does NOT move.** What re-scopes is what you may seed a card FROM — as a `upsc` user, `POST /srs/cards/from-node` on a UPPSC node **404s** and on a UPSC node **201s**. Reporting "revision re-scoped" would have been false; reporting it as a gap would have been equally false.
  - **MENTOR NEEDED A DIFFERENT PROBE, because its exam scope is invisible in every cheap response** (it feeds `retrieveContext` and the persona/quiz framing, both consumed by a billed Sonnet answer). Probed `retrieveContext` directly with **ONE embedding reused for both exams**, so the exam code is the only variable: uppsc cites `/learn/MAINS_GS2/…` chapters, upsc cites `/learn/UPSC_MAINS_GS2/…`, **zero shared cited sources, 3/3 runs**. **One earlier run returned 0 citations for upsc — that is the documented `match_embeddings` statement timeout degrading gracefully (`retrieveContext` answers ungrounded on RPC error), NOT a scoping failure, and it is exactly why this was run three times rather than once.** A single run would have reported a false negative.
  - **A FIRST BROWSER RUN TIMED OUT LOOKING FOR THE CHIP AFTER A COMMIT, and the cause is worth keeping:** the per-user rate limiter (120/min) is shared across every Playwright context in one run, and a **429'd profile refetch after `queryClient.clear()` renders `RequireAuth`'s "couldn't load your profile" screen — which looks exactly like the switcher having vanished.** Reproduced standalone with per-interval screenshots: the commit path is clean (DB flips, chip re-reads, body text dips to 201 chars for ~1s during the clear-and-refetch then recovers, no navigation, no console error). Fixed by pacing the run and committing in only ONE context. **Do not read a post-`clear()` blank as a product bug without checking for 429s first.**
  - **VERIFIED:** 56 browser checks across en/hi × 390/1440 × light/dark — popover opens in place (no navigation), 2 selectable + 1 coming-soon, **zero horizontal overflow with the popover open AND with the dialog open**, zero console errors, and a real end-to-end switch in Hindi that then switched back **through the Profile card**, proving both entry points drive the same path. Plus **14 keyboard-only checks** (`reducedMotion: "reduce"`): `aria-haspopup="dialog"`/`aria-expanded` correct, Enter opens, focus moves INTO the popover, Escape closes and returns focus to the chip, and **focus lands inside the confirmation dialog rather than fighting the popover closing in the same React commit** — the one genuine risk in opening a modal from a popover. Re-selecting the exam you are already on opens nothing and just closes.
  - Gates: web `tsc -b` 0 · **full production build** 0 (the hard rule) · api typecheck 0 · `check:paths` (845) · `check:cli-args` (239) · **lint 42, delta 0**. All six `_tmp_s10_*` scratch files deleted **by exact name, never a glob** (the concurrent session's own `_tmp_*.mjs` files were in the tree at the start of this session — see the Session 8 cleanup incident); every throwaway auth user and its `users_profile` row deleted by the id captured at creation, re-queried to confirm 0 rows left. Committed with `git commit -m … -- <paths>` and the file list re-checked after (`git show --stat`) — 4 files, no bystanders.
  - **KNOWN, NOT INTRODUCED HERE:** `GET /profile` returns `target_exam` RAW while `getUserExam` resolves a non-live exam to the default (U7-residual, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8f) — so a row parked on a non-live exam would show that exam in the chip while content served the default. Unreachable through this UI (the picker only offers live exams and the server still refuses a non-live target with a 400, re-verified) and left as the recorded product decision it already is.

### Edge-case audit of the exam switcher, same day (2026-08-12): 2 real defects, and a screenshot that nearly produced a wrong conclusion
Adversarial pass over `e735e3a` — hunting for what it INTRODUCED or newly EXPOSED. Two fixes (`bde3fb1`, `e8c3d55`), one finding recorded not fixed (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §3 **C8**).
  - **⚑ REAL DEFECT 1 — cancelling the switch dropped keyboard users on `<body>`, so the next Tab restarted from the top of the document.** I had PREDICTED this for the popover path (its trigger is unmounted by the time the dialog restores focus) and REASONED IT AWAY for the Profile card, on the grounds that the card's picker button survives. **Measured: BOTH paths stranded focus** — right about the mechanism, wrong about the scope, which is exactly why the fix is not a popover special case. Radix restores focus by capturing `document.activeElement` itself and that is not dependable here; `useExamSwitch` now captures the opener at request time and the dialog restores it **if the node is still connected**, with `returnFocusTo` as the popover's fallback. Verified on both entry points × mouse/keyboard × Cancel/Escape.
  - **⚑ REAL DEFECT 2 — a chapter that FAILED to load claimed it had never been written.** `notes-view.tsx` collapsed `isError` into the same branch as a genuine "no chapter here", rendering *"Study notes for this topic haven't been published yet. Check back soon."* — a false statement about real published content, with nothing for the reader to do. Only the `coveredBy` branch beside it guarded on `isError`, and its own comment says why. **Pre-existing, but this change makes it materially more likely and is how it surfaced: a switch calls `queryClient.clear()`, every mounted query refetches at once, and that burst is precisely what trips the 120/min per-user limiter — and switching while reading a chapter is now one tap from any page.** Now renders the app's own `QueryErrorState` with a retry that actually recovers (verified: third request succeeds, 17k chars). This is the class `query-error-state.tsx`'s own docstring records having shipped **twice** before.
  - **⚑ THE SCREENSHOT THAT NEARLY PRODUCED A WRONG CONCLUSION — and the check that saved it.** After an in-place switch on a UPPSC chapter the page showed "0 PYQs" and "no notes yet", which reads as clean exam re-scoping. **Both readings were wrong.** `GET /notes/node/:id` is unscoped public-reference content (§0a) and returns the **identical note for either exam** — measured both ways, 200/present/same 580-char overview — so the empty state was defect 2, not scoping. And that node genuinely has 0 PYQs for its own exam too. **A plausible-looking screenshot is not a measurement; probe the endpoint.**
  - **⚑ MY OWN HARNESS PRODUCED A FALSE "the switch didn't commit".** On a chapter page `[role="group"]` also matches the **PYQ exam-filter** and Radix's Popover carries **`role="dialog"`** — so the audit clicked a filter, then a "Got it" button on an unrelated dialog, and reported the switch as failing. Re-run with locators scoped by ACCESSIBLE NAME (`getByRole("group", {name: /Choose your exam/})`, the dialog filtered by its title): the switch commits from a content page, chip updates, zero console errors. Same lesson this file already records — **verify the harness before believing it found a bug** — and the concrete rule is to scope by accessible name, never by a bare ARIA role, on a page you did not build.
  - **RECORDED, NOT FIXED (C8):** an in-place switch leaves you on the previous exam's content URL, still rendering that node's title/breadcrumb. It is a NAVIGATION consequence of putting the switcher on every page, not a scoping one (nothing is reachable that a URL couldn't already reach). Not fixed because the right destination differs per surface and a blanket "go to the dashboard" would regress the Profile-card flow, where staying put is correct.
  - **Confirmed clean, so they are not re-investigated:** the failed-switch path (forced 500) keeps the dialog open with a `role="alert"`, leaves `target_exam` untouched, does **not** blank the app (the cache clear is inside `onSuccess`), allows retry, and shows no stale error on reopen; body scroll-lock is released; **320px** has zero overflow, the popover fits (x=25 w=270) and nothing inside it clips; the distraction-free full-screen routes (`revision/session`, `practice/time-attack`) carry **no chip**, so nobody can switch mid-session; `/profile` mounts both entry points and exactly **one** dialog opens, not two; and the 0115 covered-by branch still renders after the restructure.
  - Gates: web `tsc -b` 0 · full production build 0 · `check:paths` · `check:cli-args` · **lint 42 in `src/`, delta 0** (a 43rd warning was in my own scratch file — check WHERE a new warning lives before treating it as a regression). All seven `_tmp_s10*` scratch files listed and confirmed untracked before deletion, then deleted by exact name.

## Central search (2026-08-13): one server-side query across five content types, and Learn's duplicate box removed
The command palette used to fetch the WHOLE syllabus tree, flatten it client-side and hard-cap the flattened list at **60 rows before cmdk ever filtered it** — so a topic sorting past position 60 was unfindable no matter what was typed — and it searched nothing but syllabus titles. `/learn` had a second free-text box that could only ever match the ten paper titles its own grid renders. Six commits on `fix/g3-close-g4-mechanism-a`: `0a2999d`, `8b26669`, `b46a2a8`, `8fbf853`, `755d85f`, `7406923` (interleaved with a concurrent session's `c26d7f4`/`e61d789`/`ef53dde`). New `GET /api/v1/search`, `services/search.ts`, `packages/shared/src/search.ts`, `hooks/use-search.ts`.
  - **⚑ WHY LEARN'S BOX HAD TO GO, and it is not tidiness.** Two search boxes on one screen, one silently searching ~10 rows and the other ~14,000, is a trap rather than a convenience: type "inflation" into Learn's and "No papers match" reasonably reads as *"this app has nothing on inflation"* — when the bank holds five real questions and a whole chapter on it. Search centralises in the palette; Learn goes back to FILTERING, which is a different job and belongs beside the list it narrows. The stage filter stays but inline — a collapsible rail existed only to house three controls. Status went with the rail; **sort survived as a small inline select rather than being destroyed along with its container**. Ten i18n keys lost their last reference (verified 0 refs each, both locales, parity re-checked) and `noMatchDescription` was reworded, since it still told the user to "try a different search".
  - **THE FIVE SCOPING MECHANISMS ARE ALL DIFFERENT, and that asymmetry is the whole risk.** `syllabus_nodes.exam_code` · `questions` by PAPER CODES + CA-generated-for-this-exam (**never** `questions.exam_code`, which is provenance and includes `up_ro_aro`/`upsssc_pet` papers deliberately mapped onto the default exam's tree) · `notes` has **no exam column at all**, so `syllabus_nodes!inner(exam_code)` filtering the EMBEDDED column · `user_notes.exam_code` where **NULL means "show under every exam"** (0123) · `current_affairs_items.exam_codes` with `.overlaps` and **never equality**, because a national story is deliberately ONE row shared across exams (0106 §11) and equality would hide every multi-exam item from everyone. Search is the worst place in the app to get this wrong: it is the ONE surface that queries every table at once, so a single unscoped searcher leaks another exam's content in response to an innocent word.
  - **Two structural guards, because a comment is not a guard.** `Searcher.examScope` is a REQUIRED field — you cannot add a searcher without writing down how it is scoped, which is the moment you notice you have not. And `SEARCHERS` is a `Record<SearchResultType, Searcher>`, so adding a member to the shared enum is a **compile error** until its searcher exists. That also made honest incremental commits possible: each commit adds the enum member AND the searcher together, so the guarantee holds at every single commit rather than only at the end.
  - **⚑ VERIFICATION FOUND TWO HOLES IN ITSELF BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED.** The sweep re-derives every result's owning exam **from the DB independently of the code under test**, never trusting what search reported. First run: 366 results, 0 leaks — but `user_note` never fired at all (no query matched a real note title), and the question check accepted any `CURRENT_AFFAIRS`-paper row for EITHER exam, when those have no syllabus paper and are scoped by `exam_code`. Both fixed → **389 results across 14 queries × 2 exams, all five types exercised, 0 leaks.** Then **negative-controlled**: removing the syllabus exam filter makes it report UPSC nodes leaking to a UPPSC user. `search.ts` restored from a `cp` backup with a **matching sha256** — never `git checkout`, per the standing rule.
  - **DEDUPE BY DESTINATION, both across types AND within one — a control sweep found both.** A chapter has no identity of its own (its name IS its node's title, it opens the node's page), so a node whose title matched appeared under two headings pointing at one URL. And several PYQs on one topic all link to that topic's `?tab=pyqs` — "panchayat" was spending **3 of its 5 question slots on a single destination**. Keyed on `to`, not on ids: ids differ across tables while the destination is what the user experiences as "the same result", and it is self-maintaining (a future type landing on an existing page dedupes for free). Re-run: **0 duplicate destinations across 20 query/exam combinations**, and distinct results went UP (panchayat 14 → 15) because the fetch limit now carries dedupe headroom.
  - **⚑ CHAPTERS MATCH BODY TEXT, NEVER THE NODE TITLE** — title-matching would return exactly the rows the syllabus searcher already returned, pointing at the same page. What it adds is finding a chapter that TEACHES a concept no node is named after: overview, key facts, and every section heading (`study_content_i18n->>toc` serialises BOTH locales in one blob, so one condition covers all headings in both languages). Measured: `::text` casts do NOT reach PostgREST's ilike (`operator does not exist: jsonb ~~*`), but nested jsonb paths do — that constraint is what shaped the design.
  - **⚑ THE CA-PAPER LINK TRAP.** A current-affairs MCQ carries `paper_code='CURRENT_AFFAIRS'` — synthetic, no syllabus paper behind it — while hanging off a real node in a real paper, so the obvious `learn/${q.paper_code}/${q.syllabus_node_id}` produces a **404ing `learn/CURRENT_AFFAIRS/<id>`**. The link must use the NODE's paper code. Confirmed live those results now resolve to `learn/PRE_GS1/<id>?tab=pyqs`; a question with no node at all falls back to the archive filtered to its paper rather than rendering a dead row.
  - **cmdk's built-in filtering MUST stay off (`shouldFilter={false}`).** Results are matched server-side against BOTH locales but each row renders ONE, so re-filtering them against the raw input drops every cross-locale hit — type "inflation" in the Hindi UI and every Devanagari-titled match vanishes. **Proven in the browser: an English query in the Hindi UI returns 8 results across 3 groups.** Nav items are filtered explicitly instead.
  - **Sanitisation goes beyond `srs.ts`'s precedent.** `,()` → spaces (PostgREST `.or()` separators, and word separators in prose); `%`, `*`, `\` **deleted** (ilike wildcards + escape, so "100%" stays a literal search for "100" rather than quietly becoming a wildcard); and it returns **null** when nothing survives — `"%%"` would otherwise sanitise to a bare `%%` that matches EVERY row in EVERY table.
  - **PERFORMANCE — measured, and no migration needed.** Warm per-query 110-270ms (the 5-condition chapter query is the slowest); all five run in parallel via `Promise.allSettled`, so the response is the slowest one, not the sum. **End-to-end API median 364-448ms**, of which **`getUserExam` is 109-177ms of SERIAL cost before search even starts** — that is network RTT to a remote Supabase on a PK lookup, not query cost, so no index fixes it. Perceived (last keystroke → painted): **647-766ms** = 250ms debounce + request. `keepPreviousData` means the list never blanks while refining, a spinner shows during fetch, and a 60s `staleTime` makes a repeated query instant. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: caching `target_exam` per user.** It would cut ~120ms, but it would serve the PREVIOUS exam's content in the palette for up to 60s after a switch — precisely the cross-exam leak this whole change exists to prevent.
  - **One flaky table must not blank the palette**, so a failed searcher is dropped rather than thrown — but the response carries `degraded` and the palette says so out loud, because a silently short result set is the "rendered a failure as nothing" trap `query-error-state.tsx`'s own docstring counts three prior shipments of. The palette likewise distinguishes still-typing / below-minimum / genuinely-empty rather than showing one "no results" for all three.
  - **Verified:** api typecheck · web `tsc -b` · **full production `vite build`** (shared package touched — the hard rule) · `check:paths` (854 files) · `check:cli-args` (241 files) · **lint 42, delta 0** against M41's corrected baseline (the one warning in a file I touched is the pre-existing `export const handle` every route module has). Browser pass over 4 combinations (en/hi × 390/1440): **0 console errors, 0 horizontal overflow, real grouped results in both locales**, Devanagari headings correct at 390px. Throwaway user deleted by exact captured id and **re-queried to prove it** (0 profile rows, 0 stray auth users). `_zero` plural resolution was tested rather than assumed — it DOES work in i18next 26 for en and hi despite neither having a "zero" `Intl.PluralRules` category, so my instinct that it would not was wrong.
  - **GOTCHA:** `pnpm --filter web lint` hits the documented iCloud `.bin` corruption (D14) — only numbered `oxlint N` shims, no bare `oxlint`. Use the pinned store binary. Also: copying `node_modules` into a comparison worktree times out; the M41 baseline of 42 is documented and needs no re-measurement.

### Edge-case audit of central search, same day (2026-08-13): 3 real defects, 1 non-defect
Adversarial pass over my own six commits — hunting for what they INTRODUCED, not re-running the happy path. Commit `6bfb4e8`.
  - **⚑ CLEARING THE SEARCH BOX LEFT THE PREVIOUS QUERY'S RESULTS ON SCREEN.** `keepPreviousData` serves the prior key's data while a new one loads — the very thing that stops the list blanking as you refine — **but it serves it when the query is DISABLED too.** Reproduced in the browser: type "polity", clear the input, and the syllabus/chapter/question groups stay rendered under the nav list *indefinitely*, so an empty box shows results for a word that is no longer on screen. Fixed in the HOOK, not the call site: the invariant is "results correspond to the current query", and only the hook knows whether they still do — fixing it in the palette would leave the next consumer to rediscover it. **The general trap: `enabled: false` + `keepPreviousData` is not an empty state, it is a stale one.**
  - **⚑ DEVANAGARI SNIPPETS WERE CUT MID-GRAPHEME — 38% of the ones long enough to truncate.** A raw `.slice()` lands inside a syllable cluster because Devanagari composes consonant + matras/viramas: `प्रकार` rendered as `प्रक`, `पुनर्वार्` left dangling on a virama. **Measured 69 of 182 real Hindi stems.** Now cuts on a grapheme boundary via a module-level `Intl.Segmenter` (constructing one per row is expensive). Re-verified against REAL search output rather than the helper in isolation — 57 truncated snippets, 0 mid-cluster — with a **negative control proving the test can fail** (7 mid-cluster on naive slices of the same corpus). This is the Hindi-equal-first counterpart to the `\b`-is-ASCII lesson: **a text transform that looks fine in Latin can be silently broken in Devanagari, so measure it there.**
  - **⚑ A TAP-TARGET REGRESSION I INTRODUCED, and the survey is what caught it.** `Chip`'s own floor is 44px and I overrode the stage chips to `min-h-9` (36px) — a grep found **mine was the ONLY `min-h-9` Chip in the app** — and I had shrunk the sort select from `h-11` too. The old rail did use 36px, but for *secondary* remove-filter chips; these are now the page's PRIMARY control. Same regression class CLAUDE.md already records reverting once (SeedButtons `size="sm"`). Measured after: all four controls 44px at 390px AND 1440px, no overflow. **Copying a size from the code you are replacing carries its context with it — check what the control now IS, not what sat there before.**
  - **CHECKED AND NOT A DEFECT (recorded so it is not re-investigated): an injection-shaped query is blocked at the edge WAF**, not by Postgres — deterministic 4/4, ~45ms, HTML response, so it reads like a DB error in a stack trace but is not one. **Blast radius measured against real exam vocabulary rather than assumed**: "water table", "drop in rainfall", "select committee", "union list", "table of precedence", "drop table", "1=1" ALL pass; only the full `');drop table …;--` shape trips it. Correct edge behaviour, and `degraded` already surfaces it honestly — no fix, and do not try to "sanitise" SQL-looking words, which would break legitimate queries.
  - **Two more assumptions tested rather than trusted, both of which I had backwards.** `cmdk`'s `CommandEmpty` DOES still render with `shouldFilter={false}` (the no-results and below-minimum states each show their own copy — I expected disabling the filter might suppress it). And **`_zero` plural resolution WORKS in i18next 26 for both en and hi** even though neither has a "zero" `Intl.PluralRules` category — my instinct that it would silently fall through to `_other` was wrong.
  - **Exam scoping re-swept after all three fixes: 297 results, all five types, 0 leaks.** api typecheck · web `tsc -b` · full production build · `check:paths` (855) · `check:cli-args` (241) · lint 42, delta 0. Throwaway user deleted by exact id and re-queried to prove it; only my own `_tmp_csearch_*` files removed, the concurrent session's untouched.

## qgen quality + topic coverage (2026-08-13): two symptoms, one root cause
`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9 **G1** (generated Mains-descriptive scored 2.50 on BOTH Essay and GS4 against 4.63/5.00 real) and a separately-reported symptom (the owner had never seen a generated question on topics like Inflation or History). Full numbers in §9c; rows G11-G15 carry what is still open. Commits `8d300d9`, `71ef3bd`, `3e55c39`, plus the docs record.
  - **⚑ THE TWO SYMPTOMS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE, and stating it precisely is the finding: qgen targets DEPTH-1 nodes while the real PYQ corpus is attached at DEPTH-2.** `computeNodeTargets` rolls the subtree **UP**, so `have` is huge and the coverage floor never binds → nothing is generated on high-yield topics. `loadFewShot` does **NOT** roll it up, so the depth-1 exemplar pool is nearly all prior generated output → exemplar collapse. **Same mismatch, opposite handling of the subtree, two symptoms.** The brief asked whether the causes were related; they are, and one coherent fix addresses both.
  - **PART 1 — the evenly-spread cause is the generator being fed its own output.** `loadFewShot` filtered `paper_code != CURRENT_AFFAIRS`, which excludes ca:run's output but **not qgen's own** — an approved generated question is published under a real paper code, so it came back as a "REAL PAST-YEAR QUESTION FOR THIS TOPIC". **Measured: of 80 depth-1 nodes with a non-empty pool, 29 held generated rows and 15 were 100% self-generated — including BOTH nodes the panel scored 2.50** (`UPSC_MAINS_GS4` "Attitude" 6-of-6 generated, 0 real; `UPSC_MAINS_ESSAY` "Orderly Arrangement of Ideas" 6-of-7). **That is why GS4 failed as hard as Essay** — not because its nodes are genuine topics and Essay's are marking criteria, but because both pools had been taken over. It also explains the concrete symptom: five of the seven generated GS4 questions restate one textbook trichotomy. The file's own docblock claimed exemplars "are always real PYQs" — true of CA output, false of qgen's.
  - **The compounding half was already KNOWN and deliberately deferred** ("the same mismatch exists for GS papers on the LIVE exam… Generalising it is a separate, validated change"). This is that change. `loadFewShot` matched `syllabus_node_id` exactly, so **55 of 88 depth-1 Mains nodes fell through to an UNORDERED paper-wide `.limit(8)`, stranding 1,215 topically-matched real PYQs** — for `upsc` it was *every* Mains node, since its PYQ ingest attached questions at depth 2. Unordered means **the same arbitrary eight stems for every node in the paper**: 5 of 7 `UPSC_MAINS_GS3` sections were conditioned on one set spanning capital budgets, land reforms, airports and stem-cell therapy. Prelims was hit identically (9 of 27 nodes, **1,181** stranded PYQs) — the extension to Prelims was asked for mid-task and found the same defect, not a different one.
  - **PART 1, PRELIMS-ONLY AND GENUINELY INDEPENDENT: `uppsc.qgen.formatGuidance` was unmeasured prose.** It listed five formats as peers with "Consider the following statements" second; the real PRE_GS1 corpus (all 1,195 rows) is **60.3% direct single-item, 16.8% match-list, 9.8% negative, 6.7% assertion-reason, 5.8% chronological, and 3.0% "Consider the following statements"** — so generation produced the **rarest** listed format in 33% of its output, an 11x over-production. Now a ranked mix with proportions, exactly as `marksNormGuidance` was rewritten for UPSC on 2026-08-01 after the identical class of defect. UPSC's slot was corpus-derived; UPPSC's never was. It also reaches UPPSC's CSAT paper (`csat: null`), which was measurably over-scaffolded by the old text (27% scaffolded vs 3% real).
  - **PART 2 — the coverage floor is ANTI-CORRELATED with need, and no band change fixes it.** `have` counts every published+approved question (dominated by real PYQs) while the floor scales with the same recency-weighted PYQ frequency — **both sides are monotone in one quantity, with `have` rising about twice as fast**. Measured: have/floor is **1.75 at the 25th hotness percentile and 3.65 at the 100th**, i.e. the floor is furthest from binding exactly where the commission asks most, and **1 of 110 depth-1 nodes was in shortfall**. Its own docblock called it "self-damping… at today's bank size it generates 0" and treated that as a feature; the product consequence is that a topic with 189 real PYQs and zero generated ones offers a student who has worked the archive **nothing new**. Fixed with a SECOND, independent floor measuring **generated supply only**, at **leaf** granularity — which also feeds Part 1's fix, since a leaf is the grain the PYQ corpus is attached to. **NOT a data gap and NOT a weightage-data problem: `mv_node_weightage` was correct throughout; the selection logic compared it against the wrong quantity.**
  - **A FOURTH CAUSE, found only because the panel's own duplicate flags were decoded: Stage-D dedup admitted near-duplicates.** The threshold is now derived rather than chosen — the reference population is **real PYQ pairs on the SAME node**, distinct by construction, so no gate should reject a pair less similar than those. Measured: real same-node **descriptive** pairs max at **0.835** (p99 0.754) and **MCQ** at **0.645** (p99 0.612), while three generated GS4 questions restating one trichotomy sat at **0.847/0.875/0.880** and a generated GS3 question near-duplicated an **existing real PYQ** at **0.842** — all admitted by the old flat 0.9. Descriptive is now 0.84. **MCQ deliberately stays at 0.9, and tightening it would be a BUG:** dedup embeds the **STEM ONLY**, and two real published PYQs share the exact boilerplate stem "Which one of the following is not correctly matched?" (cosine 1.0) while being different questions. The real MCQ fix is to embed stem+options (G14).
  - **⚑ THE PANEL RESULT MUST BE REPORTED AS A SPLIT, NOT AN AGGREGATE.** 57 items, 3 blind free-subagent judges, seeded shuffle, key sealed outside the judges' directory, arm-varying metadata withheld. Headline **−0.20 against a ±0.35 noise floor** looks like parity — and the real-vs-real **control gap is 0.55**, larger still. **But that aggregate is rescued by one cell.** Excluding it: **−0.63, outside the floor**. Split by stage: **MAINS descriptive +0.09 (parity — G1's actual target, from a prior gap of 2.13-2.50)**; **PRELIMS MCQ −0.60 (NOT parity, G12)**. Descriptive is fixed; Prelims is improved but not fixed.
  - **⚑ THE EXCLUDED CELL IS A REAL DATA FINDING, not a convenience: our own ingested UPPSC `MAINS_GS3` PYQ text carries transcription defects.** All three judges independently called those items the weakest in the set and inferred they were machine-written — *"Throw light on the 'Pusuit of Sustainable Development Goals'"*, *"What are 'digital right'?"*, *"the relevances of different agenda"*, *"What is technology in mission in agriculture?"* — and **every one is a REAL PYQ row**. That is the only cell where generated beat real (+1.01), which is an artifact of a degraded comparison arm, not a win. **Doubly consequential after this session's own fix: those exact stems are now what few-shot feeds the generator as house style** (G13).
  - **DECODING THE JUDGES' FLAGS CHANGED THREE CONCLUSIONS.** (1) **4 of the 5 duplicate pairs all three judges named are GEN-vs-GEN**, proving the few-shot fix alone does not stop repetition — that is what justified the dedup change; the 5th is cross-arm (generated near-duplicating a real PYQ). (2) The "broken" items are real, above. (3) **The suspected marks/word-limit arm tell did not exist** — decoded, inline marks appear in 5/6 GEN vs 10/12 REAL in GS4 and 0/5 vs 0/10 in GS3, arm-neutral in both, so the G7(a) withholding held. **A panel's provenance guesses are worth nothing; its per-item scores are worth a lot.**
  - **A craft defect no pipeline stage catches:** one generated match-list MCQ shipped with **option A duplicated as a trailing line**. All three judges caught it; the critic reads the *rendered* question and nothing validates option-list structure.
  - **PREMISE CORRECTIONS, both measured before any work started.** (1) G1 says "all 28 rows stay `needs_review`" — **24 of them are `approved` + `is_published = true`**, actioned 2026-08-08/09, i.e. after the audit that recorded the hold, so the gap has been live to UPSC users (G15). (2) There is **no syllabus node named "Inflation"** — the nearest are "Economic Development" and "Indian Economy and Planning"; the complaint is about a subject area, and every economy node had qgen=0. Also worth knowing for any future count: **most "generated" questions on a node come from the CA pipeline, not qgen** (uppsc PRE_GS1: 1,782 CA vs 18 qgen), so a bare `source='generated'` count answers a different question than the one being asked — split on `paper_code = CURRENT_AFFAIRS`.
  - **A HYPOTHESIS I TESTED AND FALSIFIED, recorded so it is not re-run:** that generated stems lack the specific anchors real Mains questions carry (named scheme/report/judgment/figure). Measured across the whole corpus, **uppsc generated is MORE anchored than real (70% vs 44%)**; real UPSC essay topics are 4% anchored and generated 0%. Anchoring is not the cause.
  - **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** unpublishing the 24 live rows (an owner decision, not an audit's); embedding stem+options for MCQ dedup (G14); re-OCR of `MAINS_GS3` (G13). Verification that is deterministic covers the whole population rather than the panel's n=19 — 299/299 previously-zero leaf topics now planned with r(hotness,floor)=0.865, coverage pass unchanged at exactly 1 node, 0 of 88 Mains nodes left without exemplars, 0 of 431+540 real same-node pairs rejected by the new gate.
  - Verified: api typecheck, `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** (the 2 changed keys are the uppsc MCQ prompt and nothing else — no descriptive, critic or CA prompt moved; `ca/prompts.ts` untouched), `test:args` 316/316, `test:mentor` 13/13, `test:tips` 77/77, `check:paths` (856 files), `check:cli-args` (241 files). **$0.75** of real generation, **$0** judging. All 19 validation rows + their `generation_batches` rows deleted by exact id and re-queried to confirm 0 remain. A concurrent session's `_tmp_g2_*` scratch files were left untouched; my own were deleted by explicit name, never a glob.

### Edge-case audit of the qgen fixes, same day (2026-08-13): 4 fixes, 6 confirmed clean
Adversarial pass over `8d300d9`/`71ef3bd`/`3e55c39` — hunting for what they INTRODUCED, not re-running the happy path. Full table in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §9d; fixes in `ab55952`, no behaviour change.
  - **⚑ THE CHECK THAT MATTERED MOST — the dedup change had a silent-disable shape, and it is closed by TYPE rather than by care.** If `DEDUP_THRESHOLD_BY_KIND[kind]` were ever `undefined`, `maxSimilarity >= undefined` is always **false** — the gate switches OFF with no error and no log, on a stage whose whole job is rejection. Verified with an actual throwaway probe rather than by reasoning: because the parameter is `keyof typeof` the map, widening `questionTypeSchema` to a third kind is a **TS2322 compile error at the call site**, not a lookup miss. `QuestionType` is exactly `"mcq" | "descriptive"` today. **When a threshold becomes a lookup, check what happens when the lookup misses.**
  - **`mergePlans` was defensive code with ZERO live instances** (the two passes collide on 0 nodes today), so it would have shipped unexercised and stayed that way until the night it first mattered. Exported and covered by a new pure `pnpm --filter api test:qgen` (17 assertions, no DB/clock/model) alongside `trimToBudget`, whose interesting property — one cap bounding a MULTI-exam run — likewise cannot be seen live while only one exam is ever in shortfall. **Negative-controlled**: first-count-instead-of-max fails 2 assertions, dropping `kind` from the merge key fails 1; `topup.ts` restored from a `cp` backup and confirmed **sha256 byte-identical** (never `git checkout`, per the standing rule).
  - **A dead export I had written myself:** `DEDUP_THRESHOLD` called itself "back-compat" for nothing — no importer anywhere in the repo, and its only "reference" was a stale sentence in the module docblock still naming it as the live gate. Both removed. **A back-compat alias for zero callers is not caution, it is clutter that misleads the next reader.**
  - **THE POPULATION MY OWN FIX NEWLY EXPOSED, and it is the thing an audit of this change had to check: the fresh-supply floor makes 388 leaves generation targets, 364 of them depth-2 — a population qgen had NEVER generated for.** Two specific risks: a NULL `exam_stage` would render `"Paper: X (undefined)"` straight into the prompt, and a leaf has no children so few-shot tier 2 returns nothing. Measured across all of them: **0 NULL/invalid `exam_stage`, 0 empty titles**, and of the **341** nodes topup would actually generate for, **0 get zero exemplars and 0 get a thin 1-4 set**. Clean — but it was not knowable without checking, because every earlier verification in the session ran on depth-1 nodes.
  - **A provenance trap in my own new code, safe today and now documented so it stays that way:** `freshTargetsFor` narrows generated supply by `questions.exam_code`, the column this repo repeatedly warns is PROVENANCE and must not define scope. It is safe only because a GENERATED row is stamped with the exam that generated it (**measured: 0 of 2,241 live generated rows disagree with their own node's exam**), and the real correctness boundary is the node-set check. The comment now says: if that stops holding, delete the filter — do not weaken the node check.
  - **Recorded, not fixed:** dedup is scoped PER NODE, so a coverage plan on a depth-1 section and a fresh plan on one of its depth-2 children could generate overlapping content in one night **without ever being compared**. Measured 0 such collisions today (the single node coverage fires on is childless), so latent rather than live; the real fix is subtree-aware dedup, which belongs with G14's stem+options work.
  - **Checked and deliberately left alone:** the childless `descendants()` lookup that 181 of 388 leaves now pay is **the minimum possible** — it IS the query that answers "does this node have children", so there is no cheaper way to know. Not waste.
  - **⚑ TWO CONCURRENT-SESSION HAZARDS, both caught before they did damage.** (1) `prompts:snapshot` reported **5 CHANGED keys, all `ca/*`** — the other session's in-flight `ca/prompts.ts`. **Running `--write` would have baked their prompt changes into the committed baseline under my commit**; 0 `qgen/*` keys changed by this audit, so nothing of mine needed writing. (2) The working tree failed `tsc` mid-audit, entirely from their `ca/sources.ts`. Proven rather than assumed by checking HEAD out into a **git worktree** (never `git stash` in this checkout) and typechecking there — **exit 0**. Every commit used `git commit -- <explicit paths>` and was re-checked with `git show --stat`.
  - **A false green in my own harness, the D15 trap hit again:** `tsc … | head` printed `EXIT=0` from `head`, and a standalone `tsc` invocation silently ignored the project config and reported unrelated module-resolution noise. Re-run bare, and check the exit code of the compiler rather than of the last pipe stage.
  - Verified after every fix: api typecheck **0 errors in my files**, `test:qgen` 17/17, `test:args` 316/316, `test:mentor` 13/13, `test:tips` 77/77, `check:paths` (858 files), `check:cli-args` (243 files), and **0 `qgen/*` prompt keys changed**. All scratch scripts deleted by explicit name; the concurrent session's `_tmp_g2_*` files were left untouched throughout.

### Continuing the audit (2026-08-13): the dedup thresholds were WRONG, and the retraction is the finding
Picking up G12 (prelims did not reach parity, −0.60). Diagnosing it produced a fix, and auditing that fix retracted part of an earlier commit. Rows G16-G18 in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md`; commits `8bf9ee3` + the docs record.
  - **G12's cause, measured rather than guessed: the prelims gap is DUPLICATION AND CRAFT, not an inability to write authentic MCQs.** Decomposed by dimension: `craft` **−0.77**, `substance` −0.67, `authenticity` only **−0.35**. **5 of 8 generated prelims items carry a judge flag** — 4 duplicate-ish across two pairs, 1 malformed option list — and **the 3 unflagged ones average 3.78 against real's 3.94, inside the ±0.35 noise floor.** So the arm is not uniformly weaker; a minority of duplicated items drags the mean. **Checked and ruled OUT as the cause:** option-structure defects are 4 of 2,167 generated MCQs (0.18%, 2 live), while real PYQs are worse at 3.90%.
  - **⚑ THE RETRACTION, and it is the most important thing here: the tightened dedup thresholds I shipped in `8d300d9` were derived from an UNDER-SAMPLED measurement and are reverted to 0.9.** That commit claimed "0 of 431 real descriptive pairs rejected" at 0.84 — **true of the sample, false of the population.** Sampling 12 nodes per kind and 10 questions each undercounts badly; measured EXHAUSTIVELY the conclusion inverts: **descriptive n=6,108 → 13 false rejections at 0.84 (3 at 0.90); MCQ n=14,031 → 24 false at 0.85 (5 at 0.90).**
  - **AND THE REASON IS NOT NOISE — THE COMMISSION GENUINELY RE-ASKS.** "Critically analyse the role of Sardar Patel in the Unification of India" (MAINS_GS1 **2025**) against "Discuss the role of Sardar Patel in the unification of India" (**2023**) sits at **0.9254**; UP irrigation projects 2022/2019 at 0.9178; the philosophical basis of probity 2018/2020 at 0.9034 — all real, distinct, separately-set questions. **So the "generated duplicate" and "authentic re-ask" distributions genuinely OVERLAP and no cosine threshold separates them, because semantically they are the same thing.** A generator producing something that close to an existing PYQ is producing something exam-authentic. **The panel's flagged near-duplicates (0.847-0.896) are therefore unreachable by this stage at any safe threshold** — duplication needs a different mechanism (comparing the FACT under test, or a same-run diversity constraint), not a tighter cosine. Recorded as G16/G18.
  - **Tightening would also have fought my own previous commit.** At 0.85, **24 of the 25 rejections are scaffolded formats** — assertion-reason and match-list, whose stems are long boilerplate — which is precisely the class `71ef3bd`'s corrected `formatGuidance` exists to produce MORE of. Two changes, one session, pulling against each other; only the exhaustive measurement made that visible.
  - **WHAT SURVIVES, and it is a real win: dedup now compares STEM + OPTIONS**, both sides rendered through one shared `dedupTextOf` (a mismatch there would produce meaningless similarities that still look like cosines). A match-list stem is boilerplate and all its content is in the options: two real published PYQs sharing the exact stem "Which of the following is not correctly matched?" embed at **1.0000** on the stem alone and **0.3621** with their options — under stem-only they were indistinguishable from a true duplicate at ANY threshold. **Strictly better at every threshold** (MCQ false rejections 11→5 at 0.90) and it removes the cosine-1.0 collision class outright. **Provable no-op for descriptive** (0 of 1,693 real descriptive PYQs render differently), so nothing about that kind changed.
  - **⚑ A REAL DATA DEFECT FOUND BY THE MEASUREMENT, NOT BY ANY PRODUCT SURFACE: a duplicated real PYQ is LIVE with CONFLICTING ANSWER KEYS.** `pyq:uppsc_prelims_2023_gs1:q102` and `:q106` are byte-identical in stem and options, both published and approved, but keyed **B** and **A** — so one of them marks a student wrong for the right answer. Exactly 1 such live pair exists bank-wide. **Not fixed here:** deciding which key is right needs the real 2023 answer key, and re-keying or deleting a live question is an owner call (G17).
  - **MY OWN VERIFICATION HARNESS WAS WRONG AGAIN — third time this session, and it nearly produced a false conclusion.** A check fed a node's own existing questions back through `dedupCandidates` and reported `maxSim=1.0000 duplicate=true` as "would reject a real question" — but the existing-bank fetch includes the very row being fed back, so it was **self-matching**. The real comparison had to be done pairwise, outside the DB round trip.
