Separately, an early probe rendered stems with `stem_i18n.en` only while the gate falls back to `stem_i18n.hi`, which manufactured 11 "empty" rows that do not exist. **Both times the code was fine and the harness was not — render through the SAME function the code uses, and never feed a row back against a set that contains it.**
  - **The generalisable rule, now written into the threshold's own docblock so it is not re-derived: a threshold derived from a SAMPLE of a population you can measure EXHAUSTIVELY is not a measurement.** Both dedup populations are a few thousand pairs — small enough to enumerate completely, which is what should have been done the first time.
  - Verified after the revert: api typecheck 0 errors in my files, `test:qgen` 17/17, **0 `qgen/*` prompt keys changed**, `check:paths` (859 files), `check:cli-args` (244 files); live re-check that the gate still fires (a node's own questions 8/8 flagged), still does not over-fire (a different node's real questions 0/8), and that the descriptive path is unchanged. The concurrent session's `ca/*` files and `_tmp_g2_*` scratch files were left untouched throughout; every commit used explicit paths and was re-checked with `git show --stat`.

## Floors raised for test-series supply + the qgen "stall" diagnosed (2026-08-13)
Raised `qgen/topup.ts`'s coverage and fresh-supply floors to build a genuinely larger pool for `docs/test-series-design.md` §6, and diagnosed the stall that doc records. **The stall was never an outage**, the coverage band turned out to be the wrong lever, and `MAX_PER_NODE` was raised, measured, and put back. Real spend **$1.08**, all of it the quality gate; every synthetic row deleted by the exact batch ids the run captured.
  - **⚑ THE STALL: `generation_batches` was silent 2026-08-01→08-10 because the planner CORRECTLY PLANNED NOTHING.** `docs/test-series-design.md` §6.6 read that silence as a broken workflow and pointed at the stale-checkout guard. Both are wrong. The planner writes a batch row only when it generates, so **silence is ambiguous between "broken" and "nothing to do"** — and on `origin/main`, which is what a `schedule:` trigger runs, only the COVERAGE floor existed, and at `[25..80]` it measured **0 of 14 uppsc prelims sections and 0 of 54 uppsc mains sections below floor**. It "resumed" on 08-11 with **7**, then **6**, then **4** questions — and those three numbers ARE the proof: one node (`upsc/UPSC_PRE_CSAT` "Interpersonal Skills"), floor 26, `have` 19→20→22, converging. Not a pipeline recovering; a single node filling. Stale-checkout is independently ruled out — `origin/main` took commits on every day of the gap, so the 14-day guard never fired. **Confirmed in real time rather than inferred: mid-session the production cron inserted `mcq req=4 acc=4 mode=batch` on exactly that node, the exact count the old-band dry-run predicted.** Diagnostic for this class is `qgen:topup --dry-run`; a green Actions run cannot distinguish the two.
  - **⚑ THE COVERAGE BAND IS A STEP FUNCTION, NOT A DIAL — so it cannot be the supply lever, and this is measured rather than argued.** Because `have` counts every published+approved question in the subtree and a mature section holds 71-292, on uppsc prelims: `[25..80]`→**0** questions, `[40..150]`→**18**, `[60..250]`→**872**, `[100..400]`→**2433**. There is nothing between "inert" and "floods the sections that are already richest". It is therefore sized as what it is — a cold-start net (`MCQ_FLOOR` [25..80]→**[40..150]**, `DESCRIPTIVE_FLOOR` [5..20]→**[10..40]**, 188 questions total today) — and the real lever is the per-LEAF FRESH-supply floor: `FRESH_MCQ_FLOOR` [3..12]→**[12..48]**, `FRESH_DESCRIPTIVE_FLOOR` [2..6]→**[3..10]**.
  - **Pool before→after a full fill:** uppsc prelims **1510→1796** (PRE_GS1 1240→1357, **PRE_CSAT 270→439**), upsc prelims **54→657** (UPSC_PRE_GS1 17→430, UPSC_PRE_CSAT 37→227), uppsc mains **180→991**, upsc mains **2→508**. **Prelims and Mains are deliberately NOT scaled by the same factor** (mcq 4x, descriptive ~1.7x): §6.3 measured Mains as "FULLY FEASIBLE from PYQs alone" while naming the Prelims CSAT ladder as the one real gap, and the tree agrees — the hottest CSAT leaves carry ZERO generated questions (uppsc `Arithmetic` hotness 47.3, `English Language Comprehension Skills` 59.4; upsc `Basic Numeracy` 139.4, `Comprehension` 123.0), because ca:run never maps a current-affairs item onto an aptitude skill.
  - **⚑ THE FLOOR SETS THE DESTINATION; `QGEN_BATCH_MAX_USD` SETS THE SPEED — which is the answer to "will this outpace review capacity?".** `trimToBudget` caps every night at one shared budget, so simulated on the real tree the weekly inflow is **~1,650 requested at BOTH the old and the new bands**; only the fill DURATION changes (3→8 nights, ~$31 one-time). If review cannot absorb it, the lever is the budget env var, not the band. And review is not currently the binding constraint: re-measured, the `needs_review` backlog is **381, not the 1,027 that doc claims**, and **none of it is generated MCQs** — qgen's 709 lifetime rows are 703 decided at a median 5.6 days, with a demonstrated burst of **495 decisions in one day**.
  - **⚑ BUT qgen OUTPUT HAS NO BULK-APPROVE PATH AND CA OUTPUT DOES** — `bulkApproveCaHighConfidence` is scoped `paper_code = CURRENT_AFFAIRS`. Measured: **99.6% of all qgen output meets `isHighConfidenceQuestion`, and the human agreed with that gate 97.2% of the time.** So the cheapest operational win available is generalising that path; it is worth ~1,600 hand-selections during this fill alone. Recorded as Q9, not built — auto-approving generated questions is a product decision.
  - **PROPORTIONALITY RE-VERIFIED AT THE NEW BAND, and it is the RANK correlation that shows it** (item 2 of the brief): rho(hotness, supply) uppsc prelims **0.088→0.483**, uppsc mains **0.169→0.852**, upsc prelims **0.176→0.865**, upsc mains **0.014→0.901**; top-10%-of-leaves share of the pool collapses 86.7→23.1 / 77.8→23.0 / 100→15.4; **leaves with ZERO generated go to 0 everywhere.** The previously-starved nodes are served — upsc `Basic Numeracy` 0→48, `Comprehension` 0→44, uppsc `Arithmetic` 0→37, `Medieval India` 5→26. **Honest residual: uppsc prelims stays the least proportional (0.483) because 1,510 of its 1,796 questions predate this change — a floor guarantees a minimum, it cannot un-skew supply that already exists.** And the linear band SATURATES at `max`, so the hottest leaves are relatively (not absolutely) under-served; the flagged ones are all at the band ceiling.
  - **⚑ `MAX_PER_NODE` 20→48 WAS TRIED, MEASURED, AND PUT BACK — the one place the brief's "raise it proportionally" instinct is wrong.** A real run at the raised counts: **count 10 → 10 accepted (100%), count 26 → 23 (88%), count 37 → 24 (65%)**, dedup rejecting 0/3/11. Embedded pairwise through Stage-D's own `dedupTextOf` against an equal sample of the SAME node's real PYQs, the count-37 set is **TIGHTER than real** (mean 0.432 / p95 0.732 vs real 0.398 / 0.559) while counts 10 and 26 match their controls (0.545 vs 0.555; 0.418 vs 0.434). `GEN_CHUNK` is 5, so count 37 is 8 calls each carrying `variantHint` ("avoid framings likely used in earlier sets") — pushing away from the dominant question form exactly when `formatGuidance` asks the generator to stay on it. **Cost of keeping it at 20: exactly one extra night (8 vs 7).** Lowering is cheap and raising is not, so the asymmetry decides it.
  - **QUALITY GATE — blind 4-judge panel, 48 items, 24 generated vs 24 real PYQs from the SAME nodes**, provenance hidden, deterministic `mulberry32` shuffle (seed 20260813), key sealed outside the repo. Noise floor overall ±0.377 / auth ±0.563 / craft ±0.278 / subs ±0.396. **PRELIMS MCQ (16v16): overall +0.14, authenticity −0.06, substance +0.19 — all INSIDE noise**, i.e. parity; only craft (+0.28) clears its floor and that is inflated by a malformed option in the REAL arm. **MAINS descriptive (8v8): +1.13** — but see the control-arm caveat below. **The defensible claim is "no quality deficit is detectable at the new volume", NOT "generated beats real".**
  - **⚑ THE PANEL FOUND, BLIND, EXACTLY THE TWO DEFECTS THE MEASUREMENTS PREDICTED.** (1) `duplicate_of_another_item` is the generated arm's dominant flag — **21 judge-votes vs REAL's 5**, and 3 of the 4 ≥2-judge duplicate pairs are GEN/GEN. (2) **All three CSAT items carrying a GS-only scaffold were flagged `off_format` by 3-4 of 4 judges** without the judges being told the paper's real format mix — independent corroboration of the `uppsc.qgen.csat: null` finding below.
  - **⚑ AND IT CLOSED THE DEDUP QUESTION FOR GOOD: the duplicate and legitimate-re-ask distributions are not merely overlapping (G16) — they are INVERTED.** The generated same-fact duplicates every judge caught measure **0.7833** (two "who founded Jaunpur" questions, same answer, different wording), **0.7613** (UP dairy), and **0.7921** (a generated item near-restating a REAL PYQ on UP silk schemes) — while G16 measured the commission's own legitimate re-asks at **0.9034-0.9254**. A threshold low enough to catch these sits **0.15 BELOW** where genuine repeat questions live. **Do not propose tuning `DEDUP_THRESHOLD_BY_KIND` again; the data now rules it out in both directions.** The fix must compare the ANSWER/FACT.
  - **⚑ `uppsc.qgen.csat` IS `null` AND THE RESULTING MIS-SHAPED OUTPUT IS LIVE — with a clean in-repo control.** UPSC has an authored CSAT-specific `formatGuidance`; UPPSC deliberately does not, so UPPSC CSAT is generated under the **General Studies** guidance. Measured against **each exam's own real corpus** (the comparison must be per-exam — UPSC CSAT genuinely uses numbered-statement combination options at 12.7%, UPPSC's does not at 0.6%): GS-only scaffolds run **uppsc real ~2% vs generated 24.1% (12x)** while **upsc real ~13% vs generated 14.3% (parity)**. Same generator, same pipeline; the exam with the slot authored matches its corpus, the one without does not. **70 of 291 LIVE generated uppsc CSAT questions carry a GS-only scaffold**, and the text shows the mechanism is worse than format — they are recall items ("Assertion (A): Sympathy and Empathy are the same concept") on a paper that tests skills. **This floor raise sends 169 of uppsc's new questions into exactly that path.** Deliberately NOT fixed in the same commit: authoring the slot changes the live exam's prompts and its own docblock says it needs 3-arm control validation, so mixing it with a floor change would make both unattributable.
  - **⚑ THE REAL-PYQ CONTROL ARM IS DEGRADED, AND NOT ONLY IN `MAINS_GS3` (G13).** All 10 lowest-scoring items in the pack are REAL. Causes: an ingest defect (`…2020_gs1:q25` stores option D as `"1 3 4 4"`, a repeated code), lost superscripts (`…2024_csat:q65` renders `x³ – 6x²` as `"x3 – 6x2"`), and **a live wrong answer key** — `…2024_csat:q62` "3-digit largest prime number is" keys **C = 999**, which is 3³×37; the answer is **D = 997**. It is published, approved, `key_provenance = official_commission`, `answer_key_verified = true`, and carries no `blind_resolve`. **Plus a genuine INSTRUMENT bias**: real UPPSC Mains questions ARE often short and direct ("What are the main features of…"), and judges marked exactly those down as "shallow for Mains" — so `substance` partly measures a preference for elaborate framing. Both reasons are why the Mains +1.13 must not be read as generated superiority.
  - **HARNESS LESSONS.** (1) **Three subagents reported "48 items scored, file written" with `tool_uses: 0` in ~8 seconds and had written nothing** — the repo's own "check the output path before believing a report" rule, in the false-POSITIVE direction. Verified rather than trusted; re-dispatched with the items INLINE and the JSON as the reply, so no tool use was needed either way. (2) A background command piped through `| tail -40` buffers everything to process exit, so a Monitor tailing its output file sees nothing until the end — fine for a completion signal, useless for progress. (3) `cd` does not persist between Bash calls here; two scratch files landed in the repo ROOT (no `"type": "module"`, so top-level `await` fails with a confusing `cjs` error) before I switched to absolute paths.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `test:qgen` 17/17 · `test:args` 316/316 · `test:mentor` 13/13 · `test:tips` 77/77 · `prompts:snapshot` **168 byte-identical** (constants and comments only — no prompt text touched) · `check:paths` (859) · `check:cli-args` (244). **Teardown proven, not assumed:** 57 questions + 3 `generation_batches` rows deleted by the exact ids captured at insert time, then re-queried — 0 remain, the production cron's own batch **survives**, and the residual +16 rows are fully accounted for as ca:run (12) and the production qgen cron (4).

### Acting on the four findings, same day (2026-08-13): a whole mis-keyed paper, and three shipped fixes
Asked to fix what the floor session had found. Three of the four are closed; the fourth turned out to be far larger than recorded and is quarantined with its repair specified. **$0.17 of generation; every scratch script removed.**
  - **⚑ G22 WAS NOT ONE WRONG KEY — THE WHOLE 2024 UPPSC CSAT PAPER IS MIS-KEYED, 100 live questions, ~63% wrong.** The recorded finding (one item keying 999 as the largest 3-digit prime) was the visible corner of a **systematic positional misapplication**, verified against the official marked booklet **D.STF-2-24**: booklet Q1-Q5's answers `(a, d, b, d, c)` are exactly our q1-q5 keys `(A, D, B, D, C)` — **while our q1 IS booklet Q17, our q3 IS booklet Q19 and our q5 IS booklet Q14.** The key was applied BY POSITION onto a question list that is a shuffle of the booklet, so each row carries a different question's answer. Independently confirmed by direct computation on the arithmetic items — 15 of 24 spot-checked keys wrong (LCM(306,657) keyed 22318 not 22338; 53×34+21 keyed 1800 not 1823; subsets of a 3-element set keyed 9 not 8). A second, separate extraction defect compounds it: our option order is an **A↔D swap** of the booklet's on **24/24** checked.
  - **ROOT CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL AND EXPLAINS WHY ONLY THIS PAPER: 2024 is the only ingest with no key cross-check at all** — `blind_resolve` 0/100 (CSAT) and 0/150 (GS1), `key_series` 0, `series_alignment` 0, against **145/145** for 2023 GS1 and **150/150** for 2020 GS1. It was the first paper ever ingested, predates `ingest:resolve`, and was never re-gated.
  - **⚑ AND 2024 GS1 WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN INFERRED — IT IS CLEAN.** Same ingest run, same missing gate, so the tempting move was to quarantine both. Verified against its own booklet instead: **7/7 keys correct and option order identical**, so its 132 live rows were deliberately left alone. Acting on "same run, same gap" would have wrongly pulled 132 good questions. Its only defect is 3 rows with corrupted option arrays, all already unpublished.
  - **ACTION: all 100 CSAT rows set `is_published=false` + `needs_review` with `meta.key_quarantine` carrying the diagnosis.** Reversible, uses the app's own state, preserves every row's text and options. **Downstream cost measured rather than discovered later: 411 `test_questions` rows across 47 tests** — `mock:PRE_CSAT:3` drops to **83/100**, `sectional:PRE_CSAT:english-comprehension` to 78/113, and **one user's custom test to 0/5, i.e. empty**. That is the price of not mis-grading: a full-length mock carrying 17 wrong keys produces a meaningless rank. 21 existing attempts are historical and untouched. **REPAIR (not attempted here — hand-transcribing 100 answers from scans risks writing new wrong keys): content-match each row to its booklet question, take that question's marked answer TEXT, and set the key to whichever of OUR options carries that text — never reuse letters.** `ingest:align-key` exists for exactly this, and re-keying restores all 47 tests.
  - **G21 CLOSED — `uppsc.qgen.csat` authored, and the repo contained its own control.** Same generator, same pipeline: GS-only scaffolds run **uppsc real ~2% vs generated 24.1% (12x)** while **upsc real ~13% vs generated 14.3% (parity)** — and upsc is the exam that HAS the slot authored. Per family the damage is concentrated, not spread: Interpersonal & Communication real 76% direct / 3% AR vs generated **34% / 23%**; stems 2-3× too long everywhere (real medians 48-117 chars by family, generated 106-378). **UPSC's CSAT text was deliberately NOT copied — the two papers are near opposites**: UPSC CSAT is ~a third self-contained passage items at a ~230-char median, UPPSC's is **0.1% over 400 chars with a median of 72**, because its comprehension passages are printed once for a block. UPPSC also has Hindi and English LANGUAGE sections, which UPSC's CSAT lacks entirely.
  - **⚑ G21 VALIDATED EMPIRICALLY — generated on that very node under the new norm** (12 requested, 6 accepted, $0.32; the other 6 were dedup rejections, the node already holding 182 questions). Same classifier for all three arms: **REAL** n=96 medLen **67** direct **76%** AR 3% match 0% stmts 2%; **generated under the GS guidance** n=86 medLen **167** direct **34%** AR **23%** match **15%** stmts **13%**; **generated under the NEW norm** n=6 medLen **112** direct **83%** AR **0%** match **0%** stmts **0%**. **Every GS-only scaffold went to zero and direct-single moved 34% → 83% against a 76% target**, and the stems read as real CSAT — situational judgement ("what should the manager ideally do?") in place of "Assertion (A): Sympathy and Empathy are the same concept". **Two honest limits: n=6 is tiny (one item = 17%), and stem length improved 167 → 112 but did NOT reach the real median of 67**, so that half of the norm is only partly effective. Rows torn down by exact batch id (0 remain).
  - **GOTCHA, and it is the D15 trap for the third time in this thread: the first attempt at that run reported "exit code 0" while node had actually thrown.** The command was piped through `| tail -40`, so the reported status was `tail`'s. The real failure was `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND api.anthropic.com` — a DNS drop 47 minutes into polling an Anthropic batch that had never left Stage A. **Confirmed it wrote nothing** (the `generation_batches` insert happens only after polling succeeds — 0 batches, 0 questions on that node), then re-ran through the SYNC path instead, which cost 2x and finished in under a minute. **Do not pipe a command whose exit code you intend to trust.**
  - **⚑ THE SNAPSHOT HARNESS HAD NO uppsc-CSAT FIXTURE, so this change moved a real prompt while the harness said "168 byte-identical".** Every other uppsc qgen fixture carries `FIXTURE_PAPER`, which can never equal `papers.prelimsCsat`, so all of them take the GS branch. Added the missing fixture (plus the critic's), importing `PRELIMS_CSAT_PAPER_CODE` rather than retyping it. Result: **0 changed, 0 missing, 2 new** — no existing prompt byte moved, and the LIVE exam's aptitude norm is guarded from here on. Wiring separately verified **13/13** (CSAT branch taken, GS untouched, `upsc` unaffected, the frozen `uppsc_tone` schema key intact in both).
  - **G25 CLOSED — Stage-D now catches same-FACT duplicates by ANSWER, the half cosine provably cannot do.** Measured **exhaustively**, per G16's own lesson that a sample is not a measurement: **84 false rejections in 59,238 real same-node pairs (0.142%) against 80 generated duplicates caught**, and inspection shows a good share of the 84 are genuine commission re-asks. Two design points, both decided on data rather than intuition: **combination closures are excluded** and that is load-bearing (comparing them takes false rejections **84 → 1,385**, because ~2/3 of MCQ answers in this bank ARE closures like "2 and 3 only"); and **a single number is kept while a list of small integers is a code** — excluding bare numerics whole was tried and is worse (8 fewer caught, no better rate), so the feared "two different sums, one answer" collision simply does not appear. **No stem-similarity floor**, deliberately: the true positives sit at jaccard **0.00-0.20**, exactly where text similarity is blind. `test:qgen` 17 → **41** assertions; the exclusion is negative-controlled (disabling it fails 5) and `dedup.ts` was restored **byte-identically, sha256 verified**.
  - **G23 partly closed.** Fixed the 4 live notation defects the scan found (`x1, x2,...,xn` → `x₁, x₂, …, xₙ`; `μg/m3` → `μg/m³`; `1 m3`/`10000 m2` → `m³`/`m²`), each targeted by exact `external_id` AND an exact expected substring so a row whose text had changed would be skipped rather than blindly rewritten. Scope was measured, not guessed: only 4 candidates bank-wide. Two published rows with a duplicated option text remain — repairing those needs the original paper, so inventing a distractor was declined.
  - **A TEST CAUGHT MY OWN RULE, which is why it was written first.** The initial combination-answer classifier rejected `"22338"` — an LCM answer — as a code. Rather than patch by intuition I measured both treatments over the whole bank and let the data choose. **Process note: my earlier claim that a wrong key here was "an owner call, not an audit's" (the G17 precedent) was right about re-keying and wrong about doing nothing** — G17's ambiguity was which of two keys is correct; here the answers are arithmetically decidable and the paper is provably broken, so quarantine was available and inaction was not neutral.

#### Edge-case audit of those fixes, same day: 5 defects in my own changes and 2 live consequences of the quarantine
  - **⚑ THREE DEVANAGARI DEFECTS IN THE NEW DEDUP RULE, all latent, all mine.** (1) **The normaliser dropped `\p{M}`** — matras and the anusvara are Unicode MARKS, not Letters, so `[^\p{L}\p{N}\s]` stripped them and **दोनों ("both") shatters to "द न" — as does दिन ("day")**, i.e. two unrelated Hindi words normalise to the SAME string and would be called duplicates (same for नहीं vs नहि). Verified by codepoint, not by eye. (2) **`isCombinationAnswer` was ASCII-only**, so every real Hindi closure passed through as a substantive answer; replaced with a script-agnostic token rule that requires BOTH that every token is a marker or a bare index AND that at least one marker is present, so a bare number ("22338", a real LCM answer) is still kept. (3) **`answerTextOf` used `??` where it needed `||`** — an option whose English text failed to extract stores an EMPTY string, which is not nullish, so the Hindi fallback the line exists for could never fire.
  - **All three are LATENT** (every MCQ in the bank carries English option text), and the measured numbers confirm it: real false rejections unchanged at **84 of 59,238 = 0.142%**, generated duplicates caught **80 → 83**. `test:qgen` **41 → 51**. ⚑ **Worth keeping: (3) also means an earlier "0 rows use the hi fallback" measurement was MEANINGLESS — it was counting rows the operator had already excluded. The conclusion (0 affected today) survived, but only by re-measuring, not by that reasoning.** And the test written first is what exposed (3), while the Devanagari cases exposed (1).
  - **⚑ THE QUARANTINE CREATED TWO LIVE PROBLEMS OF ITS OWN.** (a) **200 ORPHANED embedding chunks** — `ingest:embed` only covers `is_published = true`, so unpublishing the 100 questions left their chunks in the RAG store still retrievable, and that is worse than it sounds because mentor retrieval now APPENDS the verified correct option to a question chunk (G4-A): the mentor could have stated a known-wrong key **as verified**. Deleted, scoped by exact id — verifier orphans **439 → 339**, coverage 100%, 0 missing. **The other 339 are PRE-EXISTING and deliberately NOT purged**; `--purge-orphans` clears all 439 at once and those predate this session. (b) **5 tests left with ZERO servable questions**, one of them published and listable (`pyq:PRE_CSAT:2024`) plus 4 user `custom` sets — `startAttempt` throws "No questions to attempt", the Session-12 listed-but-unstartable failure mode. All 5 unpublished with `meta.emptied_by_key_quarantine` recording why and that they return when the paper is re-keyed; all had 0 attempts.
  - **The notation repair had been ENGLISH-ONLY** — 3 of 3 rows still showed the broken form in Hindi on a Hindi-equal-first product. Fixed, and those rows' embeddings (stale, since the stem changed but the chunks predated the edit) deleted and regenerated via `ingest:embed --missing-only`.
  - **⚑ AND MY AUDIT CHECK FOR THAT WAS ITSELF WRONG — the harness-before-the-code trap, third time in this thread.** It reported `upsc…2022_gs1:q44`'s Hindi as clean because the regex used the GREEK mu (μ, U+03BC) that the ENGLISH stem uses, while the Hindi carries the MICRO SIGN (µ, U+00B5) — two different codepoints that render identically. The Hindi did still say `µg/m3`. Caught only by a final consolidated check that matched on the UNIT rather than on the preceding character. **Match on the token you actually care about, not on a neighbouring character that may differ by codepoint between locales.**
  - Verified after every fix: `tsc` 0 · `test:qgen` **51/51** · `test:args` 316/316 · `test:mentor` 13/13 · `test:tips` 77/77 · `prompts:snapshot` **170 byte-identical** · `check:paths` (860) · `check:cli-args` (245).

## CA generation volume raised 40 -> 70 (2026-08-13)
Same pattern as the qgen floor raise earlier the same day: Session 12 fixed CA relevance (`ce909bb`, a triage-prompt tightening on durability) and topic coverage (`c1df806`, subject desks + fair rotation); this raises the POOL now that the quality is trustworthy. `src/ca/volume.ts` (new) is the one home for the three run-budget knobs; `MAX_TOTAL` 40 -> 70, `MAX_PER_SOURCE` (15) and `DAYS` (3) deliberately unchanged on measurement. Commits `d26cfec`, `92eb3de`, `a3cb751`, `a6dcc86`.
  - **⚑ THEY WERE IN TWO PLACES.** `ca:run`'s CLI defaults and `ca/scheduler.ts`'s dev node-cron each carried their own inline `40/15/3`, so raising the CLI alone would have left a local `pnpm dev` quietly running the old volume against the same production DB. `runPipeline` defaults nothing, so there is no third copy.
  - **NOT the CA quiz SET sizes** (`assemble.ts`'s `PRELIMS_MAX`/`DAILY_*`) — those are downstream assembly. This is the pool they draw from.
  - **WHAT THE MEASUREMENT SAID.** Supply is not the limit: all 11 feeds through the pipeline's own local filter gave **228 fresh eligible items** at `--days 3`, a run took 40 and left **188** behind, 129 of them under 24h old. Reviewer CAPACITY is not the limit: backlog 25, all under 24h old, against a demonstrated **778 questions actioned in one day** and 1,889 lifetime, versus generation at ~30-47/day. What IS starved is the **daily mains sitting** — per-set fill since the caps were raised is daily prelims 15/15, 7/15, 15/15, 15/15; weekly prelims 50/50; weekly mains 19/20; **daily mains 1/5, 2/5, 2/5, 1/5, 1/5, 2/5, never full** — because only **4.1%** of items score `mains == 3`, the only tier that yields a descriptive question, against demand of 5/day/exam x 2 live exams.
  - **⚑ DO NOT AVERAGE THE SET FILL OVER ALL SETS.** The weekly caps were raised 20->50 and 5->20 on 2026-08-12, so older sittings were built at a lower cap and drag the mean into a weekly-prelims starvation that does not exist. Per-set, oldest-first, is the only honest read. I nearly reported the artifact.
  - **WHY 70 AND NOT MORE — THE WALL CLOCK, NOT THE MONEY.** Triage is batched and off the critical path; collect costs 1 enrich per kept item plus node-classify and MCQ generation PER RELEVANT EXAM. Measured **1.9 sequential calls/kept item at one live exam, 2.78 at two** (each extra exam ~+0.88; a third lands near 3.7). Across the six real dual-exam runs of 2026-08-11/12: 64-79 critical calls, 10.5-12.7 min, one 17.8 outlier => 0.164 min/call typical, 0.262 worst. Against a 50-minute timeout (+~2 min setup): 40 -> 11/18 min (2 exams) and 14/23 (3); **70 -> 19/31 and 25/40**; 100 -> 27/44 and **36/58, which exceeds the cap**. 70 is the largest budget that survives a worst-rate run AFTER a third exam goes live. Raise it further and raise `timeout-minutes` in the same commit.
  - **⚑ AND THAT TABLE ASSUMED ONE BATCH PER RUN, WHICH IS NOT GUARANTEED — found on a SECOND edge-case pass by testing the load-bearing assumption of my own model instead of re-running its happy path** (`9924f73`, `2540a39`). `collectBatch` is called in a **loop over every entry `listPendingBatches()` returns**, so a batch that has not finished before the next 6h tick submits another leaves the run after that collecting BOTH. Measured over 97 real batches: submit->collect p50 **5.47h against the 6h cadence**, p90 7.18h, and **1 of 95 collect events (1%) collected 2 batches** (max ever 2) — rare, not hypothetical. At 2 x 70 = 140 items the same arithmetic gives **40 min typical / 63 worst** at two exams and 53/83 at three, so **the OLD 40 budget was safe even on a double-collect (24/37, 31/49) and 70 was not.** `ca-run.yml`'s `timeout-minutes` raised **50 -> 75** to cover the measured two-exam worst case. A three-exam worst-rate double-collect (83 min) is still uncovered and that is ACCEPTED, not chased: it compounds three tail events, and a hard-cancel degrades safely — per-item persistence, uncollected ledger rows stay `pending` and retry, an already-persisted item re-inserts as 23505 `skippedDuplicate`. The one real casualty is `ca:embed`, whose `if: !cancelled()` does not run on a timeout; nightly-settle's own `ca:embed` closes that within a day.
  - **A SECOND CEILING, found only by auditing my own commit: `MAX_PER_SOURCE` caps a run at `sum of min(supply, 15)`, measured at 99 items on real feeds.** So a budget past ~85 buys progressively less and past 99 buys nothing at all until that is raised too. My first draft claimed raising it "would change behaviour ONLY on days when few desks have anything" — **wrong, and wrong in the direction that matters**, since someone raising MAX_TOTAL to 120 would quietly have got 99 with no way to see why. The conclusion (leave it at 15) was right for 70; the reason attached to it was not.
  - **TWO MORE OF MY OWN CLAIMS CORRECTED, both overstated the same way — confident, tidy, not quite what the data says.** (1) "The budget binds on EVERY run ... 160.4/day = exactly 4 runs x 40" is too neat: clustering 21 days of items gives **4.7 runs/day** (manual and dev-scheduler runs happen on top of the cron), a **median of 38** against the 40 cap, and only 47% landing in 38-42 — 49 of 99 came in under 38, i.e. supply ran out before the budget did. What survives is "binds on a TYPICAL run", whose evidence is better anyway: the run log prints "budget spent" with leftovers. (2) "The reviewer is over-provisioned by an order of magnitude" is true of capacity and **misleading about latency**, which is what governs conversion: a daily sitting draws on `created_at` within `[day-1, day+1)` AND approved (`approvedCaQuestionIds`), so a question has about ONE DAY to be approved or it never enters the sitting it was generated for. Measured on the last 7 days: **p50 21.9h, 53% inside 24h** — so roughly HALF of what is generated reaches its own sitting. That argues FOR the raise (the shortfall is ~2x and volume is the only lever not requiring the reviewer to change habits) but it is not 1:1. **Quote the 7-day window, never the lifetime p50 of 209h**, which is dominated by historical catch-up bursts and describes a regime that no longer holds.
  - **VERIFIED AT THE NEW VOLUME AGAINST A REAL RUN, not a simulation.** A production batch (30 items) was pending from `origin/main`'s pre-Session-12 code, submitted from **only two feeds** — the concentration defect visible live — so its 30 content_hashes were captured up front and excluded by hash (smoke-tested: 30 of 32 excluded). The 70-item batch was then submitted and drained. Results on those 70: **gate-reject 25.7% vs a 40.0% 21-day baseline; prelims>=2 30.0% vs 32.8% (parity); mains==3 11.4% vs 4.1%**; 8 desks contributing 10/10/10/9/9/9/9/4 with the economy desk keeping **9/9** and environment **4/4**; **155 distinct syllabus nodes** touched by 70 items, the top-mapped being Fiscal and Monetary Policy and Indian Economy — the exact class that had 0-2 items in the whole 5,178-item corpus before Session 12. **22 questions generated across BOTH exams** (uppsc 7 mcq + 2 descriptive, upsc 10 mcq + 3 descriptive) placed on **19 distinct nodes**, and **5 mains descriptive from one run** against the 1 the previous 30-item collect produced.
  - **⚑ THE MARGINAL-ITEM TEST is the one that actually answers "does it hold at volume".** Rotation is round-robin, so items 41-70 of a run are exactly the ones the old budget never reached — deeper, slightly older items from the SAME desks, not new desks. Measured within the single run: items 1-40 kept 77.5% / prelims>=2 30.0% / meanP 1.27, items 41-70 kept 70.0% / prelims>=2 **30.0%** / meanP 1.17. The 7.5pp keep-rate difference is inside binomial noise at n=30 (SD ~7.9pp) and `prelims>=2` is identical. **No degradation at the margin.** Do not read the marginal set's higher `mains==3` (16.7% vs 7.5%) as an improvement — that is 5 of 30 against 3 of 40, noise.
  - **AND A STRUCTURAL PREDICTION, confirmed twice: raising the budget DEEPENS every desk rather than re-shaping the mix.** Max per-desk share drift between 40 and 70 was **1.1pp** on an even-supply snapshot and 2.9pp on the run's own skewed one. Breadth is owned by rotation (Session 12); volume multiplies it. It does NOT extend indefinitely — the subject desks are shallow (economy 13, science 14, environment 4 fresh) where the general feeds are deep (39, 89), so the three general feeds' share goes 40.0% at MAX_TOTAL 40 -> 42.9% at 70 -> 44.4% at the 99 ceiling. A second, independent reason 70 is the stopping point.
  - **INFLATION SPECIFICALLY (the named check): "Retail inflation reaches 19-month high of 4.45% in July 2026" was captured and scored P2/M3** — top mains tier — from `the-hindu-economy`, plus a monetary-policy item (polymer banknotes, P2/M2). Baseline for contrast: monetary policy was **1 item in the entire 5,178-item corpus**. ⚑ **My own sweep regex over-counted it**: `\bcpi\b` matched "CPI(M)" in an obituary of a Marxist theorist, so the genuine count for that run is 1 inflation item, not 2.
  - **THE DURABILITY GATE FIRES AT VOLUME**, seen live: "Quarkitech raises Rs 2 crore in pre-seed funding" ARCHIVED with "a named startup but the news item is purely transient — funding rounds do not survive the news cycle as testable facts". That is `ce909bb`'s tightening working on exactly the marginal material the raise newly reaches.
  - **REVIEW LATENCY DEMONSTRATED IN-SESSION:** the run's 5 descriptive questions were inserted `needs_review` at 07:15-07:32 UTC and approved by a human at **08:45-08:48, all five inside a 2-minute window** — 74-93 min, well inside the daily-sitting window. All 17 MCQs remained `needs_review`. Confirms the code reading (nothing is auto-approved; measured lifetime, 87 CA descriptive approved against 1 rejected).
  - **A LIVE BUG IN A NEIGHBOUR, found by asking which reads the raise makes grow** (`a6dcc86`): `ca/verify-mcqs.ts` had **no `.order()` anywhere** while paging `current_affairs_items where mcq_question_ids <> '{}'` — **1,206 rows against PAGE_SIZE 1000, already past one page**. A skipped row drops its questions from the facts map, and a question with no facts is deliberately skipped rather than blind-verified, so the failure mode is CA MCQs quietly never getting their confidence check. **HONEST STATUS: not observed biting** — paging the old query twice returned the same complete 1,206 rows, because an unchanging table read by a seq scan comes back in physical order; the realistic trigger is a concurrent writer, and `ca-run` writes that table throughout while sharing no concurrency group with `ca-verify-mcqs`. A sweep of all of `src/ca` found exactly this one real site (`candidate-prefilter` is `.in()` chunking over bounded 40-id sets, not offset paging).
  - **CROSS-VERSION BATCH STRADDLE IS SAFE**, checked because a bigger batch takes longer and is likelier to be collected by a different code version (this one took **>30 min**, exceeding `--wait 30`): `RELEVANCE_GATE = 2` on both `origin/main` and HEAD, and Session 12's change is purely a triage PROMPT already baked into submitted requests. Migration `0125`'s `source_snippet` is absent on `origin/main`, so old code simply leaves that column null.
  - **COST, disclosed:** $0.0469 per kept item measured in the dual-exam era. Anthropic ~$4.5 -> ~$7.9/day (~$135 -> ~$236/month); GitHub Actions ~56 -> ~92 min/day, i.e. **~1,700 -> ~2,800 min/month for ca-run alone** against the 2,000/month a Free private repo has historically had (`docs/operations.md`) — it was already near the line and this takes it past. This session's own verification spend was ~$3.35.
  - **⚑ 70 DOES NOT CLOSE THE MAINS GAP** (~1.5/5 -> roughly 2.5-3/5). The bottleneck is the 4.1% `mains == 3` rate, not the budget; closing it by volume alone needs ~400 items/day, which the wall clock forbids. The cheaper follow-ups are that gate, or a second descriptive question from a mains-3 item rich enough for two — both quality changes needing their own validation, not a knob.
  - **⚑ INERT IN PRODUCTION UNTIL `origin/main` MOVES.** It is 22 commits behind and Session 12's CA fixes are not deployed either, so the scheduled cron is still running the pre-Session-12 pipeline.
  - **THREE HARNESS ERRORS, all mine, none in the product** — the recurring lesson in this file, hit again: (1) `pgrep -f "src/ca/run.ts"` **matched the waiter shell's own command line**, so every wait loop spun forever and reported "STILL RUNNING" long after `ca:run` had exited — wait on a recorded PID with `kill -0`, never a pattern that matches your own waiter. (2) A sweep for unordered paged reads flagged the very line I had just fixed, because the ~1,100-char comment I added pushed `.order(` outside the regex's 900-char lookback. (3) `cd apps/api && tsc` printed `TSC_EXIT=1` from the failed `cd`, and I committed on that false green before re-running properly (exit 0, and the commit proven comment-only by diffing non-comment lines).
  - Also noted, out of scope: **magazine deep dives are at 4 ever, none since 2026-07-10**, despite a monthly cron.
